The desk beside the pane is on screen whatever the lane's own open state, so a press in a page's body there, from the first press after ⌘. or ⌘E, makes that page the keyboard's as on the usual screen, and ⌘W and the other View chords act on it (`deskOnScreen`, the web's form of the core's `pair_desk_drawn`).
The fold lasts only while that pane stays on the left: moving to another pane, leaving the mode or a restart brings the desk back standing, and so does a View opened or chosen onto it (D-57).
Pane zoom stays its own command (D-65, replacing D-57's merge of zoom into focus mode): ⇧⌘↩ (Alt+Shift+Enter on Windows and Linux) on a pane, and the menus' Zoom item, fill that tab's terminal area with the pane, tab by tab, whatever the desk does; only ⌘. turns focus mode on and off, and the menus keep Focus mode as its own item.
When the agent of a pane drawn alone opens a View, it comes onto the desk in front and the desk stands beside the pane as it does for any pane in focus mode (D-53).
A file the operator opens onto that tab's desk while the pane is drawn alone, from the Explorer, ⌘P or ⌘K, or by `hide file open` in that pane, stands the desk beside it with the file in front, and the pane stays on the left since the operator opened it (D-21); the desk's last View closing then leaves the pane alone again with the mode on.
The left half draws the pane's own terminal, moved from its tab's canvas, never a copy; the tab's other panes are out of sight with their terminals alive, fed and at their size, and the tab's pane layout and the engine's zoom are not touched while the mode is on (D-16, D-26).
The left terminal's grid changes once on entering and once on leaving, and once each time ⌘E folds or stands the desk beside it (B29, D-57); the other panes are out of sight already, so ⇧⌘↩ on the pane does nothing while focus mode is on and the menus' Zoom item is off with why (B29, D-65).
The desk takes the right side; a tool opened meanwhile stands beside the desk at its width, the desk keeping the rest (B15), and Expand, the desk's button beside its `✕` or ⇧⌘↩ with the keyboard on the desk, fills the body with the desk and returns to the pane and desk, focus mode staying on and the left terminal keeping its width under it so neither step resizes it; leaving focus mode while the desk is expanded leaves the lane closed and unexpanded (B29, D-59).
The divider between the left terminal and the desk is the lane's divider part: dragging it moves a guide and lands once on release, so the left terminal's grid changes once per drag; the split is one ratio for the mode, kept while focus mode is off and after a restart, each side kept above its own minimum; a body too narrow for both minimums splits at the ratio alone (D-47).

Focus mode is unmistakable in three places at once (D-17, B22, D-61): the tab row's place holds a band in the focus mode accent's tint, `◧ 집중 모드`, the name the left pane's head shows, and `원래 화면으로 ⌘.`, which leaves; the left pane's ◧ is drawn pressed in the accent, its hint and accessible name `원래 화면으로 ⌘.`; and the same mark stands on the paired agent's sidebar row.
The band is as tall as the tab row, so the body is as tall as outside the mode and entering moves no terminal row; while the mode is on the tab row, its group selector and its + are hidden under the band and out of the pointer's and the keyboard's reach, and the operator moves to another pane or tab with ⌃⇥ and the sidebar, the band's name following the pane the mode moves to.
The band holds nothing else: no to-do, question or note chips (D-62); the desk draws no pin, which is a way in only (D-42), and an expanded desk covers the band as it covers the tab row.
The desk keeps its `✕` at the right end of its tab row, the same part, icon, hint and place as on the usual screen, after the `⋯` and Expand (the ◧ beside it is not drawn); in focus mode it is ⌘E's fold, the same `pair_view_desk` event and not a lane close, so the pane takes the full width with the mode still on (`closeDocuments` in `actions.ts`, D-57, D-63).
The left and right always have one owner (D-09): bringing another agent's desk tab in front takes that agent's pane to the left, a tab the operator opened leaves the left pane (B24, D-21), and choosing another pane of the tab from the sidebar, on this machine or a device, takes it to the left with its own View on the right, or, when it has nothing to show, draws it alone with the mode still on, so the pane chosen is on screen (B25, D-45, D-53).
Entering by ◧ (the pane's or the desk's), ⌘., and choosing another pane from the sidebar, gives the left pane the keyboard, so the keys, ⇧⌘W and the text scale reach the pane on screen; a desk tab the operator brings in front moves the left pane and leaves the keyboard on the desk, where the operator pressed, and leaving gives the keyboard back to the pane that held it before entering (B26).
The desk's tab strip shows only the left pane's tabs, what its agent opened and what its terminal's links opened, by the one owner rule below, and what the operator opens while focus mode is on (an Explorer, ⌘P or ⌘K open), which stands in front (D-60, narrowing D-41).
Every other tab folds into one `다른 것 N ▾` (`Others N`) menu at the strip's end: another agent's, in the same Agent tab or another, listed under its agent, and what the operator opened before the mode, listed under `내가 연 것` (`Opened by you`); choosing one brings it in front, an agent of the same Agent tab taking the left with it (D-41).
The rule holds the same after a close and after the mode moves to another pane or tab: a closed front tab is followed by the newest tab the strip shows, never by a folded one; the core names the folded tabs (`folded` on the focus mode), and the web only reads them.
Which pane a desk tab belongs to is the core's one rule (`owner_pane` on each display): an agent's View its opener's pane now, a card its owner's (a record's or a plan's session, a mission's Conductor, the agent that made a pull request), as the card's path line shows it, and a View opened from a pane's terminal links that pane, by its terminal; the last open names it, so an agent's View a shell's link opens again is the shell's (D-54).
The sidebar stays visible and live; a hidden pane that waits on the operator reaches Needs You, and focus mode draws nothing more for it (B23).
The band's `원래 화면으로`, the pressed ◧ or ⌘. from anywhere leaves focus mode for the tab's terminals alone: every pane of the tab comes back at its place and size, a tab zoomed before the mode is unzoomed, and the lane is closed, desk and tool column alike, so leaving looks the same whatever the screen was before (operator decision 2026-11-24, replacing B26's screen before and D-51).
Focus mode is one mode for the window, not a tab's (D-45, replacing D-15): while it is on, going to another agent's pane by ⌃⇥, the sidebar or a tab switch keeps it, that pane on the left and its agent's View in front of its own tab's desk; a pane with nothing to show, such as a shell, is drawn alone at the full width (D-53, replacing D-45 and D-49's tab layout); turning it off turns it off everywhere, and nothing turns it on again when the operator comes back to a tab.
The mode is saved, so a restart finds it on, drawn on the pane the operator is on.
Only ⌘. (Ctrl+Shift+. on Windows and Linux), the pressed ◧, the band's `원래 화면으로` and the left pane closing end it (D-46); a View closing or leaving the desk never does (D-64, replacing D-46's end on the left agent's last View): the desk stands only while it holds a tab the strip shows, so once the left pane's tabs and what the operator opened during the mode are gone it folds and the pane is drawn alone over the body with the mode on, the folded tabs staying folded and never coming in front (D-53, D-60); ⌘E folds and stands the desk with the mode on (D-57), Escape in the desk only moves the keyboard, and the desk draws neither its `✕` nor its pin.
The sidebar and the rail stay as they are in focus mode, and the rail's desk button is ⌘E there: it folds the desk beside the pane and stands it again, the mode staying on (D-63, replacing D-46's keyboard-only desk button); it is off, like ⌘E, while the desk holds no tab the strip shows, and while folded its badge and hint count the left pane's Views, the pane head's `뷰 N개`. The desk's `✕` is the way to fold it with the pointer, and the rail's desk button or ⌘E stands it again.
It is drawn pressed while the desk stands, and while it is folded it is drawn unpressed, its hint and accessible description giving the number of the left pane's Views, none for a pane with nothing to show; choosing a View from the pane's `뷰 N개` stands a folded desk with that View in front.
Leaving focus mode folds every desk it drew and closes the lane, a tool opened meanwhile with it; each desk keeps its Views and the View focus mode put in front, so ⌘E or the rail's desk button stands it again as it was last seen (operator decision 2026-11-24, replacing D-51 and D-44).
The keyboard goes back to the pane that held it when focus mode came on, when that pane is still in the tab, else it stays on the left pane, and a tab zoomed before the mode is unzoomed by one zoom on that pane, so the tab is drawn with every pane at once.
Moving to another agent while focus mode is on folds the desk left behind the same way; the lane stays open while the mode stays in the same Workspace, so a tool opened meanwhile stays beside the next desk, and a Workspace the mode leaves has its lane closed.
The left pane closing, its agent ending or the pane leaving the tab ends focus mode with no notice, and the tab's remaining panes come back (D-22, B28).
Web owner: `web/src/PairLayer.tsx` (the band and the left half), `web/src/PaneView.tsx` (the pressed ◧), `web/src/pairView.ts` (what ◧ offers, and when focus mode is drawn), `web/src/DeskPairControls.tsx` (the desk's ◧ and the folded tabs), `web/src/PaneGrid.tsx` (`PairPaneCanvas`, and the paired tab's canvas giving its panes up); core owner: `hide-core/src/runtime/pair_view.rs`.
Regression owners are `web/e2e/pair-view.spec.ts` and the focus mode tests of `hide-core/src/runtime/tests/desk_views.rs`.

### Opening, preview, and Open to the side

A single click on an Explorer file or a History row opens it in the active area's preview view (italic title), and the next single click replaces that preview in place, so browsing leaves one tab per area rather than a trail.
Each area has at most one preview, and a click never touches another area or a pinned view.
A double-click on the row or the tab, Keep open, or the first edit pins the preview where it is, including a double-click that lands while the file's first read is still running; because the first edit pins, a document that is dirty, saving, or whose save failed is never a preview in any area that shows it.
Opening a file that is already shown moves to its view instead of adding a tab, choosing the one used last when several views show it.
The keyboard goes with every open from the Explorer (a click, a double-click or Enter), a History row, ⌘P, Open to the side or a terminal link into the view that shows it once the core places it, so ⌘W, ⌘S and typing act on that file rather than on the pane that printed the link; a page the operator opens (a terminal link's URL or HTML file, the Explorer's Open in Browser, Open server, or a row's, a card's or ⌘K's link) takes it the same way once the core names the display it opened, so ⌘W closes that page, while a View area's New tab gives it to its address field and a page's own new tab leaves it with that page; a card the operator opens (a pull request, a session's record or plan, a mission, or a card the Project tool opens, from a row, a chip, a mark, 내 차례, ⌘K, the Overview's `레인에서 보기` or another card) takes it once the snapshot that includes the open shows the card in front of the drawn desk, on whichever Agent tab or checkout the core brought forward for the card's owner, after the owner's pane the core focused with it, and a card that frame does not show in front of the drawn desk (a connected device's tab) leaves the keyboard where it was, so the close chord (⌘W, Ctrl+Shift+W on Windows and Linux) closes the card rather than asking to close the agent the core focused with it, and Escape answers as in any desk View, the same in pair view, while a pane head's title keeps the keys in its terminal and a Hide AI branch's card leaves them where they were (`sendCardOpen` in `web/src/actions.ts`); a folder a link reveals opens nothing to type in, so the keyboard stays in the pane; the tree's arrow keys only move its selection, so the keyboard stays in the tree until something opens.
Opening a file while the lane is closed or on another tool opens it on Files first.
Diffs are placed by the same rules.

Open to the side (from the Explorer's file menu, a History row's menu, or ⌘↵ on a ⌘P result) is the only way to show one file twice.
It puts a pinned second view in the area beside the active one, trying right, then left, then below, then above, or in a new area on the right when there is only one area; when that area already shows the file, its view is focused instead, and with no view open at all it opens in the empty area.
From the only area, Open to the side is a split, so it is offered only where Split right would be, and otherwise stays listed, disabled, with its reason.

While Desk is the screen, ⌘P and ⌘K are the same palettes with their picks opening where Desk shows things (PRD desk-2-ui D-23, D-24; `web/src/deskPalette.ts`):
with a session open beside Desk ⌘P lists that session's checkout, and with none it lists every project's files at once (hided's `file_index_all`, at most 80), each result led by a muted `project ›`, the project Desk's conversation named last first and then `Other projects`.
↵ opens the file as the tab panel's italic preview tab, as the app's ⌘P does, and ⌘↵ (Ctrl+Enter off macOS) opens it to the side, a second view of the file in the panel that the window holds and no Workspace's layout does; the panel stands beside the session, or at the window's right edge with none, and ⌘P never opens or closes the Explorer column.
Its footer says when the list was cut at 80 matches and how many projects could not be listed.
In ⌘K the same rows are found, with the missions added, and before anything is typed Desk's ⌘K lists the sessions, the pull requests and the missions, up to eight of each, instead of Related and Recent; ↵ on a session opens it beside Desk and ⌘↵ goes to its tab, and a pull request or a mission opens in the tab panel and opens no session.
Every other screen's ⌘P and ⌘K are unchanged.
Both views show one document: an edit in either appears in the other at once, and each keeps its own scroll position, cursor, and selection.
Closing one of them leaves the document, its text, and its unsaved state in the other; closing the last one goes through the same save and conflict protection every document close has.
Dragging a tab moves its view and never copies it.

### Dragging a view

A dragged View tab lifts a floating copy that follows the pointer, while the tab keeps its place and nothing on screen resizes.
Over a tab bar, an insertion line marks where the tab will land: in its own bar the drop reorders, and in another area's bar it moves the view there with no split and no copy; when that area already shows the same document, the moved view lands at the line and that area's view of the document gives way, so no area holds one document twice.
Over the left, right, top, or bottom edge of an area's content, the half of that area the drop would create is highlighted with a short label such as `Split right`, and the drop creates that area and moves the view into it.
Only one destination is highlighted at a time, and moving to another edge or bar replaces it.
Where the view cannot go - it is the only view of the area whose edge it is over, the area cannot be halved at its minimum size, the Workspace is at its area or depth limit, or the target is not a View area - no overlay appears and the pointer shows the forbidden cursor.
Escape, a release outside a valid target or outside the window, and a target that disappeared or became ineligible before the release all keep the original order and layout; only a valid drop changes the layout, once.
Nothing is resized, reattached, or saved while a drag is in progress, and the drag itself is never stored.
A drag never moves a view to another Workspace, never turns an Agent tab into a view, and never splits a terminal.

### The View tab menu

A right-click on a View tab, or the menu key while the tab has focus, opens its menu with these items in order: New tab, Keep open, Split, Move, Copy path, Select in File Tree, the OS file manager's reveal, Close view.
Split and Move open submenus ordered Right, Left, Up, Down; Move appears only when at least one neighbouring area exists.
Keep open appears only on a preview view.
Select in File Tree shows the Explorer with the file's row unfolded and selected and opens nothing; the reveal follows [the reveal rule](#explorer-file-management), and a page has neither.
Both are disabled with the reason while the file is unavailable or cannot be read yet.
A Move item appears only toward an area that exists in that direction, and moves the view into it without a split.
A Split direction the Workspace cannot make stays listed, disabled, with its reason under it; when every direction has the same refusal, the Split trigger shows that reason once and stays disabled.
The labels say where the view goes, never "Move to Group".
Close view closes the view and never the file on disk; the menu has no file deletion and never closes a pane or a tab.
Opening the menu moves no focus and changes nothing, and Escape closes it.
Next and previous View area focus and View area grow and shrink are chordless registry commands like the Agent area ones, and ⌘↵ on a ⌘P result opens that file to the side, so splits, moves, closes and resizes can all be done from the keyboard once the operator binds the area commands.

### View states

A view whose file is being read shows `Opening…`.
A restored view whose device or root is not ready says what it waits for (such as `Waiting for <device> to connect`) and reads its file by itself once that is ready.
A view whose file cannot be read shows why, with Close view and Retry, and its tab title reads as struck through; either action acts on that view alone and leaves the active area where it was.
Each state belongs to its view alone, so one missing file never blanks another view or area.
A file a view shows that changes on disk, written by an agent, an editor or a command, shows its new contents in every view of the Workspace in front within a moment, on this machine and on a connected device, the way a page reloads when an agent opens it again.
An image, a PDF or a video keeps its last picture until the new one arrives, so it does not blink.
A text or Markdown document follows the file only while it holds nothing of the operator's: a draft, a save under way or a conflict keeps what the operator has, and the next save meets the change as a conflict with Reload and Keep editing; a draft typed while the new contents are read is kept too.
A file removed while it shows keeps the last contents in its view and puts nothing on the screen.
At most 32 shown files are watched at once, the first in the order the areas lie; a file past them shows the contents read when it opened, as before.
After a restart the app reopens the last Workspace as it was left: its areas and their sizes, each area's tabs in order with its preview, pinned views, and active view, the active area, the lane's tool, tree and its widths, and each Agent tab's desk, the desk standing beside the agents; unsaved text returns from the browser's drafts, and the engine's current tabs and panes are used as they are.
A first run, or a last Workspace that no longer exists, starts on the Overview.
The engine connecting before the initial path catalog and saved View arrive does not mean the last Workspace is gone; the existing starting screen waits for that saved choice to settle.
The unanswered-connection fallback does not settle a connected session's pending saved Workspace; a settled connection failure still opens the Overview.
A layout file that cannot be read is kept aside and the app starts on the Overview, where the operator picks a Workspace and continues with a new layout.
A Workspace stored by an earlier build, with the one-tool lane, with the File Views and Tools columns, or with the side panel or layout before them, restarts once with its lane closed at the default widths and its views, tabs and areas as they were (PRD lane-two-columns B23, D-10).

### Browser displays

A View area's new tab offers only what can become a display, never a tool or a pane.
The View strip’s +, its New tab menu item, and the new tab chord while the area holds the keyboard open a distinct browser display in that area with no address and the address field focused.
Its body shows Open with File (⌘P), opening the file palette, and Diff only while the checkout has changes, opening a palette of changed files.
Picking either replaces that empty tab in place; entering a URL navigates the same tab, and closing an untouched tab creates no recovery entry.
The new-tab page offers no tools or panes; ⌘P remains the file palette everywhere.

A web page is a view like a file: it opens in the active area, has a tab, splits, moves, and closes like one, and comes back after a restart at the address it last showed.
A page with an address carries a globe mark and the page's title, else its host, else a local file's name; its tooltip and accessible name carry `Page`, the title, and the full address.
It opens from `hide browser open` in a connected pane or a shell inside a registered checkout, from clicking or pressing Enter on an HTML file in the Explorer or its Open in Browser menu item, from a page that opens a tab (beside that page, which stays in view: in the area next to it, else in a new area to its right), and from the address field.
A sign-in popup a page opens is a small window of its own above hide, so the sign-in can report back to the page and close; ⌘W closes it.
A link that would open another app (Slack, Zoom) asks first with the app's name, and opens in that app only on Open.
On a connected SSH device, the native page uses that device's localhost or consented checkout resources; a route failure appears in the page's existing failure state.
Opening an address the Workspace already shows moves to that view and loads it again instead of adding a second one.
The view's own toolbar holds Back, Forward, Reload (Stop while the page loads), the address, which shows a web address without its scheme until it is focused, the device size button, Comment and Open in default browser; focusing the address selects the whole address, Return loads what was typed, and Escape puts the page's address back.
Open in default browser (issue 2293) opens the address the page shows now in the operating system's default browser, the way out for a sign-in that cannot finish in Hide (SSO, a passkey), and leaves the page as it was; it shows only for a web address, never on a local file's page, and not on a connected device's `localhost` page, which the default browser would read as this machine's.
A page zoomed off 100% shows its zoom as a small badge at the address field's right end, and nothing at 100% (PRD browser-width-zoom B6-B9); the badge opens − · zoom · + · `Reset` (`100%로`), which stays open while the operator steps through Chrome's steps from 25% to 500%, disables − at 25% and + at 500%, leaves the keyboard in the shell, and goes with the badge once the page is back at 100%, as it does on ⌘0.
Pages of one host share their zoom, so every other page of that host shows the same badge, in any area or Workspace.
The device size button lays the page out at a phone's (390), a tablet's (768) or a desktop's (1280) width instead of its area's (B10-B15): Fit, the default, is its icon alone, and a chosen size shows its width, with the scale the page is drawn at when the area is narrower (`1280 · 39%`).
A narrower size draws the page at its own width centered at the top of the area, the sides left empty; a wider one fits the area's width, scaled down; the page's own viewport is the chosen width at any zoom, a phone or tablet size lays out as one does (its meta viewport applies), and the browser identity stays the same.
While a size is chosen the zoom badge is hidden and ⌘= ⌘- ⌘0 on the page zoom nothing, neither the page nor a terminal's text; Fit brings the page's zoom and its badge back.
The size is the display's own: it stays through a tab change, a move to another area and the page's own navigation, and a reload of the window or a restart returns it to Fit.
A sign-in popup, an image or a PDF, and a page in a plain browser tab have no zoom badge or device size button.
A page that cannot load says so in its place with the address and the reason, and Reload tries again; nothing else on screen changes.
While the palette, a menu or a dialog covers a page, the page is shown as a still picture of itself from its first frame, so the overlay draws over it and the page is never seen on top; a page with no picture yet is blank for a moment until one arrives, and an older picture turns to the page as it is now within a few frames; the page comes back live when the overlay closes.
A tooltip never turns a page into a picture; a tooltip, such as a tab's or a page toolbar button's, opens on the side of its trigger where the whole of it shows clear of every page, and keeps its usual side when no side is clear.
While the shell drags something (a tab, a divider, a lane divider, a tree item), every page is shown as its still, so the guide or preview draws over it and a drop lands in the shell rather than the page; the pages come back live at release, and a drag inside a page is the page's own.
In a plain browser tab the view keeps its address on the toolbar row, level with a document header beside it, and below it reads `Pages open in the Hide desktop app.`; a web address offers Open in browser, and nothing else is drawn in its place.
[BROWSER_DISPLAYS.md](BROWSER_DISPLAYS.md) owns which addresses a page may hold, the `file:` boundary, and the page's lifetime.

### View openers and comments

Each area's selected View shows its actual opener's 14px provider mark at the right of the address or path toolbar, while tabs retain their type mark and title without repeated opener logos: a page, a file, a diff, an image or a PDF opened by `hide file open`, `hide diff open` or `hide browser open` from the agent's pane, or by the operator clicking a path or an address in that agent's terminal (PRD view-opener-feedback D-02, D-04, D-24).
A View the operator opened from the Explorer, ⌘K, the file tree or a new tab's address field, and one a caller Hide could not bind to a pane opened, wears no mark; so does one opened from a link in a pane no agent runs in, which no agent opened, though it is that pane's (PRD lane-desk-pair-view D-54).
The mark follows the agent that last opened the View; a link inside the page and an agent's `hide browser` command keep it, and an address the operator types takes it off.
Two sessions that open the same View both keep it in their strips, and the mark is the one that opened it last (PRD session-files D-26).
Its hint, above the toolbar mark, says the agent's name, `Opened this · <product>` and `Click to go to its pane`, and the mark's accessible name says the same; the tab's tooltip also identifies its opener, including on background tabs.
Opening, waiting and unavailable Views retain a path toolbar with the same opener, and a missing or unknown opener creates no invented identity.
Pressing it brings the agent's pane forward with the keyboard, leaving the View selected; an agent whose pane closed is found again by its session in another pane, and one found nowhere draws its mark dimmed with `(closed)` in the hint and goes nowhere.
A pane whose agent opened Views that are open, or from whose terminal's links the operator opened them, a shell's included (PRD lane-desk-pair-view D-54), shows `N views` after its PR mark, a list of each View's kind mark, name, address or path and unsent comment count, counted once however many tabs show it, newest open first, an agent's open again of an older View bringing it back to the top; this list is what the agent keeps, whether or not a View is on a desk, and a row puts that View on the desk of the Agent tab in front, in front of it, opening the lane when it is closed, the chip's count unchanged (PRD lane-desk-pair-view B3, D-04).
Two tabs are the same View when they show the same file, the same diff, a page at the same address or the same outside file, which has no path and is told apart by its grant (`Shown` in `hide-core/src/view_layout.rs`, which Desk's outputs compare too); a page that names no address yet is the same as no other tab.
A View the operator has not looked at since the agent opened it ends its row with the `볼 것` mark, an eye in the warning badge, whose hover says `볼 것` (PRD unseen-agent-views B10); a row stands for every tab of its View, so it keeps the mark while any of them is unseen and its press then brings that unseen tab forward.
While any of them is unseen, the icon of `N views` carries a warning dot, so the list need not be opened to know one waits; it is a dot and not a second eye, because the agent's own mark already shows the eye.
That eye, in the pane header's first row, is a button of its own beside the record button (`LookButton` in `web/src/components/status-mark.tsx`): pressing it does what pressing the agent's row does, bringing the pane and its newest View to look at forward onto the desk of the pane's Agent tab, the operator's own press opening the lane, so no surface draws an eye that does nothing (D-40, B4); the desk stands beside the tab's panes, every one still in sight, and focus mode is entered only by ◧ (lane-desk-pair-view D-52).

Comments: a page's toolbar Comment button turns Comment mode on, with `Click an element or drag an area · Esc` over the page, an outline on the element under the pointer, and the page's clicks taken as comment targets until Comment or Esc turns it off.
The button shows on only once the page is held for Comment; until then the page still takes its own clicks, and a second press takes the turn-on back.
A local HTML page (a `file:` page inside a checkout) takes Comment exactly as a web page does: the outline, a click on an element, a drag over an area, the pins and the note.
Comment that cannot start, or that ends on its own, says why in the button's hint, in words the operator can act on (reopen the page, reconnect the window, answer the dialog in the page), and the button shows its warning colour until the next press; the operator's own turn-off says nothing.
A file or a diff takes a comment from the `+` on the line under the pointer or the cursor (beside the line number, or beside the text in Markdown Live, which numbers no lines; on the line's first row, however many rows the line wraps to or tall a Markdown Live heading makes it), a drag down the gutter or the line numbers for a range, or ⇧⌘. (Ctrl+Shift+. on Windows and Linux) on selected text; a range of removed diff rows is on the old side, any other on the new.
An image or a PDF takes one from an area dragged while its toolbar's Comment is on, which is disabled, with the reason in its hint, while the file cannot be read; a PDF comment names its page, and an area stays on the same content at any zoom.
A picked area, rows or element stays marked for as long as its note is open (an area keeps the outline a saved region comment has, on a page over its still), and Cancel or Esc takes the mark with the note.
A note opens under its last line (beside an area), with `⌘↩ to add`, Add and Cancel; Esc cancels, an empty note cannot be added, and a View with 50 comments shows `50 / 50` and why in the note's place.
On a page the note opens beside the picked element or area over the page's still, and a pin opens its comment there; a press beside either closes it, and the page takes the keyboard again.
A comment shows under its line, or as a numbered pin and outline while Comment is on, with its number in its recipient's letter, edit and delete; a line comment's card is as wide as the visible text and stays beside the gutters when the lines scroll sideways.
A deleted comment leaves `Comment deleted · Undo` on the View's line for a moment, and Undo brings it back at the same place and number; there is no confirmation.
A comment whose lines changed moves with them, or says `Changed` and quotes what it was when no line with its text is left; a changed image or PDF marks its area comments `Changed`.

A tab with unsent comments carries their count, orange while some came back undelivered, and one line under the View, never over the page, says `N comments · M views` with `Send N → <logo> <agent> ▾`; the line is not drawn while the View has none.
Send N sends every unsent comment for that agent, from every View, as one letter from the operator ([delivery.md](delivery.md#the-operators-view-feedback)); ▾ offers `This view only`, the other agents in the checkout with their status marks (and logos only while the agent logo view mode shows them), and for a closed agent `Reopen and send`, which starts its session again in a new pane of the checkout and sends.
A View the operator opened opens its Send as `Send N comments to…`, a choice of agent.
After Send the comments leave the View and the line says `Sent N to <agent> · delivered when it rests` with Go to pane for a moment, or that it is delivered when its device is back; a Send that could not go keeps the comments and says why with Retry, which sends again to the same agent and the same Views that Send asked for (`This view only` stays one View), and a letter that expired brings its comments back marked `Not delivered` with Send again.

Web owners: `web/src/ViewComments.tsx`, `web/src/viewFeedback.ts`, `web/src/editor/comments.ts`, `web/src/editor/useLineComments.tsx`; core owner: `hide-core/src/runtime/view_feedback.rs`; test owners: `web/src/viewFeedback.test.ts`, `web/e2e/view-feedback.spec.ts`; original visual boards are maintained separately.

### Narrow windows

Whether the two columns stand side by side depends on the Workspace body's width, not the window's, so hiding the sidebar with ⌘B can bring them side by side again: the viewing column narrows first and the tool column after, and when both cannot stand side by side they stack, the one the rail last brought forward drawn and the other behind it (PRD lane-two-columns B19).
The lane opens at any width: in a body narrower than the lane it takes the whole body, giving up the viewing column's width down to its minimum first and the tool column's after, and a body narrower than one column's minimum is still the lane's whole width, never the sidebar's.
When the View areas cannot all have their minimum, only the active area shows, with an area switcher to the others.
Widening the window brings back what the Workspace stores, the lane's columns, widths and the area sizes, because none of these narrow arrangements is stored, and another Workspace's lane never changes while one is narrow.

### Library masters

PRD right-lane B25 drew the lane, rail and pin parts of its approved v3 boards in `Component / Workspace columns`, and the lane states in `Screen / Workspace`; those boards are maintained outside the repository, so PRD lane-two-columns (D-12) draws its two-column states in the gallery's `RightLaneScene` instead: closed, over the agents, the Explorer beside the documents and alone, expanded, PRs, Memory, Changes and the narrow stacked lane, and the Project tool with its week empty, partly filled and filled (`project-empty`, `project-partial`, `project`; PRD project-panel-results B21).
Delivery reviews actual captures in both themes through the public [design workflow](DESIGN_WORKFLOW.md).
The production gallery and View components provide the public visual reference; original boards are maintained separately.
Their sheets draw every state as refs: the View tab sheet draws preview, pinned, hover, active-in-the-active-area, active-in-another-area, dirty, unavailable, diff, a long title, and the floating drag copy; the placement sheet draws a reorder, a move into another area, a right and a down split, and an ineligible target; the tab menu sheet draws a preview's menu and a pinned view's menu with a disabled Split and its reason; the area states sheet draws each view's opening, waiting, and unavailable states.
The browser display's toolbar and its loading, load failed, and plain browser tab states are on `Component / Browser file diff toolbars`.

### Agent panes and the Agents explorer

A pane header's first row is the pane: its state on the left, what can be done to it on the right, and nothing in it changes place when a control shows or hides; the turn row under it is the conversation.
On the left stand the move grip, the ancestor path, the status mark, the agent's logo and title, then the fork mark, the PR mark, the plan chip, the views and ◧ button, the Answer tag and the letters-waiting pill; on the right stand zoom first, then the desk's way back, the children chip, the connection and limited-integration icons, a zoomed pane's zoom chip, the overflow control and close.
The grip and zoom are drawn on the pane that holds the keyboard, on any other pane while its header is pointed at or focused, and always on a screen with no hover, keeping their place while hidden; zoom leads the right side so the controls always drawn stay at its end and the place it keeps while hidden reads as the title's margin, not as a gap between icons; a zoomed pane's zoom chip is always drawn, since it names the panes it hides (PRD lane-desk-pair-view B20, B21, D-31, D-32).
The views chip and ◧ are one button of two cells, drawn on a pane with something to show beside its desk (D-43, D-53), drawn like the head's other icons with no box around them: one hairline between the two cells, only the hovered cell filled, and ◧ pressed in the focus mode accent while the pane is in focus mode (D-48).
In it, the left cell, `뷰 N개` with its unseen dot, lists what the agent opened as before, and the right cell, ◧, puts the pane beside its desk; a pane with nothing to show, an agent that opened no View included, draws neither (D-49). Below the tight breakpoint the views cell gives way, and below the narrow one ◧ too, which the pane menu then holds.
◧ shows the pane beside its Agent tab's desk (focus mode), its hint `집중 모드` (`Focus mode`) with its chord ⌘. (Ctrl+Shift+. on Windows and Linux); on the pane in focus mode only its cell is drawn pressed, in the focus mode accent, and its hint reads `원래 화면으로` (`Back to the usual view`), and a press brings the screen before focus mode back.
A pane with nothing to show beside it, a shell or one whose agent has opened no View yet, its session or not, draws no ◧ on its head, to keep the heads quiet, until it is the pane focus mode draws alone, where ◧ is drawn pressed; its pane menu and terminal menu offer ◧ as on every pane, and it enters focus mode drawn alone (B19, D-49, D-53); the core lists the panes whose agent has a View to show (`panes_with_views` in the Workspace's snapshot), which the head alone reads.
The pane menu holds both, at its top: `다른 에이전트에게 말 걸기` (`Talk to another agent`, an agent pane's `@`) and ◧ with the same words as the button; a right-click in the terminal draws ◧ after Zoom terminal and `@` with the pane menu's items.
A narrow pane folds its head from the left side's end, and the overflow control and close stay whole at every width.
Below 440px the pane draws neither ◧ nor zoom (◧ stays in the pane menu, zoom in the terminal's menu), and the mission, plan and views chips keep their mark and count without their word and cells, as the Answer tag drops its wait.
Below 300px the PR mark and the views chip go until the pane widens again, the session record and the View area still holding them, and the title keeps the room; the status mark, the logo, the fork mark (a side fork is what makes a narrow pane), the mission or plan chip as its mark and count, the mission chip with its node cells along its bottom edge (a Conductor in a narrow window opens its map from it), the Answer tag and the letters pill stay.
Whatever still does not fit is cut at the left side's end, never drawn over another part or past the close control, and the marks before the title never give up their width to it.
`개요에서 보기` is the pane menu's first item among the agent's own actions, with no shortcut and no header button; it opens the Overview on Work with the pane's session chosen, as a click on it there would (PRD pane-open-in-overview B11; the header button of B1-B3 was folded into the menu by operator decision).
An agent pane's `@`, chosen from its pane menu, opens a list under the overflow control of the other agents on the same device, found by name, task or project as typed; choosing one types its reference, as Copy name and ID writes it, into this pane's prompt with no Enter and gives this pane's terminal the keyboard, while Escape closes the list back to the overflow control; the pane's own agent is not offered, nor are this machine's agents in a device's pane, because a bare id would name another pane there; a pane of this machine is offered the devices' agents too, because their reference carries a full address, and a shell pane has no `@`.
Dragging the grip, the title or unused space in the first header row moves that pane after the pointer crosses the ordinary drag threshold; a short title click still opens its record, and a short click on the grip, a plain title or unused space gives that pane the keyboard, as a click in its terminal does.
While the drag runs, a chip with the pane's name follows the pointer, and a card or hint a hover opens (the plan chip, a hint) stays closed until the drag ends.
Header buttons, relationship rows and terminal text keep their existing actions.
A pane drops on another pane's left, right, top or bottom edge in the selected group's Agent areas, on a tab title to join beside its selected pane, or on that area's New tab button to separate into a tab.
A centre, self, Files, other checkout, device or group is not a destination.
Hovering a tab shows an inert preview without selecting or reading it, waking its session, moving the keyboard, or changing a terminal's grid; Escape, blur, outside release and a changed source or target cancel it.
The pane menu offers the same destination tabs, panes, edges and New tab action without a new shortcut.
A sole pane dropped on its own area's New tab button keeps its tab; in another area it moves that existing tab.
A successful move focuses the destination and removes an empty source tab, including a named tab, collapsing an empty non-root area without opening a replacement shell.
The operation keeps the running terminal, conversation and responsibility relationships; a manually placed delegated pane stays where it was put.
Related tabs cannot start another geometry operation while the move is pending, while other tabs and terminal input remain available.
A definitive failure offers Retry locally.
A move whose answer does not arrive is settled by Hide itself and never by a status check the operator presses: it ends as done where the pane is at its destination, and as a failure with Retry anywhere else or when the pane's position cannot be read, so the bar shows only "Moving pane…" and then, when it failed, why.

An agent pane's head carries no task chip, no checklist popover and no chord for one (PRD session-record-goal-flow D-01, B1); the PR, letter and branch marks in the same place stay, and the session record holds the whole record.
A turn the agent's provider refused for a usage limit or an API error ends as the label's `한도`, and the second row and the turn card draw it as `막힘` with its line as the cause (B9); only the session record tells the two apart.
A plain shell keeps its terminal mark.
A pane's terminal stands 8px in from the pane's sides and 4px from its top and bottom, so its text never touches the pane's border, and its grid is the one that fits that inset box.
On an agent pane whose session Hide knows, the status mark, the logo and the title are one button, except a `볼 것` mark, which is a button of its own just before it ([View openers and comments](#view-openers-and-comments)): under the pointer or focus they light together and open nothing, since a card under the head kept rising in the way of the terminal (the turn card opens from the turn on the second row, and the record shows the whole title), and a press shows that session's record as a card tab in front of the desk of the pane's Agent tab ([Tasks, card tabs and the PR list](#tasks-card-tabs-and-the-pr-list)), opening the lane when it is closed, while the keyboard and the input cursor stay in the pane's terminal (PRD overview-device-tasks B22, sidebar-task-lens B15); the Overview's `레인에서 보기` lands on that desk the same way, but gives the record the keyboard, since it was pressed away from the pane.
A press while the desk shows that record in front folds the desk, and the fork mark and the PR mark keep their own presses outside the button (B23).
Whether the desk shows that record is read from its front card tab and whether the lane draws the desk, both of which the core keeps.
A plain shell, and an agent pane whose session is not known yet, keeps the plain head with nothing to press; the head becomes the button in place once the session is known, on a device's pane as on this Mac's (B24).
Over a narrow window the record shows in the lane floating over the panes (`web/src/PaneHeaderBand.tsx`, `PaneRecordButton`).
The provider logo is drawn on a pane's own header, and in the heads and places that are about the agent or its provider itself: the start picker, Settings, the footer's usage chips and the Usage pages, the session record's head, the tree popover's head, the last-words popover's head, the phone's detail head, the Hide AI tool's ⚙ chip, the first-run tiles, and the selected View's toolbar ([View openers and comments](#view-openers-and-comments)).
The sidebar's 프로젝트 and 태스크 rows and the Agent tabs draw it only by the operator's choice or while ⌥ is held ([Agent logo view mode](#agent-logo-view-mode)); ⌘K and the recent-pane list and the phone's list lead with the status mark alone in both modes (PRD agent-mark-quiet); a session record's children, the PR card's session rows and the child tree popover's rows draw the logo after the status mark in both modes (PRD record-card-readability D-06, D-09).
A pane header draws exactly one logo, the pane's own agent, for each of the seven supported agents: Claude Code, Codex, Grok, OpenCode, Pi, omp and Antigravity, the same logo Settings and the first-run choice draw.
A row without a logo names its agent by product name (`Claude Code`, `Codex`, the adapter's label, never the raw kind) in its hover hint and its accessible name, and the pane header's logo and title say it the same way.
An agent that runs with a profile other than its built-in default keeps the title its agent has on the default, and its logo wears the profile name's first letter, upper-cased where the script has case, as a small badge on the logo's lower-right corner, where a Hide AI row's owl sits: on its Agent tab, its sidebar and 태스크 rows, its pane header and its Overview node and rows (PRD agent-profiles B17, operator decision 2026-11-09).
While the rows and tabs draw no logo ([Agent logo view mode](#agent-logo-view-mode)), the badge stands alone after the status mark, so the profile shows in both modes.
Its hover hint and accessible name say the agent and the profile, `Claude Code · work`, where a default agent's say `Claude Code`; an agent on its default draws no badge, and a Hide AI agent with a profile keeps its owl and says the profile in words only (`ProfileBadge` in `web/src/AgentMark.tsx`, `agentWithProfile` in `web/src/agentRow.ts`).
Only a plain shell and an agent Hide does not support draw the neutral `>_`.
A logo drawn dark on a transparent ground (Grok) sits on the light plate so it stays visible on the dark theme; the others are drawn as published ([BRAND.md](BRAND.md#third-party-agent-marks)).
A child pane's ancestor path names each ancestor from the root to its parent, `›` between them, and each name opens that pane; a root has none.
The names drop before current identity or actions when the pane narrows, leaving `›` with its accessible name.
The PR mark is the one the 태스크 row of the same agent draws, from the same list the core lays on the session (with-hide/hide-private `docs/task-identity.md`, One list per session): its own PRs and every descendant's, a descendant that ended included (the `#number` when that is one PR, each state's count when several), pressing which lists each PR with who holds it, so a child's pull request is seen from the pane that delegated it; under the pointer or focus it opens below the header what the 프로젝트 row's mark shows, the PR card for one PR and the list for several.
The fork mark, a small fork icon after the title, is drawn only on a pane Fork agent made; its tooltip and accessible name read `Forked from <source name>` while the source still runs the conversation it was forked from, and the mark is then a button, reachable by keyboard, that opens the source; once the source closed or moved on to another conversation it reads `Forked from another pane` and opens nothing (`web/src/PaneHeaderBand.tsx`, `PaneForkMark`; the fact is the core's, `PaneForkSnapshot.forked_from`).
A delegated child and a handed-off root have none, and neither does a fork made while forks were children (PRD fork-operator-root B20, B21).
A device's pane header wears the mark too, as its rows do, although a device's pane offers no fork.
The letters-waiting pill follows the title and the fork mark while letters wait on the pane for a possible draft, as the core decides ([delivery.md](delivery.md#delivering-a-held-letter-now)): the envelope, the count from two letters, and `지금 전달` (`Deliver now`) on the popover fill with a border edge, which stands apart from both a focused and an unfocused header in Light and Dark.
The whole pill is one button, reachable by keyboard and pressed with Enter, and its hover and accessible name read `대기 중인 편지 N통 지금 전달`; a pane narrower than the pane breakpoint drops the word and keeps the envelope and the count, with the same action.
Pressing sends `deliver_letters_now` and nothing else: the pill goes and releases the recorded draft hold, and the doorbell submits the bell line through the engine's `prompt_agent` once its guards pass and the actual composer reads empty.
Actual text, a menu or an unreadable composer keeps the letter waiting; the press never clears or submits the operator's draft and asks for no confirmation.
The line begins with 🦉 (U+1F989, no variation selector); Codex CLI on Windows may omit it, and the saved bell still identifies the letter ([delivery.md](delivery.md#recognizing-the-bell)).
No other hold of a letter reaches the screen, and nothing changes on the sender's pane (`web/src/components/letter-mark.tsx`).
The tree popover's `개요에서 보기` opens the Overview on the agent's device with the card of the task it belongs to selected and the agent's own record in the panel (PRD overview-device-tasks B59); a resting or cleanup card's fold opens with it, so the selected card is drawn.
The zoom control names the panes it hides and unzooms the tab; the input pane keeps its accent perimeter and header tint.

The second row over the terminal shows the pane's turn, `<request> → <answer>` with no word before the request (the button's accessible name still begins `요청:`), with a child-tree button at its left when direct children are known (PRD pane-last-request).
The request is the operator's last one, the label's one-line summary of it, read against the conversation (`제안대로 진행 + 애매한 상태 더 점검` for `ㅇㅇ 그렇게 해 … 점검들 좀 해줘`), in quiet text; until the label has summarized it, with summaries off, after a failed judgment or for a turn before the summary existed, it is the request's own first line, and a request of images alone reads `이미지 N`.
A letter another agent sent after it does not replace it; a delegated child with no operator request shows its sender's last request the same way.
The answer is the operator's ask, the agent's own Working progress, for a finished turn the operator has not looked at, `끝남` in the success colour and its result sentence, or, while the agent waits on working children, `자식 기다림 · 자식 N개 작업 중`, the words its sidebar row says; once the operator has looked at a finished turn the row goes.
The progress says only what the request does not: the label's line never restates the request, and a turn whose events show nothing beyond it yet has no line, so a working turn with no progress shows its request alone, with no arrow and no `작업 중`, since the status mark and the working line already say it works.
The request shows only with an ask, while the agent works or waits on working children, or for a finished turn not yet looked at: a pane that waits on anything else, rests or finished and was read has no row (only the children button when it has live children, and not even that while the turn row is off).
A quiet turn (progress, a result, a children wait or a request alone) sits on the head's own surface under a hairline, and an ask keeps the raised surface with its warning edge.
How many lines the row takes is the View menu's choice ([Pane turn row mode](#pane-turn-row-mode)): one line by default, the request cut at two fifths of the row when an answer follows and each part cut where it overflows, an ask included, so no turn grows over the terminal on a narrow pane.
The children button stays at the first row's left, the request is never folded into an icon, and the rows overlay the terminal without changing its grid.
The whole turn, request and answer together, is one button the keyboard reaches once, named by its request: a press anywhere on the request or the answer's words, or focus on it, opens the turn card under the row, and a second press, Esc or a press outside, on the terminal too, closes it, while a press inside the card leaves it open; the pointer passing over opens nothing, because a card rising on hover kept covering the terminal, and only lights the turn as one, its words brightening on the hover fill.
The button lies behind the row's words (`RowOverlayButton`), so a control in the row keeps its own press and opens no card: the children button, `열기`, a person's turn's `다 했어요` and copy, `외 N건`, and the raised agent's name and a person's turn's place, which keep their hint and selection; this holds in one line and in two, and with the turn row off the pane's own ask on the head stays the trigger.
With the card open, Tab from the turn or the title moves the keyboard into the card to `기록 열기`, the card stays open while focus is in it, Shift+Tab before its start goes back to the trigger, Tab past its end and Esc in the card close it and return focus to the trigger, which keeps it closed until the pointer or focus comes anew; only Tab moves focus in, so the pointer never takes the terminal's keyboard, and a card the pointer only shows is no Escape layer, so Esc reaches the terminal.
The card reads the session as one conversation top to bottom on a rail, at its own width (`--size-turn-card`, still stopped by the window's width) and at most the space below the request or seven tenths of the window's height, scrolling inside past that.
First come up to two earlier turns, oldest first and dimmed, each ringed in how it ended (the warning colour for a question or a block, the success colour for done); then who sent the request (`나` or the sender) and when, over a bubble with the request's summary and its own first two lines, and who sent a later request under it.
Then the agent's answer to that request, once the turn it began is over (a later request began another, or the agent stopped): the label's line for how the turn ended in bold over the first two lines of the agent's own last message in that turn read as words (a table row, a code block and a rule passed over, and Markdown's heading, list, quote, emphasis, code and link marks left out; `hide-core/src/labels/facts.rs`, `Reply`), dimmed, or that message alone in the foreground when there is no line (summaries off); `전체 보기 ▾` shows only when the message says more, opens the whole message in place, read from the agent's session when pressed and drawn as Markdown with its tables and lists, in a box of its own height (`--size-turn-card-answer`) that scrolls inside so the letters under it stay in view, and `접기 ▴` closes it; a whole message that cannot be read says so in one muted line, and with summaries off, whose turn end is never judged, the card offers no whole message.
Between the answer and those agents sits `나를 기다리는 것 · 질문 N`, its head and a thin rule beside the list in the question colour (`--open-ask`, the blue of the pane head's `질문 N`) with no box, fill or warning colour, since a kept question is no call to act, and each question in the normal text colour: the questions the agent put to the operator that still wait, oldest first, each with when it was asked and `무시하기`, the same list and the same press as the session record's ([status-model.md](status-model.md#a-question-still-waiting); `OpenAskList`, `web/src/components/open-asks.tsx`).
A line put away stays struck through with `되돌리기` while the card is open; the box is not drawn while nothing waits, and the card's last line is not drawn while it would only repeat the box's first question.
Then the agents the agent worked with since the request, under `함께 일한 에이전트 N · 편지 M통` with `전체 보기 ▾` at its right: one line per agent (one device, pane and session), the agent last heard from or written to first, at most six and the rest counted as `외 N명`.
Each line is the agent's screen name (the name it registered under unless Hide made it up for the pane, such as `hide-claude-w1-p2`, else its title, else its runtime's name, as the bell names a writer; [delivery.md](delivery.md#names-on-the-wire-and-on-screen)), a mark, that letter's first line, a small `막힘 N회` when a block went between them either way, and the time.
The mark is the last thing it said that matters: `막힘` in the error colour, `보고` in the success colour, `질문` (a request to this agent) or `답장`; `진행` when all it sent were interim reports; and when it said nothing, what this agent last sent it, marked `답장 기다림` while that letter waits for its answer, which ends once the same agent writes back a report or a reply (the ledger's own wait is not changed by that).
An agent this one started has no line of its own for the start: its line is its letters, or `시작` while it has written none.
`전체 보기 ▾` opens, in the same place, every letter it sent (`↗`) and received (`↙`) and every agent it started (`+`) in time order, each with its mark, and `접기 ▴` closes it; interim reports (`progress`) are counted there in one line, `중간 보고 N통 접힘`, and only the newest eight letters are listed, the older lines (letters and starts alike) counted in `이전 N개 생략` above them.
Last comes the agent's half now, the answer's verb as a pill in its state's colour (warning while it asks, the working colour while it works, success when finished) with the label's words for the turn, never the agent's own words, which could still be the previous turn's while a new one starts; when the answer above is how the agent stands now (no later request, nothing asked, not working) the answer wears the verb and this line is not drawn twice.
A turn that ended waiting says what it waits for when its row knows the cause, in the words its sidebar line leads with (`자식 기다림`, `CI 기다림`, `답장 기다림`, `백그라운드 기다림`), and `대기` with no proven cause; an earlier turn's end stays `대기`, since the cause it had then was not kept.
`기록 열기` shows the session record in the lane; a part with nothing to say is not drawn, and nothing on the card opens a letter (`web/src/PaneTurnCard.tsx`, `web/src/turnCard.ts`).
A connected device's agent shows the same card: its letters are in the same ledger under the address the core names its pane by, and its turns are read by the same label worker.
The second row is absent when there is no turn to show and no child button; a pane whose terminal is not live has no ask, request or progress on it, because its body says why.
The core publishes the ask (`hide-core/src/agent_state/header.rs`, `Header.band`): the pane's own Approval, Answer or unread Blocked first, the lead raised descendant next, then the pane's own read Blocked and Stopped, which is work left.
A pull request's verb (Fix, Review, Merge) is the header PR mark's colour and a finished turn is the status mark's dot to read, so neither draws a row; the PR mark carries no review word either.
An ask stays until it is answered: once the operator has had the pane focused it dims to `--opacity-read-status`, as its row's request line does, and a question the operator read but did not answer keeps its row (`Band.read`).
The pane's own question or approval says only what is asked, in the warning colour, and the stable wait, with no verb, since the rail and the colour say whose turn it is; a block starts with `막힘`; an Approval names no command, since the core reads none, and shows the ask's own default words.
Work left starts with `일 남음`, the word the row's line uses, in the foreground colour on the head's own surface with no warning rail, because the warning colour is kept for what the operator answers; with the turn row off its mark on the head has a plain border, and the turn card draws it as a quiet answer, not an ask.
A raised descendant's ask starts with the originating agent's name and `(자식)` for a direct child or `(하위 세션)` for a deeper descendant, what to do, the parent that has not received a letter, the elapsed time and Open.
The hover gives the originating agent's product, path, checkout and wait when known; a draft still names its originating child while Open goes to the parent holding the input draft.
Open goes to the raised descendant, or for a draft to the parent whose input holds it, and is disabled with the connection reason beside it while that pane's device is not connected.
An ask is quiet text on the secondary fill with a warning rail; the request text gets three shares of the flexible space, and the asking agent and an unreceived-letter explanation get one each, so they do not consume the whole reason at narrow widths or enlarged text.
Without an ask, the row shows the current pane agent's own nonblank progress only while its state is live Working, and a finished turn's result sentence only until the operator looks; idle and disconnected states cannot retain either sentence.
The label worker retires the previous turn's sentence as soon as it proves a new human turn, even when the agent stays Working, before the new turn's analysis arrives.
The row does not substitute a child's line or the pane title for missing progress; a wait on children names only how many work.
The children button stays at the row's left, or on the head before its tools while the turn row is off, whenever the pane has live direct children, with an ask or progress on the row or neither: a tree icon, the status mark of the first direct child that has one, in tree order, the number of direct children when there are several, and a chevron open the existing tree popover ([status-model.md](status-model.md#the-descendant-mark-and-the-tree-popover)); a child of unknown state draws no mark, and with a device away or no child to mark the face keeps the number alone.
The row never lists individual child names; child names, lines and navigation remain in the popover.
Its tooltip and accessible name spell out the live direct child total and every state count; an ordinary child's question, approval or error is its parent's to answer, so it is named only there, and a dot on a name is a reported completion, not verified delivery of a task.
The button opens with the raised branches expanded whenever its core raised count is positive; `외 N건` remains only when the children button is unavailable.
If the parent or a known descendant's device is not connected, the button keeps the last topology total and the reason in its tooltip and accessible name; the connection reason is also visible beside any progress until the connection returns.
With no live children there is no children button, and no empty separator.
Working keeps a thin blue line along the top and adds its row when the core has a valid sentence or a request to show; CI wait, waiting and idle have neither.
The row and the blue line overlay the terminal and change neither its viewport nor its PTY grid.
Pending and failed moves appear at the initiating popover, ancestor step or Open with the existing retry rules, plus the Agent-area notice when the source pane is no longer visible or the move started from a child's name.

Why the terminal is not live is said where the terminal would be, never on the second row (`Header.notice`, drawn by `web/src/PaneNotice.tsx`): the core publishes at most one notice, the device being away first, then terminal restoration failure, sleep, and the stream's own state.
Sleep, restoration failure and a local stream that can be attached again draw bodies of their own, described below; every other notice covers the terminal with its words on the card fill: `연결 안 됨 · <device>`, `다시 연결하는 중…`, `헬퍼가 허용되지 않음 · 설정 > 기기에서 허용하세요`, `사용할 수 없음`, `종료됨`, or a failed exit in red with the actual code.
A start or a close that passes by itself is a small caption over whatever the terminal shows.
Connection and sleep recovery controls stay in the body, and limited integration stays a header icon.
The engine's explicit restoration failure is a terminal fact, independent of a saved sleep record; the pane's agent runs no process, so its row reads stopped and at rest rather than unknown ([status-model.md](status-model.md#where-each-axis-comes-from)).
Its pane body says the terminal could not be restored, names its folder and offers Close pane; raw details remain in diagnostics.
Visiting this pane preserves a saved conversation and its title without waking it, and neither Wake nor attach Retry is offered as terminal recovery.
A missing folder alone never establishes restoration failure.
An explicit close rereads the failed terminal's incarnation and failure before sending the close, and keeps the pane until the engine confirms removal.
A whole-tab close requires the same pane membership; a nonfailed peer must be quiet or the same terminal included in the existing close confirmation.
A newly active or unknown peer requires a fresh close decision.
Close only this leaves external descendants running; the existing subtree choice owns whether they are included.
The fresh read and close share the admitted deadline, so a slow status read cannot send a close after its permission expires.
Other active or unknown panes and descendants keep their close guards.
A failed terminal has no recoverable live layout, so a close containing one does not enter the ordinary undo stack.
Closing the last failed terminal preserves the project registration without trying to start a replacement in the failed folder.

The pane menu (from its overflow control or a right-click on the header) starts with `@` and ◧, then groups the parent and other siblings under Related sessions, offers Copy name and ID, then Close terminal, which asks about the agents it spawned as Closing an agent that spawned others says; opening it moves no focus and marks nothing read.
The pane's own children are not in it: its tree button opens them.
The menu names the pane as its header does and is drawn from the pane as it is now, not as it was when the menu opened: a session's label arrives after its first turn, and the heading already open then reads the new label and offers Fork session and Sleep agent as the core now says.
Fork session groups the new-tab and right-side destinations without changing which sessions can fork.
A right-click in the terminal focuses that pane, like a click, and opens a longer menu: Copy (only over a selection), Paste, Select all, and Find; then Split right, Split down, Zoom terminal or Exit zoom (disabled on a tab's only pane), and ◧; then the pane menu's items; an item with a chord that does the same shows it, ⌘C and ⌘V included.
The Zoom pane chord on a tab's only pane does nothing either, on this machine and on a device: the core does not send it and logs `pane.zoom.single_pane` (`remote.control.zoom_single_pane` for a device).
Copy name and ID, here and in an agent row's menu, copies the pane's reference as one line, `@<name> (pane <id> · <task>)`: the name the engine knows the agent by (its kind when it has none, a shell pane's title), the id a reader on this machine addresses the pane by, which `hide pane read`, `hide pane send` and `hide request send` take as it stands: a pane of this machine bare (`w9J:p52`), a pane of a connected device with its full address (`remote:<device id>:pane:w2:p7`), and the agent's task label, cut to one short line.
The reference never holds a line break, so pasting it into a prompt never submits it; an agent that reads it reads and writes that pane by the id as written, wherever the pane is, and sends a letter to it when it is a registered agent.
An agent row in the sidebar, and an agent's mark on a pull request row, can be dragged onto a terminal: the drag shows the reference under the pointer, and the drop types it with a space after it, never Enter, and focuses that terminal, so the operator only adds what to say.
A drop on the agent's own pane is refused, and so is a drop of a bare id of this machine on a pane of a device or of a device's address on another device's pane, because a bare id names a pane only on the machine of whoever reads it; a device pane's reference drops on a pane of this machine.
The right-click leaves the drag selection as it was, so Copy copies what the operator selected; the program in the pane never hears it.
Copy joins a row whose text reaches the last column to the next with no break, except, in a pane with no agent and off Windows, a row the program ended with a line break, the same rule a link's join follows (Terminal links).
A drag held past the pane's top or bottom edge scrolls its history toward the pointer, faster the farther away it is held, and the selection grows with it, as in a native terminal; Copy then takes every row it covers, the ones scrolled away included.
A wheel or new output moves a drag selection with the text it covers, drawn only for the part on screen; scrolling back while part remains visible draws the whole highlight again.
Once none of it is on screen the highlight goes, but Copy (⌘C, the menu, Ctrl+C where a selection makes it copy) still takes the text that was selected, until the next click, drag, select all or key; Ctrl+C interrupts again only after that.
A program that takes the mouse (Claude Code's fullscreen mode, Codex, OpenCode, pi, vim with mouse on) gets the press, the drag and the release, as in Ghostty, so it selects, scrolls and copies by its own rules; holding ⇧ makes the drag Hide's selection instead, limited to the screen there, since that program keeps its history itself.
What such a program copies reaches the clipboard of the machine whose Hide window shows the pane, on this machine's panes and on a connected device's alike: the copy it sends as a terminal's clipboard write (OSC 52) is written once by the Hide window in front, and only from a pane on that window's screen, as Ghostty takes a copy from a program over ssh, and never again when the window reloads, reconnects or draws the pane again; a window behind, a pane behind another tab, another screen or a zoom, or a phone, leaves the clipboard alone.
A click with nothing dragged reaches the program only when it takes the mouse.

A pane whose agent sleeps (PRD agent-sleep) shows its state in place of the terminal, which stays hidden until the agent is back because the shell under it is not what the operator was talking to: Sleeping with the last progress line and Wake agent; Waking… with how old the resumed conversation is; or `Couldn’t resume this conversation` with the core's plain reason, Retry, and Start new session.
Its second row carries no ask while it sleeps, and typed input to the pane goes nowhere, from the first moment its end begins until the agent is back: keys, a dropped file's path, the find keys and a phone's reply alike ([The phone app](#the-phone-app)).
Opening the pane's tab by a committed move (a row, a tab, a checkout, a relation) wakes it in the same pane with its conversation; a Recent Panels preview does not, and neither does a click inside the tab already on screen.
Wake agent, Retry and Start new session take a mouse click as they take Enter: everything a pane draws over its terminal (these bodies, the reconnect caption, the attachment refusal, the header band) lies above every layer the terminal's renderer draws, so a click there never reaches the terminal (`web/src/terminals.ts` gives each terminal its own stacking context).
The pane menu offers Sleep agent on an awake agent's pane of any machine, this one, a device it connects to, or the machine the window runs on when that machine's core runs on another machine, disabled with the core's reason when the agent is working, waiting for the operator, of another kind, of a kind that closes its pane on a machine other than the core's, or has no recorded conversation; on a device that is not connected it is refused at once.
Archive session (`세션 보관`, PRD session-archive) stands right under Sleep agent, or first in that group when the agent cannot sleep, on a root agent's pane; a delegated child's pane draws it disabled (`맨 위 세션과 함께 보관돼요`), as does an agent whose conversation Hide has not seen and a pane of a device that is not connected, each with its reason; a shell and Hide AI's own agents offer none ([The sidebar's 태스크 list](#tasks-card-tabs-and-the-pr-list) says what it does).
Fork agent and Fork agent to the side follow it in the same group (PRD fork-operator-root): both are offered on a local agent pane with a recorded conversation (`fork.available`) and send `fork_pane` with the placement and the operator as its origin, which starts that conversation again as a new session the operator owns; on an agent that has a fork command but has not reported its conversation yet both are drawn disabled with the core's reason, and on a shell, an agent with no fork command or a device's pane there is neither.
Fork agent opens the fork in a new tab immediately right of the source's tab in its Agent area, or at the end when the source's tab has closed meanwhile; Fork agent to the side splits the source's tab and puts the fork to the right of the source.
A start command being accepted does not release held input: the fork must have its expected agent running in that pane and be ready, or use a kind that does not report readiness, as ordinary delivery permits.
A start that fails closes the fork and discards its held input rather than typing it into the shell.
Failure to confirm input readiness preserves the successful fork pane and discards held keys; the existing input-loss notice appears only if keys were actually held.
Either moves the keyboard to the fork as soon as the engine has made its pane, as a new tab does, with the fork's tab or split drawn from that moment rather than from the engine's later layout, unless the operator chose another view after the press or the Agent area has no room to show the tab, where the fork then waits; what is typed before the fork's agent has started reaches that agent in the order typed, an Enter a moment after the keys before it so the agent submits it, never the shell that starts it, with the node retaining it until the fork ends, up to 64 KiB and 30 seconds per holder.
Crossing either bound discards that queue and shows a dismissible notice asking the operator to type again once the pane is ready; later keys remain refused until readiness or the input deadline, at most 115 seconds from the fork worker starting, releases the guard, and late readiness never replays discarded keys.
This rule covers NodeLink keys and phone input; a phone waits for the same node holder before its reply or quick key is written.
A second press while a fork of the same pane is starting, either item, is refused with `pane.fork_already_running`, and a fork whose agent does not start closes the pane it made and shows the same failure notice for both.
On such a window's own machine, bringing a tab forward wakes what sleeps there once that machine's engine has it in front.
Web owner: `web/src/PaneView.tsx` (`SleepBody`), `web/src/sleep.ts`, `web/src/PaneRelations.tsx`.

An implemented provider whose sleep policy closes its pane retains a separate sleeping conversation row in its checkout's expanded rows.
The row keeps its confirmed title, names its provider by product name in its hint and accessible name, says in its hint that memory was low when that is why it slept, uses the existing moon and row tokens, and offers Wake explicitly; it has no pane-opening action, lineage badge, input target or numbered pane shortcut.
Saving and closing retain the original live row until authoritative close confirmation, while a sleeping or waking row follows the core's published phase.
An unknown close or wake offers Check status, disables that action while a check is pending, and never retries an external effect automatically.
The current Claude Code and Codex keep-pane behavior above is unchanged.
Web owners: `web/src/components/sleeping-session-row.tsx`, `web/src/sleeping-session.ts` and `web/src/sidebar.tsx`.

The sidebar is one list of operator-owned roots under projects and checkouts, with Needs You (`내 차례`) and Unread (`읽을 것`) raised above it.
A root whose descendant is raised enters Needs You itself, at any depth, and the raised descendant never appears there on its own row; its second line is what to do, with no verb, name, count, PR or path.
