A mission whose node waits on the operator with no Conductor or worker row to carry it is its own Needs You row, above the agent rows in this list and, in the 태스크 list's 내 차례, among the tasks by when its node began to wait, newest first as the asks stand, after the asks that came later (operator decisions 2026-11-08 and 2026-11-14; `bandRows` in `web/src/taskList.ts`), laid out as the agent rows beside it: the turn mark, the mission's mark (`--kind-mission`, `KindIcon`) where an agent row draws its logo and only while the rows draw logos, the mission's title with how long its node has waited at the title line's end, `<node> · <why>` (`외 N건` for its other such nodes, the safety net counted among them), or for a mission whose only wait is its safety net that net's line (`걸린 일 N건 · Conductor가 아직 풀지 못했어요`, `이 미션에 Conductor가 없어요 · 걸린 일 N건`), the mission's project as the place line (left out where the group already names the project, and never made up when the catalog does not hold it), and under them `확인하기`, which opens the mission, led by `지휘 다시 열기` only while no Conductor holds the mission and by `터미널로 이동` for a safety net its Conductor holds, every line on the title's column; the node's choices are not drawn on the row, since read without the context of the mission's 내 차례 card they left the operator guessing (operator report on M41); the row opens the mission's card as a desk tab; a right-click on the row, or its `⋯`, which takes the time's place under the pointer, offers `확인하기`, `터미널로 이동` while a Conductor holds the mission and `치우기`, which takes the row out of Needs You until something raised after it asks the operator, as an agent row's 치우기 does (`mission_put_away`, the mission's `put_away_unix_ms`; operator decision 2026-11-20). Opening the mission reads nothing: unlike an agent row, the mission's row stays in 내 차례 until what it asks is resolved or it is put away (operator decision 2026-11-23, [status-model.md](status-model.md#the-four-groups)). The Overview's `나를 기다리는 것` lists the same mission first with the same line and buttons, the mission's mark where an agent's logo stands and its project after the title as the agents' asks there show theirs, also when the map draws no session; a map narrowed to one project lists and counts that project's missions only, and the whole map every one the scope names. The device's Needs You number (the device rail's badge and the 태스크 list's 내 차례 band; the sidebar's Overview and Missions rows draw none) counts it once with the agents, from the device scope's `mission_asks`; which raises the mission carries is the core's one rule (`item`: waits on the operator, no row carries it, the mission still asks, the node is not dropped), with its safety net, read by the count and the list alike, so a cancelled mission keeps its record and leaves both (`web/src/components/sidebar-mission-row.tsx`, drawn by `web/src/sidebar.tsx` and `web/src/TaskLens.tsx`, `web/src/OverviewGraph.tsx`; core owners `hide-core/src/runtime/missions.rs` `on_row`, `item` and `publish_mission_asks`, `hide-core/src/agent_state/tally/scope.rs`; PRD mission-node-simple D-19).
A node's choices are one part wherever they stand, the mission's 내 차례 and the node's panel (`AttentionChoices`, `web/src/components/mission-attention.tsx`); this row and the Overview's `나를 기다리는 것` open the mission with `확인하기` instead.
Before the choices it says which of the node's finish lines failed in a few words: a CI, a check, a review or a verification by its kind (`리뷰 실패`), and the lines the plan wrote, whose sentences run long, as one count (`완료 기준 3개 실패`), each failed line in full on hover, so the choices stay on the row however long the plan's sentences are.
First comes the pull request the raise is about with its mark and the reason the PRs list gives it (`충돌`, `머지 큐 · N번째 대기`, `CI 실패`, the core's `agent_scope.prs` row; the state's word only when the list holds no row), and a line of the node that failed and that reason does not already say (`리뷰 실패`), so the choice is made with the cause in view.
The choices themselves and their order are the core's (`AttentionReason::choices`, sent as the raise's `choices`, and the core refuses any other): `Conductor에게 다시 맡기기` first while a Conductor holds the mission and the raise is not its own failure, which settles the item and hands it to the Conductor, or `그대로 두기` for an unplanned pull request with no Conductor; then what the cause takes (`끝으로`, `노드에 붙이기`, `다시 진행`); and `이 단계 빼기` last, which never sends on one press: the first turns it into `빼기 확인` in place, a press elsewhere puts it back, and only the second press sends.
A mission whose every node is finished but could not be closed because a finished node's check line is empty says so on the reason line, `작업은 끝남 · 확인 기록 빠짐 N개` (N is the number of finished nodes with a line empty, which the core counts from the book: `unmet_nodes` on the raise, `unmet` on its Needs You subject; the line has no number where the core sent none). Its choices are `완료로 닫기` first and `Conductor에게 다시 맡기기` after it while a Conductor holds the mission, and no `이 단계 빼기`, since every node's work is finished. `완료로 닫기` closes the mission as done and records on every finished node's empty lines that the operator passed them (`운영자 판단`, the same record `끝으로` leaves); like `이 단계 빼기` it never sends on one press, the first turning it into `닫기 확인` in place and a press elsewhere putting it back, because it ends the mission with those lines unproven. The question is about a mission whose every node is finished: once any node works again it goes with its choices at the next evaluation, and a press on it before then is refused and passes nothing.
The phone shows the same reason with the step's pull request mark and takes no choice.
A root whose agent opened a View the operator has not looked at is in Needs You with the `볼 것` mark, an eye in the warning badge, and the newest View as a chip under its line, its name and `외 N개` for the others (`LookChip`); the line is the agent's own, what it said or how its turn ended, as on any row, and the eye's hover still says `볼 것 · <newest View>` (operator decision 2026-11-23, replacing PRD unseen-agent-views B2's line).
Pressing the row goes to the agent only: its pane comes forward with the keyboard and no View opens; the row is read then, so it leaves Needs You for the group its own axes give it, with its own mark, and keeps the chip wherever it is listed until the View is seen or closed; a later open is news again ([status-model.md](status-model.md#a-view-to-look-at), operator decision 2026-11-23).
Pressing the chip opens that View in focus mode, the agent's pane on the left and its desk on the right with the View in front, the agent's other Views as tabs behind it, and the keyboard on it ([Focus mode](#focus-mode)), and the chip goes once the operator looks at every such View, or each is closed (operator decision 2026-11-23, replacing B3; B9, B13).
A row on a project's tree keeps to one line and draws no chip: its eye says there is something to look at, and the raised row above, or the pane head's eye, opens it.
A question, approval or block on the same row keeps its mark and line, the View's chip under it, and the row stays in Needs You after it is answered while a View is still to look at and the row unread (B11).
`볼 것` counts with the operator's turns on badges and the rail while the row is unread, and notifies as the row's other Needs You entries do (Agent notifications, below): one notice per open the operator has not looked at, `볼 것 · <newest View> · <project>`, taken back once the row is read (operator decision 2026-11-08, replacing B12).
A root whose agent, or a descendant at any depth, declared a person's turn with `hide agent ask-human` is in Needs You with a key in the warning badge in place of the `!`, named `사람 차례` on hover, and the line `<인증|허용> · <place>`, the place as the agent wrote it, a URL's query and fragment included, plain text that can be selected and copied and is never a link, with the device's chip when the agent runs on a connected device; a long place is cut on the row and whole in its hover and on the pane head ([status-model.md](status-model.md#a-persons-turn-an-agent-declares), PRD ask-human B9, B10).
The turn leads every other ask on the row, which are counted behind it (`외 N건`), and stays while the agent works or Hide AI reads its turn otherwise (B11, B12).
`다 했어요` (Done) is one part, `HumanTurnDone` in `web/src/components/human-turn.tsx`, a secondary button the keyboard reaches, under the line on the project list's and the 태스크 list's Needs You rows, on the declaring child's own tree row (sidebar, 태스크 and the tree popover), on the pane head of the declaring agent, of its root and of a parent between them, beside `답하기` in the Overview's `나를 기다리는 것`, and on the phone's row and detail; it is disabled, with the connection's reason beside it, while the declaring agent's device is not connected (B19, B21).
A press sends the agent one letter and takes the key and the line down everywhere; a parent between the root and the declaring child gets only the descendant mark and unread on its row, never the key or Done there, while its pane head says the turn with Done as the root's does (B14, B15, B18).
Wherever Done stands, a copy icon stands right after the place, one part, `HumanTurnCopy` in `web/src/components/human-turn-copy.tsx`: a press puts the whole place on the clipboard of the screen it was pressed on, exactly as the agent wrote it, a URL's query included, and the icon turns into a check for a moment (`복사됨` on hover); it opens nothing, because the operator may need the place on another machine than the one showing it, and the place keeps its look (operator decision 2026-11-08).
On a screen with a pointer the icon is drawn only while the pointer is over the place's line, or anywhere on its row in the 태스크 list and the sidebar, while the keyboard is on it, and while it shows the check; it keeps its width at rest, so the place does not move, and adds nothing to the line's height, and a screen with no hover, the phone included, always draws it (operator decision 2026-11-11).
A refused write leaves the icon as it was and goes to the log; the phone draws the same part as a touch target, writing to the phone's own clipboard.
A mission worker's turn shows on its node of the map as a key and `<인증|허용> · <place>` under the title (B25), and stands in the mission's 내 차례 as a row of its own: the key mark, the node's chip, `<인증|허용> · <place>` with Copy and the device's chip, `다 했어요` and `터미널로 이동` (operator decision 2026-11-13, replacing PRD ask-human D-18; `HumanTurnItem`, `web/src/MissionTurn.tsx`).
The mission list stands that mission in its 내 차례 with `확인하기` and the line `<node> · <인증|허용> · <place>`, and the device's Needs You still counts the turn once, on the Conductor's row.
What to do is the core's `detail`: the label's reply, else the first line of a native question waiting now in the agent's own words (up to 40 characters, `…` when cut), else the label's line, else the verb's own words (`답을 기다림`); a native question therefore reads as itself with Hide AI off and while the label still describes the turn's work ([status-model.md](status-model.md#task-identity)).
An idle or done parent still owns its children, and a closed pane or ended parent agent makes them orphan roots.
A parent waiting on a working descendant stays Working; a descendant that asks waits on its parent, so the parent keeps its own state, unread because the ask is news to it ([status-model.md](status-model.md#a-quiet-row-that-waits)).
A root with direct children has a chevron at its left and starts folded; the opened roots persist across launches and device reconnects.
Folded, it wears its descendant mark, the turn mark and N for raised descendants, and nothing when its descendants are working, asking it, idle or done: a parent waiting on a working child already shows its ring and that child's line, and a child's ask that is not raised already turns the parent unread (PRD session-record-redesign D-04).
The core still computes the working mark; no surface draws it.
Opened, its children follow most urgent first, joined to it by rails, and a child with children has its own chevron; grandchildren are one step further in, and a grandchild with children opens the tree popover from its chevron.
A child that works on another device than its parent follows the parent the same way: the parent's row opens it, and it is the parent's tree that draws it, since the child's own device lists no line for a worktree only delegated children work in (`session_folds.child_only`).
Opening a pane on another device from a child row or chip, a band's Open or a relationship step brings that device to the front, and opening a pane of this machine while a device is in front brings this machine back (`openPaneEvent`).
Past five siblings the rest wait behind `N개 더`, which opens them in place.
A tree row is one line with no branch line; a child on another device names that device on a second line, dimmed with `연결 안 됨` and not openable while it is not connected, and a child working in another worktree is not drawn again under that worktree.
A worktree whose unresolved agents are all delegated children has no checkout line, no purpose line and no fold, because the children are reached from the root that owns them.
An agent counts as a root when the core marks it so (`agent.state.root`): an orphan whose parent ended is a root, so its worktree keeps its line.
The primary, the focused checkout, a dirty or unpushed worktree and an Inactive or Cleanup one keep the line or fold they had.
A row carries the PR mark (Purpose, pinning, and PR chrome) the 태스크 row of the same agent draws: a root draws its session's list, its own and every descendant's PRs, each once by URL, whether its children are folded, behind `N개 더`, in another checkout, on another device or ended, and a child or grandchild row shows only the ones its own session holds or made (PRD project-tree-pr-family).
The mark is one PR's state and `#number`, or each state's count for several, dimmed while GitHub cannot be read; its hover shows the PR card for one PR, and for several lists each PR worst first with its state, its title and, on a root, the sessions that hold it, any of which opens, with the last read time while GitHub cannot be read.
An opened root therefore repeats a child's PR that the child's own row shows too: the root's is the gathering, the child's is its own.
A row whose pane has letters waiting on a possible draft carries a muted envelope just before its time, with no number, the quietest mark on the row; its accessible name says `Letter waiting`, and clicking the row goes to the pane as always, where the pill delivers them.
A tree row that asks draws what it asks on a second line until answered, and an AI question leaves that line once read; a blocked tree row draws its cause there instead, read or not.
A Conductor's mission decision on that line ends with an eye and how many open Views the decision links for the operator to look at (`views` on the ask; a View closed since stays on the card but leaves this count), wherever the line is drawn (the sidebar, the Overview, Desk), and with none it draws no eye; the row opens the mission's card, which lists them (`AskLine`, `web/src/components/agent-tree.tsx`).
A raised row outside the tree keeps its request line until answered, dimmed after reading an AI question; an unread result line stays bright until read.
Quiet detail lives in the tooltip, and no progress value is invented.
Hover, focus, selection and the open popover change fill and ring without adding lines or moving adjacent rows.
A row is at least 28 high, or 44 with its second line, and grows with text scale rather than spilling.
While a close names a pane, its row retains closing feedback.
⌥1 to ⌥9 select the first nine visible root occurrences top to bottom, counting duplicate raised/tree rows only once; tree children carry no number.
Web owners: `web/src/PaneView.tsx`, `web/src/PaneHeaderBand.tsx`, `web/src/PaneMention.tsx` (the pane menu's `@` list), `web/src/PaneRelations.tsx`, `web/src/components/sidebar-agent-row.tsx`, `web/src/components/agent-tree.tsx` (the verb, ask line, descendant mark, rails and chevron the three surfaces share), `web/src/components/pr-mark.tsx` and `web/src/prMark.ts` (the PR mark every surface draws), `web/src/components/letter-mark.tsx` (the letters-waiting pill and envelope), `web/src/components/agent-tree-popover.tsx`, and `web/src/sidebarTree.ts`.

### Work Hide AI does for a home

Hide AI starts agents for the operator by itself (today a suggestion's `문서 고치기` or `이슈로 남기기` in the Hide AI tool's 제안 tab, a ready branch's `PR 올리기` or `다시 맞추기`, and a Memory batch's `N개 문서에 반영`), and the work belongs to a screen, its home, not to the operator's agent lists (PRD hide-ai-work-home).
The 제안 tab's row being applied is the home of that work: it shows the progress, the branch or the pull request, the failure and the agent link, and it is where the work is read and acted on (PRD hide-ai-suggestions B11).
A fix's agent commits on its branch and stops; its pane stays open until the operator decides the branch, so `에이전트 ↗` still reaches it, and a merge, a discard, `PR 올리기` or `다시 맞추기` ends it (PRD hide-ai-branch-first-apply; the PRD's technical structure closed the pane when the branch was ready, and this was changed so the operator can still ask the agent about its branch).
The fact that Hide AI started an agent comes only from the coordination record of the spawn (`docs/delivery.md`), never from a branch or worktree name: a checkout the operator made by hand on a branch called `hide-memory-m9` is an ordinary checkout.
The work is named in words, `제안 반영하기 · s12` for a suggestion and `Memory 반영하기 · m72` for a promotion that was under way, and never by an icon of its own, because Hide AI will start other work, each kind with its own home.
`docs/status-model.md`, Work Hide AI does for a home, owns the ownership value, the group rule and the turn table; this section owns what the screen draws.

While the agent works, finishes or rests, the work is in no list: not the sidebar's tree or Working, not the 태스크 list's 작업 중, 끝남 or 쉬는 중, not the phone's list, not Overview's requests and graph, and it moves no badge, rail count, checkout count, desktop notification or push.
The agent is still found by ⌘K, the recent panel, the tab strip and `hide agent list`, and its pane header keeps the owl; only the lists that enumerate the operator's work leave it out.
The sidebar tree has no `Hide AI` head, and a checkout Hide AI made is drawn as an ordinary checkout only while it is the front checkout or an agent the operator started works in it; the Hide AI agent's own row is never in a tree.
The same rule applies inside the Checkouts fold, so a fold's count is of the checkouts it draws; the cleanup fold lists every cleanup candidate, because reviewing them is its job, and that is where a worktree kept after its pull request settled is found.

The operator is called out in two cases, and both are one row in the sidebar's Needs You and the 태스크 list's Needs You with the owl beside the status mark, the task name and a place line naming the task where the branch would stand (`herdr-ide › Memory 반영하기 · m72`).
The agent asks: a question, an approval or a block, with the same read rules as an operator's row, so an AI-inferred question leaves once read and a block or native question stays until answered.
The home has a turn: the agent committed on its branch and stopped, so the second line is `바뀐 것 보기` until the operator decides it (PRD hide-ai-branch-first-apply B3); the attempt's pull request is open, ready, passing or unchecked and nobody asked for changes, so the second line is `PR #924 리뷰` and the row stays after it is read until the pull request is merged, closed, made a draft, or gets a requested change or a failed check; or checks failed and the agent is no longer working, so the second line is `CI 실패` until the agent works again.
A draft, running checks and a requested change are the author's turn and draw nothing.
A row has one line, and its second line is the agent's ask or its block's cause, then `CI 실패`, then `PR #n 리뷰`; the core decides the turn and the screen only words it.
The row's accessible name carries `Hide AI`, the task and the turn (`Hide AI · Memory 반영하기 · m72 · PR #924 리뷰`).
An attempt with no live agent (a failure with no pull request, an open pull request after the operator closed the pane) never has a row; the 제안 tab alone shows it.

Choosing the row, by click, by its digit or from its desktop notification, does not go to the pane: it opens the lane on the Hide AI tool's 제안 tab of the row's Project with that row open, and the `에이전트 ↗` link there opens the pane.
A Hide AI agent that asks the operator something is the exception: its answer goes into the session, so choosing its row opens the pane as any agent's row does, and the 제안 tab opens only for a decision the screen takes, such as a ready branch.
A row whose Project the core has not yet named opens the pane as any row does.
The phone's Needs You row opens the agent's detail.
The digits that number agent rows follow the drawn order, so a Hide AI row is numbered with the Needs You rows above the tree and no checkout of its own takes a digit.

When GitHub reports the attempt's pull request merged or closed, the row leaves Needs You, the agent ends and its pane closes, and the worktree is removed when its tree is clean and no other agent or running program is in it; the branch is always kept, and a pull request reopened later does not revive the attempt.
A worktree that is dirty or in use stays and is named in the diagnostic log, and it appears in the disk cleanup review; nothing is removed while GitHub's answer is missing, and an attempt already merged or closed before an upgrade is retired the same way on the next start.
A kind of Hide AI task this build does not know, or a record with no task, shows its agent as an operator's row naming its task (`Hide AI` alone for none) and leaves the rest of the ledger alone.
A feature that makes Hide AI start a new kind of agent names the kind's home in code, and chooses what its turn and cleanup are, before it has a row.
A connected device's agents follow the same rules, and they hold across a restart of hided because the ledger is persisted.
Web owners: `web/src/hideAiOrigin.ts` (the words, the place line, the turn line, whether a tree draws a checkout, where a row opens), `web/src/AgentMark.tsx` (the owl, the badge and the pane header's mark), `web/src/AgentRowMark.tsx` (the row's mark by view mode), `web/src/sidebar.tsx` and `web/src/components/sidebar-agent-row.tsx` (the rows), `web/src/TaskLens.tsx` (the 태스크 row) and `openListedAgent` in `web/src/actions.ts`; core owners: `hide-core/src/coordination/started.rs`, `hide-core/src/agent_state/axes.rs` and `hide-core/src/runtime/memory_promotion.rs`.

### Work an external tool keeps

A bot the operator made or approved in its own space (봇 공간) in Settings › 봇 starts agents for its own purposes, and while it stays connected in its own space those agents are the tool's work, not the operator's (PRD hide-external-control D-09): the agent it started and the agents that one started under itself with `--parent here` (a Conductor's workers among them).
`docs/status-model.md`, Work an external tool keeps, owns the ownership value and the group rule; this section owns what the screen draws, with-hide/hide-private `docs/tasks-and-pr-list.md`, The sidebar's 태스크 list, how the 태스크 list draws it, and with-hide/hide-private `docs/missions.md`, A mission a bot conducts, what such a mission gives the operator.

Each bot connected in its own space has a cell of its own on the device rail, after the devices ([Device rail](#device-rail)), and its work is drawn there and in no device's cell (PRD hide-external-control D-45, operator decision 2026-11-14).
In a device's cell the agents are in no list of the operator's: not the sidebar's tree, Needs You, Done or Working, not the 태스크 list, Sessions, Overview's requests, graph or counts, not a Project tool's session count, a checkout's count or the device's tile, not the phone's agent list, and they move no app badge, desktop notification or push (B26, B27).
The tool reads them and answers them itself, so an agent's own question, approval, block or finished turn never reaches the operator while the tool keeps it.
They are still found by ⌘K, the recent panel, the tab strip and `hide agent list`, and Settings › 봇 counts each bot's agents and missions.

A mission such an agent conducts is the tool's too (B30, D-36): a device's mission screen, the sidebar's `미션` number, its 내 차례 rows, its decision, gate and safety-net cards, the device's Needs You, its notices and the phone's mission list leave it out, and the tool's cell lists it.

A tool's cell is a scope as a device is, and which scope is in front follows what the center shows, never a choice kept beside it (operator decision 2026-11-15): the tool's Overview or mission page, or on a Workspace a pane of an agent the tool keeps, puts the tool's cell in front; anything else puts the device the core focuses in front.
The rail's selection, the top row's name, the sidebar's lists, the Overview and the mission screen all read that one scope (B40).
So every path that moves the center moves them with it, ⌃Tab, ⌘K, ⌥n, a Needs You row, a notice, an Overview node and a mission's node alike: landing on a pane of the tool's agent selects the tool's tile, as landing on a pane of the Mac mini selects the Mac mini, and landing on any other pane selects that pane's device and shows the operator's lists.
A View reads as the pane in front of its Workspace does, since the View areas stand beside that pane and the keyboard returns to it.
Selecting a tool's tile shows its Overview as the center's page at once (D-54, B47), with this window's own machine in front under it, and the 개요 and 미션 rows of its cell open the tool's own Overview and mission screen; which cell a window shows is the window's alone.
A page that names a tool forgets it once the cell goes, so Act again on the same tool brings its tile back without taking that page into it (B44); a pane of the tool's agent in front reads as the tool's again once its work is the tool's again, as it would on its device.
A device the Overview named, its own switch's or the page's, gives way on both moves, so once the cell goes the Overview, the rail and the sidebar show the same machine:

- The sidebar has the operator's `프로젝트 | 태스크 | PR` switch and the 태스크 list's `상태 | 프로젝트` switch, on the same stored choices, and each list holds the tool's work alone (operator decision 2026-11-15).
  The 프로젝트 tab lists only the projects the tool works in, only the checkouts that hold its sessions, and under each only the tool's session trees, a delegated child under its root, as the operator's tree draws its own; it has no raised `내 차례` or `읽을 것` section, since nothing in the cell is the operator's turn, a checkout's badge counts the tool's agents there, and none of the operator's own tabs, tab groups or sleeping conversations in that checkout is drawn.
  A project on another device names its machine after its name.
  The 태스크 list draws the tool's work by state, `도구가 답할 차례` first, then `작업 중` and `끝남`, then the folds, or by project.
  The PR tab lists the pull requests of the tool's projects whose checkouts hold one of its sessions.
  The 태스크 and PR lists carry the operator's project chip in its place, on the same stored choice, reading the tool's work: its menu lists the projects the tool works in on every device it reaches, a project on another device naming its machine, each counted from the tool's 태스크 list and PR tab, offers the project in front first only when it is one of the tool's, and its turn and `+N` are `도구가 답할 차례`, drawn muted, a pull request counting as open and never as the tool's turn.
  The chip narrows the tool's lists while the stored project is one the tool works in; while it is one only the operator works in, the tool's lists stay whole and the chip reads `모든 프로젝트`, so a choice made on the operator's side never empties the tool's cell, and a choice made in the cell narrows the operator's lists on that project's device as theirs does.
  `도구가 답할 차례` is what waits on the tool's answer: its agent's question, approval or block, read or not.
  Nothing in the cell takes the warning colour, since nothing in it is the operator's turn: a Conductor row's mission chip keeps its `!` in the grey of the cell's count (operator decision 2026-11-14), and no row names the tool, since every row is its work.
- A task on another device, such as an agent the tool started with `--machine`, names its device on its row (B45).
- A cell with no work says `아직 맡은 일이 없어요`, in each of the sidebar's three lists and in the Overview's work tab (B42).
- The mission screen lists the missions the tool keeps, on every device, its head naming the tool; its turn group reads `도구가 답할 차례`, nothing takes the decision colour, and neither a row nor an opened mission's map head names the tool, since every mission there is its own.
- The Overview draws only the tool's agents, on the projects it works in, and names the tool in place of its device switch; the device's Overview never draws them (D-48), but for a person's step or a View that put one in the operator's 내 차례, which the operator's Overview draws on its graph and in `나를 기다리는 것` as every place 내 차례 shows does (operator decision 2026-11-14).
  The map's filter reads the cell's 태스크 list: its `도구가 답할 차례` chip counts and the side column lists the tasks the sidebar puts there, a task waiting on a person counts as `작업 중` and a finished one under the results, and nothing on the map is `내 차례`.
  The map's centre is the tool, drawn with its tile's letters and without the `+` a device's map gives `나`, since what the operator starts is never the tool's work; for the same reason the tool's map has no start circles, no Add project place, no project `+` and no `New agent`.
  Its side column lists what waits on the tool under `도구가 답할 차례`, the missions waiting on its answer among them, where a device's lists `나를 기다리는 것`.
  The Projects tab counts the tool's tasks in each project, even one the operator works in too, the tool's turn as `도구 차례 N` in no warning colour.
  The places dragged on a tool's map are its own; the filter and the hidden and folded projects are one choice for every map, as they are across devices.
- The Overview and `미션` rows of the sidebar draw no number in a tool's cell, since their numbers are the operator's (B41).
- Every press in the cell lands on the tool's work: a session row opens its pane, and a checkout row, or a project row through the checkout it opens, brings that checkout forward as the operator's does while the tab it shows is the tool's, else opens the tool's session there that the keyboard was in last, or its first, since the checkout would otherwise show the operator's tab and leave the cell; a mission row of the tool's mission screen opens beside the tool's list, on whatever device the mission runs; and on the tool's Overview page a session's record opens in that page's panel while the session is the tool's.

The operator can answer in a row's pane from the cell; the answer takes the row out of the tool's turn, and the work stays the tool's (B43).
⌥n numbers the list the cell shows as it numbers a device's: the 태스크 list's rows as the chip narrows them, else the 프로젝트 list's session roots.

The tool's name is drawn wherever the tool's work reaches the operator's own cells, and never inside the tool's own cell (B31, D-15): as a quiet badge cut short in a narrow space after the title of the agent's pane head, its ⌘K result, and the mission screen's row and the map head of a mission its agent conducts, and, in the sidebar's rows (the projects list and the 태스크 list, an agent's or a mission's) and the Overview's `나를 기다리는 것` card and graph node, as muted words leading the line under the title, `nightly · 어느 순서로 할까요`, since such a first line keeps only the title, its chips and the time (operator decision 2026-11-14; `ToolLed`).
The name is led by the bot's owl, and its hint says the work is the bot's (`봇 nightly의 일`); once the bot lets go the name stays as where the work came from, and the hint says so (`봇 nightly에서 시작됨`).

An agent a 전체 bot started is the operator's from the start, in every list, count and notice as the operator's own (the row's `tool` with `owns` false), and says who started it (mission-87 c3).
Its logo wears the bot's owl as a corner badge, where Hide AI's owl sits on an agent Hide AI started, in 내 차례, the sidebar's rows, the pane head and the Agent tab; with logos hidden the owl stands alone in the logo's place.
Its hint and accessible name end with `<name>(봇)이 띄움` (`Started by <name> (bot)`), and the pane head's quiet tool badge is drawn only for work a bot keeps, not for this.
Web owners: `BotFace` in `web/src/components/bot-mark.tsx`, `BotBadge` in `web/src/AgentMark.tsx`, `botStarter` in `web/src/bots.ts` and `agentWithProfile` in `web/src/agentRow.ts`.

One thing of the agents reaches the operator anyway, as one row in the sidebar's Needs You and the 태스크 list's Needs You of the device's cell, never in a tree, the row staying in the tool's cell too (B29, D-37): a step only a person can take (`hide agent ask-human auth|permission`), the agent's own or a child's, which also notifies on the desktop and the phone.
A View an agent the tool keeps opened with `hide file open`, `hide diff open` or `hide browser open` shows exactly as an operator agent's does, but inside the tool's cell only: its row carries the `볼 것` mark and the View is in that session's Files list, until the operator looks at it (D-38, D-55, B29).
It calls no one: the row stays out of 내 차례, the counts, the app badge, notices and the phone, and in the cell it does not move the row into `도구가 답할 차례` or add to the tile's grey number.
A View the tool itself opens, with no Hide session behind it, is the operator's own open as any caller's that is no agent.
When the operator disconnects the bot, the bot leaves, or the operator changes it to 전체, all its work is the operator's from that moment, still running: its agents and missions enter the lists, counts and graph at once, and their next question is the operator's turn; the tool's tile goes, and a window showing its cell shows This Mac (B16, B44).
Changing the same connection back to its own space takes the work back, and a new connection under the same name takes none of it.
A connected device's agents follow the same rules.
A checkout Hide made for the tool's agent is drawn in a device's tree by the rule of a checkout Hide AI made ([Work Hide AI does for a home](#work-hide-ai-does-for-a-home)), only while it is in front or an agent the operator started works in it, and without Hide AI's task line; once the tool lets go it is an ordinary checkout.
The phone has no tool's cell (B46; `docs/INSTALL.md`, What each system supports); a tool's person's turn on the phone's list leads its second line with the tool's name, as the desktop's sidebar row does, and a View its agent opened is not on the phone at all.
Web owner: `web/src/toolCells.ts` (which cell is in front, `frontTool`, read from the center's page and the pane in front, and what it holds, `toolSidebarProjects` the projects its 프로젝트 and PR tabs draw, `toolProjectChoice` the stored project read for the tool), `ToolProjectFilter` in `web/src/SidebarProjectFilter.tsx`, `ToolTaskLens` in `web/src/TaskLens.tsx`, `ProjectList` in `web/src/sidebar.tsx` and `SidebarPrs` in `web/src/SidebarPrs.tsx` with their `tool`, `BotTileView` in `web/src/components/device-rail.tsx`, `toolGraphActivity` in `web/src/overviewGraphModel.ts` (the map's filter in the cell), `drawnInTree` in `web/src/hideAiOrigin.ts` (the tool's checkout), and `ToolMark`, `ToolLabel` and `ToolLed` in `web/src/components/tool-mark.tsx` (the tool's name); core owners: `hide-core/src/coordination/started.rs`, `hide-core/src/agent_state/axes.rs`, `hide-core/src/agent_state/tally/scope.rs` (`ToolCell`) and `hide-core/src/runtime/access.rs`.

### Agent logo view mode

The View menu, Help and Settings stand together, in that order: stacked at the foot of the device rail, their hints and menus opening to the right, or, while the rail is hidden, at the sidebar footer's right edge after the usage chips, opening upwards.
The View menu is an eye button whose hint and accessible name are `보기` (`View`).
It opens beside its button with the label `에이전트 로고`, two choices, `행 왼쪽에 표시` and `숨기기`, with a check on the current one, a separator, and a muted line that is not a choice: `⌥ 누르고 있으면 잠깐 보기` (`Alt` on Windows and Linux).
Arrow keys move through the choices, Enter chooses, and Escape or a click outside closes the menu without changing anything and returns focus to the button.
Choosing the checked choice only closes the menu.
While the daemon is not connected both choices are disabled and the check stays on the last value received.
A browser has no Agents hold, so its menu has no key line and no peek.
The choice is a core preference, `ui_state.agent_logo_mode` (`shown` or `hidden`, `hidden` when never chosen), changed only by `agent_logo_mode_set`; `ui_state_update` never changes it, the check always reads the core's value, every window of one daemon changes at once, and it survives a restart.
A mode the core refuses leaves the check and the screens as they were and records a diagnostic, with no alert; a change the core accepts but cannot save is drawn as chosen until the next start and records `ui_state.save_failed`, as the theme does.
Hidden is the default and draws the screens as before.
Shown draws the agent's 16-pixel logo after the status mark and before the title in the sidebar's agent rows (a sleeping row after its moon), in 태스크 rows and child rows, and in the Agent tabs; a row's second and branch lines indent past it in the 태스크 list, and a kind with no logo draws the neutral `>_`.
A row Hide AI started wears the owl as a badge on the logo, and a profile agent's row its profile badge, which stays after the status mark while hidden.
Search, overlays, Overview, a Project's conversation list, the graph, Task boards and the phone never follow the mode, and the pane header, the usage chips and the Usage pages draw their logos in both modes.
Another computer's screen shown in this window draws with this window's mode, not that computer's ([Device rail](#device-rail), Another computer's screen).
While hidden, holding the Agents key (⌥, or Alt) draws the same logos for as long as the number keycaps show: the peek is the existing hold state, with its 0.15 second delay, its end on any other key, on a window that loses focus and while a layer is open, and it adds no key listener.
Shown, the same hold shows only the keycaps.
Changing the mode or peeking never changes a row's height, the scroll position or the selection.
Web owners: `web/src/components/view-menu.tsx`, `web/src/agentLogoMode.ts` (the one rule), `web/src/drawnDisplay.ts` (whose choice a screen draws: its core's, or the showing window's), `web/src/AgentRowMark.tsx`, `web/src/TabBar.tsx`, `web/src/TaskLens.tsx`, `web/src/components/sidebar-agent-row.tsx` and `web/src/components/sleeping-session-row.tsx`; core owner: `hide-core/src/runtime/events.rs`.

### Pane turn row mode

The View menu's second section, after the agent logo choices and their key line and a separator, is labeled `요청·진행 줄` (`Request and progress line`) and offers `한 줄` (`One line`, marked `기본`, `Default`), `두 줄` (`Two lines`) and `끔` (`Off`), with a check on the current one; the keyboard, Escape, a repeated choice and a disconnected daemon behave as for the logo choices.
It shapes the second row of every agent pane ([Agent panes and the Agents explorer](#agent-panes-and-the-agents-explorer)) in every window of one daemon at once, and its name is how many rows the turn takes over the terminal: one, at most two, or none:

- One line keeps every turn on one line: the request at most two fifths of the row when an answer follows, the answer in the rest, each cut where it overflows, an ask as much as a quiet turn; the whole request stays on the turn card the turn opens.
- Two lines put the request (after the children button) on its own line and the arrow and answer on the line under it, each cut, so a turn takes at most two lines; a turn with only a request or only an answer takes one, and a parent with no turn to show keeps its children button alone on one.
- Off draws no row and no request, progress or result; a pane with live children keeps the children button on the head before its tools (`PaneHeadChildren`), whose warning border and tree carry a raised descendant's ask, and Working keeps its thin line.
  The pane's own approval, question, block or stop rides the head after the title as its verb in words, in the warning colour (a stop in the text colour) with no border, fill or wait, since the status mark before the title already says it is the operator's turn (`web/src/PaneHeaderBand.tsx`, `PaneAskMark`; operator decision 2026-11-21); a question with others waiting reads `질문 N`, counting them all, so the head holds one mark; it is named by its verb and what is asked, opens the same turn card as the turn on the row does and dims once read as the row's ask does; an ask is never hidden by the mode.
  The questions the agent put to the operator that still wait are words in the question colour (`--open-ask`), `질문 N`, with no border, fill or warning colour, while the agent works too; a press opens the turn card, and a pane with none draws nothing.
  One line and Two lines end the second row's turn with them, `· 질문 N` right after the answer, or after the request when no answer follows, as a row's line ends with `· 백그라운드 N`, so the head keeps its width for the title (operator decision 2026-11-21); beside the pane's own question, which is one of them, the tail counts the others, `· 외 N건`, right after the question's words, and the wait keeps the row's end; a pane with no request and no answer to show draws the row for `질문 N` and the oldest question's words, cut; Off keeps them on the head after the title (`PaneOpenAsksMark`), unless the pane's own question rides there, whose mark then counts them all.
  No row of the 태스크 list, the project list or the sidebar counts them ([status-model.md](status-model.md#a-question-still-waiting)).

The choice is a core preference, `ui_state.pane_turn_row_mode` (`one_line`, `two_lines` or `off`, `one_line` when never chosen), changed only by `pane_turn_row_mode_set`; `ui_state_update` never changes it, an unknown stored value opens one line with the `ui_state.pane_turn_row_mode_unknown` diagnostic, and it survives a restart.
The mode never changes the terminal's viewport or its PTY grid, because the row overlays the terminal.
Another computer's screen shown in this window draws with this window's mode, not that computer's ([Device rail](#device-rail), Another computer's screen).
Web owners: `web/src/components/view-menu.tsx`, `web/src/drawnDisplay.ts` and `web/src/PaneHeaderBand.tsx`; core owner: `hide-core/src/runtime/events.rs`.

### Subagents

An agent's own subagents, the ones it runs inside its session, are drawn where its children are, on the work map, and every list says when an agent rests on them, so a pane running six of them never looks like one working alone and a resting agent whose subagents still run never looks idle (operator decisions 2026-11-14, reversing issue #810, and 2026-11-15).
A subagent has no pane and no row of its own, so it is not a delegated child: nothing about it opens anything, and its mark, a small working-blue dot (`SubagentMark`, `--size-subagent-dot`, the dot the work map circles a session with), stays smaller and lighter than a child's status mark and is never the dashed ring of an agent that rests and waits.

- The pane's children chip, at the left of its second row, or on its head while the turn row is off: one chip for everything working under the agent, the children part as before (the tree, the first child's mark and the count), then after `·` the subagent mark and how many subagents run, `≡ ● 2 · • 3 ⌄`; a pane with subagents and no children shows the chip with the subagent part alone, `≡ • 3 ⌄`.
  Its hint and name add `서브에이전트 N개 작업 중` to the children's counts, and only a child's ask colours its border, since a subagent asks the operator nothing (`PaneTreeButton`, `web/src/PaneHeaderBand.tsx`).
  The pane head has no subagent chip of its own (operator decision 2026-11-15: one chip, not two).
- The chip's popover, the child tree: after the children, a `서브에이전트 N` group lists the subagents the agent names, oldest first, each as one dimmed line, its mark, its kind, its task and how long it has run (`SubagentLine`, `web/src/components/subagent-row.tsx`); a kind or task the agent does not say is left out, and a line with neither reads `서브에이전트` and the time.
  The lines are disabled options: the keyboard passes over them and a press does nothing.
  Past the ones listed, one line says `외 N개`; an agent whose events name no subagent by id (Grok) lists none and says `서브에이전트 N개 작업 중` in that one line.
- A sidebar row (the project tree, the 태스크 list) draws no mark on itself for them: a working row with subagents looks like one without (operator decision 2026-11-14, after a title mark read as an ellipsis, a "more" menu or the unread dot).
  A row with subagents has a parent's chevron, and opened, lists them after its children on the same rails as the same dimmed lines with nothing to press and no hover, as many as a parent shows children (`navigator.siblings_shown`), then `외 N개`; folded, as rows are until opened, it draws none (`sidebarTreeLines`, `web/src/sidebarTree.ts`).
  They also reach a row through its line, when it rests on them or a finished turn left them running, below.
- The Overview work map: one small blue dot per running subagent, eight at most, slowly circling just outside the session's circle, which itself stays as it is (`SubagentOrbit`, `web/src/overviewGraphNodes.tsx`); a reduced-motion setting holds them still.
- The phone: the same lines as the desktop rows, and under a row the same list as an opened desktop row.
  The row's toggle counts both, `자식 2 ● 서브에이전트 3 ⌄`, the subagent mark setting the two counts apart, and a row with subagents and no children gets the toggle with that part alone; opened, the children come first, then up to five subagents as dimmed lines with nothing to tap, lighter than a child's row, then `외 N개`, or the one line `서브에이전트 N개 작업 중` for an agent that names none (`AgentItem`, `web/src/mobile/ListRows.tsx`; the core sends the count and the first five as `subagents` and `subagent_rows` on each row, `PhoneAgent` in `hide-core/src/agent_state/tally.rs`).
- Nothing is drawn for none, nor for a count Hide cannot read: an agent that declares none, a hook that is not in or switched off, a session older than the hook (the reasons the core keeps in `PaneChildren.instrumented`), each exactly as none.
  The done count is drawn nowhere.

A resting agent whose subagents still run waits on them, as one waits on its children ([status-model.md](status-model.md#a-quiet-row-that-waits)): the still dashed ring, in Working, its line `서브에이전트 N개 기다림 · …`, and with children working too, `자식 1개 · 서브에이전트 2개 기다림`.
Every wait line counts what it waits on, `자식 N개 기다림`, `서브에이전트 N개 기다림`, `백그라운드 N개 기다림`, `답장 N개 기다림`, and running checks keep `CI 기다림 · PR #N`.
A turn the label read as finished is not hidden by the subagents it left, while the operator has not read it: the unread dot, the line ending `· 서브 N개 남음`, its hover `서브에이전트 N개 남음`; once read, the row waits on them again, and when the last one ends the row ends as background work does, with one Done notice.

The count comes from each agent's hook.
Hide's hook keeps a `working` and a `done` count per pane and session and reports them to the core through hided; the core keeps them in `agent_hooks::facts`, lays them as `PaneHookTokens`, and reads the running count once for both the pane head (`PaneChildren.subagents`) and the row (`SidebarAgentSnapshot.subagents`, laid before the lineage by `Runtime::lay_subagents`, and for a device's rows from that device's tokens and kit), through `sidebar::running_subagents`.
docs/agent-hooks.md owns the hook.
`working` is how many subagents run now, and `done` is how many this session has finished; a new session in the pane starts both again from zero, so `done` is a running tally of the session.
Each count is separately knowable, and a count the core cannot read is `None`, never zero, in the core's facts; the screens above draw it exactly as none.
Claude Code, Codex, Grok, OpenCode, omp and Antigravity report a count, and Pi (it runs no subagents) and Cursor declare none; the declaration is `subagent_counts` in `hide-agent-adapter/src/declarations.rs`, and no agent reports a blocked subagent.
Codex's count follows its subagents past the main turn's end, as Claude Code's does: a subagent still running when the main agent's turn ends stays working until its own end, one the main agent stops itself leaves without being counted done, and a quit and resume of the session counts none of the subagents the quit cut off (docs/agent-hooks.md, Which subagents run).
With the count, the hook lists the running subagents its agent names by id, oldest first and at most 16 (`HookSubagent`, `report::SUBAGENTS_REPORTED`): the agent's own words for each one's kind and task, made one short line (64 and 120 characters), and when the hook first heard it run; the core keeps them with the count (`agent_hooks::facts`) and lays them on the row as `SidebarAgentSnapshot.subagent_rows`, never more than it counts.
Each agent says what it says, and a line shows only that (docs/agent-hooks.md, What the hook keeps of each subagent):

| Agent | Kind | Task |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Claude Code | `agent_type` (`Explore`, `general-purpose`) | the `description` of the main agent's `Agent` call that started it |
| Codex | none: every one is `default` | the name the main agent gave the task (`docs fidelity`) |
| Grok | its events name no subagent by id, so none is listed, only counted | |
| OpenCode | the child session's agent (`explore`) | the child session's title |
| omp | the agent definition the spawn named, unless the general `task` | none |
| Antigravity | none | the message its parent opened it with |
Settings > Agents lists no subagent count either: the kit's feature table has no `subagents` entry.

### Closing an agent that spawned others

Closing a pane or a tab asks about the agents spawned from it only when one of them runs outside what closes (PRD close-agent-subtree).
Those are the live descendants of every agent pane that closes, on this machine or a connected device, asleep or not, less the panes that close anyway; a device that is not connected contributes none.
With none, the close is the ordinary one: a quiet pane closes at once, a working or attention pane asks the Stop-work confirmation, and an unknown status shows the status notice.
A pane whose agent is still starting (`시작 중`) never shows the status notice: it closes at once, or asks the Stop-work confirmation when its start carried the operator's first prompt, which the close would otherwise drop ([status-model.md](status-model.md#close-protection-is-separate-from-read-state)).
A target whose own status is unknown still shows only the status notice.
The Stop-work confirmation has one line under its title and lists every pane that closes, named as the sidebar names it, with its status mark; a working, asking or unknown pane adds its status word, and a quiet one is dimmed with its state in the mark's tooltip and its accessible name.
While it is open the list is live: a pane that settles stays listed and dims, one that starts working or asking brightens, and its title, line, buttons and focus stay as they opened.
While a listed pane's status is unknown, `Stop work and close` is disabled, `Keep open` takes the keyboard if the button held it, and a `Check status` in the confirmation reads the status again without closing it.
`Stop work and close` sends the close the confirmation shows at the press.
Otherwise one sheet opens in place of the Stop-work confirmation, titled `이 에이전트와 자식 에이전트 N개를 닫을까요?` (a tab: `이 탭과 자식 에이전트 N개를 닫을까요?`), N counting only the descendants outside.
It has no sentence under the title, and lists the target first and then its descendants in tree order, indented by depth, each with the sidebar's status mark and name and a device chip when it runs on another device than the target; the list scrolls inside the sheet when it is long.
Closing a tab lists every agent in the tab as a target, each followed by its own descendants, so a working agent beside the parent is shown before it closes.
A line above the list counts, with the sidebar's marks and neutral text, the descendants waiting for an answer to a question, approval or block (`답 대기`), working (`작업 중`), holding an unread result their row marks with a dot (`확인 안 한 결과`; a delegated child's finished turn is its parent's news, so it reads quiet here as on its row, status-marks B24) and unreadable (`상태 모름`), leaving out a kind with none; those rows are bright and add their status word in neutral text, and a quiet one (idle, read, asleep) is dimmed with no word, its state in its mark's tooltip.
A list longer than the ten rows it shows whole folds its quiet descendants that have nothing below them needing the operator: when two or more do, the list shows the targets, the rows that need the operator and the quiet ancestors on their path, then one line `끝난 자식 N명` (en `N finished children`) with a chevron, which opens the folded rows below it in tree order and closes them again.
Each row is focusable, and its accessible name is its name, its device when it differs, and its status word.
`이것만 닫기`'s tooltip and accessible description say what it leaves: the children keep running and come up into the operator's own list.
Colour in both sheets is the status marks' and the one destructive button's (`모두 닫기`, `Stop work and close`); `취소`, `Keep open` and `이것만 닫기` are neutral.
Its buttons are `취소`, `이것만 닫기` and `모두 닫기`, and `모두 닫기` holds the keyboard when it opens, so Enter closes the whole subtree; Escape or `취소` sends nothing and gives focus back.
While a descendant's status is unknown, `모두 닫기` is disabled, `취소` holds the keyboard, and a `상태 확인` in the sheet reads the status again without closing it; while a target's own status is unknown, `이것만 닫기` is disabled too.
A descendant whose pane's terminal failed to restore runs nothing and is listed as a finished child, never as unknown, so it never disables `모두 닫기` ([status-model.md](status-model.md#where-each-axis-comes-from)).
`이것만 닫기` closes the target alone as the ordinary close, with no second question; its direct children become the operator's roots and their own children stay under them.
While the sheet is open its list is live, as in the removal dialogs: a descendant that appears shows up, one that goes drops out, and the title's N and the count line follow.
`모두 닫기` sends one event naming the target and exactly the descendants the sheet shows at the press: one that appears after the press is not closed, and one already gone counts as closed.
The core checks each listed pane again as it arrives; if one now needs a status check, an earlier close of one is still unresolved, more than 64 are listed, four closes with children already run, or every close slot is taken, nothing closes and the notice says which and what to do.
A tree of any listed size is otherwise admitted: its panes take the close slots it holds a few at a time instead of all at once.
A pane whose earlier close was refused is simply closed again, since asking again is the retry.
If every descendant goes while the sheet is open, it turns in place into the target's Stop-work confirmation, a quiet target dimmed, whose `Stop work and close` closes the target alone; a descendant that appears under an open Stop-work confirmation turns it into this sheet the same way.
Either one closes by itself only when its target pane or tab is gone or its device disconnects; otherwise it waits for the operator's answer and runs nothing on its own.
A sheet that turns into the other leaves the keyboard on the sheet itself, never on a close button an Enter meant for the old one would press.
The core closes the deepest descendants first, each pane only after every descendant below it is gone, and the target last, so no descendant ever surfaces as a root on the way; each row reads `closing…` until it goes, and the target's row counts the panes closed so far over every pane the close takes, the target included (`닫는 중 12/34…`).
A descendant whose close is refused, times out, or loses its device keeps itself and its ancestors, the target included, open while the other branches finish; the close failure notice shows and the detail goes to the diagnostic log.
Closing the same target again lists only what is left.
The local panes and tabs closed this way are one Reopen closed tab entry, named after the target: one ⇧⌘T brings the target back first and then every descendant closed with it, each under the parent it was closed under, grandchildren included, and the keyboard stays on the target.
Each agent comes back as itself: its conversation resumes, and its letters, the watch on it and its parent relationship continue in the new pane; a descendant left open is drawn under its reopened parent again.
A member that fails to come back stays in the entry together with every member under it, which is not started, so no child reopens without its parent; the other branches still come back, the notice says how many did not, and the next ⇧⌘T retries what is left.
While the tree is still closing, ⇧⌘T waits with `The latest close is still being confirmed.` rather than reopening an older item.
A device's close leaves no entry, as before.
Every entry to a pane or tab close uses this: ⌘W or ⌥W, ⌘⇧W or ⌥⇧W, the desktop menu's commands, a tab's ×, the tab menu's `Close tab…`, the pane header's ×, the pane menu's `Close pane`, and the agent row menu's `Close tab…`; closing a View, the phone app and the Overview are unchanged.
Web owner: `web/src/close.ts` (which sheet, which panes and descendants, and their states), `web/src/Overlays.tsx` (both sheets), `web/src/components/subtree-list.tsx` (the list both the sheet and the removal dialogs draw); core owner: `hide-core/src/runtime/tree_close.rs`.

### The doorbell's lines in a terminal

Hide types `🦉 <writer> <kind>: …` into a Claude Code, Codex or Grok pane to wake it for a letter, and the agent echoes it as a prompt row (`❯ 🦉 …`, `› 🦉 …`).
It is a letter between agents, not something the operator owes an answer to, so the pane draws that row, and the rows the agent wraps it onto, as blue-grey words (`--terminal-letter-text`) on a pale band (`--terminal-letter-band`), an opaque mix of the theme's background so the words keep 4.5:1 (`scripts/check-token-contrast.mjs`) and the band stays distinct from the Needs You amber; Needs You stays the place for what the operator has to do.
The shell reads only the shape of a row: it begins with the agent's prompt glyph, an owl and a space, and the rows indented under it begin with text rather than the agent's own output marks.
It does not know who typed the row, so these limits stand: a line a person types in the same shape (`❯ 🦉 hello`, also while it is still a draft in the composer) is marked too, and a Grok row that its keyboard focus frames (`│ ❯ 🦉 … │`) is not marked until focus moves.
The selection limit below is the third.
A line that does not match the shape, such as `❯ 🦉가 뭐야?` or one with no prompt glyph, is drawn as the agent drew it.
Telling a typed line from a letter needs the core to mark the line it typed, which is not built.
The text and the copy are unchanged: the shell lays two decorations over the agent's own cells and rewrites nothing; where the agent paints its own background on a row, the band replaces it for that row.
The band is a bottom-layer decoration, so a drag selection (`term.select`) is painted over it and stays visible on a letter row, and the words are a top-layer foreground-only decoration, which the selection does not hide; `web/src/terminalBellLines.selection.test.ts` holds this against the WebGL addon's own cell color resolver.
Third limit: that holds for the WebGL renderer only. When the terminal falls back to xterm's DOM renderer (WebGL failed to start or its context was lost), the band is drawn over the selection, so selecting a letter row does not show the selection background on it; the copied text is unchanged. It is an emergency path, and showing the selection in both renderers needs an overlay designed apart from decorations, which is out of this feature's scope; no test holds this behavior, since it follows the renderer.
The prompt glyphs and the output marks that end a wrapped line are the agents' declarations (`BellEcho`, [delivery.md](delivery.md#the-bell-line)), not a list in the web shell.
Web owner: `web/src/terminalBellLines.ts`, read after a frame by `web/src/terminals.ts`; cost contract: [PERFORMANCE_TESTING.md](PERFORMANCE_TESTING.md#the-doorbells-lines-in-a-terminal).

### Terminal links

A terminal pane links what its program prints, whichever program it is: an http(s) URL, a path, and a link the program marked itself (OSC 8).
A plain-text path is a link only where it names something on this Mac, so a word that merely looks like a path draws nothing; the check runs when the pointer reaches a row, never per frame.
A relative path is looked for under the pane's folder and then under its checkout's root, `~/` names the home folder, and a location written after it (`:12`, `:12:5`, `#L12`, `(12,5)`) is where the file opens.
A link may be written against prose with no space: brackets and quotes of any script, sentence punctuation, and Korean, Japanese or Chinese words before or after it.
So `열어뒀어요(http://127.0.0.1:8391).`, `캡처는("/tmp/a.png")입니다`, `「docs/a.md」를`, `“https://example.com”`, `[label](src/a.ts)`, `{"path":"/tmp/a.png"}` and `--file=/tmp/a.png` each link only the address in them.
These places are found by the kind of character, a Unicode class, not from a list of marks: a link starts after an opening bracket or quote or an `=`, ends before a closing one, stops at sentence punctuation such as `,`, `。` or `、`, and a run of Hangul, kana or Han letters is cut once, where its script meets the link.
A run that touches `/`, `.`, `-` or `_` belongs to the name, so `docs/회의록에`, `회의록/a.md` and `한글.md` are checked only as written.
The original spelling is checked before treating punctuation, attached prose or a location as context: if `docs/a.md)에`, `docs/a.md)`, `(src/a.ts:12:5)` or `src/a.ts:12:5` is itself a file name, that complete name wins.
A drive path a Windows shell prints with backslashes, such as `C:\Users\example\AppData\Local\Temp\shot.png`, is a link like its `C:/` spelling.
Among the bounded spellings, the longest existing path wins, and of two the same length the one that keeps the token's start; a failed or budget-skipped longer check never establishes a shorter link.
A path ends at a closing bracket whatever follows it, so `docs/a.md)foo` links `docs/a.md` when only that exists, but never at an opening one, so `app/(auth)/page.tsx` never links `app/`.
In Korean prose such as `보드 보기 (docs/README.md)에 C안을 추가했습니다.`, only `docs/README.md` is underlined and clickable when that is the actual path.
A URL cannot be checked, so it is the longest spelling from its scheme whose brackets balance, which holds no quote and which does not end on sentence punctuation: in `PR #682 (https://github.com/o/r/pull/682)이 끝났습니다`, only `https://github.com/o/r/pull/682` is the link, `https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercury_(planet)` keeps its brackets, and `https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/서울이` links all of it.
Ranges follow the terminal's actual cells, including wide glyphs, combining sequences and wrapped rows.
A path or URL the terminal wrapped at its last column, or a TUI broke at its own margin and indented, is one link across its rows; a URL spans rows only where the terminal wrapped it, because it cannot be checked.
In a pane with no agent, a URL spans rows only where the program's text ran past the last column: one that fills its row exactly and ends with a line break stays one link, apart from a word starting the next row (hide-private issue 312); on Windows the pane cannot tell, and such a URL still joins ([INSTALL.md](INSTALL.md#what-each-system-supports)).
In an agent's pane a URL the agent marked as a link (OSC 8, as Claude Code and Codex mark theirs) ends where its mark ends: one that fills its row exactly stays one link, apart from a word starting the next row, and one the agent broke across rows is one link across them (hide-private issue 591); this holds on Windows too.
A URL the agent did not mark (OpenCode's, or one inside a code block) still joins the next row's first token at the last column, because an agent's screen breaks its own long lines with line breaks and a URL cut there would open only its first row.
A drag copy reads the same ends: a row the link or the pane says ended keeps its line break, and a URL the agent broke is copied whole.
Under the pointer a link is underlined and the pointer becomes a hand; a click on it is the link's and never reaches the program, and a drag across it selects its text and opens nothing.
A click opens it in the front Workspace: a URL or an HTML file (`.html`, `.htm` or `.xhtml`, case-insensitive) as a browser display, another file in View at its line (an ordinary tab, not a preview), a folder revealed in the Explorer.
A file in another registered checkout opens the same way in the front Workspace and moves no checkout: the pane that printed it stays on screen, the Explorer keeps its tree and selection, and the file stays that checkout's document, so its own Workspace shows the same text and closing one view leaves the other.
A folder in another registered checkout brings that checkout forward to show it in its Explorer, as does any path while the Overview is on screen, on any machine: a checkout on a node or device comes forward once that machine's engine has moved there, and the file opens then; with no Workspace in front a URL goes to the default browser.
Right-clicking a URL offers Open in Hide, Open in default browser and Copy link above the terminal's ordinary editing and pane actions.
Right-clicking an existing path offers Open, Open in default application, the host's Reveal in Finder/File Explorer/file manager and Copy path; an HTML file in a registered checkout, or outside every checkout where the window opens outside files, offers Open in browser and Open source instead of Open.
Open source opens that HTML file in View at the line its link names.
These choices use the exact target under the pointer, including an OSC 8 label, and keep that target after the pointer leaves it; Copy path copies its physical path without the line suffix.
Opening or dismissing the menu moves no checkout, opens nothing and sends no click to the terminal program; a late path check cannot reopen a dismissed menu or replace a newer one.
A right-click opens only the menu, never the link, also on Windows, where the menu comes after the button is released.
⌘-click (Ctrl off macOS) hands the link to the operating system: a URL to the default browser, a path to its default application or, for a folder, a Finder window, within the limits below.
A file outside every registered checkout, such as `/tmp/shot.png`, opens on a plain click in the desktop app's own window on the core's machine as a read-only tab of the front Workspace, with an External (외부) badge beside its name (PRD outside-path-in-hide).
An image, a PDF and a video open in the viewers a checkout file uses, an HTML file as a browser display, and any other text, a script such as `.sh` or `.py` included, as read-only text at the link's line that saves nothing.
Holding the pointer on the badge shows the file's full path; there is no warning and no confirmation.
The tab splits, moves and closes like any View tab, the front checkout and the Explorer's selection stay as they were, and the file never appears in the Explorer.
A second click on the same file brings its one tab forward, reads the file again from disk and goes to the new link's line; an HTML page and its Open source are two tabs.
A text file of 16 MiB to 256 MiB shows the too-large notice a checkout text file of that size shows; a file that cannot be read opens the tab's ordinary unavailable state with a Close button.
While the Overview is on screen the pane that printed the path brings its Workspace forward and the tab opens there.
The tab lasts as long as the operator keeps it: it survives a reload of the window, and it is gone after the app quits or hided restarts, when a click opens it again.
Another window, a browser tab of the web shell, the phone, or a window on another machine, opens no outside file: a click there goes to the operating system as before, and the tab another window opened shows dimmed with its name and the hint that only the window that opened it shows it, never its contents or its path.
A program, an application bundle and an installer are only revealed; audio, a folder, a file over 256 MiB and any other binary go to the operating system on a plain click, as does a click past the 32 outside files the app holds at once.
An agent on the core's machine opens the same tab with `hide file open <path>` (an HTML file as its text) or `hide browser open <path>` (an HTML file as a page): it lands in the active View area of the agent's own checkout as that agent's open, waits in Needs You as 볼 것 until the operator looks at it, and `hide view list` and `hide view status` show it as `external` with who opened it and nothing of the file.
What a click hands the operating system the agent is refused instead, with a reason and its next step (`file_missing`, `file_unavailable`, `unsupported_kind`, `not_html`, `outside_files_full`, `outside_file_no_window` without a desktop window); an agent on a connected device is refused `outside_file_on_device` and copies the file into its checkout.
The operating system opens only a plain document (text, source, a PDF, an image, audio or video) or a folder whose name has no extension; anything else, such as a script, a program, a spreadsheet, an application bundle or an installer, is revealed in Finder, never opened.
A program's OSC 8 link takes the same routes with no confirmation dialog: `file://` and `vscode://file/` addresses are paths, any other scheme is not opened, and a refused link goes to the diagnostic log.
A pane on an SSH device, and a page outside the desktop app, links URLs only, because its paths name files this Mac cannot check.
A path with a space in it is a link only where the program marks it (OSC 8), since plain text gives no way to tell where it ends.
Web owner: `web/src/terminalLinks.ts` (detection), `web/src/terminalLinkProvider.ts` (the check and the routes), `web/src/terminals.ts` (pointer-to-cell lookup), `web/src/PaneRelations.tsx` (target-specific menu), `web/src/actions.ts` (`openLink`, `openTerminalPath`), `web/src/editor/lineRequest.ts` (the line), `web/src/externalFiles.ts` and `web/src/ExternalDisplay.tsx` (an outside file's tab); desktop owner: `desktop/src/main/localPath.ts`, `desktop/src/main/externalFiles.ts`; daemon owner for an agent's open: `hided/src/outside_open.rs`; core owner: `hide-core/src/runtime/view_areas.rs` (`external_open`, `external_grants`) and `hide-core/src/runtime/workspace_control.rs` (`open_external`).

## The Project tool

Web owner: `web/src/ProjectTool.tsx`, `web/src/projectStatus.ts` and `web/src/ProjectDetail.tsx` (the worktree table, 남은 것 and 작업 비용 as card tabs on a desk); core owners: `hide-core/src/agent_state/tally/project_status.rs` (the counts and 흘린 것), `hide-core/src/runtime/strays.rs` (마무리 맡기기, 놔두기 and 되돌리기), `hide-core/src/usage_ledger/project_cost.rs` (each session's spend) and `hide-core/src/usage_ledger/tasks.rs` (the tasks and the week).
The Project tool is the rail's first tool and is about the project of the checkout in front (PRD lane-two-columns D-14, B26).
Its head names the project, then its repository, path and machine on one dim line, then ⚙, which opens Project Settings, and `✕`, which closes the tool column.
The left of the screen is state and the middle is work under way; this column is what came of it (PRD project-panel-results D-01), so it holds no list of what waits on the operator, which the sidebar's 태스크 and PR list already show.

Under the head one line counts `세션 N · PR N · 워크트리 N` (B1, B2).
세션 turns the sidebar to 태스크 narrowed to the project, PR to the PR list narrowed the same way, and 워크트리 opens the worktree table in the viewing column, reading pressed while the table shows, when a second press folds it.
A project with no GitHub remote has no PR item, and after a failed read a ⚠ and the last read's age stand beside its number (B3).
While Hide AI's summaries are on and the project has open missed work, `놓친 일 N` in the missed-work colour (`--missed`) ends the line and opens the Overview's backlog with the project's 놓친 일 unfolded (B4).
A number is a quiet bar until the core has answered, and a 0 is drawn as 0 (B5).

`흘린 것 N` lists the worktrees where work ended unfinished: changes not committed, commits not pushed, or a pull request whose CI failed, where no agent works or waits on the operator (B6, B7).
A pull request that only has a reviewer's change request is not one, and neither is a checkout of the default branch.
A row shows a mark for what is left, the name of the task that last worked there (its branch when nothing names it), what is left (`커밋 안 한 변경 4파일`, `푸시 안 한 커밋 3`, `#1815 CI 실패`, as many as apply) and how long ago.
With nothing left the part is not drawn and the disk line moves up; past five rows it shows five and `+N 더` (B8).
Pressing a row brings the session that last worked there forward with the keyboard, when it is still open, and opens the evidence in the viewing column (B9): `남은 것`, the worktree's changed files and its commits not pushed, or the failing pull request's card when CI is all that is left.
`남은 것` lists the changed files, each opening its diff when the worktree is in front, and the unpushed commits from the front checkout's header read, or the committed files of another worktree; the core reads that worktree by its own path, apart from the checkout in front, so it lists whatever else is on screen.
A read that failed says so in one warning line with its reason, never `nothing left`; a failed read of a list already shown keeps that list under a line saying it is the last read.
Hovered or focused, a row's age gives way to `마무리 맡기기` and `놔두기`, both reached with Tab and read by name (B10).
`마무리 맡기기` writes fixed words for what is left to that session (`커밋하고 PR 열어줘`, `브랜치 푸시하고 PR 열어줘`, `#N CI 실패 원인 고쳐줘`, together when several apply), or, with no session open, starts an agent of the same kind there (else the one the operator last started) with the same words (B11).
While it sends the button shows progress, and once sent it stays off until the row goes, so a second press sends nothing; once the agent works the row goes and the session shows as working on the left (B12); a failure keeps the row, turns the button back on and goes to the diagnostic log (B13).
When that agent ends with something still left the row comes back (B14).
`놔두기` takes the row out and leaves `되돌리기` in its place for a few seconds; a new commit in the worktree or a new CI failure on its pull request brings the row back (B15).
The core keeps 256 놔두기; past that the oldest goes, its row comes back, and the diagnostic log says so (B16).
An agent that edits files without staging them is seen when its turn ends, since the core reads the project's worktrees again then (B17).

The disk line shows the project's layers in one bar, its size, `머지됨 N · X GB 비울 수 있음` and `정리`, which opens the disk cleanup sheet; with nothing to free it is the bar and the size alone (B18).
While measured it is a skeleton, a project measured only in part is dim with no size, and a project on a connected device has no disk line (B19); how the panel's watch starts and stops a measurement is in Disk allocation and cleanup.

The column keeps one gap between its parts, the disk line included.

`최근 7일` shows the merged pull requests (with the open ones), what one cost and its tokens, how long one took (from the first call to its merge, with `실제로 일한 시간`, the working time of every session added up), the missions (with how often a node was opened again), and the seven days' cost with a bar per day (B21).
Each bar has its weekday under it, the last reading `오늘`, a small dot over it on a day something merged, and a legend (`비용`, `머지`) beside the total; hovered or focused, a bar's hint opens below it, clear of the legend, and reads `<day> · $X · 머지 N`.
A figure with nothing to show yet has its words dimmed, its card as any other's, and says what fills it instead of `–`: `PR이 머지되면` for the per-pull-request cost and time, `아직 없음` for missions; a figure with a value is drawn as usual.
A week with no task, no cost, no mission and no pull request merged or open draws no number at all (a week a person merged in is not empty, since the pull request counts are the project's own): the week and the costly tasks lie faint and colourless under `이번 주엔 작업이 없어요`, `에이전트가 여기서 일하면 머지된 PR, 비용과 시간이 쌓여요` and `에이전트 시작`, which opens the start panel on the project; the faint shapes are fixed decoration that is neither read nor pressed, while the core's first reading is still the full grey bar (B5).
The per-pull-request figures average the tasks whose pull request merged in the seven days; a task with no pull request counts only in the total and in the line `결과 없이 끝난 작업 N개 · $X ›`, its words muted and only its numbers in the warning colour, which opens the 작업 비용 table on 결과 없음 and is absent at 0 (B22).
The cost follows one rule everywhere (B23): a quiet bar before the core's first reading, `$0.00` when the reading found no priced call, `–` when no reading has succeeded, the last amounts after a later failure, an agent with no price named apart with its calls in no amount, `≥` when only part is priced, and `이 기기에서 실행한 것만 집계해요` for a project on a connected device, whose calls this machine does not read.
`비싼 작업` lists the three costliest tasks with their result in a fixed column (`작업 중`, the pull request's mark and number, or the result's word), so every name starts at one edge, then the name, `+N` child sessions, cost and time; a row opens the table with that task unfolded, and `전체 보기` opens it on 전체 (B25).
A task is named as the sidebar names its session: the agent row's title while it is open, else the record's own title (a name the operator gave first), and `<agent> 세션` only when the record has no title but its tool's name.
`Hide AI 제안 N ›` ends the column as one row under a rule while Hide AI has suggestions for the project and opens the Hide AI tool's 제안 tab (B26).

The 작업 비용 table opens in the viewing column as `작업 비용 · <project>` (B27): the seven days' total and day bars, the amounts by model and by agent, then `전체 · 머지됨 · 진행 중 · 미션 · 결과 없음` with each filter's count, and the tasks costliest first with their result, cost, tokens, time taken and working time.
