Below 520px the tokens and working time move under the task's name, so every item stays at the 360px minimum (B35).
A task is a top-level session with its children and grandchildren; a child that made its own pull request is a task of its own, so the tasks never add up past the seven days' total (B28).
Unfolded, a task lists its sessions under their parents with agent, cost, tokens and working time, `가격 없음` for an agent with no price (B29).
The result is the first of `작업 중` (a session works or waits on the operator), the pull request it made (open, merged or closed; one it only worked on when it made none), `변경 남음` (changes left in the checkout it ran in, for the task that ran there last), `main 커밋` (a `git commit` one of its sessions ran, as its session file recorded it, in a checkout on the default branch or whose worktree reads merged) and `결과 없음` (B30, D-20).
A mission is one row with its pull requests merged out of made; unfolded it lists each node with its pull request, cost, retries and reopenings (B31).
Under 결과 없음 the result column holds `마지막에 한 말` and `대화 보기`, which brings the session forward while it is open and otherwise opens its record; the words are the line the record kept for the session's last judged turn, else the first line of its open row's last words, and empty when neither is left (B32, D-21).
A filter with nothing reads `이 필터에 맞는 작업이 없어요` with `전체 보기`, a project with no task in seven days `최근 7일 작업이 없어요` (B33), and past 200 tasks a line says `나머지 N개는 7일 합계에만 들어 있어요` (B34).
Hide AI's own use is in no task and stands under the table as `Hide AI 사용 $X` (B24).

The core derives the counts and 흘린 것 only on a publish whose inputs moved, and builds the tasks only when the ledger worker reports, the record's summaries change or those counts are derived again; nothing on those paths reads a file or runs a process (B37; [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md#project-status-and-cost)).

## Desk

Moved to a private document: `docs/desk.md` in with-hide/hide-private.

Web owner: `web/src/DeskScreen.tsx`, `web/src/deskRoom.ts`, `web/src/components/desk/`, the Desk row in `web/src/components/sidebar-header.tsx`, the row click in `web/src/components/sidebar-agent-row.tsx`.
While Settings › Labs › Desk is on, the sidebar's Desk row sits on its own line between the Overview row and the `미션` row, with the warning mark while a session Desk started asks the operator, and is drawn selected while Desk is in front; with the switch off there is no row.
While Desk is in front its row also wears the green `켜짐` mark (`Badge variant="success"`) and shows a `✕` under the pointer or focus, and pressing the row leaves Desk; its hint says `작업 화면으로 돌아가요 · 마지막에 본 곳: <project › place>` with the recent-tab chord.
Leaving Desk goes to where the operator was before it (`deskReturn` in `web/src/keyboard.ts`): the mission or automation whose detail was the place used just before Desk while it is still a row, otherwise the tab or screen used before it, the head of Recent Panels, so a terminal running no agent, or a Workspace the keyboard never went into, is still where Desk goes back to; All projects when neither is left.
The row's press and the chat head's `나가기 ✕` at its right end go there, as switching Desk off while it is in front does.
Next recent tab (⌃⇥ in the desktop app, `` ⌥` `` in a browser, Ctrl+Tab on Windows and Linux) pressed in Desk's room walks the recent place order, of which Desk is itself a place (see [Recent navigation](#recent-navigation)); after a mission or an automation its first chord lands where leaving does, and with no place before Desk it does nothing.
The head of the session beside Desk carries a ↗ button before `파일` and `터미널 ⌘J`, its words `원래 화면에서 보기 · Desk에서 나가요` in its hint so the head keeps its room for the session's path and name at the panel's first width: it opens that session's own tab (its terminal with the sidebar and files) as a ⌘-click (Ctrl-click on Windows and Linux) on its sidebar row does, and Desk leaves the front; `터미널 ⌘J` still shows its pane in place.
A session chip in Desk (a reply's, a handed line's, a thread's, the composer's) is named by the session's sidebar row while the sidebar lists it, the title every surface calls it by, whatever name the words that named it carry, so an agent id, pane id or branch an agent wrote never shows; a session the sidebar no longer lists and Hide keeps no record of keeps the name the words gave, dimmed, and has no press. A Desk card is named by its session's sidebar row once it has one.
A session chip whose pane is gone but whose record Hide keeps (a session Desk named, its record read off the core's lock and the session-sync thread by `desk_records`, `hide-core/src/runtime/desk/named.rs`) is named by the record's title with its status slot empty and its agent's logo, at the secondary opacity, and can be pressed; its hover shows the app's session card from the record (its line, project, place and pull requests), and a press opens the record beside Desk read-only: the head says `끝남` after the title and carries no children chip, own-tab, `파일`, `터미널` or menu, the second line names another device's machine and the record's pull requests, the conversation is read from the record, and in the box's place one line holds `끝남 · <time>` and `이어서 하기`, which resumes the session where the session record's row would and opens it beside Desk rather than in its own tab, or the session record's one-line reason it cannot (the same request and reasons as the record screen and the session line, `resumeRequest` in `web/src/useSessionResume.ts`) (`DeskRecordPanel` in `web/src/DeskScreen.tsx`, frame K). The chip of the session open beside Desk is ringed (`data-desk-session-open`). Desk keeps at most 64 such sessions in its store (`MAX_NAMED_SESSIONS`, `hide-core/src/desk.rs`), the one named least recently that no chip of the newest lines names going first; an entry a later Hide wrote that this one cannot read is dropped alone, a session named on another machine id reads as no record, and a record read that failed (not one the record lacks) is asked again for that session alone, after 30 s doubling with each failure in a row up to 15 minutes.
When a Desk turn starts a session and nothing is open beside Desk, neither a session nor the tab panel, the first session the turn started opens beside Desk once its pane exists, read from the turn's own job items and never from its words, a start that failed passed over and one the agent retried under the same job opened; with something open beside Desk it stays a chip, a second session the same turn starts opens nothing, and a letter handed to a running session opens nothing (`newSessionBeside` in `web/src/deskRoom.ts`, frame J).
A mission Desk's answer names as `@mission(<id> <title>)` is drawn as its title where the answer names it, and its mission chip in a row of its own right behind the paragraph that names it (several missions of one paragraph side by side, wrapping to the next line when the room runs out; `after` of `MarkdownChip`, `web/src/MarkdownText.tsx`), which opens the mission's map in the tab panel; Desk's chip is two lines (Doop N2 가): its mark (✓ once done), number and name, then its cells, `k/n` or its state's word and, while the operator's turn waits, `! 내 차례` with the chip edged in the warning colour; a planning mission says `계획 중` and a cancelled one `취소` with no cells (the cancelled chip wholly dimmed), a merge wait and a finished one keep their cells and `k/n` and add their word in the merge colour and the done colour; it is 168 to 280px wide and only the name is cut short, the whole in its hint (`chipLine`, `cellsFromCounts`, `web/src/missions.ts`; `DeskMissionChip` and `MissionChipOf`, `web/src/components/mission-chip.tsx`, one rule for a job card's chip, an answer's and the head of the session that conducts a mission, the cells read from the store's mission and, for a job card whose mission the app does not hold, from the counts it carries); a mission the app does not hold keeps the answer's title with no chip.
The core reads both kinds of mention from the finished answer (`session_mentions` and `mission_mentions` in `hide-desk/src/lib.rs`), a title or name keeping parentheses that close on its line, and carries them on the reply; an answer still streaming shows its text alone and turns its mentions into chips when it ends.
A mention is a chip wherever it stands in the answer, in a table's cell, a list item or a quote as in a paragraph (the table's cells are drawn apart from its lines, `TableWidget` in `web/src/editor/markdownLivePlugin.ts`, and draw the same parts).
Desk's instructions tell its agent to name a session by the quoted task name `hide ps` shows and a mission by the id `hide ps` prints after `mission`.
A file path in Desk's answer or the session's words, once the turn has said it, is a chip like the session chip, its kind's icon and its name, when it names a file inside a registered project of the session's machine (a relative path read under the session's own folder; Desk's own folder is no project): a press opens it in the tab panel as the app's file view, and the pointer shows its tile and under it its full path. A path outside every project that names a file or folder of this computer is the same chip with a small ↗, whose hint is one line, the file manager's item and the path (`Finder에서 보기 · <path>`, `파일 탐색기에서 보기` on Windows, `파일이 있는 폴더 열기` on Linux), with no preview, and a press only shows it there, selected in its folder, never opening or running it; the path never reaches the core. A path that names nothing, a folder inside a project, an outside path of a connected device's session (`docs/INSTALL.md`, What each system supports) and a path inside a fenced code block stay words; inline code that holds a path alone becomes the chip, and code with other words in it stays code. One rule reads them (`web/src/answerPaths.ts`), at most 32 per answer, a project's file checked by reading its first byte on its machine and an outside path through the desktop host's check, each answer kept 10 s; every answer on screen shares one line of checks, four read at once, one read per file however many answers name it, and a check with no answer in 10 s leaves that path as words while the others become chips. Desk's own answer has no project folder, so only an absolute path in it becomes a chip.
Desk's conversation keeps its foot in view while the operator reads there and stays where they scrolled otherwise (`holdsFoot` in `web/src/desk.ts`, followed in `web/src/DeskScreen.tsx`).
It follows sizes, not keys: a reply drawn after Desk opens, a card that grows, or the box under it gaining a line keeps the foot in view, so Desk opened again shows the newest answer rather than the top.
Typing in the box never moves the conversation: only scrolling up lets go of the foot, and reaching it again holds it, while the small scroll the browser makes itself as content grows below counts as neither.
While Desk's agent works, one line at the foot of the conversation says what it does now and how long the turn has run (`WorkingLine` in `web/src/components/desk/working-line.tsx`): `생각 중`, `답하는 중` once it has written, or the step its newest tool call is on, named as the turn's folded steps count it (`세션 보는 중`, `파일 읽는 중`, `편지 쓰는 중`, `세션 시작하는 중`, `명령 실행 중`; the core's `desk_live.step`, cleared when the agent writes again), from the send until the turn ends, the same for every agent, and not while an approval card waits on the operator. Only the word moves, a slow shimmer about every 3 s, still under reduced motion; the line's height is fixed; when the turn ends the line goes and the folded steps line above the answer stays. The thread of the session beside Desk draws the same line at its foot while the session works, its word from the newest line of its running turn (`파일 고치는 중` for an edit), its time from the row's last prompt; the turn's own fold says `작업 · 파일 n · 명령 m` while it runs too.
While Desk is in front, pressing an agent's row in the sidebar, in a project's tree or in the 태스크 tab, opens that session beside Desk instead of bringing its tab forward, and the row is drawn selected with a hint saying it is open beside Desk; ⌘-click (Ctrl-click on Windows and Linux) brings its tab forward as on any other screen.
Every other way to an agent (an ⌥ number, a notification, another computer's show) brings its tab forward whatever screen is in front.
⌘E (Ctrl+Shift+E off macOS, the app's Toggle right lane chord) in Desk's room is what it is in a work tab, the session panel being Desk's right side: it closes the session open beside Desk, as its `✕` does and the tab panel with it, and pressed again opens the session just closed beside Desk through the same `openDeskSession` a sidebar row's press uses, with the keyboard in its input box as a work tab's ⌘E gives it back to the pane; with no session closed during this visit of the room, or with the closed session's pane gone since (closed, archived, woken in another pane), it opens nothing, forgets it and only the log says so (`closed` in `web/src/deskRoom.ts`, the web's memory alone, since the core keeps no open session and the room still starts with the right side closed; operator decision 2026-11-25).
An output the tab panel cannot read (deleted, no permission, the device not connected) says why in plain words inside its tab, with `다시 시도` that reads it again, never a code such as `not_found`; the code goes to the diagnostic log and no banner or notice appears (`FileOutput` in `web/src/DeskOutputView.tsx`, PRD desk-2-ui B28).
An output of a session on a connected device is read from that device: a card's output carries the View's device and checkout, and a line the session typed that no card lists takes the device and checkout of the session open beside Desk, so the read never goes to this machine's boundary (B27).
The session beside Desk has a head of one line, its status, agent, ancestors, work name, children chip, `파일` and `터미널` as icons, its menu and `✕`, and a quiet second line only when there is something to know: the machine when it is another device (this machine is never named), a device that is not connected, the mission it conducts as Desk's two-line mission chip (its number and name in it) with the Conductor pane's hover card and its Show task steps pin (`PaneMissionCard`, `web/src/PaneMissionChip.tsx`), or the one it works a node of by its number and name, and its plan chip (`SessionHead`, `web/src/components/desk/desk-session.tsx`).
Under the head one shelf gathers what the session made, its outputs and its pull requests (the pane's PR list, which a View that showed one does not repeat), grouped by kind (`이미지 n · 보기 n · 파일 n · PR n`) on one line that never wraps, the image group with two of its pictures overlapping; a group holding an output the operator has not looked at ends with the look eye the sidebar draws (`StatusMark` `look`) and stands first, and the eye goes once that output is shown in the tab panel (`desk_output_seen`), a PR never carrying one since it is not a View.
A thing alone of its kind shows itself in place of its count (its name, an image with its picture, a pull request as its mark), shows its tile (below) under the pointer, or the PR card every PR mark shows for a pull request, and opens in the tab panel with one press; a larger group opens its list under the shelf, its files and pages as tiles three to a row and its pull requests as the app's PR list rows (stale, closed and who holds each as every PR list says them), six of them and `+ n개 더` for the rest, and a tile or row opens in the tab panel; ⌘-press opens a pull request on its host; the output in front of the tab panel is ringed (`OutputShelf`, `web/src/components/desk/output-shelf.tsx`). An output a turn opened in the conversation (`보기를 열었음`), and a folded turn's outputs, are the same tiles in a wrapping row, read from the machine the session runs on, or from the card's own place when the card lists it, as a press opens it (B27); a pull request among them stays a row, and a Desk card's outputs chip stays as it was.
Every file or page an output shows is one tile, 150 by 100 with its name under it (a file chip's card says the path instead), the same in the conversation, the shelf's list, the shelf's hover and a file chip's hover (`PreviewTile`, `web/src/components/desk/preview-tile.tsx`): an image is its picture, a PDF its first page with its page count (`12쪽`), a video its first frame with the play mark and its length, a page the still the tab panel's page view has (an HTML file opened as a file has none and is its kind's icon), a Markdown or text file its first heading or line and the three lines after it; with no preview it is its kind's icon and one reason, `읽는 중…` with the spinner, `못 읽었어요 · 다시` where only `다시` presses and reads it again, `너무 커서 미리보기 없음 · <size>`, or `<machine> 연결 끊김` for a file on a device that is not connected, which is not asked for. Views a turn opened one after another sit side by side in one row, folded or open. A preview is made in the window, for every tile drawn, from the file's bytes read over the file channel (a device's node reads its own file), once per file until it changes on disk, and kept for the window's life: at most 64 kept, the least recently shown let go first, two made at once, a text read only to its first 4 KiB, an image past 8 MiB, a PDF past 32 MiB or a video past 64 MiB not read past its first byte, and a preview with no answer in 30 s counted as unreadable and stopped, reading and drawing nothing more (`web/src/filePreview.ts`).
A failed Desk turn says its reason once: the agent's own words stay in its reply, the notice says `답을 끝내지 못했어요` in the failed (red) colour, the one notice drawn so (a blocked turn and a failed install keep the warning colour, a stopped Desk agent is neutral words), and adds the agent's reason only when the reply above did not say it, without the protocol's error name (`Internal error:`); the reply's small `중단함` is left out beside that notice, and an older notice's `다시 시도` is gone once a newer message was sent, and the `다시 시도` of a failed or blocked turn is hidden while any message waits in the line, so an older message is not sent again ahead of the one waiting; it returns when the line is empty (`failureReason` in `web/src/desk.ts`).
The `@` menu with nothing matching what follows `@` stays open as one row, `맞는 세션이나 프로젝트가 없어요`; ↵ then sends the words and Esc closes the row.
The chat's head starts with Desk's own mark (`DeskMark`, the one the sidebar's Desk row draws) before `Desk`, and ends with `＋ 새 대화` and `나가기 ✕`.
A turn an agent recorded as interrupted (the thread's `interrupted` origin, the same for every agent) is one quiet line `중단됨` in the conversation, never the operator's bubble.
An approval card is what Desk's agent asked, as the agent named it: its kind (`셸 명령`, `파일 수정`, `파일 읽기·검색`, `다른 작업` with the agent's own title for anything else), and its `폴더` line, the folder the core names (where a command runs, the folder that holds the files an edit changes, Desk's work folder for a read).
An approval card stands on the ask surface the question line above the box and the sidebar's 내 차례 rows ask with (`border-warning/50 bg-warning/10`), its kind (`셸 명령`) first in the warning colour and then `Desk가 이 일을 하려고 해요`, with no `!` mark; `거절…` is a words button, `항상 허용` and any other answer are secondary buttons, and `한 번 허용` is the card's one primary in the warning fill (`Button variant="ask"`, `DeskApprovalCard`, `web/src/components/desk/desk-chat.tsx`). A command longer than two lines on an approval card is folded to two, with `전체 보기` under it that shows it whole and `접기` that folds it again; the card's buttons are the agent's own options, `거절…` first, then the agent's other answers, `항상 허용` and `한 번 허용` last, in the agent's order within each, each named by its kind with the agent's own words as its hint, and by the agent's words alone where two share a kind or the kind is one Hide does not know; a card that offers no rejecting option has Hide's own `거절…` first, which ends the request without `중지`; what the operator presses goes to the agent as that option, and the answered card says `항상 허용함`, `한 번 허용함` or `거절함`, or the agent's own words for an answer Hide has no word for (`DeskApprovalCard` in `web/src/components/desk/desk-chat.tsx`; feedback 1, frame A2, option 가, operator decision 2026-11-25, PRD session-docs-and-assistant D-92).
Under Desk's box, before the agent, `🛡 다 허용 ⌄` names how Desk answers what its agent asks and opens upward: `Desk가 일할 때 묻는 방법` over two rows, the chosen one lit with a green ✓ (`DropdownMenuRadioItem mark="check"`) and each name in the text colour over a description in the quieter one, `다 허용`, marked `기본`, `묻지 않고 바로 해요.`, and `에이전트대로`, `<agent>의 요청마다 그 선택지 그대로 물어요.`, naming the agent chosen; the choice is one `desk_settings_set {permission}`, held by the core for every agent Desk offers (`DeskPermissionMenu` in `web/src/components/desk/desk-composer.tsx`).
The session beside Desk has no such choice: its box writes to the session's own agent, whose permission is its own.
Desk's box and the session's box are one `DeskComposer`, padded inside as ⌘K's input is (`px-md`, `py-sm`), with 📎 on the text's start line.
Files dragged from the system anywhere over Desk's column (its head, conversation and box) light Desk's box with the border it has for a drag, and dropping them there attaches them to it; anywhere over the session panel beside Desk (its head, shelf, conversation and box) does the same for that session's box, while a sleeping session's wake line stands in its place takes none. Nothing else is drawn: no overlay, no dashed box, no words, and the dragged file looks as the system draws it; the sidebar and the tab panel take no drop (`FileDropZone`, `web/src/components/desk/file-drop.tsx`). What a drop attaches, a file past the cap and the reading band are the box's own, as a pasted or picked file's.
Under `다 허용` the core answers every request with the agent's allow-once option and draws no card; a request that offers no allow-once option is a card under either choice. Under `에이전트대로` every request is a card, but a line whose every command is `hide`, writing nowhere but `/dev/null` or a standard stream (`2>&1`, `>/dev/null`), which the core answers allow-once itself; a line that runs another program (`| head`), substitutes a command, reads or writes a file, or names a `hide` other than Desk's own is the agent's to ask (`hide_line` in `hide-desk/src/permission.rs`). Desk starts each agent in its own mode with the operator's own settings, as the terminal does, so what an agent asks and what it runs without asking is its own; Codex starts in its CLI's own default, since its ACP adapter reads no mode from its settings.
↵ in Desk's box sends while a reply is being written too, the stop button staying: the message waits below the reply as the operator's bubble, dimmed, its files drawn on it, with `대기 중` and a `✕` that takes it out before it goes (`desk_queued_remove`); the box lets the words and the files the send carried go once the core lists the message waiting, and a message the agent could not take (its sign-in, a session that did not open) comes back from the core as the first waiting one, words, chips and files together, so the box never gets it back and the next send carries only what is written then; a message whose file Desk's folder no longer holds stays in the conversation with `파일이 없어 보내지 못했어요` under it and no `다시 시도`, is never sent again, and the next waiting message goes, the operator attaching the file again in a new message; a message whose files the ones still waiting to be sent leave no room for is refused before it is sent, its words and files staying in the box under `보낼 차례를 기다리는 첨부 파일이 너무 많아요`; when the core says the line is full (`queued_full`, at `queued_max`, now eight) the words stay in the box under `대기 줄이 찼어요`. While the agent's refusal holds the line (`queued_held`), each waiting bubble reads `다시 시작하면 보내요` instead of `대기 중`, and the notice that stopped the line carries the restart that frees it (`다시 시작` on any kind, `로그인했어요 · 다시 시작` on the sign-in notice), which sends `desk_restart`, since an agent that cannot ask has no box to send from: the core frees the line and sends the first message at once, the rest one by one as each turn ends, a full line included.
What the room beside Desk holds and how it opens and closes is `docs/desk.md`'s.

## Hide AI: the Hide AI tool

Moved to a private document: `docs/hide-ai-tool.md` in with-hide/hide-private.

## The mission screen

A merge turn names the most recently held PR still open in the last GitHub read, with `+N` for the other open PRs; merged or closed PRs stay in the node history and never replace an open merge target.
The turn card, sidebar, mission list and phone read the same open PR list (`node.merge.prs`); the closing report names all of them.

Web owner: `web/src/MissionScreen.tsx`, `web/src/missionList.ts`, `web/src/components/sidebar-header.tsx`; core owner for which mission's events are sent: `hide-core/src/runtime/mission_events.rs` (`mission_shown`).
Every mission is listed on a screen of the page's own beside the Overview and Desk, which lists the missions of the device in front on the rail, every project of it, and opens one's map beside the list; it edits no mission (PRD mission-screen D-01, D-02).
A mission pressed anywhere else opens its map as a card tab on the desk of its Conductor's Agent tab, under the rules of a pull request's card (operator decision 2026-11-09, replacing D-09, B12, B14, B19; PRD lane-desk-pair-view D-13).
There is no Mission tool in the right lane (B18).

The sidebar's `미션` row sits on its own line under the Overview row (under the Desk row while Desk is on, see Desk) and above `Add project`, the waypoints mark before it and, on its right no number, since the missions that wait on the operator are already in the one 내 차례 number and their own rows (a decision, letters the Conductor has not got, a worker the core raised, a person's turn a worker declared, a merge or join order, the safety net, no Conductor), its hint `미션` (operator decision 2026-11-25, replacing the count of open missions with a 내 차례); the tabs below stay 프로젝트, 태스크 and PR (B1).
Pressing it replaces the center, whatever was there, with the screen on its table, every project and 끝남 folded, never on the last mission shown, and the row is drawn selected while the screen shows (B2, D-04); `Open missions` (미션 열기) in the command palette and the View menu does the same and has no chord until one is bound (B21).
Every `터미널로 이동` on the screen (the Conductor's row, a row of 내 차례, a node's panel, a checker) asks the core for that pane as a sidebar row does, a device's pane on its device, and the screen gives way to the Workspace once the pane is in front.
While it is on its way a quiet `여는 중…` line stands at the top of the screen, and when the pane does not come (its terminal closed, which reads `그 터미널은 이제 열려 있지 않아요.`, its device or the core refused it, or no answer within 15 seconds) the screen stays and that line says why with Dismiss; it is the same line All projects, the Overview page and Desk draw for an open pressed there (`web/src/OpeningStatus.tsx`, drawn by `CenterScreen` in `web/src/App.tsx`; with-hide/hide#2505).
A node's or a checker's button is off only while no pane is named: a named pane whose agent row is gone is still asked for, so the press answers instead of doing nothing.

The table's head reads `미션 · <device> · N개`, then the project chips, `모든 프로젝트` chosen first; a chip narrows the rows to that project and the count to its missions, a chip whose project lost its last mission reads as every project again, and a chip that leaves out the mission the detail shows returns to its table (B3).
The rows are grouped 내 차례, 진행 중 (plans included), 머지 기다림 and 끝남, top down, each head with its count and a group with none not drawn; an open mission with anything in its 내 차례 stands there whatever its state, so that group's count is the sidebar row's number; 끝남 shows the last three to close and `모두 ›` opens the rest in place (`접기` folds them) (B4).
Here `모두` includes the loaded closed missions: the newest 128 that ask no one, plus any closed mission still asking someone; open missions always remain loaded.
The shelf keeps at most 2,048 older closed missions; past that, the oldest close loses its index row, body and event history, with a diagnostic, and new work is accepted.
Startup reads the index one row at a time and retains the same newest closed boundary.
If storage keeps failing, at most 32 shelved bodies and histories wait for saving; a new create then returns `mission_save_pending` with a retry message and a diagnostic, preserving those records until saving succeeds.
Existing live missions can still close and remain loaded while that pending limit prevents further shelving.
Older closed missions are shelved and absent from the snapshot, this screen, chips and map cards; `hide mission list` still lists their title and project with `shelved: true`, and explicit `hide mission show <id>` or `hide mission reopen --mission <id>` reads their file without retaining a separate cache.
A row shows the mission's number, the state pill (in 내 차례 the operator's action in the hold colour with no `!`, since the group and the row's border already say it is their turn: 답하기, 편지 읽기, 막힌 곳 보기, 확인하기, 머지하기 or 합치기; a mission no Conductor holds draws no pill, since its `이어받기` button says it, while the phone, which has no button, draws `이어받기` as that row's pill), the mission's title, its one line (the question, `Conductor에게 편지 N통 대기 · <what to do>` for letters the Conductor has not got, the stuck worker's node and line, a worker's person's turn as its node and `<인증|허용> · <place>`, `머지 순서` with each pull request's PR mark, `걸린 일 N건 · Conductor가 아직 풀지 못했어요` for the safety net, `남은 노드 n개` or `보고만 남음` for a mission no Conductor holds, or `이 미션에 Conductor가 없어요 · 걸린 일 N건` when raises stand there, or `지금: <node>` with its worker's mark), its project (the catalog's name, else the last folder of its path), its steps as a cell per node with `n/m`, and how long it has run; it draws no device chip, since every row is the device's in front (B5, D-11).
While a mission shows letters to its Conductor waiting (`Conductor에게 편지 N통 대기`), a worker raised only because its own letter to the Conductor is held (a draft, an approval, three bells, undelivered) is folded into that line in 내 차례 by one rule (`shownEscalation`, `web/src/missions.ts`); a worker raised for anything else keeps its own item.
While a mission runs, a ready pull request its plan left to the operator to merge (`merge.state` `due`, PRD mission-destination D-11) stands in 내 차례 as `머지할 차례` with its node's chip, which stands the mission in the list's 내 차례 with `머지하기`, and makes the mission its own Needs You row with the line `<node> · 머지할 차례 PR #n`; a pull request the mission's scope `pr` leaves to the operator stands in none of them until the mission waits on its merges, where the merge order stands as before (D-35).
What waits on the operator stands in 내 차례 above the map and nowhere else on the page: the summary beside the map lists it in no group of its own, and the node's card wears a thick border and ring in the hold colour (`--mission-hold`, 내 차례's colour), a dot of it on its corner and `내 차례`.
A node raised to its Conductor is never a card of the operator's (the principle of 2026-11-11, with-hide/hide-private `docs/missions.md`): what only its Conductor moves and it has not settled, a raise (a waiting node's failed wait among them) or a stopped worker's warning, is the mission's one safety net card in 내 차례, however many nodes it holds, each row worded from the typed fact (`경고 · 일꾼이 멈춤`, a raise's reason).
A failed CI, changes asked for and a review failing at its round limit are the working worker's to answer and never a row: a worker that stops on one is warned as stopped and one that ends is raised, and the row counts from then (operator decision 2026-11-23).
The core derives it (`safety_net` on the mission's view): it stands at once while no Conductor holds the mission, and with one only while the Conductor rests (its turn ended, it asks the operator nothing and waits on no task of its own; its workers at work are not its move), once the latest of the oldest raise, the Conductor's arrival and its rest stood 20 minutes (operator decision 2026-11-23).
Its head says `걸린 일 N건이 M분째 그대로예요` with `Conductor가 아직 풀지 못했어요` under it and `터미널로 이동`, which brings the Conductor's pane forward; with no Conductor it says `이 미션에 Conductor가 없어요 · 걸린 일 N건` with `지휘 다시 열기`, and the Conductor row steps aside so the page says it once.
Under the head is a row per held node: its title, which picks it on the map, its raise's reason, and how long that raise stood (`web/src/MissionTurn.tsx`).
A raise only the operator settles, a gate reached with no Conductor that no decision asks yet or a Conductor's failure recorded before, is a row of its own in 내 차례: the node, what to look at or the reason, and the raise's choices.
A node an open decision is about, the one it stopped or another it names (`hide mission decide --node <key>...`), never gets a card of its own while the decision waits: the decision is the operator's one card, and lists the other nodes under its why as `같은 판단을 기다리는 노드:` with each node's title, which picks it on the map (`web/src/MissionDecision.tsx`).
A node whose move did not come is raised as stalled (`hide mission help`, A move that did not come) to its Conductor like any raise, and reaches the operator only through the safety net, its reason line saying which move: `멈춤 · 시작할 수 있는데 시작 안 함`, `멈춤 · 경고 뒤에도 일꾼이 움직이지 않음` (a stopped warning; a failed review's warning stays the Conductor's and is never raised), or `멈춤 · CI·리뷰·머지를 1시간 넘게 기다림 · #N` (never for a pull request stacked on a node of the mission not merged yet, which waits on that node, `기다림 <node> 머지 먼저`); there is no card of its own, and each is raised once.
A mission's delay (`hide mission delay`) and a node's own hold (`hide mission node --hold`) are one state of a node, which the core decides once (`Mission::wait_of`) and the map, the phone and `hide mission show` read: the delay holds every node not done or dropped, a node's own hold says why before it, and it goes by itself when its time ends (PRD letters-missions-simplify D-07, B21).
While a node waits so, it carries no warning of any kind and raises no stall; once the wait ends each is judged again, and a stopped worker's five minutes count from then (B22).
A worker asleep is no wait: its card says asleep and it gets no warning (B23).
There is no warning about pushes: a stopped worker is told by its stop, and one that goes round in circles by its review's round limit (D-32, B32).
A node in progress draws how it moves now (`nodePace`, `web/src/missions.ts`; operator decision 2026-11-13), on its card, its panel's head and its hover alike: while its worker works, or Hide cannot read its worker's row, its ring turns and its card wears the working border and ring; while its worker rests on someone (`기다리는 중 · <what>`, a grey line under its title) or Hide put it to sleep, its ring stands still cut into five dashes in the working colour, as an agent's waiting mark is, and its card keeps the working border without the ring; while no one moves it, its worker stopped with nothing to wait on or no worker standing for it, its mark is a grey disc (`--mission-stopped`) with two pause bars, a full shape that never reads as the empty dashed ring of a node still waiting to start, its card wears a plain solid border in that colour, its current step's cell turns that colour and `지금` before the step reads `멈춘 곳`, the map's head, the mission's list row and its chips draw its cell in that colour, and the lines out of it are not lit.
A stopped node's line, in the stopped colour, says why: `멈춤 · <why>`, the why `사용 한도 · <the agent's own line>` (its reset time when the agent gave one), `API 오류`, `보고 없음` or `일꾼 없음`, cut with `…` when it runs long, and the hover says the whole sentence with an API error's own line and how long ago; its worker row says `멈춘 지 <time>` in place of how long the node has run, and no time before the core timed the stop.
A worker's stop (its program runs and its turn ended; hide-private `docs/missions.md`, A stopped worker) and a node no worker stands for are timed from the first warning judgment that sees them, within the minute decision, and a stop that turns into a worker gone keeps its time; the stop's Conductor's letter waits five minutes from then, and both are the Conductor's to act on, so they add nothing to 내 차례, whose hold colour stays the operator's turn alone (`useStopWords`, `web/src/MissionMap.tsx`; `NodeWork::Stopped`, `NodeWork::NoWorker`).
The phone's step draws the same ring and frame from the step's work state, which its frame carries alone, and a stopped step says `멈춤` or `멈춤 · 일꾼 없음` without a cause or a time.
A waiting node carries a grey pause line under its title, `기다리는 중 · <why> · <time>`: the why is cut with `…` when it runs long, the remaining time stays whole at the line's end without `남음`, and the hover shows the whole sentence (operator decision 2026-11-11; `NodeWaitLine`, `web/src/components/mission-hold.tsx`); the mission's row in the list carries `보류 N` for the nodes held on their own in quiet words at its end (named `보류한 노드 N개`), counted in neither 내 차례 nor its colour, a delay being its pill's to say; both go by themselves when the wait ends or the node finishes.
The phone's step card draws the same line from the frame's `wait`.
Every mission chip (the Conductor row's, the Conductor pane's and Desk's) wears its mission's colour on its mark, and a running mission no one moves, a node stopped and none at work, wears the stopped grey in place of the working colour (`missionTone`, `missionStalled`; hide-private `docs/tasks-and-pr-list.md`, A mission on its Conductor's pane; operator decision 2026-11-20); every one marks the operator's turn with `!` in 내 차례's colour, keeping its progress: a decision (`판단`), or the safety net or a raise only the operator settles (`확인 필요`), as its name says; a mission delay adds a grey pause, and the chip's name says `새 작업 보류 · <why> · <time>` (`useDelayWords`), and the state pill of the mission's list row and of its map says `보류 · <time>` with a pause mark in place of `진행 중` (`missionTurn`, `missionDelay`, `web/src/missions.ts`; `useDelayPill`, `web/src/components/mission-hold.tsx`).
A node waiting on the Conductor is not the operator's turn: it reads as work in progress, `Conductor 차례 · <why>` in quiet text in the summary's 진행 and on its card, in its own look's border; `Conductor 차례` is the one word for the Conductor's turn wherever a node, its panel or the phone says whose turn it is.
`Conductor가 정한 것 N` in the summary beside the map, for an open or closed mission, is folded by default and opens or closes on a press.
It lists the finish lines the Conductor skipped on its own with no decision of the operator's, oldest first: the node, which picks it on the map, `<line> 넘김 · <why>`, and who and how long ago (`web/src/components/mission-summary.tsx`); it asks nothing, `hide mission show` prints the same list and the closing report names each with its time in Unix milliseconds.
A closed mission's report ends with one line about the operator's own checkout, which Hide adds once the close's pull ends, a moment after the close (hide-private `docs/missions.md`; `hide-core/src/runtime/mission_pull.rs`): `내 폴더도 최신으로 당겨왔어요 (N커밋)`, `내 폴더도 이미 최신이에요`, `내 폴더 N커밋 뒤 · git pull`, or `결과는 <destination>에 들어갔어요 · 내 폴더는 <branch>` (`detached HEAD` for a detached one).
It is drawn as the rest of the report is, in the map's folded report and in `hide mission show`, and a later close of the same mission replaces it rather than adding a second; the phone carries no mission report, so it shows none of these.
A pull Hide skipped (a failed fetch, a `HEAD` that moved, a branch that does not track `origin/<destination>`, a project on a connected device) adds no line and shows nothing, no alert, banner or sheet: the reason is in the diagnostic log (`mission.folder_pull`), and a cancelled mission or one where no change reached the destination says nothing of the folder.
A node's gate (PRD mission-human-gate) reaches 내 차례 as the mission's decision, worded by the core in the interface language: what to look at, then `확인하고 알려 주세요` (`Check <what> and tell me`), with `계속 진행` and `고칠 점 있음` (`Go on`, `Something to fix`); the node's view carries the gate's state (`ahead`, `open`, `passed`), when it was asked and when it has waited 24 hours, and once passed where the answer came from (`card`, `phone`, `pane`) and its words, and its line is tagged `볼 것` among the finish lines; how the map, the panel and the list draw it belongs to their own screens.
Before the plan is approved, `내가 볼 곳 N` above the map says once when, `각 단계의 리뷰·CI 전에`, and lists each place as what to look at in bold with its node under it in quiet words that pick it on the map; the list wears no hold colour, since none of it is the operator's turn, and gates are changed by asking the Conductor to replan, with no separate screen toggle or approval event.
The plan list wraps the full review subject and node title at any width, and nothing in it is cut; only the map card's strip has the two-line limit.
While the plan waits for approval the map's head counts its steps, `14단계`, in place of the cells and `0 / 14 끝`, as a plan under review does, and the row naming the Conductor under the head, like a plan's own session row, is one line of words with no box of its own.
A node's gate is an eye strip of at most two lines, readable without hover: tinted ahead, filled with the yellow border and `내 차례` when reached, grey with a check once looked at.
An ahead gate counts only in that list, the strip and the mission row's quiet grey eye count beside its progress, with `내가 볼 곳 N` on hover; it adds no turn, sidebar count, badge or notification, and dropped nodes and closed missions count none.
The picked node's panel has one card headed `볼 것` and when, with what to look at as its headline; while its decision asks about that node, the panel's question and choices replace the gate card and attention part.
The screen compares the core's `long_wait_at_unix_ms` with its shared clock to show `오래 기다림` in grey, without sending an answer or notification; once passed the panel records where the answer came from and its words, including `창에서 답함`.
A reached gate with no row to carry it uses the existing mission item in Needs You and adds what to look at to that item's reason.
The phone reads the same named gate fields and uses the same strip, plan list, grey count and answer-source wording in all four interface languages (`web/src/components/mission-gate.tsx`, `web/src/mobile/MissionDetail.tsx`, `web/src/mobile/MissionsScreen.tsx`).
After a mission starts, the phone keeps a later planned node with an ahead gate on the shared mission graph as a step not begun, with its name and strip, adding no turn count.
Every row starts with the mission's number, `M39` for the core's `mission-39`, in muted monospace, and the map's head and the Conductor pane's chip (`⚑ M39 · ●●●●● 1/6`, where the word 미션 stood: the number muted, a dot, then the progress in bold so the two never read as one number) use the same number, so a mission said aloud is found on any of them; it comes from the core's id only, an id without a number shows none, and the `M` keeps it apart from a pull request's `#` (`missionNumber`, `web/src/missions.ts`).
A mission no Conductor holds carries `지휘 다시 열기` at the end of its row, which takes it over without opening it; a press anywhere else on the row opens the mission (B6).
With no mission on the device the table says so above the start guide: a moving picture of a mission (the Conductor, three workers side by side, one decision, a check beside each part as it finishes, purple then green, two checking the demo, then main), `한 번 말하면, 여럿이 나눠서 끝까지`, three dots (나란히 일하고, 다른 모델이 검증, 판단만 나에게), the `/hide-mission` and `$hide-mission` calls, and `아무 에이전트 터미널에서 부르면 여기에 미션이 생겨요`; with missions left out by a chip it says none match and offers `모든 프로젝트` (B7).
The picture loops in about ten seconds and runs only while it is on screen in a window that shows; with reduced motion it is one finished frame; its colours are the mission tokens in both themes (B4).
Before the core's missions first arrive the table draws quiet skeleton rows and no count; a mission record that cannot be read is one muted line, `미션을 읽지 못했어요`, in place of the rows with no count, and the reason goes to the diagnostic log (B8).
On a device that does not answer the head marks it `연결 안 됨`, the rows keep their last values dimmed and count no time, and `지휘 다시 열기` is disabled until it answers (B9).

Opening a mission narrows the table to a list on the left, in the same groups and order, each row its mission mark (a ✓ or ✕ once closed), its title and, in 내 차례, the action's words under it with no `!`, with that mission drawn selected, holding the keyboard when nothing else did (an open from the table), a project chip that leaves the mission out letting go, and its detail fills the rest: the kind mark with its `?`, the title and project, ⚙ (the defaults, Settings › Mission) and `✕`, then the map as a mission's map draws it (B10; [A mission's map](#tasks-card-tabs-and-the-pr-list)).
A node's worker on the map and in its panel reads as the agent item it was started as, and before it starts as the item its plan names, marked planned: its agent's logo with the profile's badge, the profile's name (its name now while the machine lists that profile, else the name it started with, or `없는 프로필` for a planned profile the machine no longer lists; none for the built-in default), then its model and effort (PRD agent-profiles-phase2 B12).
A checker reads the same way, and the Conductor's line names the profile its pane runs with.
A reviewer or verifier beside a node's card names its role on hover (`mission.role.*`): the reviewer `코드 리뷰`, the verifier `검증`; the worker's mark leads the card's worker row and the `워커` row of the card's own hover.
The checker's hover leads with its agent and state and puts the role's name under them, and its accessible description is that name; the gallery's `scene=mission&state=running&hint=role` shows a checker's open and `hint=node:<key>` a card's.
A node's card is five rows one spacing apart, each starting at the card's left edge: its mark and title, the band of what the operator does (the gate's `볼 것` and `머지할 차례`), its worker's mark, model and time, its steps, and under a hairline its bottom line.
A start gate (`when: start`) is the same band: faint while what its node depends on still works, the operator's turn once it is done, and, once passed, gone from the card and kept in the node's panel (PRD node-presets D-09).
A waiting node (`wait`) has no worker, no time and no branch line; its card says what it waits for and how that stands (PRD core-wait D-13, D-15 to D-17, option A of the operator's decision on with-hide/hide#2848; `waitCard`, `web/src/missionLine.ts`, drawn by `WaitCardLines` and `WaitStateMark`, `web/src/components/mission-line.tsx`, on the desktop and the phone alike).
Its head's mark is its state in place of the ring: an hourglass in the hold colour while it waits, a help circle in the hold colour while a read could not say, a green check once met, a red X once failed or past its deadline; its title stays quiet.
Under the title, always, the condition in one sentence after the icon of what it waits on, in the foreground while it is open and muted once met or failed: `#2528 머지되면` behind the pull request's mark lit by its state, `#1232 CI 통과하면`, `main에 release/1.3 들어오면` (`기본 브랜치에 …` when its base was not read), `mission-91 끝나면` or `mission-91 · gitlab-write 끝나면`, the pull request named by the reference the core carries beside it (`outside.reference`), `!12` on gitlab.com, as every mission screen names one; a branch is followed by a fixed word, never by a particle that depends on its last sound.
Under a hairline, its foot only when it has something: a waited mission's progress cells and `끝 2/7`, with the hours left at the right in the hold colour, after a clock, only under a day before its deadline (`5시간 남음`); `끝남` in green once met; once failed, how in red, `머지 없이 닫힘`, `CI 실패 · <first failed check> 외 n`, `기다리던 미션이 취소됨`, `노드가 계획에서 빠짐` or `기한 지남`, with whose turn that is at the right, `Conductor 차례` (or `내 차례` when the core raised it to the operator); `확인 못 함 · 다시 읽는 중` in the hold colour while a read could not say.
Its border is dotted while the wait is open or failed and solid once met; the card's name, its hover, the node panel's line, the mission list's line and the phone's map and step list say the same words, the phone's frame carrying the failed checks and a read that could not say too; the gallery's `scene=mission&state=waits` shows each target in each state.
A waiting node no one judges any more, dropped from its plan or in a mission cancelled before it was met, draws no live state: its card is its look's, struck through or cancelled, with no condition and no foot.
An agent asleep on its own wait says what it waits on under its title in the sidebar and on its sleeping conversation's row, with the hours left under a day, `#1232 CI 기다림 · 5시간 남음`, the whole line in the row's hover where a narrow sidebar cuts it.
An agent's wait reaches no Needs You, and a waiting node's failure is its Conductor's raise, which reaches the operator only as any raise does, through the mission's safety net once it stood unsettled.
A node that changes code ends in its branch line, `#n <branch> → <destination>` behind its pull request's mark as the PR list draws it, a person before the destination when the operator merges; a general node ends in its preset's icon (리서치 a magnifier, 검증 a shield with a check, 배포·출시 a rocket, none a document) and, while planned or worked, its name (`일반` with none); once done, its result line itself followed by `›`, or `통과 n/m ›` in green or `실패 n/m ›` in red when a verdict was recorded, whatever the preset.
The icon is grey planned, blue worked, green done and red on a failed verdict, and a node that ended on a failed verdict wears a red mark in its title row (D-08); the desktop and the phone read both lines from `web/src/missionLine.ts`, the node panel's facts line leads with the same icon and name, and its result part, the card's hover and the Conductor pane's results show the same result, the panel with the verdict under the conclusion.
A done node's panel starts with its result, `결과`, and its finish lines, `완료 기준`, before its plan, which follows as history (with-hide/hide#2637): `결론` is the Conductor's line for the node or, while it gave none, the first line of the worker's last report to the Conductor, dimmed as the Conductor's line is once Hide's facts moved past it, and the panel says it once; `문서` lists the Views the node's worker, or an agent under it, opened in the node's current attempt, oldest first, each a row of the decision card's kind (`LinkedViewRow`, `web/src/components/linked-view.tsx`) with no `who linked it`, which brings an open View forward or opens a closed one again from what was kept, in one `mission_view_reopen` naming the node; a node not done shows neither, and its plan comes before what it made.
The node panel's facts line says where the node's time went, `작업 1.5h · 리뷰·CI 9h · 기다림 3h`, in place of its total, the words `hide mission show` prints, with the same `m` and `h` in every language (`nodeSpans`, `web/src/missions.ts`): 작업 from its start, or from when it went back to work from a finish, until its worker's own lines stood met, 리뷰·CI from then until it finished or now, and 기다림 from when it could have started until it did; a part the record cannot say is left out, and a node that started but whose 작업 and 리뷰·CI cannot be said, such as one finished before Hide recorded these times, keeps its total rather than its wait alone (mission-82).
Every finish line with the worker's `summary` carries it under its words with `└`, a passed one too; a skipped line's verdict carries the summary above its reason, and a failed line's verdict carries it as before.
Long Korean text in the conclusion and the summaries wraps at word boundaries (`break-keep`), and a word longer than the row breaks anywhere.
The words are the core's: its preset table (`PRESETS`, `hide-core/src/missions.rs`) names the presets and `hide mission show` the kinds, and `web/src/missionKindWords.test.ts` fails when the Korean catalog says another word.
A node that waits on one finish line of another (`depends_on` `"core:c1"`) is joined to it by an edge that names the line (`c1`) beside its middle, level and in the muted text colour; while it waits its card's waiting text names the line after its node (`계획 3단계 · #1703 c1 기다림`), and once that line is met, as the line shows on the map, it says where it starts, `출발 가능 · Core 위에 쌓기`, until a worker starts it (mission-82).
A stacked node's destination is the pull request it stands on, that pull request's mark in its state's icon and colour (grey when no node of the mission holds it) and its number in grey, never bold, so the node's own number leads; the card's name says `PR <n>` (D-57).
A branch line is one row while it fits, the number a step further from the branch than the icon is from the number; otherwise the number keeps the first row alone and `<branch> → <destination>` takes a second row from the card's left edge.
A line without a number, a branch before its pull request or a plan, stays one row.
Where the branch and the destination do not both fit, they share the row: a name shorter than half of it stays whole, two longer ones take half each, and a cut name ends in `…` with its whole name in the hover and the card's name.
A direct change's `PR 없이` follows the destination and moves with it (D-56).
A numbered line with `⚠`, 내 차례 or `!` at its right end is always two rows: the number, never cut, and those marks on the first, the marks taking only the width it leaves (a warning's words go first, down to its icon), and `<branch> → <destination>` on the second; a line without a number follows the same rule with its icon in the number's place, keeping one row only when its path fits between the icon and the marks, the marks never wider than the icon leaves so a warning's words give way first; a result line keeps the marks at the right end of its one row, the line taking what they leave, and the card's name says each mark in full (D-56).
A card's hover (`web/src/components/mission-node-hover.tsx`) repeats its mark, title and band, then one row per fact under a grey label at the card's text size: `상태` (the state word in its colour, a resting or absent worker, the time), `워커` (mark, model and effort, a device away), `단계` (the bar, `n/m` or `n/m 끝`, the step now), `들어가는 곳` (the branch line with no name cut), `PR` (the current pull request's mark and its state's word), `머지` (who, and under it in small type Settings' owner and the plan's reason when the plan moved it), or `결과` (the result line in full) for a general node; `머지하는 사람을 바꾸려면 Conductor에게 말하기` stands under a hairline only when the merge differs from Settings, and the hover holds nothing to press (D-58).
The map's head names the branch the mission's results go to and the options the mission applies; with-hide/hide-private `docs/tasks-and-pr-list.md`, A mission's map, owns how they are drawn, and the gallery's `scene=mission` states `plan`, `dest-merge`, `dest-operator`, `dest-direct` and `dest-none`, with `hint=options` or `hint=node:<key>` for a hover, show them (PRD mission-destination).
The model reads by the name that item's own model list shows for it, the list Settings and the start panel show (a gateway profile's `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash` for `opus`, `catalogFor` and `modelLabel`, `itemNames` in `web/src/missions.ts`), and as the start passes it (`opus`) while that list is not read, where the list does not name it, and for a worker on another machine than this one, whose lists Hide AI does not read.
Those lists are read only while a screen reads them, so a drawn mission map is one such screen (`ai_settings.start_observing`), and the map keeps no list of its own.
A node's worker that its Conductor spawns without `--profile` starts as its plan names it, the built-in default included, rather than with the Conductor's own profile, so what the map shows before the start is what runs ([delivery.md: Missions](delivery.md#missions)).
A node's `크게 ›`, a worker's or checker's record and a pull request's card, pressed in the detail, leave the screen for the Workspace in front, whose desk shows them as card tabs, or for the Overview's panel at Home for a record (D-09).
↑ and ↓ in the list show the previous or next mission's detail; `✕` returns to the table with the same scroll and project chip (B11).
A mission removed or no longer held while its detail shows returns the screen to its table with no message (B22).
The page tells the core which mission the detail shows, or none, with one event (`mission_shown`), which the core holds unsaved, only for a mission its book holds, and uses to send that one mission's event record (`mission_events`); leaving the screen says none, the page names it again whenever its connection comes back, and with two windows on missions the last one to name a mission wins (D-16).
While the page names none, the core sends the events of the mission whose card is the front tab of the active area of the front Agent tab's desk while the desk is drawn; the desk is the core's, so the card needs no event of its own.
In a window too narrow for the list beside the detail the list stacks above it and the table leaves out its project column, which the chips still name (`missions-split`, 760 px of the screen's width); a long title truncates with its full text on hover (B23).
On the screen, a mission of another device than the one in front brings that device's rail tile forward first, then opens on the mission (B13).
⌃Tab (⌥` in a browser tab) walks the recent places: from a mission's detail, which is itself a place, the first chord lands on the place used before it, and from the table on the most recent place, as from the Overview; Esc closes an open menu or popover and nothing else, neither leaving the screen nor closing the detail; choosing a task, a project or a pane in the sidebar leaves the screen for it (B15, B16, B17).
Which mission is selected, the chip and the scroll are the page's own and are not saved; a mission's card tab is saved with its desk like any card, and a lane saved on the Mission tool loads with no tool column (B20).

### A mission's card tab

Web owner: `web/src/CardBody.tsx`, `actions.openMission` in `web/src/actions.ts`; core owner: `hide-core/src/runtime/detail.rs` (`DetailItem::Mission`).
Every other place a mission is pressed opens its map as a card tab on a desk: a mission row in the 태스크 list's 내 차례 or the 프로젝트 list's Needs You, a mission in the Overview and the Conductor pane's mission chip on the desk of the Conductor's Agent tab, and an agent row's mission mark on the desk of that row's pane's tab (operator decision 2026-11-09, replacing B12 and B14; PRD lane-desk-pair-view D-13).
The popover a pane head chip opens ends in one row, above a line, that says what to press to open the whole thing: `지도 열기 ›` under a mission and under a plan's steps alike (operator decision 2026-11-09).
A mission's popover is headed by its number and title, then one line of its state (the cells and `n/m` while it runs, `✓ 끝남 · <걸린 시간>` once it ends) and the tally, with the decision, the nodes a worker is at, or the Conductor's report and what it left below; a plan's popover keeps its state pill, cells and `n/m` in the head, since a plan has no title.
The card follows the pull request card's rules and adds none ([The right lane](#the-right-lane)): it opens on the desk of the Conductor's Agent tab, whose pane comes forward, or on the desk in front while no Conductor of this machine holds the mission; moving to another pane of that tab, the Conductor's own workers included, leaves it where it is, and another tab shows its own desk; its tab's `✕` closes it, a closed lane opens on the desk, and in a narrow window the lane stacks as it does for any card; a pull request pressed in it opens as another tab of the same desk, the map's tab staying beside it, and there is no `←`.
Away from a Workspace (Home, the Overview, or this screen with no checkout in front) the mission's checkout comes to the front first, as a card's project does, and a mission whose checkout no list holds opens on this screen instead.
Its tab holds the waypoint mark and the mission's title; its path line holds the card's full name, a small `모든 미션` that opens this screen's table and ⚙ (Settings › Mission); under it is the same map the screen's detail draws, its 내 차례, a missing Conductor's `이어받기` and a mission no longer held.
The `?` opens the one window telling a mission from a task, the same one a plan's page opens from the kind mark in its head (`미션`, `태스크`); it shows both side by side, a Conductor sending work to three workers at once and one agent finishing its steps in turn, each as a short picture that plays when the window opens and again on `다시 보기`, lights the side the press came from, and with the reduced-motion setting on draws each picture finished and offers no replay; Esc or a press outside closes it ([A mission's map and a plan's map](#tasks-card-tabs-and-the-pr-list)).
Settings › Mission has no `?`: `hide-task` is folded into `hide-mission` (2026-11-09), so the page that sets a mission's defaults does not teach the difference, and its title stands alone.
A mission deleted with its Conductor's session keeps its card tab, whose map says the mission is gone, until the operator closes it.

## The automation screen

Web owner: `web/src/AutomationScreen.tsx`, `web/src/AutomationForm.tsx`, `web/src/automationDraft.ts`, `web/src/automationWords.ts`, `web/src/automationCall.ts`, `web/src/components/sidebar-automation-row.tsx`; hided owner of the window call: `hided/src/automation_screen.rs`; core owner: `hide-core/src/runtime/automations.rs` ([ARCHITECTURE.md, Automations](ARCHITECTURE.md#automations)).
The automations of the window's own core are listed on a screen of the page's own beside the mission screen (PRD automations D-02).

The sidebar's `자동화` row sits under the `미션` row with the repeat mark, the number of finished runs not looked at on its right (none at 0), and its hint `안 본 회차 N개`; pressing it replaces the center with the table and draws the row selected while the screen shows (B1).
With no automation the screen welcomes as other empty screens do (`EmptyWelcome`): the Hide AI owl, `자동화가 아직 없어요` over `매일 아침, 평일, 매주처럼 정해 둔 시간에 에이전트가 할 일을 대신 해요.`, [+ 새 자동화], then `이렇게 시작해 볼 수 있어요` with three starting points, one to a row in a single column, each with its mark, its schedule and its task (`주간 분석 리포트` · 매주 월 09:00, a report with charts that it opens; `아침 브리핑` · 평일 09:00, a summary of what came in; `실패한 CI 고치기` · 6시간마다, a fix opened as a pull request; three different kinds of work with three different kinds of result), and last the line that an agent can make one when asked in words (B2).
A starting point opens the new automation's form filled with its name, task and schedule, which the operator reads, changes and makes like any other (B4).
The head reads `자동화` with the count as a badge and `이 기기에서 정해 둔 시간에 도는 에이전트` under it, and [+ 새 자동화] once there is one.
The automations are cards, as many to a line as the screen holds at 280 px each, one to a line on a narrow window (operator choice 2026-11-21, replacing the one-line rows that ran too long).
The cards stand in two groups, each drawn only when it holds one (mission-104 S-1): `↻ 반복 N`, the automations that repeat, in the core's order, then `◷ 한 번 N` in the once colour, the once automations still waiting, nearest time first.
A once card's largest line is its date and how far off it is (`12-02(수) 09:00 · 7일 뒤`, counted in calendar days once it is a day or more away and by the clock closer than that, the same count its detail and the form's read-back give), over `◷ 한 번` and the task; paused, it keeps its date without the count.
Below the groups, past a dashed rule, `▸ 끝난 한 번짜리 N개` is folded by default; open, each once automation whose time ran is one compact row, its result mark, its name with the unseen dot and `11-25 18:56 · <result>`, newest first, which opens its detail.
The head's badge counts the automations that are not done, and the arrows move through the cards, the fold's toggle and its open rows in the order they are drawn; back from a detail with only done ones left, the keys are on the toggle.
Each card's head draws the agent's logo, the name with the unseen dot and a state badge beside it (`● 도는 중`, `⏸ 멈춤`, or a pause the operator fixes, such as `로그인 만료`, in red), and ▶ at its end, which runs it now, off with its reason while a run goes.
Under it the next run is the card's largest line, its time and how long until it (`내일 09:00 · 9시간 뒤`, `곧` in its last minute, `—` while paused), over the schedule (`↻ 매일 09:00`); then the task in at most two muted lines; and last, past a rule, the newest five results as marks (✓ ✗ – ■, ● while one goes) with the last one's words (`✓ 12분`, `✗ <reason>`, `– 건너뜀 · <reason>`) and, at the end, the folder (`기본 폴더` or the project's, with `· 새 워크트리` when asked); a card the operator paused is dimmed (B3).
When the core set its saved book aside as unreadable (`automations.unreadable`), one warning line above the table names the file it kept, until an automation is made.
The arrows move between the cards in the list's order and Enter opens one; every icon-only button has a name (B38).

[+ 새 자동화] and [편집] open the form in the screen: name, task, the agent, model and effort from the list an agent's start uses (the last choice by default), the folder, and the schedule (B4, B26).
The folder is `폴더 없음 · 기본 폴더에서` by default with `~/hide/automations/<name>-…` under it, as the core's preview names it for the typed name with `…` where the id comes, or one of this machine's listed projects; a Git project adds `회차마다 새 워크트리에서` (B5).
The schedule is one row of pill choices at body size, the chosen one filled (operator choice 2026-11-25): 한 번 first, then 매일, 평일, 매주 (its days as pills of their own), N분마다 (5, 10, 15 or 30, from each hour's start, with no time), N시간마다 (1 to 12) and 직접 입력 (a cron line); 200 ms after typing stops, the core reads it back as `<schedule> · 다음: <when> · 이 기계 시간대` (B6).
한 번 opens on tomorrow with a date and a time, and 내일, 3일 뒤 and 1주일 뒤 as outline buttons of the inputs' height that set the date by the calendar of the machine that runs it (mission-104 S-2).
The core reads it back as `12-02(수) 09:00 한 번 · 7일 뒤 · 이 기계 시간대`, the date in bold, and the form sends the date and the time only, never a time named from now.
What the core refuses (a cron that runs two times closer than five minutes or is unreadable, a week with no day, a once time already past or more than 366 days ahead, a date that is no date, an empty task, a name taken) is a red line under its field, and [만들기] is off (B7); an empty name or task has its line once the field is left.
An edit of a done once automation that keeps its passed date is no refusal: the line reads `11-25(수) 18:56 한 번 · 끝남` and [저장] stays on, so its name or task can be fixed; a date the operator moves is read again and refused when past (S-3).
[만들기] opens the new automation's detail, and [만들고 지금 실행] opens the manual run it started (B8), waiting for the snapshot that holds them when the answer comes first; an answer after the form was cancelled moves nothing, and back on the table the keys are on its first row.
An edit sends only what changed: the worktree choice is sent only when the folder or the box moved.
The preview, the make, the edit and a run's reopen are one `automation_call` each, answered to that window alone by an `automation_result` frame; every other press is a window event whose result the snapshot shows.

The detail shows the task, the schedule, the next run, the folder, the agent with its model and effort, [지금 실행], [멈춤] or [다시 켜기], [편집], [폴더 열기] (in the desktop app on the core's own machine only) and delete, then the runs newest first (B11, B25); under the task, `최근 N회 · 토큰 61.5K` sums the tokens of the runs Hide's usage ledger read (B39); no price is shown (operator decision 2026-11-21).
Opening it marks its unseen runs seen, so the sidebar's number drops.
A once automation's detail reads its schedule as `◷ 12-02(수) 09:00 · 한 번` and its next run as that time until it runs, then `끝남`; [지금 실행] still runs it, one whose time was taken (done, or its run going) has no [멈춤], as the core refuses that press, and an edit that gives it a new date has the core turn it on again (mission-104 S-3).
A run reads its time, its result, the tags `수동`, `놓친 회차`, `이어서 대화 중` and `대화를 찾을 수 없음`, and once the ledger read its conversation the tokens it spent, `토큰 12.3K`, with no price, on its row and its terminal's head (B39); a skipped time and missed times are lines of their own, and times skipped one after another for the same reason are one line with no time of its own, `건너뜀 · <이유> · N번 · 오늘 14:00–18:55` (`어제 23:30 ~ 오늘 01:10` across days).
A run going opens its terminal in the screen, live and typed into, with [■ 멈추기], which ends it as `멈춤 · 사람이 끝냄` (B16); the run owns that pane, so its head has no ⋯ menu and no ✕.
A run's screen is laid out as pair view from the start (operator decision 2026-11-21): the terminal on the left half and, on the right, the desk of the run's tab, drawn by the desk's own areas (`ViewAreas`).
That desk always leads with a fixed card tab, `요약` (`Summary`), placed by the core each time the screen says it shows the run (operator choice 2026-11-24, the summary in the same tab strip, first): its tab has no ✕ and no drag, its tab menu offers no close, split or move and ⌘W does nothing on it, the core refuses to close it (the window's close and an agent's `hide view close` alike) or move it off the run's desk, it is never stored and it is dropped with its tab, and it shows in no pane's Views list or Files strip nor in `hide view list`.
A View the run's agent opens while the operator watches comes in front as a tab after the summary, which stays; with nothing else on the desk the summary is in front.
A run's agent opens while nobody watches its pane, so what it opened waits on no desk, in its pane's list, and while the run's desk holds no View but its summary the core brings those Views onto it each time the screen says it shows the run, the last opened in front, leaving a desk that already holds a View as the operator arranged it (`gather_run_views`, operator decision 2026-11-21).
The summary is the run's result card (operator choice A): how the run ended in large type beside its tone mark, when it ran with its trigger and the agent and model, the agent's last answer (`에이전트의 마지막 답`, or `이 회차에 남은 답이 없어요`; none for a run still going) on an edge in the result's tone, then `결과`, the task folded to three lines with [펼치기], and at the bottom the last five runs oldest first as marks, the shown one ringed, each opening its run, with `N회 중 M번 끝남`.
`결과` lists everything the run's agent opened for the operator in this run, newest first, a file, a diff or a page, each a row with its kind's mark and its name, including a View whose tab was closed since and the same View opened again, which is one row at the newest place (`Run.results`, at most 32 for an automation across its runs, the oldest dropped first; a View whose address is longer than 1,024 bytes, an outside file and a card are not listed).
Pressing a row opens that View on the run's desk in front (`automation_run_result_open`): an open one comes in front, a closed one opens again from what the core kept of it, shown as the View of the run's agent; a row carries no 볼 것 mark and no note that its tab is closed, because the tabs already carry 볼 것.
A run going that opened nothing yet keeps the `결과` heading over the quiet line `에이전트가 무언가 열면 여기에 모이고, 옆에 탭으로도 열려요`; a finished run that opened nothing draws no `결과` section.
When the run has no tab (its terminal closed, opening, or its conversation missing) the right half is the summary alone with no tab strip, its result rows shown and not pressable.
Arriving, the window moves the core's focus to the run's pane (`focus_pane` with `reveal_unseen`, no opening), which makes its tab's desk the one in front; leaving the run's screen by any path, ← or the sidebar or another screen, gives the focus back to the pane the operator was on, unless something the operator pressed already moved it off the run's pane; the answer and the results ride the run's row only while a window shows it (`answer`, `results`), so the section does not carry a paragraph and a list for every run.
A run's tab is never in its project's tab row, nor in the sidebar's project tree with its agent, nor in the tab lists the window draws: the run is reached from the automation screen and from Needs You only.
While a run's terminal shows, the window tells the core (`automation_run_viewing`) and tells it again every five minutes, inside the core's 30-minute hold; the core keeps that terminal attached and open, and reads a turn that stops there with no cause as the operator's.
Pressing a finished run whose terminal is closed asks the core to open its conversation again in the screen (B17); a terminal that slept while shown says so with [다시 열기] (B18), a conversation the core cannot resume says `대화를 찾을 수 없음` and opens nothing, and a refused opening (another opening of the automation still starting, `automation_opening`) is a line above [다시 열기].
Delete asks `<name> 자동화와 회차 기록 N개를 지울까요? 작업 폴더와 결과 파일은 남아요`, adds `도는 회차도 멈춰요` when a run goes or is open, and offers [자동화 지우기] and [그대로 두기] with neither chosen (B27).

An automation the core stopped for a person is its own Needs You row in the 프로젝트 list, the 태스크 list's 내 차례 and the Overview: its name with the Needs You mark, `<reason> · 고치고` and [다시 켜기], which turns it on and takes the row away; the row opens its detail (B23).
A once automation whose run ended, succeeded or failed, is its own Needs You line in the same three places until that run is opened (`agent_scope.automation_looks`, mission-104 S-4): the Needs You badge with the clock in place of the `!`, its name, `자동화 · 한 번 · ✓ 끝남 · 4분` or `✗ 실패 · <reason>`, and the run's last answer as one quoted muted line.
Pressing it opens that run in the screen, and the core takes the line away once it is opened; it counts in 내 차례 and the app icon like any other line there.
When such a line appears the window shows one desktop notification, under the Needs You switch, with the name as its title and the same words as its body; a line already there when the window went live is not notified, a line that goes takes its notification back, and a click opens the run.
A run's agent row reads `↻ 자동화: <name>` where a task's origin stands, and when it asks, is blocked or opened a View it stands in Needs You like any agent (B15).
There its mark is the Needs You badge with the repeat arrow in place of the `!` or the eye, or the clock when its automation runs once (`hide_ai.once`), hollow once read, and it draws no Hide AI owl, since Hide only started it (operator decision 2026-11-21).
Pressing it opens the run in the screen; a finished run opened there is dealt with, so the core resolves its row and it leaves Needs You until the run's agent asks again, while a run that still goes, works or waits on an approval keeps its row (`put_away_viewed_run`, `hide-core/src/runtime/automations.rs`).
Once looked at, each automation's newest run that came to Needs You, a once automation's or one whose agent opened something (`has_results` on the run's record, said for every run since its 결과 ride only while it shows), rests as its own row in the 태스크 list's 쉬는 중, and in no other list (operator decision 2026-11-26): the hollow badge with the clock or the repeat arrow, its name, and `자동화 · ✓ 끝남 · 4분` (`자동화 · 한 번 · …` for a once automation) in a resting row's grey, among the resting tasks by when the run ended.
It is no project's, so 프로젝트별 puts it outside the projects and a project filter leaves it out; a newer run looked at takes its place, an automation that stands in 내 차례 again rests nowhere else, a run that only asked and opened nothing does not rest, and pressing it opens that run in the screen (`automationRests`, `web/src/ownAsks.ts`).
What the run's agent opened and the operator has not looked at is a chip on the row's last line and on the run's line in the run list, its file mark, name and `외 N개`, on a run row whose only call is that View too, whose line is then the run's own; a run's screen is laid out as focus mode already, so the chip opens the run there with what its agent opened in front, as the row does (operator decision 2026-11-23).
An automation's own folder is a workspace the core marks `unlisted`: every list a person picks a project from reads the one listed filter (`listedWorkspaces`), so no sidebar project, project filter, start target, search row, recent move, Overview project or 프로젝트별 group of the 태스크 list shows it, and its checkout's place reads as the run's origin (B30).

## Terminal image attachment boundary

Web owner: `web/src/attachments.ts`, `web/src/PaneView.tsx`, `web/src/explorerDrag.ts`, `web/src/terminals.ts`. Core owner: `hide-core/src/runtime/attachments.rs`; device staging `hide-host/src/attachments.rs`.

Dragging an Explorer file onto a terminal focuses that pane and inserts the original absolute path as quoted text, without submitting it.
Image files instead read through the checkout's existing bounded file-bytes stream and enter the same binary attachment flow as an external image drop, retaining their original format and filename.
One Explorer image read is admitted at a time, capped at 20 MiB, and a page reconnection, receiving pane closure, or terminal transport generation change during that read discards the result.
Folders and files from another device show the forbidden drop cursor and insert nothing; moving files inside Explorer keeps its existing behavior.
Image acceptance and display remain the receiving program's responsibility, as for other terminal attachments.

Dropping local file URLs into a visible terminal focuses that receiving pane and starts one attachment intent through the existing ordered writer.
Paste captures PNG or TIFF clipboard images at the same ingress; pasted text goes to the pane as one paste, which reaches the program in bracketed paste exactly when the program turned that mode on, so a multi-line paste into an agent's prompt is never sent a line at a time, and keys keep their existing terminal behavior.
The core reserves the original pane and connection generation before asynchronous image preparation, file validation, or transfer, so later focus changes cannot redirect the attachment.
Clipboard images are normalized into private PNG files off the input thread, while the core owns transfer state, held input, retry, and cancellation.
Local files keep their original paths after validation; a device's attachments are staged through that device's link and insert only the resulting device paths.
