Add, or Enter, registers that folder with one `create_workspace` to that machine's node, judged and answered as a picked folder is; the list's own states are a loading line, `이 폴더를 읽지 못했어요` with `다시 시도`, an empty folder, no match for the filter, and a machine that disconnected while it was open.
`Clone from URL` (web owner: `web/src/CloneFromUrl.tsx`) and `Create new project` work on the host chosen, through its node, each in its own view of the same dialog with a `Back` that returns to the first view and puts the keyboard on the row it came from (D-07).
`Clone from URL` shows a `Git URL` field that holds the keyboard, and a `Parent folder` field with a button that opens the native folder picker on this Mac and the folder list, as `위치 고르기` with `이 위치 사용`, on any other host, which opens on the parent the field shows and leaves the folder the core keeps for Browse folder alone; the parent starts in the folder most of that machine's projects live in (the latest added wins a tie), else at home (`~`); Create new project starts there too.
The folder the URL names is shown as `Clones into a new folder <name>`, and that machine's node is asked whether it is free under that parent (`Call::NewFolderTarget`), so `Clone` is enabled only for an https, ssh (`git@host:path`, `ssh://`) or `file://` URL, a parent inside home, and a name nothing holds there yet (`already_exists` covers even an empty folder, since a clone never writes into a folder it did not make); each refusal is shown inline under its field.
While the clone runs the dialog shows Git's stage, worded in the window's language (`web/src/coreError.ts`), and percent with a progress bar and a `Cancel` button, and the fields and `Back` wait; closing the dialog leaves the clone running, and opening it again shows it; the new folder already exists while Git writes it, so Browse folder lists it then, and a cancelled or failed clone removes it; a folder moved or replaced while it was cloning is not added, and the clone says so and points to Browse folder.
A finished clone is registered like a picked folder and the dialog closes when the project appears, or says it could not be added and that the repository is on disk for Browse folder; a failure (authentication, a stalled transfer, a missing repository) stays inline in plain words chosen by the core's `failure` code, with Git's own last line beneath it when Git gave one, and `Clone` enabled again, and a cancel says nothing was kept.
`Create new project` (web owner: `web/src/CreateProjectView.tsx`) shows a focused `Name` field and a location row naming the folder it goes in (`Will be created in ~/…`) with the full path under it, which follows the name as it is typed.
The folder starts as the parent of the most recently added project on that machine, else home; the location row opens the native folder picker on this Mac, and the folder list on any other host, opening on the folder the row shows, to choose another.
The host's node answers a `project_target` probe for every name and folder on the same home line the create is checked on, so a name that is not one folder, a folder outside home, something already standing at the path (`already_exists`) or a project already added there is said inline and `Create project` stays disabled until the path is free.
`Create project` (or Enter) sends one `create_workspace` with `new_folder`; the core has that machine's node make the folder and run `git init` in it, and registers it, and the dialog closes when the project appears or keeps the failure with its reason.
A folder holding nothing, or only `.git` and a Finder `.DS_Store`, is what a create that failed after making its folder leaves, so it is not `already_exists`: the view says the project is made in it, and a retry continues there rather than being refused or making a second folder; a failed create never deletes the folder it made.

Under the other ways the first view shows `가져올 수 있는 프로젝트` (`Projects to import`) once the chosen machine's node has answered, and nothing while it looks, when it found nothing, or when it could not be asked (PRD device-add-project D-09 to D-14, B20 to B29).
The node gathers the folders every agent with a session reader worked in on that machine (Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Cursor, Grok, Pi, omp, Antigravity; an agent that gains one joins with no change), the projects Paseo keeps (`projects/projects.json` in `$PASEO_HOME` or `~/.paseo`, archived ones left out) and the workspaces Herdr saved (`session.json` in Herdr's config folder), and only reads them, so Paseo and Herdr keep working beside Hide.
Each folder counts as the project a registration of it would add, so a repository worked in from linked worktrees and subfolders is one row, and home, a folder outside home, the home dotfiles repository, a folder that is gone, a folder inside Hide's own `~/.hide` (where Desk and Hide AI run their agents; a Hide worktree there still counts for its repository) and a project already added on that machine are not rows; the rows are those active in the last thirty days, newest first, at most twenty.
An ⓘ beside the heading says, on hover, where the rows come from and that those apps stay as they are.
When the rows come from more than one source, a row of rounded chips under the heading, wrapping when they do not fit, reads `전체 N` (`All N`) and one chip per source, an agent's logo, its name and how many rows it named, the most first, the one shown filled; a chip shows that source's rows alone, and pressing it again, or `전체`, shows every row; a row two sources named counts in both.
A row is a checkbox, the project's name over the folder it sits in (`~/projects` for `~/projects/app`, the whole `~/…` path on hover) in mono, its source marks at the right (the agent's logo, with its product name on hover, or `Paseo`, `Herdr` as an outline badge) and how long ago it was active; the whole row checks it.
Above the rows `모두 선택` (`Select all`), or `보이는 N개 선택` (`Select the N shown`) under a chip, checks or clears every row shown, half-checked when some are, and `최근 작업 순` (`Most recent first`) names the order; the rows scroll under it past the folder list's height.
Nothing starts checked: under the rows a disabled `추가` (`Add`) stands at the right, which once rows are checked reads `N개 추가` (`Add N`) and adds every checked row.
Each add is the `create_workspace` a picked folder sends, with a request id of its own, one at a time, so the dialog stays open: a row on its way says `추가하는 중…` in place of its time, a row whose project appears on that machine leaves the list, a refused row stays checked with its reason under it and is sent again by the next `추가`, and a row already sent is not sent twice; a project added meanwhile from another window or the CLI, or another add's refusal, answers no row.
A Herdr row adds the folder alone, as a project with one terminal tab; the Herdr import below brings its tabs, layout and conversations, and a workspace imported later in the same folder joins that project rather than making a second one.
A source the node cannot read (a file it cannot open, one that is not a regular file such as a pipe, past 4 MiB, in a form it does not know, an agent's session folder it cannot list, or one it had not reached after 20 s) leaves out only its rows and is logged as `add_project.import_source_dropped` with the source and the reason; nothing shows on the screen.
The first-run wizard's project step shows the same list, since it draws the same views.
### Opening a folder from a terminal

CLI owner: `open_path` in `hided/src/cli.rs`; daemon owner: `hided/src/project_open.rs`; core owner: `hide-core/src/runtime/project_open.rs`; page owner: `showProjectOpened` in `web/src/opening.ts`; desktop owner: the `hide:come-forward` channel in `desktop/src/main/host.ts`.
`hide .`, `hide <path>` and `hide open <path>` open a folder as a project from any terminal, the way `code .` opens VS Code.
The path is read on the machine the command runs on, from that shell's working directory.
A folder a registered checkout holds, the checkout itself or a folder inside it, brings that checkout in front, the deepest one when worktrees nest, and registers nothing.
Any other folder is registered by the same `create_workspace` Browse folder sends, so it is judged by the same rules and opens on the first terminal tab its registration starts; a folder outside home is refused with the advice to add it from Add a project, since only the dialog asks that question, unless a project the operator already added holds it: that registration was the answer, so the checkout comes forward as one under home does, and a link inside it that leads out of it is still asked about.
Then every Hide window of the core's machine shows that project in place of All projects or an Overview, as a click on its sidebar row does, and the desktop window comes forward; a browser tab shows it too but cannot raise itself.
With no window open the command opens Hide as bare `hide` does, already on that project, and with no Hide running it starts Hide first.
A path that is not a folder, or one that cannot be registered, is answered with the reason and what to do next, and the command exits non-zero; nothing else opens.
A command's name wins over a folder of the same name: `hide status` is the command, `hide ./status` opens the folder `status`, and a word that is neither is answered as neither.
On a connected device the command works in a Hide pane there: the device's helper judges the folder against that device's home, the device comes forward on the project, and its first terminal opens there once the helper has confirmed the folder.
A plain SSH shell on the device works the same way while the device is connected: its helper takes this one command from a process of the account it runs as, and nothing else that shell runs gains a pane's reach ([INSTALL.md](INSTALL.md#what-each-system-supports)).
There it runs one open at a time and six a minute; past either, or with the device not connected or open in more than one Hide, the shell hears why and what to do, and the command exits non-zero.

### Removing a project's registration

`Remove project…` removes only Hide's registration and never deletes files, worktrees, sessions, or engine workspaces.
A project with panes is not refused; under the title and the path one facts line names the panes that close, the running agents that stop (left out at zero), that only the registration goes (a row the engine shows without one goes with its panes), and that files stay on disk; `Keep project` is a neutral button.
On confirmation the core closes every pane in the project's checkouts and waits for confirmation before removing the registration and its row; a timeout or refusal leaves the project registered with the reason in the error banner, and a repeated request continues from the panes that remain.
When an agent in the project spawned agents that run outside it, the confirmation lists them as Delete worktree does, with `Remove only` and `Close N agents and remove` (the destructive one) in place of its one button, neither holding the keyboard; the second closes those agents deepest first and removes the project only once all are gone, a refusal or timeout leaving the project registered with the reason in the error banner and a retry continuing from the agents that remain.
Removing the project that holds the focused checkout moves focus and pane selection to the next project.
An add that lands mid-removal cancels the removal and says so, rather than losing the project it just opened a pane in; a completed removal disappears from the snapshot and a repeated request is a quiet no-op.
A row the engine shows without a registration offers `Remove project…` too: its confirmation counts its panes the same way and says the row goes with them, the confirmation closes those panes, and the row leaves once the engine drops its workspace; a timeout leaves the row with the reason in the error banner, and repeating the removal closes the panes that remain.

## Retired Home

Core owner: `hide-core/src/runtime/home_retire.rs`, `hide-host/src/home.rs` (see ARCHITECTURE.md, Retiring Home). Web owner: `web/src/startTargets.ts`, `web/src/StartPanel.tsx`.

Hide keeps no Home (PRD retire-hide-home): no `~/hide` is made, no start target, sidebar row, project menu or phone target is named Home, and an agent or a tab always starts in a project's checkout.
A start with no project to start in offers `프로젝트 추가` in place of its start control, which opens Add a project with the front device as Host; the phone's start sheet and the empty screen offer the same.
The first launch after the update removes, on each device, only what the old Home's marker lists as Hide's: its links, its guide and its `CLAUDE.md`.
A link the operator changed, any file they added and a `~/hide` with no marker stay as they are.
When anything is left in `~/hide`, or Hide recorded a session or an open pane there, it stays as an ordinary pinned project named `hide`, and the agents that ran in Home read and resume as that project's agents; otherwise the empty folder and its registration go.
Nothing is shown while this runs: a failure keeps the project and goes to the diagnostic log, and the next launch tries again; a device whose helper has not updated is left alone until it does.

## First run

The startup's real connecting state draws the blinking owl above the lowercase hide mark and brand line, then a thin indeterminate bar with the host's localized preparing sentence.
It shows no progress percentage and never delays the shell; failure stops and hides the loading scene so the existing Retry and Copy diagnostics remain primary.
Reduced motion keeps the owl's still and a static bar; hidden or offscreen decoration pauses, and leaving its screen releases its listeners.
The wizard's existing agents, notifications and project steps carry their matching brand owl without changing their actions or platform policies.
The ready Home with no projects carries the ai owl.


Core owner: `hide-core/src/runtime/first_run.rs`, `hide-core/src/runtime/kit.rs` (`apply_agent_onboarding`). Web owner: `web/src/FirstRunWizard.tsx`, `web/src/firstRun.ts`, `web/src/NotificationControls.tsx`, `web/src/agentNotices.ts`, `web/src/ScreenIntro.tsx`. Desktop owner: `desktop/src/main/notify.ts`, `desktop/src/main/notificationPermission.ts`, `desktop/src/main/host.ts` (`showBadge`, `openNotification`), `desktop/static/status.html`.

The desktop app shows `Starting Hide…` while it reaches Hide, and never the word hided; a failure shows its reason in one line with Retry and `Copy diagnostics`, which copies the app's build, the reason code and where the host log is, and shows the same text to select by hand when the clipboard refuses it (PRD first-run-onboarding B1, B2).
On a machine whose kit has never run, the first-run wizard opens over the shell: Agents, Devices when this computer knows another machine, Notifications and how to start.
The progress bar names the actual route, with three steps after a complete read that found no machine and four while machines are listed or their read is loading or failed.
Each step after Agents has Back; Back changes the core-owned step alone and never repeats an installation, reconnect, permission request or test notification.
The core saves the agent selection, checked devices, project machine and path alongside progress, so returning or reloading retains explicitly disabled agents and the earlier notification choice.
Only complete clone URLs without user information, queries or fragments are saved as setup drafts; other clone input stays in the open form and is still passed unchanged to Git when Clone is pressed.
Finishing setup clears its project draft.
Nothing but each step's own buttons moves it: Escape, a click outside and a close button do nothing, and the step on screen is the core's (`ui_state.first_run`), so quitting, reloading or a failed save reopens the step not yet finished, and the wizard never returns once its last step is done or skipped (B5, B6).
Each step's main button sits at the footer's right with its keycap, `⌘↵` (`Ctrl+Enter` off macOS), which presses it from anywhere in the step; Enter on the step itself presses it too, while tiles, links and buttons keep their own keys; while macOS blocks Hide with the switch off that button is `Turn on notifications` (B40).
The agents step is the first-run agent choice (Settings: each machine's install kit, below); the core moves the wizard on once this Mac's kit pass for it has landed with every chosen agent in place.
Returning to Agents shows the saved choice, or the machine's enabled agents when no draft exists; installed agents start enabled only for the unanswered initial choice.
An old installation or discovery answer can settle its own operation but cannot advance a different step.
Devices lists the Host entries of this computer's `~/.ssh/config`, the same list and filter as Add device (no wildcard or negated Host); reading them contacts no machine (`ssh -G` resolves an address without connecting).
It reads no Herdr configuration: Hide runs no Herdr, and the work an earlier Herdr held comes in through [the import](#importing-a-herdr-session-into-hide).
Hosts that lead to the same `user@host:port` are one row however many aliases name it, named by the config's first, while a different user or port is its own row; rows follow the config's order.
Each row names the machine over its address (`user@host:port`, or its alias when `ssh -G` could not resolve it).
The step is titled `다른 기기에서도 에이전트를 돌리나요?`, and says the machines come from the ssh config and that nothing is contacted or installed before Connect; under the list one line says a machine not listed is added later in Settings › Devices.
In the Windows app it also lists this PC's default WSL distribution first, read from WSL's own registry without starting it, as `WSL Ubuntu` over `이 PC (WSL)` (PRD wsl-device B1, D-13); another distribution is not listed here and is added later from Add device.
Loading, a failed read with Retry, a proven empty result, a Host `ssh -G` could not resolve (with Add device's reason) and already-added machines are distinct states; a config that exists but cannot be read, or an Include whose folder cannot be opened, is a failed read with Retry, a list cut at a size, Include depth or file bound keeps the step, and only a complete read that found no machine (no Host, a missing config reading as none, and no default WSL distribution) skips Devices, in the first run and in Help's walk.
When Devices was skipped that way the last step says `다른 기기는 나중에 설정 › 기기에서 추가할 수 있어요.` under its two ways.
A WSL list that could not be read is a failed read too, with the same Retry, and never an empty one, so a PC that has WSL does not lose the step to a registry it could not read (B1).
Every row starts unchecked, and its name and address wrap when they need to.
The WSL row starts checked until the operator unchecks it, which the draft keeps (`first_run_choices.devices` stays null until a row is checked or unchecked), and it names no address.
Its Connect is the install consent, as Add device's is: nothing is installed in WSL before it, and it registers the distribution's device, which installs Hide's helper and hooks there, and brings its `WU` tile to the front in the same event, so the project step adds to it and a WSL agent can be started at once (B2).
Before Connect the WSL row lists no agents: only the distribution's own helper reads what is installed there, and reading it any earlier would start WSL before the consent, so the row is `WSL Ubuntu` over `이 PC (WSL)` alone (B1, by the operator's choice).
After Connect its install runs as any device's does, and its agents show where a device's kit already shows them: the project step's agent choice and Settings › Agents.
A default distribution on WSL 1 cannot be checked and says `WSL 1이에요. WSL 2로 바꾸면 연결할 수 있어요:` over its command `wsl --set-version <distribution> 2`, and one whose setup is unfinished says `배포판 설정을 먼저 마치세요` (B29); a PC without WSL has no WSL row, and its step is the Hosts alone, skipped when there are none.
Checking a device changes the draft alone; Connect selected explicitly allows Hide's helper, command and selected agent integrations on those checked devices through the existing Add device connector and install owner: a Host registers as Add device registers it, under its alias on the default server.
After Connect a row whose ssh asks the operator shows the device row's own sign-in part in place (`web/src/settings/SignInPrompt.tsx`): ssh's sentence over a masked field with `로그인` and `취소`, a new server's fingerprint with `이 서버 믿고 연결`, a page to confirm in the browser with `열기`, or `보안 키를 터치하세요`, and the row reads `로그인을 기다리는 중`; one line under the list says moving on without answering ends only the questions still waiting.
A row that did not connect shows the device row's three states (`DeviceLink` in `web/src/settings/DeviceRow.tsx`): a sign-in needed or refused with `로그인` in Retry's place, a changed server key with `ssh <alias>` and Copy and no retry, or `연결하지 못했어요` over ssh's last line with `다시 시도`; one line says a failed device shows the same in Settings › Devices.
Connecting and installing hold the Connect control, and the main button reads `계속` meanwhile; neither it nor `지금은 건너뛰기` is ever held by a connection or a waiting question.
Leaving the step (Next, `지금은 건너뛰기`, Back, or Help's walk moving on or closing) ends the sign-ins its own Connect started (`first_run_sign_in_withdraw`), whatever they show, as closing a screen ends its own; the device then reads `로그인 필요` in Settings › Devices, a sign-in the device row or another screen started since is left alone, and connected devices stay connected.
Connect selected counts only checked rows not yet tried; a failed row is tried again by its own action alone, while successful machines remain added and are not connected again by Back or re-entry.
`지금은 건너뛰기` continues with this machine, and failed discovery can be skipped without treating it as proof that no machine exists.
Project reuses Add project's host selector and folder, clone and create views; adding a remote folder offers the enabled agents from that remote machine's kit and never borrows this machine's sign-in state.
Its What changes opens a list with one row per place Hide writes, the path in mono with the agents it is for (`Codex, OpenCode, Pi`) and no piece named, so a skill folder agents share is one row, and the `hide` command's link last (B11).
A chosen agent that did not go in keeps the step with `<agent> was not set up: <reason>` and Try again (B12).
An agent whose copy is older than Hide's minimum is still a switch, on or off as chosen, and its tile says `업데이트 필요` (`Update needed`) in the warning tone; under the tiles one line per such agent names it with its versions (`Claude Code 2.1.200 → 2.1.278 이상`) and the one command that updates it with Copy, or its install page, since a tile is itself a switch and holds no button.
Its pieces refused for that reason do not hold the step and are no `was not set up` line: the update puts them in at the next kit read ([agent-hooks.md](agent-hooks.md#updating-an-agent-that-is-too-old)).
A pass that could not run (the kit link failed, or the pass listed no agent) keeps the step, says `Could not set up agents on this device:` with why the kit could not run (it was unreadable, this Hide is not the installed app, or its installer did not start), leaves the choice unanswered, and reads the agents again; the step says the same whenever the kit could not run here, as after a relaunch, and never that no agent is installed; `Try again` then records nothing and only has the agents read again, and once they are back Continue sends the choice anew; the walk Help reopens does the same, staying on the agents step with that line and no banner (B12, B44).
A first pass that stopped before changing anything still opens the wizard on a Mac no pass has answered for, since nothing was set up and the `hide` command, hooks and letters wait on it: the agents step draws no tile and says the machine row's line (Settings: each machine's install kit, below), and `Try again` runs the same pass again, which checks again what stopped it, records no choice, and once a pass runs brings the tiles and the question back.
Clearing a refusal can take a terminal and a restart, so that step alone can be left: `Not now` closes the wizard with the question unanswered, and it opens again only for a different refusal, when Hide's daemon starts again, or with the question once a pass runs; an install that was set up before gets no wizard for a later refusal, only the machine row's line.
The notifications step's description names the system that shows the notification (macOS, Windows, or `your desktop`), and its footer offers `Not now` and `Turn on notifications` at the right, with `Also on your phone →` at the left.
On a fresh install, `Turn on notifications` enables Needs You only; Done stays off to avoid announcing every completion (operator decision 2026-10-13).
Turning them on stores that choice and sends one test notification, `Notifications work`, which is what makes macOS ask; while macOS's dialog waits the step says `Waiting for your answer…` and holds `Turn on notifications`, an allowed answer shows `Notifications are on` with `Send a test`, and a refusal shows `macOS is blocking Hide's notifications` with `Open System Settings`, which opens Hide's page of the system's notification settings; once answered the footer offers Continue, drawn as a secondary button while macOS blocks, and a blocked state is not counted as on (B14, B15).
The step reads macOS's permission every two seconds while it is open, one read at a time, so a change in System Settings shows within seconds; an already allowed Mac shows `Notifications are on` at once, with Continue, which turns the switch on with the step; nothing is said before the first read; a permission Hide cannot read shows `Hide can't read the notification setting` with `Send a test`, and the reason goes to the host log (B16-B18).
On Windows and Linux the step has no permission to show: turning on stores the switch and sends the test once (B19).
A desktop with no notification service (Electron says notifications are not supported, or on Linux the last notification failed to show, until one shows again) shows no `Turn on notifications`: the step says `This desktop has no notification service, so Hide can't show notifications here.` with Continue, and Settings > Notifications shows the same line under Needs You; both switches stay off and locked, with no test (D-24, B47, B48).
Settings > Notifications' desktop group has independent `Notify me when an agent needs me` (Needs You) and `Notify me when an agent finishes` (Done) switches; while either is on, the permission row says what macOS allows (`macOS permission`, `✓ Allowed` with `Send a test`, `! Blocked by macOS` with `Open System Settings`), or on Windows and Linux `Test notification` with `Send a test`, and the group ends with `Sent only while no Hide window is in front. While you are notified when an agent needs you, the app icon shows how many items need you.` (B30, operator decision 2026-11-23).
`Not now` goes on with both switches off, even after `Turn on notifications` was pressed while macOS still asks, in the same event that leaves the step, and `Also on your phone →` opens Settings › Devices with its phone group in view over the wizard, which returns to the step when it closes (B21).
The last step asks `How do you want to start?` under `Either way, you can do the other one later. Practice is always in Help (?) > Practice Hide.`, with two ways, each a whole-width button with no choice to confirm and no Continue, then Back at the footer's left and `Skip for now` at its right (operator decision 2026-10-24).
The first way, with no chip that ranks it over the other (operator decision 2026-10-27), is `Try it on a practice screen first` over `Your real agents and files stay untouched`, drawn as the same plain bordered button as the second, only its graduation cap in the practice's amber (`--practice-accent-strong`) as on the sidebar's invitation; it ends the wizard and opens the [Practice window](#practice-window) on the mission flow, handing over a mission, the Home behind it empty.
The second is `Add a project` over `Start right in your own repository`: it shows Add a project under `Add your first project`, with its Host select, Browse folder, Clone from URL and Create new project, its refusals and its clone progress, and `Skip for now` (B22, B25); a folder pick cancelled there leaves those views as they were, and their Back returns to the two ways.
With a Herdr save no Hide Herdr runs offered to import (`status.herdr_import`, kind `import`), a third way follows them, `Import Herdr work` over `<n> tabs open with the same layout and folders. Herdr is left as it is.`: it imports that save and ends the wizard (PRD engine-bundle-4-herdr-removal B14).
Which of the two is on screen is the project draft's view (`first_run_choices.project_form.view`, empty for the two ways), so a reload reopens it.
`Skip for now` ends the wizard to the empty Home in either.
Add a project's Host list has no Add device, which would take the wizard's place, and only a project on the chosen host counts as the one added.
The first project added ends the wizard and opens the start panel once, on that project, with the first agent that is on and signed in (in the order Claude Code, Codex, Grok, Pi), else the first agent that is on, and the keyboard in its text box; with no agent on it opens no panel and the wizard closes to the empty Home, whose Start an agent is the way on; a later project opens nothing (D-15, B23, B24).
For a target on the core's own machine, the start panel says `! Not signed in` right after its agent menu whenever that agent is one whose sign-in Hide checks and its last read was signed out (B23, D-06).
A remote target never uses the core machine's login result; its kit reports installation and enablement, without claiming remote sign-in (B37).
A browser tab shows the agents step alone, with no progress bar, and Continue there ends the wizard, so the sidebar's invitation is its way into practice (Practice window, below); the notifications and start steps are the desktop app's (D-14, B26).
The desktop app's Help > Set up Hide opens the same steps from the first as this window's own walk: the agents step starts from the agents switched on now and its Continue switches only the agents whose switch changed, the way Settings > Agents does; Escape and the close button end it, and a step not continued changes nothing (B45, B46).

With Needs You on, the desktop app shows a notification when a root agent newly needs the operator (a question, an approval, a block, an error, or a View it opened to look at); Done independently enables new completions.
Both are sent only while no Hide window has the keyboard: its title is the root's row title (the provider when no task or proven native title exists), and with a second-line sentence its body is `<translated row status> · <exact sentence> · <project>` (D-09 to D-11, B31, B39, operator-approved update 2026-10-13).
The sentence stays in its original language; without one the body is the row's word and the project (`Approval · <project>`), a frame with no status code says its section (`Needs You · <project>`, `Unread · <project>`), and an empty project omits its separator.
See [With and without Hide AI](status-model.md#with-and-without-hide-ai) for the title priorities, native and inferred questions, counts and both notification channels in each mode.
hided judges the changes with the notice ledger the phone push also follows (status-model.md, Row presentation and phone notifications), so a child's raise arrives on its root, the same state never notifies twice, an agent back within ten minutes is not announced again, and nothing already waiting when the app opened is announced (B32, B33).
A View to look at is one notification, `To look at · report.html · <project>` (`볼 것 · …` in Korean), naming the newest View: a second open while the row is still unseen sends nothing, an open again of a View already seen is news again, a turn that finishes with a View to look at sends that one notification and no Done, and a question, approval or block on the same row sends its own instead (operator decision 2026-11-08, [status-model.md](status-model.md#a-view-to-look-at)).
A click brings Hide forward and opens that agent's pane, on a device too, as pressing its row does, except a `To look at` notification, which names a View and so opens it as the row's chip does, in focus mode beside the agent's pane (operator decision 2026-11-23); an agent the operator has seen takes its notification back, as a View looked at takes back its own, and a newer one for the same agent replaces it (B34, B35).
On Windows a click on a notification in Action Center after Hide quit starts Hide and opens what it named once the shell is up, as a click while it runs does.
The app icon says the 내 차례 number and nothing else, while the Needs You switch is on: root agents in Needs You across every device and project the window shows, rows folded into `more` included, with each device's missions and automations that are their own Needs You item, a once automation's ended run among them, so it says the number the 프로젝트 tab's, the 태스크 list's and the phone's 내 차례 say; a read ask is in none of them, and nothing at zero (operator decisions 2026-11-23, [status-model.md](status-model.md#the-four-groups)).
Unread Done is never counted, whatever the Done switch says (this replaces the operator decision of 2026-10-18 that added it).
macOS draws it on the Dock once it lets Hide notify, Linux where the desktop shows launcher counts, and Windows as a dot on the taskbar button whose description gives the count.
With both switches off nothing is sent and the app icon has no count; with Done alone on, completions are sent and the app icon still has no count.
The phone's own settings decide whether it receives pushes; computer-use resting applies only while an attached window enables Needs You notices (Notifications).

Settings > Devices keeps its own description.
The persisted `screen_intros_seen` field remains readable for older builds; this release has no Factory introduction.

## Factory

Factory is excluded from public source builds and official free app builds.
There is no sidebar entry, command, engine, question interception or Factory snapshot section.
Existing Factory stores and historical usage records are preserved as legacy data.

## Recent navigation

Web owner: `web/src/recent.ts`, `web/src/areaCycle.ts`, `web/src/viewFocus.ts`, `web/src/keyboard.ts`, and `CycleOverlay` in `web/src/Overlays.tsx`.

Recent place or View tab cycling (⌃Tab / ⌃⇧Tab in the desktop app, ⌥` / ⌥⇧` in a browser) follows where the keyboard is.
In a View area it walks the tabs of that exact area, in recent-use order.
The View scope includes device, checkout and area ID, so an Agent area with the same ID or another checkout cannot widen it.
A document, diff, View tab bar or visible native browser page names its View area.
Everywhere outside a View area it walks the places the operator has been, in recent-use order (issue #301): the agent panes the keyboard has been in, one row per pane across every device, project and checkout; the missions whose detail the mission screen showed; the automations whose detail the automation screen showed; and Desk's room. The order survives a reload, quitting the app and a daemon restart.
A list is where the operator picks a place, never one: the Overview, the mission table and the automation table are no rows, and neither is an automation's form, a View display or a project.
A terminal pane in a tab an Agent area of the Workspace in front draws (its normal tab or a delegated child's canvas), or that area's tab bar, puts the keyboard in the Agent area; from a tab bar the pane in use is the one the core focuses in that tab.
A place joins the order the first time the operator is on it, and a commit's passing frames on the way to its place are not visits; a mission joins once the operator moves on from it, to another place or by a commit to another mission, so ↑ and ↓ through the mission screen's list record only the mission they stop on, which reads as the first place until then; a pane that closes, a mission or automation the core no longer lists and Desk switched off are no longer rows.
A pane row is titled by the agent it runs, with the place as Recent Panels names it and `Terminal` beneath, and the agent's status mark; a pane running no agent is never a row.
A mission row is titled by the mission, with its project, `미션` and its state's pill word beneath (`herdr-ide · 미션 · 진행 중`) and the mission screen's mark; an automation row by its name, with `자동화` and its runs not looked at beneath (`자동화 · 안 본 회차 2개`) and the automation row's mark; the Desk row is `Desk` with the Desk mark and, while its sessions ask the operator, how many beneath.
From a pane running no agent, a mission or automation table, the sidebar, tools, search, Settings, a dialog, Main or Overview, the first chord lands on the most recent place; from a place (an agent pane, a mission's or an automation's detail, Desk) the cycle starts on it and the first chord lands on the place used before it.
Outside the Agent area, opening the cycle neither invents a pane origin nor records the underlying pane as visited.
The Overview modal and page supply no origin; their first cycle chord selects the most recent place, even with a Workspace View owner underneath.
Escape cancels the cycle and keeps Overview; committing closes Overview and brings that place forward.
A focused View area keeps its own scope even when it has zero or one tab or its target retired; it never falls through to places.
With no place visited, or no other place besides the one in use, the place cycle is a no-op.
Holding the chord's actual modifier freezes the order and scope and previews in Recent places or Recent View tabs without moving the committed tab, layout, screen or keyboard owner.
Repeated forward and backward chords walk that frozen order; Escape or losing the window cancels without a selection event.
Releasing the modifier on a View tab commits one in-place selection; on a pane it brings the pane's device, project, tab and pane forward with one event, as choosing it in the Agents list does, and the pane receives the keyboard; on a mission it shows that mission's detail on the mission screen as a row of the screen's list does (a mission of another device brings the device forward first, and a mission an external tool keeps opens in the tool's cell, the one list that holds it); on an automation it shows that automation's detail; on Desk it shows Desk's room.
Closed or moved View tabs leave the frozen candidates; removal of the origin area or checkout cancels, and no other area replaces it.
Places that stopped being rows leave the frozen candidates; a highlight that left moves to the next surviving place, and with none left the cycle ends.

Global Recent Panels is a separate, named command, unbound by default and assignable in Settings alongside the Agent pane and View tab commands.
It walks every unified surface in recent-use order across projects, checkouts and connected devices: terminal tabs, View files, diffs and browser displays, plus the single Overview page.
The Overview modal creates no Global Recent Panels visit.
An Overview page revisit moves its single device page row to the front; its tab, device and filters remain page-local, and a removed device retires its page row.
Recent Panels holds no Project Overview row, since that screen is gone (PRD overview-device-tasks B61).
A Global Recent Panels commit shows an Overview at once or brings one Workspace surface forward with one event; a View target also receives the keyboard.
Menu and palette invocations of either family select the next or previous valid target immediately, with no held modifier or second selection.
A bound Global Recent Panels chord keeps the same hold, release and cancellation behavior.
Recent Projects (⌥Tab / ⌥⇧Tab) retains its global project order and restores each Project's last Workspace surface, never its Overview.
Both global lists and the place cycle carry a device chip on a row outside the device in front (a pane or a mission of another device; an automation and Desk are the core's own and carry none) and move the rail, sidebar and center together when committed.
The session uses one bounded recent-surface history; the View area cycle filters it and appends normal area tabs not yet visited.
The place cycle reads the core's order of place visits (`ui_state.recent_places`, saved with the UI state); the page decides what a visit is and reports it with `place_visit`, and the fifty most recent places are kept, shell panes included, since an agent may start in one later; a state folder an earlier build wrote loads its pane order (`recent_pane_ids`) as pane places.
Until the core's order moves after a report, the page puts the place it reported first, so a cycle opened before the echo arrives starts where the operator last was.
The shown tab of the recorded keyboard area is the current visit, including native browser pages; intermediate commit frames are not visits.

Reopen Closed Tab is disabled when the session-local recent-close stack is empty or a restore is already running, and restoration works regardless of which surface currently owns focus.
Pressed while this machine's newest close is still being confirmed, it is accepted and waits: the closed item reopens as soon as the engine confirms the close, and nothing reopens if that close is refused or fails, or if another close starts first.
Restoration is one action with no confirmation: an in-flight pane shows inline progress, and a restore without a target pane shows a compact inline warning.
Missing cwd, an unavailable prior conversation, a missing file, and a retryable failure all use the same inline notice vocabulary, without a banner, card, or modal.
A definitive close refusal removes its reserved reopen entry, while an unconfirmed result keeps the entry and explains inline that Hide could not determine whether the item closed.
With no other project or tab available, navigation keeps the current selection without a modal; selecting an empty project shows its existing empty state.
Automatic pruning and concurrent-selection recovery use structured diagnostics without a modal.

The project identity is scoped by device, following the sidebar's Project > Workspace > Agents hierarchy; two checkouts of one repository share a project cycle, and panel history is one order over every project, narrowed to a project for its own last-surface lookup.
Both the recent-panel and project switchers show at most nine rows around the highlight.
Project rows show the last surface and checkout; panel rows show their project and checkout (collapsed to the checkout alone when both share a name) and their surface type.
A row's device chip follows its detail line, truncated before it would crowd the detail, with the status mark, title and checkout intact; a device absent from the registration map is named by its actual remote ID rather than presented as local.
Every device chip on an agent, a child or a descendant names its machine as the device rail does (`machineName`): the window's own machine is This Mac whatever name the core registered it under.
A panel row whose tab holds exactly one agent pane is titled by that agent's identity with its status mark; a tab with no agent or several keeps the tab label.
Recent Panels and Recent Projects history is session-local and retains only existing projects and surfaces; a deleted highlight moves to the next surviving entry without reordering the held cycle, and if none survives, the cycle cancels and keeps the current selection.

Next tab and Previous tab (⇧⌘] and ⇧⌘[ in the desktop app on macOS, Ctrl+PageDown and Ctrl+PageUp on Windows and Linux) choose the tab beside the one in use, in the order its tab strip draws, wrapping from the last tab to the first and back, as every browser, terminal and editor does.
They step through the View area that holds the keyboard, and otherwise through the Agent area in use, the strip ⌘1-9 numbers; the choice is the one a click on that tab makes, and the keyboard follows it.
A strip with one tab stays as it is.
There is no overlay and nothing to hold: ⌃Tab stays the recent-use cycle.
A browser tab has no default chord for either, because Chrome keeps both pairs for its own tabs; Settings, Shortcuts can bind them there.

## Device rail

Web owner: `web/src/components/device-rail.tsx`, `web/src/devices.ts`, `web/src/computers.ts`, `web/src/RemoteStage.tsx`, the rail branch of `web/src/sidebar.tsx`.

This section supersedes PRD home-device-rail rows D-10, D-11, D-27, B3, B4 and B12 (quick device-rail-badges): the Inbox, the rail that came and went with the first remote device, the Needs You-only badge and the footer device button no longer exist.
Quick device-rail-slack then narrowed the rail to `--size-rail` (48), ran it the sidebar's full height, took the names off the tiles and left Working to the session rows.

This Mac is this document's name for the machine the core runs on; the screen names it as its system does, This Mac on macOS, This PC on Windows and This computer on Linux, in the interface language (`common.thisMac`, `common.thisPc`, `common.thisComputer`: `이 Mac`, `이 PC`, `이 컴퓨터` in Korean).
The core sends no word for it: its device row has no `label` but the one it kept from a move (below), only its system in `host.platform`, and every surface that names it, the sidebar title, the rail, Home, Settings, the Overview's device switch, the start panel, ⌘K and the phone's start sheet, words it from that system (`systemMachineKey` and `machineName` in `web/src/screenMachine.ts`), so one screen never shows it in two languages; on its screen that another computer shows, every one of them says the name that computer's window calls it by instead (`ownMachineName`), since This Mac there would name the viewer's machine.
The rail is always shown, even when This Mac is the only device: the sidebar's full-height left column, with the single sidebar list to its right and the device title above it in the window's top row, holding This Mac, each registered device in the core's order, and a `+` (`기기 추가`, a dashed tile) directly under the last device tile.
The stored sidebar width stays the content column's; the rail adds `--size-rail` to its left while it shows, and the drag edge sits on the content column.
Each tile is a 32 rounded square with no name under it: This Mac draws the laptop glyph and a device the monogram of its name, the first letter of each of its first two words, split at spaces, dots, dashes and underscores, and a one-word name where a small letter meets a capital (`Mac mini` → `Mm`, `build-box` → `Bb`, `MacBook` → `MB`, `mini` → `M`); the tile's hint, shown to its right, is the name followed by `연결 안 됨` or each count it marks in full (`mini · Needs You 12 · Done 1`), since the pill stops at `9+`.
The selected tile is ringed (a 2px ring 2px off the tile), and one tile is selected at a time.
A tile carries at most one mark, notched into its top-right corner by a ring of the rail's fill, and it is the most urgent state: the Needs You count in a `--warning` pill, `9+` from 10, or, with no Needs You, a `--success` dot with no number while the device has unseen Done.
Working has no mark on the rail, since it is no reason to switch device; the 태스크 list counts it.
A mark's digits are `--status-foreground`, white in Light and near-black in Dark.
The counts come from the device's published `agent_scope` values; the browser does not rederive them.
A device that is not connected dims its glyph and wears a `×` at the bottom-right, with no mark, since its last counts are not current; selected, its sidebar shows only its name, `연결 안 됨` and `다시 연결`, which retries the connection in place, and never the tree it last reported; while the center already offers `다시 연결` for that device (its Work, Projects and Backlog body, or its Workspace's surface or stale line), the sidebar leaves its own out, so the same action is drawn once.
A delayed or failed agent-state query alone leaves the connected device, its tree and terminals available; retained agent activity uses the existing Unknown state until a successful query confirms it, without a disconnected message or a new alert.
A paired machine's tile is that machine's own screen, so it counts what that machine told of its own Needs You and Done (`navigator.peer_work`) and nothing while it tells nothing; this computer's work that runs on a paired machine opens in this computer's tile with that machine's mark, so it is counted on this computer's tile, and this computer's tile is the one ringed while the center shows it (`railFrontId`, PRD device-connect-iroh D-50, D-55).
Selecting a device tile sends `focus_device`, and the sidebar becomes that device's single project and session list; no row of its own names the device, while Needs You also lists the other connected devices' rows, each with its device's chip ([Sidebar hierarchy](#sidebar-hierarchy), operator decision 2026-11-08).
In the desktop app ⌥⌘1-9 (Alt+Shift+1-9 on Windows and Linux) selects the computer tile at that number, top to bottom with This Mac first, paired machines included, whether or not the rail shows and whether or not another machine's screen holds the keyboard, and holding ⌥⌘ alone floats each tile's number at its top left (see Keycaps, tooltips, and icon buttons); a number with no tile does nothing.
⇧⌘ would have been the shorter chord, but macOS keeps ⇧⌘3, ⇧⌘4 and ⇧⌘5 for screenshots, so three tiles could never be reached; ⌘1-9 belongs to the tabs.
Every tile is a button reached with Tab and chosen with Enter or Space, named for assistive technology by the device, its connection and each count it marks (`mini, 연결 안 됨`, `This Mac, Needs You 2, Done 1`).
`기기 추가` opens Settings › Devices with the Add device dialog in front, as the hidden rail's `기기 추가…` and the Add a project dialog's host list do.
Removing the device in front moves the front to This Mac; that device's agents stay on it, and the rail stays.

Each bot (an external tool, Settings › 봇) connected in its own space has a tile after `+`, under a short line, one per bot in the order the bots connected, and only the bots of this window's core (PRD hide-external-control D-50, D-51; mission-87).
A 전체 bot has no tile: it works as the operator, so its work is in the operator's lists, and Settings › 봇 lists it (operator decision 2026-11-22).
A bot's tile is the size of a device's and draws the bot's owl on its tile colour, both chosen from its name (`botLook` in `web/src/bots.ts`: one of seven owls and one of seven `--bot-*` colours), so the same bot looks the same on the rail, in Settings, in a menu and on its agents' marks, and never reads as a machine.
Since every tile is a bot in its own space, the tile marks no mode; its hint is `<name> · 봇 · 봇 공간`, with `봇 차례 N` while something waits on the bot.
Its one mark is a grey pill (`--muted-foreground`) with the number of what waits on the bot's answer, `9+` from 10, and no Needs You, app badge or notice of the operator's counts it (B39, B41).
A bot with nothing waiting has no mark; its tile opens its cell as follows.
Selecting an own-space bot's tile shows the tool's Overview as the center's page, never as a layer over the Workspace, and its cell in the sidebar ([Work an external tool keeps](#work-an-external-tool-keeps)), with This Mac in front under it, so the center no longer keeps This Mac's agent panes as if nothing had changed (PRD hide-external-control D-54, B47) and a tool that leaves Act, and its tile with it, leaves the window on This Mac (B44).
The selected tile, a device's or a tool's, follows what the center shows: landing on a pane of a tool's agent by any path selects the tool's tile, and landing on any other pane selects that pane's device.
A device's tile takes the center back to that device's Workspace; from a tool's cell, to the Workspace as the core holds it unless the pane it focuses is the tool's, else to that device's pane the keyboard was in last that no tool keeps, else to the device's Overview, so the press never lands back on the tool's work.
Which cell a window shows is the window's own: another window and the phone keep theirs.
⌥⌘1-9 numbers the computer tiles only.

**Another computer's screen** (PRD device-connect-iroh D-37, B17, B20, B21, B47, B52, B63).
The computer tiles are one group before `+`: This Mac, each device, and each other computer running its own Hide that this one is paired with and opens, drawn alike and numbered together; no tile wears a crown, and in a build that connects Hides the Settings › Devices row of This Mac wears none either, since every computer runs a core of its own (D-80).
A paired computer's tile shows that computer's own Hide screen: the rail stays, and the rest of the window, from the rail's edge, is that computer's screen as its own window draws it, with no rail of its own, the keys typed there going to it; This Mac's tile, or any device's, brings this window's own screen back.
How the two connect (direct, Tailscale, relay) is said in the device's Settings row and `hide device list` only.
A screen that cannot show says why, in the place it would stand and in its tile's hint, each in its own words: `연결 안 됨` with `다시 닿으면 자동으로 열려요`, `멈춤`, `상대가 멈춤`, `이 컴퓨터 업데이트 필요` or `그 컴퓨터 업데이트 필요`, `허가 없음`, and `확인하는 중` while the pairing settles; the tile is dimmed with its `×` meanwhile, and the screen opens on its own once it can, asking again every 5 s while its computer reads as reachable.
Which computer must update is said the same once either side asked the other to update; the place only says it and offers nothing to press, because the corner notice on that computer's own window carries the press (`지금 업데이트` or `업데이트 요청`), and no strip over the screen speaks of updates (operator review 2026-11-22).
That computer's screen names it as this window does: its title is the name this computer knows it by rather than its own `이 Mac`, and while its own operator chose no interface language it speaks this window's (`hideRemote.onViewer`).
It draws with this window's View menu choices too, the agent logos of its rows and tabs and the request and progress line of its panes, over the ones its own window chose, and draws no View menu of its own, so the View menu at the rail's foot changes what this screen draws, at once; nothing is written to that computer's core, whose own window keeps its own choices, and holding ⌥ peeks at the logos there as here (`web/src/drawnDisplay.ts`; operator decision 2026-11-25).
While this window's rail is hidden, that computer's screen, opened by ⌥⌘1-9, a work row or the Start panel, has no View menu on it: the choices are changed by showing the rail again or from this computer's own screen, whose sidebar footer then carries the View menu, and the ⌥ peek still works there (operator decision 2026-11-25).
Either side on an older build keeps the screen on its own choices and its own View menu: a desktop app here that sends no choices, or a Hide there whose page does not read them.
While the other computer's screen drops for a moment, it shows its own `다시 연결하는 중` and sends no keys.
Its connection lives only while its tile shows: another tile closes it, and coming back opens it at once, drawn from where it was.
Anything this window draws over the screen's place (a menu, Settings, a dialog) moves the screen aside until it closes, since the screen is a view of its own over the window.
On that screen the other computer offers nothing that lets a machine or a tool into it: no sharing, no allowing a pairing, no pausing or removing a computer, no approving an external tool and no pairing a phone, and a request for one is refused to that screen alone; removing a phone works.
A browser tab has no way to draw another computer's screen, so a paired computer's tile there opens its row in Settings › Devices.
The count marks of a paired computer's tile come from the counts its core sends over the connection, which the letters work adds; until then its tile carries none.

A right-click on the rail offers `기기 목록 숨기기`.
While the rail is hidden the top row's device name becomes a `This Mac ⌄` menu that lists the devices (a check on the one in front), then, after a line, the bots in their own space with their owls and their grey number, `기기 추가…` and `기기 목록 표시`; while a bot's cell is in front the top row names the bot, `nightly 봇`.
The View menu of the desktop app carries `Toggle device rail` (`toggle_device_rail`, no default chord, bindable in Settings › Shortcuts like the sidebar switch); a browser tab uses the `This Mac ⌄` menu.
The hidden state is the core's `ui_state.device_rail_visible`, kept beside `left_sidebar_visible`, so it survives a restart.

A device's Workspace in front wears the device color, `--device-remote`: a band at the start of the top row's Workspace path with the server glyph and the device's name, truncated, and a border of the same color around its panes; this machine's Workspace has neither.
Which machine is a device is the window's to say (`awayDevice` in `web/src/screenMachine.ts`): on a window of a machine whose core runs elsewhere, its own Workspaces wear neither and the core's machine's wear both.

A machine whose core runs on another machine (ARCHITECTURE.md, A core on another machine) is a device on that core's rail like any other, named by its host name.
Its `다시 연결` is answered that the machine reconnects by itself, and removing it ends its connection until it attaches again; neither adds a state or a line of its own.
The window's own machine is always the first tile and the one named This Mac (`이 Mac`), the laptop glyph (`web/src/screenMachine.ts`, `windowFirst`); on a window of a machine whose core runs elsewhere, the core's machine follows it, named by the name the operator registered it under before the core moved to it, which the core keeps (`machine_label`, ARCHITECTURE.md, A core on another machine), and by the name its machine gives itself when it has none.
No tile wears a crown: every computer runs a core of its own, so where the core is marks nothing on the rail (PRD device-connect-iroh D-80); the tile of the machine that runs the core keeps `core 실행 중` in its hint.
A Hide this machine is paired with and opens has a tile too, after the devices: lettered from its name like a device, with no mark, and dimmed with the `×` while the two are not connected or the opening is paused (`openedMachines` in `web/src/pairing.ts`, B3).
A machine that is already a device of this core has only its device tile, so no machine shows twice, and a machine that only opens this one has no tile.
Pressing a paired machine's tile opens Settings › Devices, where its row is.

This PC's WSL distribution is a device like any other, one tile per distribution lettered from its name (`WSL Ubuntu` → `WU`) (PRD wsl-device B5, D-12, D-20).
Every surface that names it says after its name where it is, the core's machine as this window names it, and never `원격`: the tile's hint and accessible name (`WSL Ubuntu · 이 PC · Needs You 3`), the sidebar title's smaller word, which is `원격` for an SSH device, the Overview's device switch, ⌘K's device row, its surface while it shows no tab, and the line under its name in Settings › Devices, in place of its Linux platform (`WSL Ubuntu · 이 PC`) (`wslPlace` in `web/src/screenMachine.ts`).
Choosing its tile scopes the sidebar, 태스크, PR, Overview, the tile counts and the ⌥⌘ numbers to its own items by the rules above (B6), and a stopped distribution reads as any device that is not connected.

A window on a machine whose core runs elsewhere shows its core's state and takes its strip, in the place and size of the connection badge, when the core cannot draw it (W1 to W3, B16):

- while this machine updates its core to this app's build, `Mac mini의 core를 이 앱의 빌드로 바꾸는 중 · 에이전트는 계속 실행돼요`, with `…`;
- while the core cannot be reached, `! Mac mini의 core에 연결할 수 없어요 · 이전 대기 중`: the node dials again by itself, and once the core answers on this build the two machines are split into two Hides (PRD device-connect-iroh B45); a node no window started splits nothing and ends the line `다시 연결하는 중` (the `core_link` frame's `splits`);
- while the dial a window's `로그인` or retry started waits on the operator, that window alone shows `… Mac mini core에 로그인하는 중` with what ssh asks under it exactly as a device's row shows it (PRD system-ssh-device-link B33): ssh's own sentence and a masked field with `로그인` and `취소`, a server's fingerprint with `이 서버 믿고 연결` and `취소`, neither chosen, a page to confirm with `열기`, or `보안 키를 터치하세요`; another window shows the link connecting and cannot answer; the answer is cleared as it is sent; Cancel, five minutes on a question, a page or a key touch, or closing that window ends the attempt, before the first question or between two as well; one press asks at most one sign-in; a connect no window made (`hide connect`, a launch) and the dials Hide makes by itself never ask, and stop at the sign-in for the window's `로그인`;
- while the link stopped on what only the operator can fix, which nothing dials again until they act, the device row's three actions: a sign-in (`! Mac mini core에 연결하려면 로그인이 필요해요`, or `! Mac mini core가 로그인을 거절했어요`) with `로그인`, which dials again and may ask; a changed server key (`! Mac mini core의 서버 키가 바뀌었어요 · 터미널에서 확인해 주세요`) with `ssh mini` and Copy and no `다시 시도`, as on the device row, since a retry would meet the same key; a Hide an update replaced on disk (`! Hide가 실행된 뒤에 업데이트됐어요 · Hide를 종료하고 다시 열면 Mac mini core에 연결돼요`) with no action, since only Hide opened again can dial; and any other such failure (`! ssh로 Mac mini core에 연결하지 못했어요 · 터미널에서 ssh mini 명령으로 이유를 확인해 주세요`) with `다시 시도`;
- after the operator ended the link here, `! Mac mini core와 연결을 끊었어요` with `다시 연결` at its end, which links it again and is, with `다시 시도` (`로그인`) and an answer to what ssh asks, the only thing the window sends while it waits;
- otherwise the shell's own connecting state.

The window takes no input while its strip shows, and sends none of what was typed meanwhile once the core is back; the strip goes when the core's snapshot arrives.
Once the two machines are split, this machine's daemon runs its own core: the window's connection drops, its address loses the node's name (`addressForRole` in `web/src/ws.ts`) and it reconnects to that core as any window of a core's machine.
A split that did not happen leaves the window as it was, the node of its core, and Settings › Devices says why on the window's own row (below).

## Another machine's work on this computer

Web owner: `web/src/guestWork.ts`, `web/src/GuestWorkRows.tsx`, `web/src/GuestPaneScreen.tsx`.

A paired machine that may open this computer runs work here, and each pane of that work is listed in this computer's 프로젝트 list whether or not that machine is connected (PRD device-connect-iroh B10, B68).
The 태스크 list does not list it, in 상태별 or 프로젝트별: the work is that machine's, and it shows in that machine's own 태스크 list and notices (D-50, D-59, D-60; operator review 2026-11-23).
A pane started in a folder one of this computer's projects holds belongs to that project: it stands under the checkout row whose folder holds it (the longest such path), after that checkout's own rows, and at the project's end, inside it, when that checkout has no row: the project's row stands for its main checkout, the checkout waits under 정리할 것, or it is a Hide AI checkout the tree does not draw (`guestPlaces`).
A project whose every checkout waits under 정리할 것 draws no row of its own, and its heads stand in its place on the columns of the work outside every project.
It is hidden with its place: folding the project hides it, and so does the closed 다른 체크아웃 fold its checkout waits in (operator review 2026-11-25, replacing the earlier rule that no fold hides it).
A fold that hides such work shows nothing of it, no count and no mark, since the machine that asked for the work keeps its rows and notices and this computer rings nothing for it; only while the center shows one of those panes does the fold that hides it, the project's row, 다른 체크아웃 or the machine's folded head, draw as holding it, as it does for this computer's own checkout in front (`guestFoldInFront`, `guestHead`).
Each place draws one machine's panes together under one head: the machine's letters in the other-device colour (`MB`), drawn only, since the words beside them name the machine, `<machine>에서 시킨 일` and how many.
Its rows are not stepped in: their marks and logos stand on the column of the place's own rows (a checkout's agent rows, a folder's), and a tree rail runs down the glyph column on their left, where a tab's guide hangs, to tie them to the head (operator review 2026-11-25).
The head folds as the list's other folds do, a row with its chevron at the end that the pointer and the keyboard press; folded, it shows its count and nothing more.
The core keeps each fold by the machine and the folder of the place it stands in (`ui_state.folded_guest_work`, `guest_work_fold_toggle`; at most 256, and past that a fold whose machine or folder is no longer listed goes first, oldest first, then the oldest of the rest opens again), as it keeps the 다른 체크아웃 fold by the project's folder, so it holds across a restart.
Every other pane stands at the end of the list under the same head, worded the same (`MacBook에서 시킨 일`), with its head where the list's folds start and its rows on a folder's column.
The row is named as that machine names the work: its agent's label or else the name its tab was given, else for a plain terminal `터미널 · <branch>` with the branch its checkout stands on, and the folder's own name only when that machine told neither (or the pane's id on its machine when its node said no folder); it follows the name when it changes there.
It starts with the status mark that machine draws the work's row with, following it as it changes there, then its agent's logo while rows draw logos (the agent that machine says runs in it; a plain terminal, or a build that tells none, draws no logo), and names its machine only through the head above it and its accessible name; it carries no count or time.
A name, a branch and an agent told are kept while that machine is away; the mark is not, so a machine that is not connected, or a build that tells none (before marks), leaves the mark's slot empty, and a build before names keeps its rows' folder names.
Screen copy never calls that machine a guest: it is named, or its work is `<machine>에서 시킨 일`.
Pressing a row shows the pane in the center as the window's own page (`Screen` `guest`), since the pane is no tab of this core's, drawn as this computer's own pane is: a tab strip of that machine's work in the same place (the project, or the machine's own group) by the rows' names, a header with the status mark, the work's name (the folder as its hint), `· <project>`, and at its end the machine's letters and `<machine>에서 시킨 일` once, and its terminal.
The operator reads it, types into it, and it follows this window's size while this window is the one typing; this computer's tile is ringed.
Splitting, a new tab, renaming and starting an agent are drawn dimmed in its menu with `<machine>에서 할 수 있어요` under each, whether or not that machine is connected (D-74), and their shortcuts say the same line as a notice in place of acting.
`닫기…`, ⌘W and the pane-close shortcut ask first (`이 터미널을 닫을까요?`, with what ends and that the machine is told); confirming sends `guest_pane_close`, from this computer's window or from the screen another computer shows of it, since either already types into the pane (D-74), and the page waits for the answer.
The machine that started the work loses its tab of it with the pane: at once while it is connected, else when it connects again or its Hide starts anew, and never with a new shell in its place.
Once it ended, the page shows what stands in its place: the next tab of that work, else the one before it, else the project row it stood under, else this computer's Workspace, never the Overview (`afterGuestClose`).
A close that did not go keeps the page and says why in one corner line: `닫지 못했어요. 이 터미널은 이제 여기 없어요`, `닫지 못했어요. 연결이 끊겼어요. 다시 해 보세요`, `닫지 못했어요. 이 컴퓨터의 Hide는 여기서 닫을 수 없어요` (an older build of that computer), or `이 터미널을 닫지 못했어요. 다시 해 보세요`.
A pane that left the list while its page is open says `이 터미널은 끝났어요`.
A program's mouse reports are not sent to another machine's pane.

A connected machine this computer opens tells its own Needs You and Done, and those rows follow this computer's in the same sections of both sidebar lists, drawn as this computer's rows are: its mark, the agent's logo only while rows draw logos, the title and the time since it changed, and under them where it runs and why it waits, the sentence its own core chose or else the ask's own words (`권한 요청에서 멈춤`, `답을 기다림`) (D-50, B49).
They stand after this computer's rows, one machine's rows together, with no head of their own; each row is shown with its machine's mark: its second line ends with that machine's chip, the one another device's row in 내 차례 wears, after the place and the reason; a short line is shared in order, not by share: the reason keeps its words (up to three fifths), the place keeps what it can, and the chip takes only what is left, from its mark and a letter up to two fifths; the row's accessible name names the machine (`PeerWorkRows`, operator review 2026-11-23, replacing the per-machine head of 2026-11-22).
They show on this computer's own window only: another computer's screen of this one shows this computer's work and no one else's.
A section is drawn for them alone, and its count, in the 프로젝트 and the 태스크 list alike, includes them.
Pressing one opens that machine's tile on that work: its screen opens the work its core told by its pane (D-78, D-81), and in a browser tab, which draws no other computer's screen, it selects the tile; rows of this computer's own work on another machine keep the outline chip and open in this computer's tile.
This computer rings nothing for another machine's rows, and they leave the list when that machine is no longer connected.

## Start panel

Web owner: `web/src/StartPanel.tsx`, `web/src/startTargets.ts`, `web/src/startDraft.ts`, `web/src/startAnswer.ts`, `web/src/agentPicker.ts`, `web/src/components/agent-picker.tsx`.

The start panel starts an agent supported by the shared adapter with a first instruction anywhere hide can reach (PRD home-device-rail D-17..D-22).
It remains independent of the inline group/draft start: a successful start makes its own tab in the target checkout's selected group rather than consuming the draft whose form happens to be open.
⌘N opens it in the desktop app, where it is also File › Start agent; in a browser tab ⌘N stays the browser's and `에이전트 시작…` in ⌘K, found by typing, opens it, on every screen.
It floats at the ⌘P palette's place and width with no backdrop, and the keyboard lands in its text box, `에이전트에게 무엇을 요청할까요?`, which grows with what is written to about six lines and then scrolls; Enter starts, Shift+Enter is a new line, and an Enter that ends an input method's composition does neither.
Opened while Settings is up, it takes Settings' place: Settings closes and the target is the front device's most recently used project.
Over the text is where, one quiet row of words: the device when the target is another device's (its chip in the other-device colour), then the project `›` the branch; under the text is who, the agent kind and the model menus drawn as words that light on hover, and `시작 ⏎`; Enter or `시작` sends one `agent_start_in_checkout` with a fresh request id, the text as the agent's first instruction, typed once the agent's screen takes input or handed to the CLI as its own argument where that is proven (ARCHITECTURE.md, the first prompt).
The target defaults to what is in front: the checkout of the Workspace in front (a worktree when that is it), a project's main checkout while the Overview is filtered to that project, and the front device's most recently used project while the Overview with no project chip or Settings is, or `프로젝트 추가` in place of the start control when it has none; a device's surface in front makes that device the target's.
The project menu lists the front device's projects, then each other device's under its name, with a separator between devices, a check on the chosen project and a search field; a device that is not connected is listed with `연결 안 됨` and its projects cannot be chosen.
Choosing a project starts on its main checkout; the branch menu, lists that project's checkouts by branch with its main checkout first, marked `기본`, then the checkout in front, marked `지금`, then the rest as the catalog lists them, with a search field and, for a Git project, `새 브랜치에서 시작…` at its end.
`새 브랜치에서 시작…` puts a new branch's name field, its base (the project's saved base, else its default branch) and an `✕` where the branch chip was; the start then creates that worktree and starts the agent in it, and `✕` goes back to the project's main checkout.
A target whose device stops answering after it was chosen stays chosen: the panel says `<device> · 연결 안 됨` with `다시 연결` and holds `시작` until the device answers, and never moves the start to another target.
A target on a paired computer, one that runs its own Hide, is that computer's own work (PRD device-connect-iroh D-59, D-73, B64): `시작` closes the panel and hands the start to that computer's screen, which its tile then shows, and that screen starts it on its own core with the same text, agent, model and branch and moves to it as its own start would (`web/src/computerWork.ts`); the panel keeps the words until that computer says the start went.
When the folder is no project of that computer, the start adds it as one first and starts once it is listed; that wait and the wait for the screen's first snapshot end 30 s after the start reached the screen, read before anything is made, so a start that did not go by then starts nothing.
Once that start is made there, it is followed to its own end whatever is opened or started after it; while its tab exists and its agent is still coming up, the words wait for the agent's own answer with no bound of their own, as a start on this computer's does.
When that end can no longer be read (that computer's screen closed, loaded again or crashed, or lost its own Hide), the words come back with `<computer>에서 이 시작이 어떻게 됐는지 알 수 없게 됐어요. 그 컴퓨터 화면을 확인한 뒤 다시 시작하세요`, and nothing is sent again on its own.
A start that did not go opens the panel again on its words and target with why: `<computer>의 화면이 열리지 않아 시작하지 않았어요` when the screen did not open within 30 s, `<computer>에서 준비가 제때 끝나지 않아 시작하지 않았어요`, `<computer>에서 이 폴더를 프로젝트로 더하지 못해 시작하지 않았어요`, `<computer>에서 앞선 시작들이 아직 끝나지 않아 시작하지 않았어요` when that screen still follows eight starts, that computer's core's own reason, or `<computer>에서 답이 없어요` when nothing answered within 160 s and no tab of it was made there; a newer draft keeps its own words and target, and an answer that arrives after the panel gave the words back changes nothing.
Where no screen of that computer can be drawn here (a browser tab, an older desktop app), `시작` starts nothing and says `<computer>의 일은 그 컴퓨터 화면에서 시작해요`, keeping the words.
The kind menu holds Claude Code, Codex, Grok, OpenCode, Pi, omp and Antigravity with their provider marks; the model menu is `CLI 기본값` then the chosen kind's catalog (Antigravity's from `agy models`, started with `--model`), and changing the kind takes that kind's list and the model last chosen for it.
OpenCode's start takes no model, so with OpenCode chosen no model menu is drawn here or in a Factory's worker candidate, and a model remembered for it from before is not sent.
The agent menu lists items, one per agent and profile the target's machine has (PRD agent-profiles-phase2 B2): each agent's `Claude Code` first, standing for its built-in account, then one `Claude Code · work` for each profile the machine lists for it, in the machine's order; there is no profile menu beside it.
A machine that lists no profile of any agent shows the agent names alone and nothing at their right, as before (B1).
Once it lists one, each item shows its account's weekly percent left at its right end, `—` where that is not known (an agent that reports no usage, a gateway or API-key account, a failed read, a device's account, which is read on this machine alone), and two items in one account show that account's same figure; the panel asks for no new reading, and a low figure never changes the item (B3, D-11).
The menu opens on the item the core would pick for the project, which is the project's choice (Project settings › Agent accounts) while the machine lists it and else the agent's default (B5, D-03); the panel remembers the agent as before and no item.
The item the menu first showed for an agent stays the shown item while the panel is open, until the operator chooses another or the project, device or agent changes (the next one shows what the core would pick), and an item the machine stops listing is replaced by the one the menu then shows: a project choice that moves in another window under the open panel does not change the account a `Claude Code` already shown would start.
The item shown is the item started, every time, on a machine with no profile at all too (the start names `default` there and the core never picks again), though not on a device whose Hide predates profiles: `Claude Code` starts the built-in account even where the project preselects `work`, and `Claude Code · work` always starts work (B4, D-02); a start that names no item, `hide agent spawn` with no `--profile`, takes the project's choice and a child of the same agent its parent's.
A device target lists that device's items, with no figure, since its usage is not read here; one that stops answering after it was chosen, and one whose Hide predates profiles, list each agent's built-in item alone, and the menu says why in one quiet line under the items (`<device> · 연결 안 됨`, the panel's own offline wording, or `이 기기는 아직 프로필을 지원하지 않아요…`), where a device whose profile list could not be read now keeps showing the last list it gave (B6).
A model remembered for the agent that the chosen item's list does not have opens as `CLI 기본값`, and the model menu lists the item's own models by the names the agent shows for them, sending the model itself (PRD agent-profiles-phase2 B5, B8).
The new-worktree dialog, Start from an issue and 맡기기 keep the agent menu without items and name no profile, so the core picks.
While the catalog is being read or cannot be read, the model menu shows the remembered model or `CLI 기본값`, is disabled, and gives the reason in its tooltip; starting is never held back by it.
Every supported agent start, here or in a dialog, is remembered by the core with its model, `CLI 기본값` included, so the next open preselects that kind and its model with no `recent` mark; the target is not remembered and follows what is in front on every open.
Escape or a press outside closes the panel and keeps the text for the next open; a start that goes clears it.
