The panel follows only the answer carrying its own request id, even after it closes: a refusal or a failed start shows its reason inside the panel and keeps the text, and a start that went brings the center to the new pane, on a device by moving the rail, the sidebar and the center together, except a start from an open the Overview's work map made (a start circle, a `+`, `나`'s `+`), which leaves the Overview in front, the layer over a Workspace and the page alike, and presents nothing (the panel's `stay`; issue #2514).
Over the Overview's layer the panel is drawn inside that layer, since a modal layer keeps the pointer and the keyboard to its own subtree and would read a press on the panel as outside.
The page opens that Workspace only after the core has selected the correlated pane and its checkout lists it, including the first start after setup.
A newer page visit cancels that pending presentation; the answer never sends a second focus request or closes an Overview opened over work.
An agent that fails to start after its tab opened puts its text back in the draft with the reason, unless a new draft took its place, so the next ⌘N shows both; the reason stays until the text changes.
A start with no answer in 90 seconds, the core's own limit for one, says so inside the panel.
New worktree, Start from an issue and 맡기기 carry the same kind and model menus with the remembered choice preselected, and what they start becomes the next default; New worktree's kind menu starts with `Terminal only`, which is never remembered.
A worktree created on this machine, from New worktree, the panel, Start from an issue or 맡기기, comes forward the way a start does: the core selects its new pane once its checkout is listed, and only while the operator has visited nothing else since, within the 90 seconds and with the same engine connection; otherwise it stays where it is, and the page never sends a focus of its own for it (with-hide/hide-private#311).
A worktree created on a device is opened once that device lists its pane, through the device's own focus.
A new worktree's branch starts from `origin/<base>` as Hide last fetched it when the local base has no commits of its own, so a base the operator fetched but never pulled is not where new work begins; a local base with unpushed commits, or with no fetched copy, is started from as it is, and the new branch tracks no upstream either way ([ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md), `worktree_control::new_branch_start`).
A worktree a mission's worker is started in, in the mission's own project, takes the mission's destination as that base, by the same rule, rather than the branch the repository's folder has out.
Settings > Agents > Starting work has no default agent of its own.

## Usage

Web owner: `web/src/components/usage.tsx`, `web/src/components/usage-history.tsx`, `web/src/components/usage-history-table.tsx`, `web/src/components/usage-amount.tsx`, `web/src/UsageOverview.tsx`, `web/src/usageHistory.ts`, `web/src/usage.ts`, `web/src/settings/UsageTab.tsx`.
The core owns account windows and shared preferences (`usage`, `ui_state.usage`); all limits refer to the core's own node, even with a remote device in front.
The declaration lists all seven agents; only agents with a declared limit source appear in the footer and popover.

The footer draws up to three pinned account windows at its left edge, and, while the device rail is hidden, the View menu, Help and Settings at its right edge.
It stays at the sidebar's foot whatever list the sidebar shows, a device's or a tool's, empty or full: the list's slot fills the column above it (`data-sidebar-list` in `web/src/sidebar.tsx`), so no list moves it.
Defaults pin the weekly window of each agent that is on by default and reports one (Claude Code and Codex), leaving a slot for the operator's own pin.
Each chip shows the agent mark and its rounded percent, naming the window when the agent has multiple visible chips.
A numberless not-started or expired chip always names its window.
Each chip has its own accessible name with agent, window and value.
Success is below 70%, warning starts at 70%, danger at 90%.
Loading or unavailable agents collapse their pins into one dimmed mark; recent stale and fallback readings keep their values until the reset.
At narrow widths the footer wraps rather than clipping its controls.

Any chip opens the 320-wide Usage popover above the footer.
Its first row shows today's estimated API-rate equivalent cost and new tokens side by side; token categories are behind `Token details`.
New tokens means input, output and cache writes; cache reads stay separate, and All tokens adds them to new tokens.
Cost is an estimate at public API rates, not the account's bill; its tooltip states this and the full history repeats it below the table.
An unpriced model or incomplete native usage makes a known subtotal a lower bound with its reason; a wholly unpriced amount says `Price unavailable`, a missing price table says `Pricing pending`, and a table older than sixty days is marked stale.
No calls means No data, never a zero dollar amount; a known free price remains zero.

Hide AI's own background calls (agent summaries, Memory, Factory, issues and worktrees), which no session file records, are inside the headline totals, bars and grass; the popover draws no separate Hide AI line, and the full history's table is where Hide AI is read apart from the agents.
Unknown Hide AI calls make the inspected day's summary and the selected period's total a lower bound, drawn `≥` with a mark and spoken as "at least", including the full-history summary, whenever that day or period contains unknown calls.
A missing model price alone does not make known tokens a lower bound.

Below the summary, a `7 days` / `30 days` segmented choice switches between seven daily bars and thirty 12-pixel contribution squares; thirty days is the initial view.
The bars show the most recent seven civil dates, labeled by weekday with Today at the end, and scale new tokens against the largest day in that period.
The squares show the most recent thirty civil dates in Sunday-first columns, with no calendar numbers or weekday labels.
The selected period's cost and new-token total stay visible while a day is inspected, below the bars or beside the grass.
The core prepares daily amounts, boundaries and quartile levels; the page selects and sums those amounts without repricing calls or reading sessions.
Closed nonzero days set quartiles, or quarters of the maximum with fewer than seven such days; level zero uses secondary and the other four use primary over secondary.
During initial backfill unread cells are outlined, and file progress and `so far` remain visible.
Hover or keyboard focus previews a day in the summary; clicking pins it, leaving restores the pinned day, and Today returns to today.
In the grass, Up and Down move one day and Left and Right move a week; in the bars, Left and Right move one day.
Home and End reach the visible range's ends.
Changing period clears the preview, keeps a pinned date within the new range and otherwise returns to today.
A day change keeps the closed detail row and grass geometry stable; expanded token categories wrap at the available width.
The summary's cost and new tokens stay on one line, shrinking only when the text size makes them wider than their half of the row, because a value that wrapped would move the hovered day out from under the pointer.
Hovering a weekly limit outlines the recent seven days, and a five-hour limit outlines today; leaving removes that outline.
Only the chosen seven or thirty date buttons are mounted, and closing the popover removes them.

Account limits follow the grass as compact rows without individual card borders.
Each row is one line: a model's window under an account window (`└ Fable`) draws its reset countdown only where it differs from the account window's, and a name too long for the line is cut with an ellipsis and given whole on hover.
Each visible agent has 5 hours and 7 days rows with a countdown, short inline bar and percent; model-specific limits are indented below their weekly row.
Not-started windows say `Not started`, never 0%.
Expired, not-offered and individually unreadable windows have no popover row; a whole malformed response makes the agent unavailable.
Loading rows show `…`; unavailable rows show `Unavailable`, with a sign-in command only for a signed-out account, or the missing CLI for an uninstalled agent.
Network and timeout detail stays in diagnostics.
The header's settings icon, named `Choose sidebar items`, opens Settings > Usage.
No usage state becomes a banner, toast or alert.
The countdown advances once a minute only while the popover or Usage tab is open; the redesign adds no polling or data collection.
With no history there is no grass section; available account rows remain, and with no visible account chips the footer's chart button opens the available history.

Full history opens Overview's global Usage tab, not a per-project ledger.
The table starts at the most recent thirty days, with seven-day, ninety-day and retained-year choices and an agent filter.
The retained year is the existing core projection's bound; Full history does not claim to expose an unlimited lifetime ledger.
Each recorded date has a summary row, followed by agent rows when expanded; today starts expanded.
Under the agent rows an expanded date has a `Hide AI` row with the same columns, itself expandable into one row per feature (agent summaries, Memory, Factory, issues and worktrees, other) with its call count and the providers that served it, and a `Calls with no reported usage` row that has a count and no amount.
The agent filter has a `Hide AI` choice that lists only the days Hide AI was counted on, with the feature rows directly under each date (no second Hide AI row); a date of only unknown calls is still a date, and its popover line reads `Calls with no reported usage` instead of an amount.
A footnote under the table says the amounts are lower bounds when any unknown call is in the period, and names the day Hide AI is counted from, since earlier days are not backfilled.
Choosing one agent hides Hide AI, which belongs to the all-agents range only.
The headline shows the selected period's estimated API cost, new tokens and separate cache reads above stacked daily new-token bars.
Only real agent and Hide AI contributions form each stack; there is no minimum fabricated usage height.
Left and Right move by day and Home and End reach the visible range's ends; selecting a day narrows the totals and detail, and Show full period clears it.
Changing period clears the selected day, while changing agent or daily/model view keeps a selected day still in range.
Columns are Date / Agent, estimated cost, new tokens, input, output, cache write, cache read and all tokens, with a leading selected-period total and an exact-number toggle.
The model view groups actual recorded date, agent, model and session-or-Hide-AI reports, never an allocation inferred from an agent total.
An absent model says Model not reported; an older history without model dimensions says those details are unavailable.
The newest 4,096 model records are retained in deterministic order with a visible limited marker when truncated; daily totals remain complete and model subtotals may then be incomplete.
Model shares use known new tokens, and Hide AI model reports may outnumber the backend attempts counted by its headline.
Year-view dates include the year, and changing the filter or page clears expanded rows.
At most thirty recorded dates are mounted per page, with agent rows only for expanded dates; an empty filter says there is no usage for the chosen agent and period.
The table scrolls sideways within its own region at narrow widths and large Interface text, never the whole page.
Account limits and agents without limit readings remain available in a collapsed Account limits section below the table.
Pi and omp show recorded provider provenance using known display names, account wording only for a recorded account provider, and no line for an unknown provider.
A bounded or partly unreadable source has the small limited marker in its own row and range.

The page sends `usage_window_visible` on every live connection and visibility change, and `usage_popover_open` while the popover or Usage tab is open.
Opening it requests a refresh only for reads older than one minute, subject to the existing backoff.
The core keeps one value of each hint, so with several pages open the last page to report decides.

## Help menu

Web owner: `web/src/components/help-menu.tsx`, `web/src/components/update-ready.tsx`, `diagnosticsInputOf` in `web/src/settings.ts`, `web/src/updates.ts`, `web/src/whatsNew.ts`; host owner of the card's close `desktop/src/main/updater.ts` (PRD sidebar-titlebar-refresh D-11, D-12; PRD auto-update D-03, D-21; the update card is the operator's 2026-11-06 decision).

The footer's `?` opens the Help menu above it: `Keyboard shortcuts` with its chord, which opens the shortcut sheet; `Practice Hide`, which opens the [Practice window](#practice-window) or brings the open one forward; in the desktop app `Update history` (`업데이트 기록`), which opens the [update history](#whats-new-and-update-history); `Copy diagnostics`, which puts About's diagnostics text on the clipboard and says `Copied` in place, or the failure when the clipboard refuses, keeping the menu open either way; `Report an issue`, which opens the repository's new-issue page in the default browser; then, under a separator, the `hide <version>` line in mono, which takes no press.
The version is the release the app was built as, such as `0.4.0`, and a development build's own version, such as `0.4.0-main.<commit>`.
When the desktop app has an update ready ([INSTALL.md](INSTALL.md#update)), it shows in three places, since macOS and Windows install it only when the app quits and an operator who keeps the app open would otherwise not learn of it:

- **The ↑ button.** A primary ↑ button heads the View, Help and Settings group, above them at the rail's foot or left of them in the sidebar footer while the rail is hidden, and stays until the restart, with no close; its name and tooltip are `hide <new version> ready` (`hide <버전> 준비됨`). When it appears a primary ring spreads out from it three times and stops, and a faint ring stays; with reduced motion only the faint ring shows.
- **The card.** The first time a version is ready, its card opens by itself beside the button (to its right from the rail, above it in the footer): `Update ready` (`업데이트 준비됨`) with a close ✕, `Downloaded hide <new version>.` (`hide <버전> 받아 놨어요.`), `Restarting keeps your agents and panes running.` (`재시작해도 에이전트와 터미널은 그대로예요.`), then `Restart now` (`지금 재시작`), `Later` (`나중에`) and `What's new` (`바뀐 점`), which opens the [update history](#whats-new-and-update-history) at that version when the releases file has it, and that version's release page on GitHub in the default browser when it does not.
  Opened by itself it takes no keyboard focus and stays through Escape and clicks elsewhere, so it never takes a key meant for a pane; ✕ or Later folds it into the button.
  The close is kept per version in the desktop profile, so the card does not open by itself again for that version, after a relaunch either, and opens once more when a newer version is ready; pressing the button opens it again as an ordinary popover, which Escape or a click elsewhere closes.
  A required update ([INSTALL.md](INSTALL.md#update): a release names a minimum version above this app) opens the card even after its close and keeps it open until the restart: `Required update` (`꼭 필요한 업데이트`) with no ✕, `hide <new version> fixes a problem in this version. Restart when you're ready.` (`hide <버전>에 지금 버전의 문제를 고친 수정이 들어 있어요. 편할 때 재시작해 주세요.`), then, in the foreground under a check, `Your work won't stop: agents and panes keep running while hide restarts.` (`하던 작업은 멈추지 않아요. 재시작하는 동안에도 에이전트와 터미널은 계속 돌아가요.`), and `Restart now` and `What's new` with no `Later`; the button's name and tooltip are `Required update · hide <new version>` (`꼭 필요한 업데이트 · hide <버전>`).
  It still takes no keyboard focus and stays through Escape and clicks elsewhere, and nothing restarts by itself: the operator restarts when their work allows.
- **Help.** A dot in the primary color sits on the `?`, whose name and tooltip become `Help · Update ready`, and the menu opens with the update: `hide <new version> is ready` (`hide <버전> 준비됐어요`), `Agents and panes keep running · What's new` and `Restart now`, above the usual items; its `What's new` does what the card's does; the last line stays this app's own `hide <version>`.

Every `Restart now` installs the update and reopens the app.
The restart sentence holds because the reopened app only replaces the daemon: each pane's holder outlives it and the new daemon adopts it, as long as a new build still speaks to the holders already running (`OLDEST_HOLDER_VERSION` in `hide-holder-protocol`; [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md#the-pane-holder)), and the package lane's update test checks the panes and their processes across a real Restart (`desktop/e2e/packaged-update.spec.ts`).
Nothing else of an update reaches the sidebar or this menu: looking, downloading and a failure show only in Settings > About.
With the sidebar hidden none of the three shows, as Help does not.
A browser tab and the phone have no button, card, dot or update line.

## What's new and Update history

Web owner: `web/src/components/whats-new.tsx` (the window, the history and the one decision a launch takes), `web/src/whatsNew.ts` (the host's state and the history open or closed), `web/src/releases.ts` (the releases file's shape, its parse, the version order and the offer); host owner `desktop/src/main/whatsNew.ts`; the file `web/src/releases.json` (PRD whats-new).

What each released version brought lives in one file built into the app, `web/src/releases.json`: the release adds its version there before the release candidate is built, after `scripts/check-releases-entry.sh <version>` has checked the entry on the release commit (its shape, its languages, the drawings and chapters that commit has, and that it is the version being released), so a version's words ship with that version and change only with a new build.
The file lists stable versions newest first; each has a date, at most three main features (a title and one line, a drawing by name, and the practice chapter that teaches it, or none) and its changes and fixes, in every interface language, English where a language is missing.
The desktop app reads it when it starts, with no network; a wrong version is left out alone, and the history keeps the newest hundred versions.
A profile it cannot write is an event in the host's log, and nothing on screen says so.
It keeps a record of the window and the history apart: the last version whose window closed, and which versions were opened in the history (operator decision 2026-11-20); a value past the version the app runs is refused.
A launch on a profile no earlier launch wrote to (neither the window's kept bounds nor the `hide` command it found) is a new install, and its version is recorded as shown at once; a first launch quit before either was written reads as new again, which records the same version again.

**The window after an update.**
On the first launch of a stable version whose entry the file has, once the core's first snapshot has arrived, a centered dialog opens over the dimmed shell: `What's new` (`새로 생긴 것`) with the version beside it in mono, a large drawing of the chosen feature, the features numbered (each a row with its title and line), a line counting the changes and fixes (`2 changes · 3 fixes`, `바뀐 것 2 · 고친 것 3`, leaving out a kind with none) with `See more` (`더 보기`) that opens their list in place, each with a `Changed` (`바뀜`) or `Fixed` (`고침`) badge, then `Update history` (`업데이트 기록`) at the footer's left and `OK` (`확인`) at its right.
A feature's drawing is its own scene when it has one (`web/src/components/whats-new-scenes/`): its real screen at its real size, from that screen's own parts and words, playing example work from the first press to the result, such as the automation scene, where a starting point becomes an automation, the night passes, the run's terminal reads the issues and the run ends with its ✓, the sidebar's count and the corner notice.
A feature without one is drawn by a practice card's move.
The drawing moves to the next feature after one loop of its own, 6 seconds for a practice move and the scene's own length for a scene; pressing a feature shows it and stops the moving; with reduced motion a practice move holds the middle of its move and a scene its last moment.
A feature a release names among its main features brings its scene, its practice chapter and its measure, the usage-statistics event its highlight names as `measure`, in its own pull request, so all three are in the build the release cuts ([CONTRIBUTING.md](../CONTRIBUTING.md#a-main-feature-brings-its-practice-its-scene-and-its-measure)).
A feature whose chapter is in the practice carries `Try it` (`체험하기`) in the practice's amber; it closes the window and opens the [Practice window](#practice-window) with that chapter's card open on the list, where `시작` or ⏎ plays it.
A feature no chapter teaches whose highlight names a Settings page (`settings`, such as `skills`) carries `Open in Settings` (`설정에서 보기`) as a secondary button instead; it closes the window and opens Settings on that page, and the feature's line says it can also be asked of an agent where an agent's command does it (operator decision 2026-11-21).
A drawing or chapter the app does not have is left out of the feature, which keeps its words, and noted in the diagnostics.
`OK`, ✕, Escape and a click outside close it; the keyboard starts on `OK`.
Once closed, that version's window never opens again.
After skipping versions (1.2.8 to 1.2.11), the window shows the main features of every version since the last one whose window closed, newest first and at most three, each with its version beside its title, and `1.2.8 → 1.2.11` beside the title; it counts and lists all their changes and fixes.
Nothing opens, and the version is recorded as shown, when the versions since have no main feature (a release of fixes only), or on a new install, where the first-run wizard introduces Hide; an app updated from a version before this window offers only the version it updated to.
A preview release (`-rc.N`) and a development build never open it, and nothing opens while an update is only downloaded: the window is decided once the new version runs.

**The update history.**
The Help menu's `Update history`, Settings › About's `Update history` beside the update line, and the window's `Update history` open it as a large dialog: the versions newest first on the left under `Update history`, each with its version in mono, its date, `<n> new features` (`새 기능 n`) or its change and fix count, a large dot for a version with main features and a small one for a version without; on the right the chosen version, its date and `The version you use` (`지금 쓰는 버전`) on the running one, a card per main feature (its drawing playing, title, line and `Try it` where a chapter teaches it, else `Open in Settings` where it names a Settings page), `Changes and fixes` (`바뀐 것 · 고친 것`) with their badges, and `Full notes on GitHub` (`GitHub에서 전체 노트`), which opens that version's release page.
It opens on the version in use; ↑ and ↓ move between versions.
A version not yet opened in the history carries `NEW`, which goes from that version alone when it is opened; after an update, the versions before the first one recorded count as opened.
It lists versions up to the one in use (below it in a preview build, every one in a development build); a downloaded update's `What's new`, on the card and in Help, opens that version's release page on GitHub, since the app carries a version's words only from that version on.
When the file has no version up to the one in use it shows only `No notes for this version yet` (`이 버전의 안내가 아직 없어요`) with `View on GitHub` (`GitHub에서 보기`).
A browser tab and the phone have neither the window nor the history.

## Importing a Herdr session into Hide

Core owner: `hide-core/src/runtime/herdr_import.rs` (`status.herdr_import`, `status.herdr_import_devices`, the `herdr_import` event), `hided/src/herdr_transition.rs` (what is found and what an import does on this machine), `hide-core/src/herdr_import/device.rs` (on a device). Web owner: `web/src/HerdrImport.tsx`, `web/src/herdrImportRules.ts`, `web/src/i18n/resources/herdrImport.ts`.

PRD engine-bundle-4-herdr-removal D-7, D-8, D-10, B11, B14 to B18; [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md#importing-a-herdr-session-into-the-cores-own-engine) owns what an import does.
Hide never stops or changes a Herdr, including one an earlier Hide started: its saved work is offered read only.
A save no Hide Herdr runs is offered read only: the sidebar's row says `Herdr has <n> tabs` with `Import`, adding `Conversations open in Herdr open here as shells` while that Herdr runs, and Settings > General shows `Import Herdr work` with the same note and an `Import` button.
A save Hide could not read shows `Couldn't read the Herdr session` with the reason under it and `Try again`; once an import has left workspaces out, the row lists each as `<workspace>: <reason>` with `OK`, which clears it.
Conversations an import left open in a Herdr that has closed since show `Herdr closed: <n> conversations can resume here` with `Resume`, which resumes each in its pane, or in a new tab of the same workspace, named for the agent, when that pane runs something else.
A dialed device's save is offered the same way, under its name, once its link is up: its sidebar row starts with the device's name, and Settings names it in `Import Herdr work from <device>`.
With nothing to import or read, no row or Settings group shows.
`hide import herdr` prints what would be imported and imports only with `--yes`.

## Practice window

Web owner: `web/src/practice/` (`PracticeApp.tsx` the chapter list, `PracticePreview.tsx` its cards' drawings, `PracticePlayer.tsx` a chapter playing, `PracticeCoach.tsx` the guide, `chapters/` the chapters, `worlds/` what they play in, `channel.ts` what the window and the shell say to each other), `web/src/i18n/resources/practice.ts` and `web/src/i18n/resources/practice/`. Desktop owner: `desktop/src/main/practice.ts`, `desktop/src/main/host.ts` (`listenPractice`, `openPractice`). Core owner: `ui_state.practice` and `practice_progress_set`.

The practice window teaches Hide on a practice screen: the shell's own sidebar, panes, lane and keyboard, drawn over a scripted world with no agent, project, file or engine behind it.
`Practice Hide` in the Help menu and the line under Add project on the Home with no project open it on the chapter list; the first run's `Try it on a practice screen first` and the sidebar's invitation open it on the mission flow (flow 3); a feature's `Try it` in the [window after an update or the update history](#whats-new-and-update-history) opens it on the chapter list with that chapter's card open, and tells an open one to show that card; the desktop app opens one window of its own, `Hide 연습` by name, 85% of the main window's size and against its lower right, so the main window's title row and sidebar still show beside it and the practice is never taken for Hide itself, but never narrower than 1280 px where the screen has the room, because a narrower practice screen draws the lane over the agents it should sit beside (`PRACTICE_MIN_WIDTH` in `desktop/src/main/practice.ts`), and a browser tab opens one tab of its own; asking again brings the open one forward, and an ask for a chapter plays that chapter there from its first step.
A new window takes the chapter from its address (`chapter=<id>`); one already open is told over the channel below (`play`), the same way in the desktop app and a browser tab on every system.

An operator who never practiced (the core keeps no chapter's progress) and never closed it sees the sidebar's invitation, whether or not a project is registered: one quiet row above the footer, like a 태스크 row, with an amber graduation cap, `Practice Hide` and under it `Start with handing over a mission`, and a × at its right, with no progress bar and no count.
Pressing it opens the practice window on the mission flow (flow 3); the first step a chapter reaches writes its progress, so opening any chapter, from anywhere, takes the row away, and opening the window on its list does not.
Its × says in its hint that practice stays in Help (?) > Practice Hide, and sends `practice_invite_close`, which the core keeps as `ui_state.practice_invite_closed` like the other UI choices, and the row never shows again; the Help menu and the empty Home's line stay.
The practice screen's own sidebar never shows it.
It is not a ⌘K row, because ⌘K goes to things and runs no command but two ([Search keyboard navigation](#search-keyboard-navigation)).

The window wears `Hide 연습` as its title while it lists and while it plays, and `Hide로 돌아가기` is always on it: at the right of the list's heading, and at the right of the top center's chapter name while a chapter plays.
It closes the practice window, and the desktop app brings the main window forward, as it does whenever the practice window closes; a browser tab the shell opened closes back to the shell's tab.
The list's lead says that no real agent, project or file is touched and that the learner may pick only the chapters they need, and a smaller line under it that Help (?) > Practice Hide opens it again any time.
It opens on the chapter list, a grid of cards that come in one after another (at once where the system asks for reduced motion).
Each card is one press and shows a small drawing of the shell playing the one move its chapter teaches (`PracticePreview.tsx`), then the chapter's number, its state (`새로`, or `새로 · <version>` for a chapter a release named among its main features (`since` on the chapter), `<n> / <m>에서 멈춤`, `✓ 봄`, or `바뀜` when the chapter's steps changed since it was played), its title and one line of what it covers.
The drawing is shapes only, never a name, a number or an agent's words; every card's plays, they hold still while a card is open, and with reduced motion each holds the middle of its move.
Pressing a card opens it as a dialog: the drawing playing, the chapter's number and step count, its title and line, `해 볼 것` with the tasks its quest card lists and each one's keys on this host with the operator's bindings (a look-only chapter lists none), and one button in the practice's amber, `시작`, `이어서` or `다시 보기`, with `처음부터` beside `이어서`; the button holds the keyboard, so ⏎ plays the chapter, and Esc closes the dialog back to its card.
The arrow keys choose a card, ⏎ opens it, and the first chapter not yet seen is chosen at first.
`이어서` plays the steps before the stopping point again on its own, quickly, the way ⏎ would, so the screen reaches the state that step needs.
A chapter seen once stays seen when it is played again.
Every start of a chapter, from the list, from `처음부터`, from `이전` over a step that changed the screen, or the next chapter from its end card, begins on the shell screen as the page first loaded it: an overlay, a focus, a relationship move still waiting or a copy left by the run before never carries into it (`freshShell` in `PracticeApp.tsx`).

A chapter playing dims everything but what its step marks, rings that, and puts a light bubble beside it with what happens and how: the command's chord as keycaps (`Or press ⌘D`), `Press the button, or ⇧⌘↩` for a step with a button, or `Press where it's marked` where this host has no chord for the command (⌥1 in a browser tab).
⏎ performs the step in the learner's place, pressing the chord, the button, dragging the pane with a hand cursor, typing what the learner would say into an agent's prompt and sending it (words the learner wrote there themselves are sent as they are, for the world to judge; the example is typed only into an empty prompt), or right-clicking, or pressing a control such as the pane head's `@`, and choosing from the shell's own menu or list with the hand; a held chord (⌃⇥, ⌥⇥) is let go a moment later, so the shell's own switcher is seen before it lands.
The bubble's button does what ⏎ does, because a press on the guide's bubble, task card or end card is no press on the practice screen.
It leaves the keyboard in the pane that held it, so a chord that acts where the keyboard is (⌘W closes that pane, ⌃⇥ starts from it) acts there, and so does the learner's own key afterwards; and it closes no popover or menu the screen has open, so a step that points into one still finds it.
What ⏎ does is played at a pace a learner can follow, one pace for every move of it (`PACE` in `PracticeCoach.tsx`): the hand points at what it presses, drags or opens before it acts, and a step it performed stays about two seconds once done, so its result is seen before the next bubble; ⏎ in that moment moves on at once.
A step the learner does by their own key, click or drag moves on as soon as it is done, and the steps the coach replays on its own (`이어서`, `이전`) go at the quick pace, because they only bring the screen back to where it was.
→ moves on from a step that only looks; on a step that waits for something to happen, only ⏎ or the learner's own key, click or drag moves on.
⏎ never presses again a control the step's press already opened while the step waits for what shows in it (flow 3's agent list on the pane head, before the next child has started), because a second press would close it.
Every step past the first offers `이전`, and ← does the same on a step that only looks: it goes back to the step before as that step began.
Over a look that left the world as it was, only the bubble moves back; over a step that changed the screen, or after the learner changed something, the chapter starts over and plays itself to that step the way `이어서` does, because a chapter can be started over from any of its steps.
The bubble's lower left carries `나가기` on every step, a step that does not move on included, and it goes back to Hide as `Hide로 돌아가기` does; the step reached is kept, so the list offers `이어서` from there next time, and the top center's `← 장 목록` is the way to the list.
The window never reads or writes the operator's clipboard: a copy made there, a menu's Copy name and ID or the page's own ⌘C, goes to the practice world, and a paste, a menu's Paste or ⌘V, takes the last copy made there (nothing before the first).
Nothing on the practice screen leaves it: a link, a window the shell would open in the system's browser (a PR card's `GitHub에서 보기`), a page navigation and a call on the desktop host (open in another app, reveal in the file manager, a notification) are held where the page meets the browser and told to the practice world (`practice/outside.ts`), and the desktop app refuses whatever gets past; a browser without the Navigation API holds all of them but a navigation the shell starts by script.
The learner can also do it themselves, by key, click or drag: a step that changes the screen is done when the world says so, however it was done.
⌘K search and ⌘P Open file open over the practice screen as they do in the shell.
While one is open, or a held chord's switcher is up, everything the guide points with steps aside (the dim, the area tags, the ring, the pressed chord, the `예시` marks and the hand), and its bubble moves beside the palette without buttons; the bubble also moves beside a right-click menu it would cover.
Every key is then the palette's but ⏎ where the guide types into the palette's field: it keeps ⏎ while the field of a step that types there is empty, to type the words, and while it is still typing them, so ⏎ opens no row on half the words ([Adding a chapter](#adding-a-chapter)); ⏎, → and ← go back to the guide when the palette closes.
A pressed chord shows large in the middle for a moment, so the keys are seen as they are used.
Every chord is the operator's own: the host's set, the system's, and the operator's stored bindings.
A chapter with tasks lists them on a card in the lower left with a check as each is done and `↺ 처음부터`; the top center names the chapter and leads back to the list.
The last step's button is `마치기`, and past it the chapter stops on an end card in the middle of the dimmed practice screen, in place of the bubble and the task card: `<n>편 완료` with the chapter's title, the tasks it taught with their chords as the task card listed them (a chapter without tasks lists none), the next chapter's number and title, and `↺ 다시`, `장 목록으로` and `<n>편으로`; ⏎ is the next chapter, and on the last chapter, which has none, the card offers only `↺ 다시` and `장 목록으로`, which ⏎ presses.
The mission flow (flow 3, 크고 긴 일 맡기기) is where the first run and the sidebar's invitation land, because a mission is the work Hide is for and a newcomer should meet it before the parts of the screen; it does not lead on into flow 4 as if the course were the way in (`next` on the chapter names the flow its end card leads to, here the first): its last step says what was decided is now a memory and to hand a mission to the learner's own project next, and its end card asks `어떻게 할까요?` in place of the summary, with three ways drawn alike: `바로 써 보기` (`연습을 닫고 Hide로 돌아가요`) closes the practice as `Hide로 돌아가기` does, `흐름 1 이어서` with flow 1's title plays it and carries the ⏎ mark, since ⏎ still plays it, and `필요한 것만 고르기` (`흐름 목록에서 골라요`) goes to the list; under them the list's line says the Help menu opens the practice again, beside `↺ 다시` (`ChoiceCard` in `PracticeCoach.tsx`).
Flow 1 (한눈에 보기) points at the three parts of the screen on a window with work in them, the third being the lane together with the tool rail beside it that picks what the lane shows: the window opens with the lane closed, so the three-part steps ring the tool rail alone, and the lane step is the learner pressing the rail's Project tool to open it (nothing has asked for a tool before that); the sidebar's lenses are pressed through, the PR tab among them, which the step says in one line; the search box opens ⌘K, whose palette then holds the keyboard until the learner closes it (⏎ on a row or Esc): the search step is done by the palette's first search but is over only once the palette has closed again (`afterClose`), and while it is open the bubble stays on that step with one line, type a name or #number, ⏎ opens it, Esc closes, never the next step's card; the tour ends on an ordinary end card after the Project tool's step.
The `find` chapter (찾고 다시 보기, kept until the Overview flow takes it over) finds yesterday's ended session in ⌘K by a word of its requests, opens its record as a card tab on the desk, finds the PR it made by `#3` and opens that card, and puts a finished task in 다시 볼 것 from its right-click menu; any word of the session finds it, the record's `세션 복구하기` brings the session back in a tab of its own, and the invented PR wears a `예시` mark so it is never taken for a real one.

Flow 2 (에이전트 여러 개 돌리기, `chapters/agents.ts`) runs a lead agent, a docs agent in a tab of its own and, on the learner's words, a reviewer, and teaches who is waiting on whom: ⌘D puts a second terminal beside the lead; any words said to the lead start a reviewer, an agent of its own in a tab of its own (the lead starts it for the operator, not under itself); a while later the docs agent finishes and goes to Unread, quietly, while the reviewer asks whether to run the tests and goes to Needs You, and the step says what Needs You holds: a question or an approval, a file an agent opened for the operator to look at, and a helper's ask its lead passes up, and that finished work goes to Unread instead; pressing the reviewer's row opens it (read, it leaves Needs You and waits first in the 태스크 list's 쉬는 중), the answer is typed in its terminal, it runs the tests and finishes by opening its notes with `hide file open`, which puts its row in Needs You again marked `볼 것` ([A View to look at](status-model.md#a-view-to-look-at)), and its row keeps the mark through the close and reopen below until the learner presses its chip.
The reviewer, finished, is closed with ⌘W (no sheet, since it is not working) and brought back with Reopen closed tab where it stood and continuing its conversation; its world answers `pair_view`, `close_pane` and `reopen_closed` and draws `recent_closed` the way the core does. ⌃⇥ then goes back to the lead; pressing the file's chip under the reviewer's row, still marked `볼 것`, opens the file in focus mode beside the reviewer and takes the mark away, the row itself only going to the agent (operator decision 2026-11-23); and the Explorer, shown with ⇧⌘E, marks the files the lead changed, new or changed.
The agents' answers are the world's script, drawn in the panes and the list; the learner only types the requests and presses the rows. The practice screen mounts the shell's close sheet, so a learner's close of a working agent in any flow asks as it would in Hide.

Flow 3 carries one job from the learner's request to the report, as a mission, in the order an operator meets it and at the pace an agent works, in fourteen steps: its world ends the Conductor's turns on its own (`PracticeWorld.later`), the Conductor's lines arrive one by one, and a step that waits on such a turn (`wait`) says so on its button and does nothing on ⏎; once the turn ends the step stays, its button turned to `다음`, so what the turn brought is seen before the bubble moves, and ⏎ moves on (a step the coach replays on its own moves on at once).
The first step already has the learner state a situation with `/hide-mission` and ask for the plan first, with no separate introduction; it opens on its scene: for a moment the practice screen shows alone, with no dim, ring, bubble or task card, and then the guide comes in, or at once on ⏎ (`scene` on the step), because the first screen a newcomer meets should be seen before a bubble covers it; the Conductor reads and plans for a few seconds, then the plan stands as a mission `planning` with every part `planned`, and the Conductor asks in conversation whether to start, which reaches its row and pane head as an ordinary question; the pane's mission chip opens the whole plan as a card tab on its tab's desk (`detail_open`), and the first plan opens the chip's popover by itself under the shell's own rule ([A mission on its Conductor's pane](#tasks-card-tabs-and-the-pr-list)).
The job is moving sign-in to email links in six parts: a survey first, then the token table and the link API on it, the sign-in screen and the mail template beside them, and one end-to-end check last, with at most two workers at a time.
The learner approves in the terminal, in words, because Hide has no button that starts a mission; the Conductor starts each part in a delegated tab under it once the parts it waits on are done and a worker is free, and the map turns to running; the pane head's second row, the Conductor's child agents, lists who it started and what each is doing and fills as the next parts start.
A while later the token table is a pull request and the Conductor asks one decision, whether to keep password sign-in, which stands in `missions` the way the core keeps it, with its choices and the one it recommends; the sidebar starts on its 태스크 list, so the Conductor's row moves into 내 차례, and pressing it opens the Conductor, whose mission chip opens the map; the learner answers on the map's decision banner: a choice marked and sent with `답하기`, or `직접 답하기…`, reaches the world as the core's `mission_answer`, and words said to the Conductor answer it too; either changes the plan, and dropping password sign-in adds a part that takes it out.
The rest runs by itself (a `wait` step) until the last worker is done and the Conductor reports, and the card's `⚙` is pointed at and read, never opened here: it is Settings › 미션, where a new mission's default scope (stop before main, or through the merge) is chosen and, as a link, the favorite models in Settings › Agents (agent, model, effort, purpose), and a change applies from the next mission, except a model added or changed, which a running mission can use at once; then the card's `✕` puts it away, the pane as it was (a step already done when the learner closed it before); ⇧⌘L (`open_project_memory`) opens Hide AI on its Memory tab, where the decision appears as the newest memory only once the work is done, from the operator's own words, and the last bubble points at that card and says what Memory is: when work ends Hide AI reads the conversation and keeps what was decided, the rules and the lessons as cards, and hands an agent that works there later the cards that fit; the rail icon is not pressed, because it opens Hide AI on its 제안 tab.
This flow's world speaks the learner's language (`practice.mission.world.*`, the Conductor's conversation, the plan, the decision and the memories), and its typed example is a catalog key (`words`), since it is the first flow a newcomer meets; the other flows' worlds stay in English.

Flow 6 (매일 하는 일은 자동화에 맡기기, `chapters/automation.ts`, new in 1.2.11) has five steps: the sidebar's `자동화` row opens the automation screen, empty and welcoming; `아침 브리핑`, the second starting point, opens the form filled in; `만들기` makes the automation and opens its detail with its next run; the world then moves through the night by itself (a `wait` step, about four seconds: the run starts at its time and its card says `도는 중`, then its agent opens a briefing and the run ends); and the run's row, now in 내 차례 marked `볼 것` with `↻ 자동화: <name>`, is pressed to open the run, with its terminal on the left and the briefing on the run's desk on the right; the step says that 내 차례 only gets a run with something to look at or answer (B15), and that a run that just finishes stays quietly in 자동화 as an unseen count.
Its last step's text also says an agent can make an automation when asked in words.
The world (`worlds/automation.ts`) answers the form's `automation_call` itself (the schedule read back with its next time, the make, the edit), `automation_seen`, `automation_run_viewing`, `automation_run_result_open` and `focus_pane` with `reveal_unseen` as the core does (the run's desk leads with its summary card, then the briefing the agent opened, which a press on its `결과` row also brings in front), and keeps the run's folder as an `unlisted` workspace whose tab is an automation's; the scheduled run is drawn at its schedule's time on the day the learner's own clock is on, so it reads as `오늘 09:00`.
The practice player draws the automation screen for the sidebar's row only in a world that sends the section (`WorldSnapshot.automations`), routes `automation_call` to `PracticeWorld.call`, and puts back the call route it replaced when it closes; a step's `enter` event tells the world the step came up, which starts the night when the learner reaches it rather than on a clock.

The window holds no daemon connection and carries no token.
It asks the shell of the same origin, over one `BroadcastChannel` (`hide-practice`), for the operator's language, chords, theme and progress, and hands each step it reaches back; the shell dispatches `practice_progress_set`, and the core keeps `ui_state.practice` like the other UI choices.
With no shell answering, the chapters still play and the list says `진행 기록은 Hide 창이 열려 있을 때 저장돼요.`.
In the desktop app the window has no preload: it stands in for the desktop host from its address (`host=electron&platform=…`), so its keyboard runs the desktop chords, and a menu command while it holds the keyboard is dropped rather than sent to the main window's shell; it closes with the main window.

The agent step ends on whichever agent the switcher stopped on and the project step on the other project, and a palette closed with Esc comes back with ⏎.
The `project` chapter, the project at a glance (`chapters/project.ts`, kept until the Overview flow takes it over), starts on the sidebar's pin: its world lists a second project, the pin waits for the pointer at the start of a project row's buttons, and pressing it (`workspace_pin_set`) moves that project to the top of the list with a line under the pinned ones, as the core lists them; it then opens the right lane beside an agent at work with the Project tool's rail icon (`toggle_lane_project`), so ⏎ reaches the Project tool even after the learner showed another one, and reads its count line and its 최근 7일, whose invented cost wears the `예시` mark; ⇧⌘E (`toggle_lane_explorer`) shows the files the agent changed, ⇧⌘L (`open_project_memory`) Hide AI on its Memory tab with the project's Memory cards (its rail icon would open the 제안 tab), and ⌘E puts the lane away.
A tool step ends when the lane shows that tool, whether by its chord or its rail icon, and the closing step when the lane is no longer drawn, by ⌘E or by the tool's ×; the world answers the pin too, and nothing opens from a tile, a row or a file, which the bubbles say.

Flow 8 (봇, `chapters/bots.ts`, since 1.2.12) shows an AI outside Hide becoming a bot the learner made.
The learner opens Settings (`settings`) and its 봇 page, presses 봇 만들기, types the name `nightly` into the make dialog (a step that types into a dialog's field names the button that opens it again, `PracticeTyping.open`, so ⏎ brings back a dialog Esc closed), reads the two modes and makes the bot; the world answers `access_call` as the core does, with an invented code, and the learner copies the sentence for the AI and closes it.
A practice screen cannot run an outside AI, so its world stages the join: a few seconds after the learner starts waiting (a `wait` step that tells the world it began), the bot leaves 연결 기다리는 봇 and is listed in the mode picked, beside `hermes`, already connected as 전체 with the person in its owl's corner.
Settings closed, the rail shows `nightly` in its own space and no tile for `hermes`, and a few seconds after the join (a `wait` step) the 전체 bot's agent asks, its row wearing `hermes`'s owl on its logo and its hint ending `hermes(봇)이 띄움`; refusing, disconnecting and a mode change are not answered, so the chapter keeps both bots.
Settings opens over every practice screen as over Hide (`SettingsGate` in `PracticePlayer.tsx`), each world answering only the events its chapter needs, and the guide keeps ⏎, → and ← while it is up, since it is a page the guide walks through rather than a palette that takes the keyboard.

Flow 10 (GitLab, `chapters/gitlab.ts`, since 1.2.12) shows a gitlab.com project read beside a GitHub one.
Its world lists `shop-api`, read by gitlab.com, and `web`, read by GitHub, each checkout carrying the host that read it and each pull request the reference its host made, so the screens draw them as they draw a real read (Code hosts).
The learner opens the PR list (`open_sidebar_prs` or the list switch's PR), reads `!12` with GitLab's mark beside `#41` with GitHub's, and opens `!12`; the world answers `pr_open` as the core does, and the card names GitLab, its failed pipeline and `GitLab에서 보기`, with no merge or 맡기기 because gitlab.com does not declare them.
Settings (`settings`) then opens on 원격 저장소, whose GitLab row stands below GitHub's because a gitlab.com project exists; every address in the world is an example.
Flow 11 (폰에서 답하기, `chapters/answer.ts`, since 1.2.12) shows an agent's question answered from the phone.
A practice screen has no phone and no relay, so the chapter's world draws one beside the screen (`PracticeWorld.phone`, `PracticePhone.tsx`), standing at the window's foot and open on the asking agent's conversation, from the phone's own parts: its message rows, its activity line and its ask card (`AskCardView`), which the phone's detail draws too.
The learner sees the agent's row in 내 차례, the same question on the phone with what the agent wrote under each choice, marks `Split into two PRs` (`practice_phone_pick`, which only marks it), and sends it with 답하기 (`practice_phone_answer`); the agent's own screen moves its highlight to that row, and a moment later (a `wait` step) takes Enter, leaves 내 차례 and runs a command, which the phone says by the clock under its conversation.

Flow 12 (폰에서 파일 보내기, `chapters/attachments.ts`, since 1.2.12) shows files sent from the phone with a reply, on the same phone drawn beside the screen, open on an agent that reported a memory measurement.
Its reply bar is the phone's own (`ComposerFiles`, `FileNoticeLine`, `Composer` with the `+` at its start), and the files over a sent bubble are `MessageItem`'s.
A practice screen has no picker, so the `+` (`practice_phone_attach`) stands in for one: it attaches a screenshot, drawn in the theme's colors (`web/src/components/sample-shot.ts`), and a log, and leaves out a 14 MB trace with its one line; a file's `×` (`practice_phone_remove`) takes only it off.
The log uploads for a moment with its band (a `wait` step the world ends with `practice_upload_done`), the learner sends (`practice_phone_send`), the files stand over the bubble, and the agent's own screen takes the files' paths before the words, reads them, and a moment later answers (a `wait` step).

Flow 7 (집중 모드, `chapters/focus.ts`, new in 1.2.11 and the chapter the What's new window's focus mode feature opens with `체험하기`) is five steps on a project with two agents side by side in one tab, one of them building a small page and having opened it as a View on its desk: `집중 모드 켜기` (the ◧ on that agent's pane head, which only an agent with a View wears, or ⌘., the `pair_view` command) draws its terminal on the left under the band and the page beside it; a look at the band, which says the mode is on and that the page the agent opened comes up beside it by itself; a step that waits about three seconds after the mode was first entered, in which the agent changes the page's heading and the page beside the terminal changes with it (`PracticeWorld.later`, whether or not the learner is still in the mode); `옆 화면 숨기기`, the desk's `✕` or ⌘E, which folds the desk so the terminal takes the width, ⌘E again standing it; and `집중 모드 나가기` (the band's `원래 화면으로` or ⌘. again), which puts both agents back side by side.
Its world (`worlds/focus.ts`) answers `pair_view`, `pair_view_desk` and a pane chosen while the mode is on as the core does and publishes what it publishes (`pair_mode`, `tray.pair` with `alone`, `shown` and `folded`, `panes_with_views`): the other agent has no View, so it is drawn alone, and a page closed from the desk leaves the pane alone with the mode on.
The page is a Markdown file the Editor draws, since a browser page is a native view the desktop host lays over the screen and a practice screen has none; its heading is what changes.

### Adding a chapter

A chapter is three new files and no change to a shared one, so chapters written side by side do not meet in a file.

- `web/src/practice/chapters/<id>.ts` default-exports the chapter (`chapter()` in `chapter.ts`): its `id` (the file's name and its catalog prefix), a `number`, a `version`, the `world` it plays in, the `preview` its card plays on the list, and its steps.
  A chapter picks one of the drawings `PracticePreview.tsx` has; a move none of them shows is a new drawing there, which is also named in `RELEASE_DRAWINGS` (`web/src/releases.ts`), since a release's feature can be drawn by a card's move.
  `chapters.ts` lists every file there by number and refuses a file whose chapter has another id or a number another chapter has; the next chapter is the next number there is, so a gap is allowed.
- `web/src/i18n/resources/practice/<id>.ts` default-exports the chapter's words in every language, under `practice.<id>.title`, `practice.<id>.summary` and `practice.<id>.<step>.kick|title|text|quest`.
  `web/src/practice/words.ts` joins every file there when the chapters load, refusing a key outside its file's chapter, and adds them to the window's words; they stay out of the shared catalog (`practice.ts` keeps only the guide's own words), because the desktop host bundles that catalog without Vite, and the catalog and copy checks read them beside it.
- `web/src/practice/worlds/<id>.ts`, when no world there fits: the world the chapter plays in.

A card's text puts its few key words in bold between `**` marks (`Emphasis` in `PracticeCoach.tsx`).
A step names what it rings (`target`, a selector into the shell), where the bubble sits (`side`), the command it teaches (`command`, never a chord), a `gesture` when ⏎ should press a button, drag a pane, type into a pane's terminal (`type`), choose from a right-click menu (`menu`) or press a control and choose from the list it opens (`pick`, the pane head's `@`) instead of pressing the chord, and `done`, which reads the world, or `shown`, a selector whose appearance finishes a step that opens a card, a popover or a page with no core event behind it; a step with neither is a look.
A step may open on its `scene`, a number of milliseconds the screen plays alone before the guide (dim, ring, bubble and task card) comes in, for a first screen that is the point; ⏎ brings the guide in at once, and a step the coach replays has no scene.
A world (`web/src/practice/world.ts`) gives the first state, answers the core events the chapter's steps dispatch as the core would (an event it does not need answers nothing), and draws the snapshot, what the core sends beside it (`frames`: the ⌘P index's answer, the open documents, ⌘K's session search, a record card tab's record and link record, the Explorer's folder listings and Git changes, and the Memory tool's Project with the Hide AI tool's 제안 section it is drawn under (`hideAiOn` in `worlds/kit.ts`), each empty when a world sends none), and each pane's screen.
What is typed into a terminal reaches the world as the terminal's `key` events and a copy as `practice_copy`; a pane whose screen the world changes is cleared and drawn again, so an agent's answer appears as the world writes it.
A world whose chapter types into a pane keeps that pane's unsent words (`prompt`), which the coach reads to send the learner's own words rather than its example.
A `type` gesture into a pane may give its words as a catalog key (`words`) in place of `text`, read when the coach types them, so the example is in the learner's language.
A `type` gesture can name a field (a palette's search box) instead of a pane: ⏎ types the words in letter by letter, the coach keeping ⏎ while the palette is open until they are in, and a field the step's `command` opens is opened by the first ⏎; a field the learner typed into on that step keeps their words, which the palette answers as they are typed.
A step done inside a palette names the command that opens it and, when the palette forgets what was typed, the words to type, so ⏎ alone reopens a palette Esc closed: it opens, then types into the empty field, then is the palette's own ⏎ once the field holds words.
A world names in `examples` what it invented that could pass for a real record (a pull request's title), and the coach marks each `예시` just past the first words it shows (a row's title, a card's heading), never past its own right edge, so the mark covers nothing beside it; the shell's own components stay as they are.
`worlds/kit.ts` holds what several worlds build with: a checkout's desk of tabs, panes and groups and the events that change it (`applyDesk`), agent rows (`agent`, with a parent named as the core names it), and the snapshot of one or more projects' desks (`deskSnapshot`), which leaves a tab of only delegated children off the strip and lists each pane's, tab's and desk's close consequence as the core does.
A tool only one chapter needs stays in that chapter's world file, and moves to `kit.ts` when a second chapter needs it.
A world's data is the practice window's own, invented for it: no world imports the gallery's fixtures.
`worlds/tour.ts` plays on `data/tour.json`, a whole snapshot kept with each time written as how long before now it was (`$msAgo`, `$sAgo`, `$isoAgo`, `$daysAgo`).
Bump a chapter's `version` when its steps change, so a learner's old progress reads as `바뀜`.
`web/e2e/practice.spec.ts` plays every chapter to its end with ⏎ alone, so a new chapter is played there without a change to the spec; a step whose ⏎ moves nothing stops it.
`web/src/practice/worlds.test.ts` sends every chapter's world, at each step the shell's commands and pane focus can reach, every command the keyboard runs and every pane focus, and fails when the world, its snapshot or a pane's screen throws; a step only a drag, typing or a palette reaches is checked from the state before it.

## Search keyboard navigation

Web owner: `web/src/SearchPalette.tsx` (⌘K), `web/src/search.ts`, `web/src/relations.ts`, `web/src/searchDetail.ts`, `web/src/searchGithub.ts`, `web/src/Palette.tsx` (⌘P and the diff palette), `web/src/components/sidebar-header.tsx`; core owner for the GitHub search: `hide-core/src/runtime/issues.rs`.

⌘K goes to things; it runs no command except `에이전트 시작…` and `브라우저에서 URL 열기`.
On the web, the sidebar's Search row, which shows `Search` and the bound chord, opens the same palette Command+K opens, and the query row carries an `Esc` keycap.
Its own wide layout is a list on the left and the highlighted row's detail on the right, one and a half times the width ⌘P, the shortcut sheet and the start panel share; a dialog under 800 px, too narrow for both, draws the list alone.

### What a query finds

A nonempty query also searches saved session goals, goal flow names and original requests in a separate `세션` group, including ended sessions within Copied history.
Enter on that group opens the record in Tools; the existing Agents group still opens the live agent.
With summaries Off it searches saved raw requests only, without generated goal or flow matches.
The core's bounded local record search runs independently of other groups, drops an answer for an older query and makes no GitHub request while typing.
The session group carries its current loading or failure state; a successful search with no session matches draws no group, preserving the palette's existing no-match state when no other kind matches.
The creator-session action in an output panel opens the same record.

A query finds an agent, project, checkout, device, issue or pull request by name, and an issue or pull request by `#number` (`#273`, or `273`); an exact number match lists first, as its own row, issue and pull request separately.
After them come the agents the core links to a pull request of that number (its session made it or its branch has it; every one the core links, a merged one included, which is more than the agent's PR mark draws), in `Agents` on every device, so `#275` reaches the agent that works on pull request 275 though no word of its row holds the number; an issue's number brings no agent.
An agent is found by its title, by the name the engine knows it by (`hide agent spawn --name`), by its place and by its state sentence; the core carries that name on the agent row (`given_name` on the wire), on this machine and on a device alike, and leaves it out when the engine has none or has only a name Hide made up from a pane id (a task's `hide-claude-w9j-p52`, a wake's or a fork's), which would print the id the rule below keeps off every row.
A query holding `:`, as every pane's id does (`w9J:p52`, as Copy name and ID carries it or `$HERDR_PANE_ID` gives it), lists first the agents whose id holds it in its case, on every device, the whole id before longer ones (`:p5` lists `w9J:p5`, then `w9J:p52`); without a `:` no id matches, and no row prints the id.
Only digits in the query match a number by substring on other rows.
The rows are `Issues`, `Pull requests`, `Agents`, `Projects`, `Checkouts` and `Devices` (while another device is registered), then `Commands` holding `에이전트 시작…` and `GitHub`; a group stands where its best result ranked and keeps its results in rank order, so grouping never moves the best match off the first row.
Search covers every connected device; a result not on the device in front carries that device's chip after its title, and choosing it brings that device forward with it.
An agent row is the agent's status mark, its title, and under it its name, place and state (`observer-instant-pane-topology · hide › main · Working`); a name the title already reads as, ignoring case, or no name at all draws nothing for it; an issue or pull request row carries its number, state and, for a pull request, its CI.

`브라우저에서 URL 열기` is listed first while the query is a web address: one written with `http://` or `https://`, or a loopback host a dev server runs on (`localhost:5173`, `127.0.0.1:3000`), read by the same rule as the View address field (`addressUrl`); any other text is a name to search.
Enter opens the address as a browser display in the Workspace in front, in its active View area, as New tab does.
Pages are drawn by the desktop app, so in a browser tab, and on a screen with no Workspace in front, the row stays listed, dimmed, with the reason under its title, and Enter does nothing.

### What an empty query shows

Nothing typed lists what is connected to the thing in front: the issues the thing works on or closes on top, then one group per checkout with a head, the checkout's pull request, and the agent lineage under it.
The agent the keyboard was in is tagged `여기` and choosing it does nothing; a parent that works in another checkout is one `↑ 부모` line under the agent, and a child delegated to another checkout stands in that checkout's own group.
In front of an agent pane the thing is that agent; in the Workspace elsewhere it is the checkout in front; a connection the snapshot does not name has no row.

Under Related, `Recent` lists the checkouts last brought to the front (PRD cmdk-recent), newest first, at most five, as the same checkout row Related uses: the branch over the project, and the device's chip after the title while the checkout is not on the device in front.
It leaves out the checkout in front and every checkout Related already lists, and fills the five from the rest of the record.
The core keeps the record (`ui_state.recent_checkouts`, ten checkouts, newest first, saved with the rest of the UI state and so still there after a restart): a checkout is recorded whenever it comes to the front by any path (the sidebar, ⌥1-9, ⌘K, the engine's own focus, a device's focus), moves to the top if it is already there, and the oldest leaves past ten.

Choosing a Recent row opens that checkout, bringing its device forward when it is on another one.
A checkout of a connected device is that device's live row; one a connected catalog no longer lists is not drawn, and the core drops the record of a project unregistered, a worktree removed through Hide or a device removed (a worktree deleted outside Hide only stops being drawn, and its record ages out of the ten).
A checkout of a device that is not connected stays as a dimmed row drawn from the names the record kept (the branch, the project and the device's name): arrows pass over it, its detail names the checkout and offers nothing, and Enter does nothing and says nothing.
Its row is the live one again when the device reconnects.
A typed query hides Recent and searches as before, and recency does not rank results.
Recent does not hold pull requests, issues or agents; ⌃Tab stays the recent place cycle and Recent is the way back to a checkout. Unlike Recent Panels and Recent Projects, which the page holds for the session, it is the core's saved record and is still there after a restart.
On a screen with nothing in front, such as Settings, the palette shows Recent alone; with no record and nothing in front it is the input alone with the placeholder `이름이나 #번호를 입력하세요`.

### The detail

The highlighted row says what it is with the facts the snapshot carries and no others (a missing value has no line): a pull request's review, branch and the issues it closes, an issue's owner checkout and closing pull request, an agent's checkout, the pull requests the core links to it (`#275, #260`) and last words, and `N분 전 읽음` for the GitHub-backed ones.
A pull request or an issue with sessions in the link record adds `세션 N`, the count the record's summary carries (PRD link-graph B35); with none there is no line, and the list and Related are unchanged.
Under `관계` it draws the same groups the empty list draws for the row, when it has more than itself; the footer line says what ↵ does.

### GitHub

Typing never calls GitHub.
Opening ⌘K asks once per app run for the project in front when nothing has read it (one of the core's own, or one of a node that dials the core; never a dialed device's), and a checkout row shows a spinner while that read has no answer and a warning mark with the last value's age when it failed; the reason is in the log.
A query with a GitHub project the core reads (its own or a node's that dials it) ends with `GitHub에서 "…" 검색`; choosing it sends one `github_search`, and the row shows a spinner, then the pull requests and issues GitHub holds under `GitHub` (an exact match to one already held is not listed twice), `GitHub에도 없음` (or `검색 결과 없음 · 일부 저장소만 검색` when the core covered only some of the projects), or `GitHub 검색 실패 · 다시 시도`.
A pull request's row, held or found on GitHub, leads with its PR mark without the number and says its state in the muted word; a found one's state is the core's (`pr_state`), as a held one's is.
The query's words match like a search box, and a query is capped at 200 characters.
The same query already searching is not started again, and an answer for an older query is dropped.
Search targets use confirmed project repository choices and valid catalog repository names; an unknown or malformed local name is resolved by the worker, while a node project without a valid name is left out.
Choosing a GitHub result opens it in the browser.

### Keys and results

Up and Down move the selection in display order, stopping at either end, while typing continues in the query field.
Return opens the highlighted result through the existing agent, checkout, Overview or Pull requests actions (an issue or memo opens the Overview on the device that holds it, at the item in 백로그 while it is not started, and once it is, at its task in the sidebar's 태스크 list with its record, and a pull request opens its PR card); Escape closes the sheet.
The selected row scrolls into view.
Filtering preserves a surviving selection by identity; a retired selection moves to the first remaining result.
Empty results have no selection, and arrows or Return require no modal acknowledgement.
A stale result is checked against the live result set before execution.

### File search

⌘P lists what hided's index ranked for the typed query, with the same focused query field and first-result selection, and never executes results from a previous query or checkout while its asynchronous index is updating.
The index follows files made or removed after it was built, on this machine and on a device: opened five seconds or more after its last walk, ⌘P lists the old files at once and says `Indexing…` only while it has none, and the list updates in place when the new walk lands.
↵ opens the highlighted file; ⌘↵ opens it beside the active View area, and the footer hint says so.

## Pane header lineage and ownership

Web owner: `web/src/PaneRelations.tsx`, `web/src/lineage.ts`, and the [Agent panes and the Agents explorer](#agent-panes-and-the-agents-explorer) subsection of Web Workspace above; the [agent workflow contract](../design/agent-workflow-review.md) is the authoritative lineage/ownership document.

The header identity, the second row, the body's notices and shared badge follow [Agent panes and the Agents explorer](#agent-panes-and-the-agents-explorer).
A relationship Open or ancestor step publishes one request-scoped pending state; repeats are ignored until it settles and Retry starts a new request.
Target retirement, refusal, timeout or remote failure preserves pane geometry and topology and retains the scoped reason, retryability and Dismiss outcome.
The shell never infers success from an old layout, sends a rollback focus or attributes an unrelated global error to the control.
A root has no Return control; a child on another device carries that device's real name in the popover.
Ordinary delegated rows stay subdued, while a child raised by the core receives operator attention under [status-model.md](status-model.md#task-identity).
A descendant change still turns its ancestors unread without changing their own group.
A pane whose native session Hide does not hear wears a small, muted broken-link icon in its header (`web/src/PaneConnection.tsx`), named `Hide integration limited` (`Hide 연동 제한`) on hover and keyboard focus.
A detected process that has not reported a native session yet carries no connection judgement or icon, just as a connected pane, a pane the core has nothing to judge for, a sleeping agent and a plain shell draw none.
The icon uses the existing ghost icon button and Hint, with no warning triangle, text badge or banner, and remains distinct from the agent's work-state mark.
The one exception is a copy too old for Hide (`update_agent`): the header wears a small ghost "Update needed" (`업데이트 필요`) badge with a warning dot instead of the icon, because the operator can fix it only outside Hide and needs to know which pane runs it; its popover reads "Not connected to Hide" (`Hide와 연결되지 않았어요`), says status, letters and Memory are off for that agent and version and the version it needs, and shows the one update command with Copy (or the agent's install page), Not now and no Reopen; nothing else on the screen says it.
Click, Enter or Space opens its existing popover; Esc closes it and returns focus to the icon without opening another hint over the popover.
A Codex on the shared server reads "Hide can't follow this Codex" with its reason, Reopen with Hide, Not now, and the existing confirmed shared-server action; a session with no hook report reads "Reconnect this session to Hide", says chatting can continue and offers Reopen and Not now without claiming when the hook was installed; a setup problem names the cause and offers no Reopen, since a Reopen would change nothing; a session Hide cannot reopen (an agent other than Claude Code and Codex, or one on another device) that started before its piece went in, as after an update, says to restart the agent to connect it.
A session Hide cannot read (`session_unreadable`, any agent whose read reports turns) wears the same icon and reads "Hide can't read this session" (`Hide가 이 세션을 읽지 못해요`): letters and mission answers to the agent wait until Hide can read it again, a new session can be read, and a letter goes now with `--force`; it offers no Reopen, which resumes the same session.
An answer on the mission banner that waits on such a Conductor session shows the answer with a warning mark and "Waiting · Hide can't read the Conductor's session" (`기다리는 중 · Hide가 Conductor 세션을 읽지 못해요`) in place of the wait for its turn.
An answer that waits otherwise says what it waits for in place of `보냄 · 차례 기다림`: `보냄 · Conductor가 작업 중` while the Conductor works, `보냄 · 입력창에 입력이 있어요` while something is typed in its input, `보냄 · 조금 전까지 작업이 있었어요` through the quiet period after the Conductor's work or the last input, `보냄 · Conductor가 선택을 기다려요` at a menu of its own; every other wait keeps `보냄 · 차례 기다림`.
The first three of these (not the menu) end with a ghost `지금 보내기` button on the line, which types the answer past the Conductor's work and a draft the screen reads empty, never over text there; after the press the line reads `지금 보내는 중`, and it goes back to its reason when the input holds text (`web/src/missionAnswer.ts`, `MissionDecision.tsx`; [delivery.md](delivery.md#delivering-a-held-letter-now)). The phone's mission page shows the plain wait and no button.
Reopen is hidden where the core says the pane cannot be reopened (a pane on another device).
Not now closes the popover only and the chip stays; Settings has no Reopen button.
Reopen sends one `pane_reopen` however often it is pressed before the core answers, shows the core's pending state as a busy button, and on a refusal known before the agent was touched leaves one line from the refusal code (the agent is working, no saved conversation, the folder is gone, the agent would not stop) while the pane stays as it was; a session that reconnects loses its chip and closes the popover with it.
A start refused after the agent was ended leaves a pane with no agent and so no chip to carry a line, and it is the one Reopen failure that is a notice rather than only a diagnostic (design principle 13): the operator can act on it, because the agent is gone from the pane and its conversation is kept, so they start it again.
The shell turns `last_error` kinds `terminal.input_hold_failed` (a new tab, split or fork exceeded its input hold bound) and `pane_reopen.not_restarted` into an important notice (`web/src/errorNotice.ts`), worded by kind (`web/src/coreError.ts`), and the notice stays until dismissed or until the front device changes, because the core clears `last_error` at its next event of any kind; every other failure of Reopen stays on the chip.
The link opens a confirmation before anything changes, in the device-removal pattern with no button focused (PRD codex-daemon-apply B1, B2).
It names the machine's Codex shared server, says autostart goes off and the server running now stops, that every Codex attached to it disconnects and continues in its pane with `codex resume`, and that Codex Hide started keeps running; it shows no count of Codex, since Codex reports none.
Its buttons are "Leave it running" and "Turn off and stop", and Escape, a click outside or "Leave it running" changes nothing.
The confirmation closes by itself whenever the popover stops offering the link, and the popover stays open behind it to show the answer.
"Turn off and stop" sends one `codex_daemon_disable` for the pane's machine and shows the answer the kit reads back: turning off, done, or the failure code's line (B3).
A failure before autostart went off leaves the setting unchanged; when autostart went off but the running server did not stop, the line says so and the link stays to try again, which then only stops it (B7, B8).
A finished answer is shown only to the operator who asked in that popover.
`PaneChildren.connection` carries the reason, whether Reopen is offered, and a Reopen's pending or refused state as a code, and `docs/status-model.md`, Not connected and what fixes it, owns what each one means; `web/src/paneConnectionRules.ts` names what each code asks of the operator.
An Overview agent row reuses the same agent identity and state presentation as the sidebar and shared child popover; a missing row means the current live projection has no agent there.
The accent header tint and 2px perimeter mark only the pane that owns input; moving input into another surface removes both while the pane remains selected.
Keys typed right after New tab or a split go to the new pane once the engine names it and its shell reads keys itself, in the order typed, so its line editor takes them: they are not echoed on a line above the first prompt, and a Backspace over a character typed in several bytes (a Hangul syllable) erases the whole character; a shell that does not read keys within 2 seconds of the first key, or a pane the engine makes later than that, gets them as they were typed.
