Outside a fork holder, keys typed while a pane's terminal is still opening reach it once it opens; nothing typed is dropped unless more than 64 KiB waits, which discards what waited and shows a dismissible notice asking the operator to type again, the pane can take a key only more than 3 seconds after it was typed, which discards that key because it is no longer what the operator means to run, or the keys have no pane to go to: the creation is refused or makes no pane, the new pane opens in the background, or the pane closes, is released or its terminal fails before it opens (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` names every reason).
Keyboard focus follows the core's answer, not the drawing, so a second ⌘D pressed before the engine confirms the first splits the original pane again.
Keys typed after a click go to the pane clicked last, whatever order snapshots arrive in: the header may briefly follow an older snapshot, but keyboard focus does not leave the last-clicked pane until the core has answered that click, and a focus move the core makes afterwards (the engine's own move, or a refused click) is followed.
Opening a pane from a sidebar row, a 태스크 row, a list, a link or a View's opener mark takes the keyboard to its terminal even when that pane is already the focused one, so the control pressed never keeps the keys and the accent perimeter shows which terminal has them.
Unread weight is never reused to mean parent, child, delegated, or selected.

## Project settings

A Git project or plain folder's gear, project menu and the Overview's project panel (Projects tab) open the same Project settings dialog without selecting or folding that project.
The gear shares the buttons with New work, More and the repository mark: they appear on hover, keyboard focus (never the focus a mouse press leaves behind: they leave when the pointer does) or an open menu and stay visible on devices without hover; `⋯` is always the last of them, after the repository mark. They stand over the end of the project name, which fades under them, so the name keeps its width whether they show or not.
Closing the dialog with Escape or its close button restores the opener's focus and leaves the screen, selected device and checkout unchanged.

The dialog is 880 by 640 when the window permits it, with a fixed 200-wide navigation list for Connections, Working style and Memory; only the page scrolls.
Choices are scoped to the owning device and canonical main-worktree root, shared by all of that project's checkouts.

Connections opens with the project's name: a field showing the name the operator gave it, with the folder's name as its placeholder while it has none, applied by Enter or `Apply` and saved like every other choice, pending, saved or failed beside it.
The name is what every surface calls the project: the sidebar's project and folder rows, the Overview and its Projects tab, the command palette, the phone, the dialogs that name it, and what agents read, such as `hide ps` and an agent's project; the folder on disk, its path and Hide's registration keep their names.
Leading and trailing spaces are dropped, an empty name gives the project its folder's name back, and a name over 64 characters or holding a line break or another control character is refused in place with its reason, nothing sent.
Two projects of one machine whose folders share a name are told apart by their parent folders, as few as make them differ (`projects/hide` and `example/hide`), and every surface that names the project reads that name (`project_settings::tell_apart`); a name the operator gave stays as given and still takes the name, so a folder of that name beside it is told apart, and two projects the operator gave one name keep it.
Another machine's project is told apart by its machine, so it keeps its folder's name.
A device's project is named here, on this core, for its device id and root: the device itself and any other machine that reaches it keep showing the folder's name.
Each change saves immediately through the core's existing state writer, shows pending, saved or failed beside its row, and keeps the last confirmed value until durable acknowledgment.
Closing does not cancel a save, and reopening preserves the failed intent's Retry.
A later window's save does not turn an already completed change back into Pending.
An unavailable device or missing project retains its last observed values read-only, with a retry at that surface; a newer settings version is also read-only with an update reason.

Connections shows the actual GitHub repository or why none was detected, with automatic detection or an explicit owner/repository override, and the existing Auto, GitHub and Local issue choices.
An invalid repository has an inline reason and is not saved.
Changing the connection does not change Git remotes, account login or permissions; provider failures stay with the provider rather than silently switching to Local.
Linear is a disabled Coming soon row.
Previously linked issues and recorded issue, pull-request and session addresses keep their original repository identity, including an unchanged legacy bare issue token.

Working style chooses the initial location for project-scoped New work: an existing checkout or a new worktree.
A new-worktree start asks for its branch and actual base before starting and preserves the task, agent and model on failure.
An explicit checkout, issue or pull-request start, CLI target, resume, wake, fork and running agent keep their existing behavior.
The base branch is also the project's existing Git comparison and merge base; an absent saved branch falls back to an existing branch with a reason.
Plain folders cannot select worktree creation or a Git base.
Check the remote in the background (원격 자동 확인) is on for every Git project, including one whose record predates it; turned off, Hide stops fetching that project and its header detail falls back to the operator's last fetch, and turned on again it fetches within the minute (PRD git-freshness B9).
A plain folder shows it off and unavailable with the existing Git project reason.

Agent accounts (에이전트 계정) lists each agent that has a profile of its own on the project's machine, with `에이전트 기본 따름 · <profile>` and then every profile; with none the group is absent (PRD agent-profiles B15).
A choice saves like the others and applies to every checkout of the project and every worktree Hide makes for it: the start panel preselects that item and `hide agent spawn` starts it, unless one is chosen directly (the start panel's chosen item always is) or a parent of the same kind runs with its own (D-32; PRD agent-profiles-phase2 D-03).
A choice whose profile the machine no longer lists reads as following the agent's default, and deleting a profile takes it out of every project that chose it.

Below the base branch, the Guardrails group (보호) holds four switches, each with one line on what it stops: Guard working folders, Guard the base branch, Block risky Git commands and Guard files (PRD agent-guardrails B1).
Every switch starts off, and a record saved before the group existed reads with all four off (B2, B30).
Guard the base branch shows the checkbox Block agents from merging pull requests and merge requests (에이전트의 PR·MR 머지도 막기) only while it is on (B10), and it stops a merge on GitHub and on GitLab alike ([agent-hooks.md](agent-hooks.md#project-guardrails)), and Guard files shows its path chips only while it is on: `.env`, `.env.*`, `*.pem`, `*.key` and `~/.ssh/` at first, each with its own remove button, and Add path takes a gitignore-style pattern or a `~/` path (B15, B16).
A path past 32 entries, past 200 characters, already listed or not a valid pattern is not saved, and its reason shows under the field, which keeps what was typed and offers no Try again, since the same path would be refused again; the field stays open until the path is saved, Escape closes it without closing the dialog, and focus returns to Add path after a path is added or removed.
Each change sends the whole set of protections and shows saving, then saved, beside the control that moved; saved appears only once the machine that owns the project has the new policy, so a failed delivery shows failed with Try again and keeps the last confirmed values (B3, B4, B31).
A plain folder keeps the three Git switches off and unavailable with the existing Git project reason, once, and Guard files stays usable; a Git project whose base branch is Automatic with no remote default cannot turn on Guard the base branch and says to choose a base branch first (B17).
When an agent installed on the project's machine is switched off in Hide or has a Hook that is not in place, Codex's untrusted hook included, one line under the group names those agents, says the guardrails do not apply to them and links to Settings > Agents; with every agent covered the line is absent (B25).
A refusal reaches only the agent, as an English reason naming the protection and what to do instead; the operator's screen shows no alert, banner or badge for it (B18), and [agent-hooks.md](agent-hooks.md#project-guardrails) owns what each protection stops.
The group promises a guardrail, not security (B28).

Memory uses the existing consent and kit; its row shows hook preparation, pending writes and project-scoped failure with Retry, while retaining the confirmed value.
That Retry applies only to enabling or disabling Memory; card actions stay in the Hide AI tool's Memory tab with their existing confirmations.
Memory promotion PR rows retain their original repository-qualified link after Connections changes; checks, status and agent identity join only that exact URL.
A legacy promotion whose original repository cannot be proven shows PR verification unavailable and keeps its PR button disabled.
Turning it off keeps existing cards.
While the dialog is open its project owns the daemon's single Memory read slot, and the tool rail's project is restored when it closes.
Open project Memory is one scoped navigation intent that closes the dialog and opens that project's usable checkout with the right lane on the Hide AI tool's Memory tab; a delayed remote focus changes the lane only on arrival, and refusal leaves the earlier checkout's lane unchanged.
The shell waits for both the target checkout and its Hide AI tool; the core's lane call then brings the lane forward, over the agents in a narrow window.
A newer explicit navigation cancels the delayed Memory choice so its later arrival cannot take the screen back.
Without a usable checkout it gives a local recovery reason.
All pages use the four interface languages, existing settings rows, system controls, dialog keyboard containment and theme tokens.
`web/src/ProjectSettingsSheet.tsx` owns the dialog and `web/src/projectSettings.ts` reads its confirmed scoped choices.

## Settings: each machine's install kit

Settings opens as one modal, from ⌘,, the sidebar's gear (the rail's, when the rail holds it) and the desktop menu's Settings…; while a paired computer's screen stands in front, ⌘, and the rail's gear alike open that computer's Settings on its screen (B20), and this window's own open once its own tile is in front again, about 880 wide (`--size-settings-sheet-w`) and as wide and tall as the window allows with the window inset (`--size-settings-sheet-window-inset`) clear on every side (PRD settings-left-nav B1, B13).
Its left is a list of pages and its right the chosen page; the list keeps its width (`--size-settings-nav-w`) and its place while only the page scrolls, down to the smallest window, 720×480 (B12, B13).
The title above the list names the computer whose settings these are, as the window names it: `이 Mac 설정` (`이 PC 설정` on Windows) on that machine's own window, and on a screen another machine shows of it the name the showing window calls it by (`window 설정`), which the core's machine group says too; that screen runs inside the other machine's window, so its own system is never read from that window (`settings.titleOf`, `ownMachineName` in `web/src/screenMachine.ts`, operator decision 2026-11-19).
The list reads, top to bottom: the group name `App` with General, Notifications, Usage, Devices and Shortcuts, the group name the core's machine goes by (`This Mac`, as the sidebar calls it, and its name on a window of a machine whose core runs elsewhere, since Hide AI and GitHub are the core's machine's) with Agents, Skills & MCP, Hide AI, Mission, Bots, GitHub and Labs, and About followed by Privacy apart at the list's foot (B2).
Its top inset uses `spacing-sm` and group labels use `spacing-xs` above and below, so the additional Usage page fits at 720×480 with the default Interface text without shrinking row targets.
When Interface text makes the list overflow, its grouped pages scroll independently while About and Privacy stay pinned at the foot; every page remains reachable by pointer or keyboard, and scrolling either the list or the page does not move the other.
Each page has its mark and its name, the names are the same in every language, and a group name is a label, never a page.
The chosen page has the selection fill; Up and Down move through the list and show each page as they go, Home and End reach its ends, and Tab leaves the list for the close button (and a remote device's chip before it) and then the page, which takes focus itself with a ring so the keyboard can scroll it before reaching its first control (B3).
A page has its title and no description line (B4); Skills & MCP is the one exception, with one line under its title that its head's controls share (the visual the operator approved on 2026-11-20).
While another machine than the window's is selected, a small device chip beside the page title names it and says that selecting it changes where files, Git and panes run, not where Settings are kept; with This Mac selected the chip is absent and no sentence about ownership is shown, and on a window of a machine whose core runs elsewhere This Mac is that machine and the core's machine has the chip.
Esc and the close button close Settings and leave the screen under it as it was (B14).
Labs holds the features still being tried, each off by default and switched on on its own page section; Desk is the first, and its page (the `Desk 사용` switch and its settings) lives there, not in a page of its own, so every `openSettings` and test that named the Desk page names Labs (operator decision 2026-11-09).
Settings opens on General unless the way in names a page: Explorer's and a device row's Add device open Devices with its Add dialog in front, Memory's and Factory's Hide AI links open Hide AI, and the first-run wizard's phone link opens Devices with its phone group in view (B11; operator review 2026-11-23).

### General

General has the Appearance group.
Appearance contains Language, Color theme, Mode, Accent, Interface text, Terminal text, Terminal font and `도구 아이콘의 숫자`; Accent shows its swatches and no hex value (PRD settings-left-nav B5; hide-color-theme B1).
Interface text, 11 to 17 pt with 13 the default, sizes every text outside the terminals and the editor's code, the sidebar included.
Text outside the terminals and the editor's code is drawn in faces the app ships, so every system draws the same letters: Inter for Latin, and Pretendard, made to sit beside Inter, for Hangul only.
Kana and Han characters, and any character neither face has, come from the system's own face for the language Language chose, so a Han character takes the form its reader expects.
On macOS that text is drawn with grayscale smoothing, so light text on a dark ground keeps the face's weight; the terminals keep the system's smoothing.
Terminal text, 10 to 24 pt with 14 the default, is every terminal's size; it carries one note that a single pane is sized with Larger text and Smaller text in that pane (⌘= and ⌘- on macOS) and the editor's text with the same keys in a document.
A pane's own size multiplies Terminal text, so moving the slider resizes every terminal and keeps a zoomed pane that much larger than the rest; each terminal takes its new rows and columns at once.
The core keeps both sizes in its ui state, where they survive a reload and a relaunch, and refuses a size outside its range into the diagnostic log, keeping the last one.
`도구 아이콘의 숫자` is a switch, on by default, that shows or hides the gray numbers on the tool rail's Project and Hide AI icons; the core keeps it in its ui state beside the other appearance choices, it waits for a live connection as Language does, and the icons' hints and accessible descriptions say the counts either way.
Terminal font is a text field for the name of an installed font, empty by default with `Default monospace` as its placeholder; leaving the field or pressing Enter sends it, Escape puts the stored name back, and the core keeps it in its ui state beside the sizes, refusing a name that is untrimmed, longer than 128 characters or holds a control character into the diagnostic log.
Every terminal draws in that font first, then in the default monospace font, and its note says that Nerd Font icons are fitted to the font's cells; a name this machine does not have turns the note into a warning that terminals use the default font.
Whatever the font, Nerd Font icons come from the bundled Symbols Nerd Font Mono shrunk to one cell, and a chosen font that has its own icons keeps them.
Korean, Japanese and Chinese text is drawn by the system's fallback at the terminal's own size, not enlarged to fill its two cells: filling them left too little room between rows to read.
Color theme chooses the palette every window draws in, Hide or Neutral, as a segmented toggle like Mode's, each name led by a small swatch of its brand color, the same in Light and Dark (Hide the owl mark's blue-grey, Neutral a grey), and no color code; it waits for a live connection, as Language does.
Hide is the landing page's Owl Field palette and the default, including for an install that never chose; Neutral is the grey chrome.
Both have their own sixteen terminal colors, each readable at 4.5:1 on its background, and the choice changes the sidebar, tabs, pane headers, terminals, editors, sheets and popovers at once, keeping a terminal's content, scroll position, half-typed line and selection.
Status colors, the accent choices and what uses them, the destructive color, and the diff, PR and file icon colors are the same in both.
The status marks' colours (the warning badge, the working ring, the green and the plain dot) are the same in both, while quiet text beside them, such as a read request line, follows the palette's `--subtle-foreground`; a quiet row's faint ring follows the palette's `--muted-foreground`.
Mode is the former Theme row unchanged: System, Light or Dark, each color theme having both.
The choice is the core's, one for every window attached to it, and survives a restart of the app or of hided; the first paint, the desktop window before the page draws and the host status page are Hide Dark, and a paired phone draws the operator's color theme in its own light or dark.
A stored value this build does not know opens Hide with only the `ui_state.color_theme_unknown` diagnostic.

### Privacy

Privacy is the last Settings tab and holds Send usage statistics, enabled by default and disabled while disconnected.
Its short note says that counts of connections, agent and mission starts, device and phone connections and practice chapters are collected with the app version and operating system, and that conversations and code are not sent.
The core persists the choice, shares it between windows and preserves it against stale generic UI saves.
Turning it off discards waiting events and prevents new ones; a request already in flight may finish, and turning it back on never replays disabled events.
Product analytics has no autocapture, session recording, person profiles or location enrichment; [the architecture contract](ARCHITECTURE.md#product-usage-statistics) owns the PostHog US destination, exact wire fields and worker limits.

### Notifications

Notifications holds every notification Hide sends, in two groups (PRD settings-left-nav B6).
The first, named for this machine and only in the desktop app, has two independently stored switches in the existing row group: `Notify me when an agent needs me` (Needs You) and `Notify me when an agent finishes` (Done).
Both default off before the wizard's choice; the fresh wizard enables Needs You only, and `Not now` disables both.
An existing install whose old shared switch was on migrates to both on; an old off or absent switch migrates to both off, so no existing notification choice silently disappears.
Each toggle sends one event and persists through the core's existing UI-state writer; a repeated choice publishes nothing and a stale generic UI save cannot replace either choice.
While either switch is on, the group shows what macOS allows with `Open System Settings` when it blocks Hide or `Send a test` when it allows, or a `Test notification` row on Windows and Linux.
The note says notices arrive only while no Hide window is in front, and that while the Needs You switch is on the app icon shows the 내 차례 number (First run above).
A notice goes to one place (PRD device-connect-iroh D-60, B59): this computer's windows get a new one only while the computer is in use, by the same rule, and a computer that is on and not in use gets none, so the same question never rings here and on a phone.
There a wait that began while no one was at the computer is not announced when someone returns; its row stands in Needs You.
Between paired computers the same rule picks one of them (B59, B71): work of a machine no one is at rings on the computer in use that may open that machine, the one used last when there are several, worded as that computer's own notices are, and a click selects that machine's tile.
A computer that does not show it within 5 s is passed over for the next one, and the phone of the work's machine rings when no computer showed it; when the operator has seen the work, the notice is taken back wherever it was shown.
A row that arrived from another machine never rings on its own.
The second, Phone, has one switch, `컴퓨터를 쓰는 동안 폰 알림 쉬기`, on by default.
Its two description lines are `컴퓨터를 쓰고 있으면 폰으로 알림을 보내지 않아요. 10분 넘게 자리를 비우거나 화면이 잠기면 폰으로 보내요.` and `알림을 받을지는 폰 앱의 설정에서 정해요.`
On rests phone pushes only while an attached desktop host reports keyboard or mouse input anywhere on the system within ten minutes, unlocked and awake, at least one attached desktop window has Needs You notifications enabled (`app_closed`), and no later admitted phone action has taken over.
The most recently used device wins: a written reply or key from any phone (including a connected device's pane), an answer, approval or wake admitted for dispatch, or a successful start permits subsequent new phone notifications.
A new authenticated, well-formed answer, approval or wake counts as phone use even when the core refuses its meaning, such as an old mission question key, a stale Done or wake, or a refused merge; this does not make that action succeed.
Reading or opening the app, authentication or format refusals and duplicate request IDs do not count.
Computer input reported after a pause of at least five seconds, or a return to in use, rests phone pushes again; continuous typing adds no reports to the existing one-second poll.
Within one second the computer wins; when typing continuously while also using the phone, the phone stays in charge until the next pause and resumed input.
This memory is lost on daemon restart and does not change the switch's wording; where two computers that each run Hide can connect, this computer's windows get no new notice while the phone is in charge.
Ten minutes without input, locking or suspending the computer, closing the last desktop window, or disabling Needs You notifications in every attached window permits phone pushes.
A root with no eligible pending notice sends no phone notification for a wait that begins while phone pushes rest, even when resting ends before its settle window closes.
It remains in the phone app's list and badge count; only a new wait or declaration after resting ends can notify, after the existing settle window.
A candidate first observed while delivery is allowed uses the routing state at the end of that window, including a device change during it.
A root that no longer waits sends nothing, and turning the pause switch off does not replay discarded notices.
Within an existing SETTLE window the lead accepts one notice for a root that still waits even if its original A resolved and only rested B remains, and wording may use the current representative subject instead of A (the same class as hide-private#873).
Off always permits pushes (`always`), subject to the phone’s own receiving choice and viewing suppression.
A saved old `off` becomes `app_closed`; whether a phone receives notifications belongs to that phone’s Settings.

### Usage

Usage follows Notifications in the App group, with a gauge mark and the localized Usage title.
The page walks the shared adapter declaration so all seven agents appear even before a read.
An agent with limit support has one card: its mark and name with Hide, then a row for each declared account window with its current value and a pin switch.
A window the account reports as not offered has no row or switch.
When a fresh reading explicitly marks a window not offered or unreadable, the core removes its saved pin so an invisible row cannot hold a chip slot; loading, failed and retained readings preserve choices.
The initial choices are the weekly window for Claude and Codex; the core stores the choices for every connected window and retains them across restart.
At three pins, an unselected switch is disabled with “The sidebar shows at most three chips. Switch one off to pick this one.” beneath that row; removing a pin enables it again.
Every window switch stays in one right-aligned column beside the first line of its label; a long value wraps below the label, including unavailable and refused-fourth-pin rows.
Hide folds the card to its header, Hidden and Show, preserves the pins, and removes the agent from chips and the popover.
The five agents without supported limits appear in No limit reading with a reason from the adapter declaration, without Hide or switches.
Grok's weekly reader is not built, OpenCode Go would need an API key Hide does not hold, Pi and omp count toward their signed-in account.
The page reuses the settings group, row and switch parts; loading is an ellipsis and a missing reading is Unavailable, never an invented zero.

### GitHub

GitHub is the GitHub row: its description, whether `gh` is signed in, and Check again, which asks the core to read GitHub again for the registered projects (PRD settings-left-nav B8).
The core reads GitHub with its own machine's `gh`, so in a window on another machine a signed-out `gh` is named on the core's machine ("Not signed in to gh on Mac mini") with the command that fixes it there (Run on Mac mini, `gh auth login`, with Copy) in place of `gh`'s own reason; on the core's own machine the row says it plainly ("Not signed in to gh") with the same command under Run in a terminal (PRD core-host-node-move B15, B1).
`gh`'s own reason stays in the core's log on every machine; a missing `gh` has no one command that installs it on every system, so its row names the state only, and so does a `gh` older than Hide's commands need (`gh가 오래됐어요. 업데이트하세요`), which the PR list and the phone's PR screen say in the same words.
Hide has no GitHub sign-in of its own, so there is no connect or reconnect action beyond that read.

### About

About shows the `hide` version.
A closed Details disclosure holds the rest: the process, lifetime, state file, page connection, the terminal engine's state and message, the environment rows, the recent diagnostics and Copy diagnostics (PRD settings-left-nav B9).

In the desktop app, an Updates row and a preview switch sit under the `hide` version (web owner `AboutTab.tsx`, `web/src/updates.ts`; host owner `desktop/src/main/updater.ts`; PRD auto-update D-20 to D-22, B25 to B28):

| State | The row says | Action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Up to date | `Up to date · checked <how long ago>`, which moves with the clock while About is open, or `Not checked yet` | `Check now` |
| Looking | `Checking…` | none |
| Downloading | `Downloading <version> · <percent>` | none |
| Ready | `<version> ready · installs when you quit`, or on Linux `<version> ready · restart to install` | `Restart now` |
| Failed | `Couldn't check for updates` or `Couldn't update`, with the reason in the app's log | `Check again` |
| Off | `Updates are off in development builds`, or on macOS `Move hide to Applications to get updates` for an app on the disk image or in a folder it cannot write | none |

The updater also looks when a window of the app takes the keyboard an hour or more after its last look, so an app left open does not keep an update a later release has passed.
On macOS, closing the last window while an update is ready quits the app, which installs it; the core and its agents keep running.
A ready update stays ready while a later check looks; a newer release that check finds is downloaded in its place, so the row shows that download, the ↑ button, the dot and Help's update leave meanwhile, and they return with the newer version once it is ready, with its card opening once, since the updater keeps one pending update.
`Get preview versions (rc)`, with a note that turning it off never goes back to a lower version, is off at first, kept in the desktop profile, and cannot be turned while updates are off.
`Check for updates…` in the app menu (macOS) or the Help menu (Windows, Linux) opens About and looks at once.
A browser tab and the phone show neither row.

### Language

General includes Language, using the existing Settings row and selector pattern.
Its options name English, 한국어, 简体中文 and 日本語 in their own scripts, plus Use system language.
The selected value comes from the core snapshot; an edit keeps the confirmed value visible until the next snapshot.
The selector remains available while connected, so a concurrent choice from another client cannot strand a local pending edit.
An explicit choice is shared by connected shell windows; Use system language resolves separately in each client, with English for unsupported languages.
[LOCALIZATION.md](LOCALIZATION.md) owns the policy and the remaining integration surfaces.

Settings > Devices shows a person only what to look at and what to do (operator review 2026-11-23; `web/src/settings/DevicesTab.tsx`).
It opens with one line, `어느 컴퓨터든 연결하면 이 Mac처럼 똑같이 써요. Mac, Windows, Linux 상관없이 터미널을 열고 에이전트를 돌리고, 한 화면에서 보고 답해요. 폰에서도 보고 답해요.`, where `이 Mac` is this machine as its system names it (`이 PC`, `이 컴퓨터`), and `체험하기 ›` after it opens the practice's connection chapter (flow 9, `connect`); `+ 기기 추가` stands at the line's right.
The machines stand in two groups, and the phones in a third after them (below): `이 컴퓨터`, the window's own machine, one row with its name and system only and, on this computer's own window, `공유하기`; and `다른 기기와 연결`, the Hides this computer is paired with (PRD device-connect-iroh B1, B3, B21, B24 to B27, B50, B51; `web/src/settings/PairingRows.tsx`, the rules in `web/src/pairing.ts`) and after them the devices it reaches over SSH or WSL, with no mark that tells the two kinds apart.
With no other machine the second group shows three steps on this computer's own window, under `다른 컴퓨터를 연결해 보세요`: `+ 기기 추가를 눌러요`, `그 컴퓨터에서 링크를 열거나, SSH로 고르면 Hide가 대신 깔아요`, and `그다음은 이 Mac처럼 똑같이 써요: 그 컴퓨터에서 터미널을 열고 에이전트를 돌려요`; any other screen says `아직 연결한 기기가 없어요.`.
`공유하기` opens the share window and makes the link as it opens, unless one is already shared, which it shows again; the link is never laid out in the list.
The share window walks three numbered steps, the one under way marked, the done ones checked and the later ones dimmed: `링크를 복사하세요` with the start of the link, `복사`, how long it has left counted down each second and `한 번만 쓸 수 있어요`; `다른 컴퓨터에서 붙여 넣으세요` with where (`그 컴퓨터의 Hide에서 설정 › 기기 › 기기 추가 › Hide가 있는 컴퓨터`), which after a copy says `붙여 넣기를 기다리는 중`; and `단어 4개를 맞춰 보세요`, which once a machine pasted the link shows its four words, what it would be allowed and `거절`/`허락`, so the allow window does not open over it; answering closes the window.
Its footer ends the link (`링크 끝내기`), a link that ran out says `링크가 끝났어요.` with `새 링크 만들기`, and closing the window keeps the link: a machine that pastes it then asks in the allow window as before.
Each paired machine has one row: its name, its system when the device the core made of it reports one, one state, and a ⋯ menu; who opens whom (`이 컴퓨터가 열어요`, `이 컴퓨터를 열어요`, `서로 열어요`) and the path the connection takes are in ⋯ › `연결 정보…`.
That row is the machine's only one in the list: the device the core makes of a machine this computer opens (`Device.peer_key`) serves its rail tile and its panes and has no device row of its own (`listedDevices`), while an SSH device that only says the same id is another machine and keeps its row.
The state line says the one thing the operator would act on first: the update one side needs, or, for a machine this computer switched over SSH or `wsl.exe` and that has no app of its own, this computer's own update of its Hide (`그 컴퓨터의 Hide 업데이트하는 중`, and when it did not happen `그 컴퓨터의 Hide를 업데이트하지 못했어요` with one cause, such as `새 버전이 시작되지 않아 이전 버전으로 실행 중이에요`, and `다시 시도`; the Hide there keeps running the version it ran; a machine that has the app, which updates it, and a WSL distribution systemd did not start, whose Hide stays on its version, show nothing of it), `다시 짝지어 주세요` with `빼기` for a machine whose key changed or that dropped the pairing, a removal the other machine has not heard yet (`그 컴퓨터에서 아직 빼지 못했어요 · 그 컴퓨터에서도 빼 주세요`), `멈춤` with `다시 켜기` (a pair with two directions names the paused one, `이 컴퓨터가 여는 것 멈춤` or `이 컴퓨터를 여는 것 멈춤`), `상대가 멈춤`, `연결 안 됨` with why on a line under the name when the relay is the reason (not reachable, or stopped for the month) and `다시 시도`, and otherwise `● 연결됨` alone.
`연결 정보…` says the path the connection takes now, one word: `직접`, `Tailscale` (a direct path between the two computers' Tailscale addresses) or `relay`, which follows the connection as it moves, as when a direct path opens beside the relay (D-86); `hide device list` says the same after `connected ·`.
Only a row whose connection goes through the relay says under its name `relay를 거쳐 연결됐어요 · 두 컴퓨터에 Tailscale을 켜 두면 바로 이어져 더 빨라요`; a direct or Tailscale row never does.
When two Hides are too far apart to connect, the row says which one must update (`이 컴퓨터 업데이트 필요`, `그 컴퓨터 업데이트 필요`).
A computer that opens the older one, with that direction on, has `<name> 업데이트` on the row: it asks that computer's Hide to update itself to the official release, and the line reads `그 컴퓨터에 업데이트를 요청했어요` until the two connect; a computer that the older one only opens gets the line and no button.
The computer that must update shows one notice in the corner of its desktop app, `<name> 연결에 업데이트가 필요해요` with `지금 업데이트`, which looks for the release, or restarts into the one that is ready; while the release downloads the notice says `업데이트를 받는 중`.
The computer that waits shows its own corner notice, `<name>의 Hide를 업데이트해야 열 수 있어요` with `업데이트 요청`, which asks the other computer to update; once asked the row says `그 컴퓨터에 업데이트를 요청했어요` and the notice goes, and a notice closed on a computer stays closed for that computer and side.
When the other computer asked (`이 컴퓨터 업데이트 필요 · 그 컴퓨터가 요청했어요`), the app looks for the release and restarts into it by itself, except where the restart asks for the system password (Linux): there it waits for `지금 업데이트` on that computer.
The notice can be closed until another computer needs the update, and a browser tab, the phone and another computer's screen of this one show the row's line only, because none of them updates the app.
A machine that opens this one and that this one does not open offers `<name> 열기 요청` on its row, which asks that machine to let this one open it; what came of the request is one line under it (waiting, `그 컴퓨터에서 거절했어요`, no answer, not connected) with `다시 요청`.
The ⋯ menu offers `이름 바꾸기` and `연결 정보…`, then, after a line, stops and removes each direction on its own (`이 컴퓨터가 여는 것`, `이 컴퓨터를 여는 것`), the two groups parted by a line when both exist.
`이름 바꾸기` opens a field with the name it has and `저장`, which keeps the operator's chosen name beside the name the machine gives itself in this computer's pairing records (`pairing_rename`); the rail tile, its tabs, the row and `hide device list` show the chosen name while there is one, a connection that brings the machine's own name again changes nothing shown, and saving an empty field takes the chosen name back so the machine's own shows.
A device switched onto that machine keeps the name it had here as that chosen name (B36).
A removal asks first, with `취소` and `빼기`: removing a machine that opens this one says it is cut off at once, that its key never enters again and that what already ran and was read cannot be undone, and under it what becomes of the work it ran on this computer, `그대로 두기` (the work stays and shows on this computer's screen only), which is the default (B26); removing a machine this one opens says this computer no longer opens it and that the work handed to it keeps running there.
While that machine has terminals on this computer (`navigator.guest_panes`), the same place is a choice of two, `그대로 두기` and `끝내기`, the second with how many terminals close at once and that they close even while that machine is away (B60); the removal then carries `end_work`, and with no terminal of that machine here only `그대로 두기` is said and nothing is sent about the work.
A machine being paired or removed shows no control that could not act: a row has at most one button beside its menu.

On Windows, sharing this computer and allowing a pairing each ask Windows for the firewall rules that let machines on the same network reach it directly, once per window and never at login (PRD device-connect-iroh D-38, B32; `web/src/settings/firewall.ts`, the host's side in `desktop/src/main/firewall.ts`).
While the computer is shared or paired and the rules are missing, a row under the share row says `방화벽이 막아 relay로 연결돼요` with `허용하면 같은 Wi-Fi의 기기와 바로 연결돼요` and `허용하기`, which asks Windows again; the row goes once the rules are in place, and macOS, Linux, a browser and another computer's screen of this one never show it.

A machine that wants to open this one opens the allow window wherever the operator is in this machine's own windows, one at a time, oldest first (`web/src/PairingDialogs.tsx`, B2, B50, D-76).
It names the machine (`연결하려는 기기: <name>`, or `이 컴퓨터를 열려는 기기: <name>` for a machine already connected that asks to be let in the other way, which shows no words), shows the four words with their places and `그 기기 화면에 같은 단어가 같은 순서로 보일 때만 허락하세요`, says what allowing gives (`이 컴퓨터에서 명령과 에이전트를 실행하고 화면을 볼 수 있어요 · 이 계정에 <name>의 Hide 프로그램과 에이전트 훅을 설치해요`), counts down its two minutes, and offers `거절` and `허락`; closing it is a refusal.
The machine that pasted a link shows the pairing's own window: `다른 컴퓨터에 연결하는 중…`, then the same four words with `달라요` and `같아요`, then the wait for the other operator, then `연결됐어요`, or one line saying why nothing was connected with `닫기`; closing it before the words are confirmed gives the pairing up.
A screen that is not this computer's own window (another computer's view of it) lists the paired machines with their state line and direction and offers nothing to press: no `공유하기`, no row button or ⋯ menu, no `+ Add device`, no three steps, and neither window (B52).
`ownWindow` in `web/src/pairing.ts` is the one place that decides it, from the `pairing` frame hided sends to this computer's own windows only; an ask from such a screen would be refused.
A row's press names its machine by its connection id, so two rows that say the same device id each act on their own machine.

This Mac and every device are one row each (PRD settings-cleanup B54, B55, B56; operator review 2026-11-23): the name, a line with its system as its helper reports it (a WSL distribution's place, `이 PC`, instead), and for a device one state at a time, `● 연결됨`, `연결하는 중…` or `연결 안 됨`, with a ⋯ menu at the end; an attempt that stopped on a reason reads `연결 안 됨` even while the link says it is trying again.
A device that is not connected says why in one plain line under its name, and a connected one whose helper failed or is not allowed says that; ssh's own last line, what the helper said and a test's stages are in `연결 정보…`.
A Windows device on which Hide was started and never answered says `Windows에서 Hide가 켜지지 않았어요 · 스마트 앱 컨트롤이 켜져 있으면 끄는 방법` (`windows_not_started`, from the start failure the device's link reports; `SmartAppControlHint` in `web/src/settings/SmartAppControlHint.tsx`), where `끄는 방법` opens the site's troubleshooting section on turning Smart App Control off; Hide cannot tell that Smart App Control was the cause (issue 2855), so the line says `켜져 있으면`, and a device on another system or a different failure never shows it.
The row carries at most one button besides ⋯: `로그인` while a sign-in is what the device waits on, else `다시 시도` where trying again can help.
`+ Add device` is one button above the three groups on the right; the Add dialog is on the page only after it is pressed, and every other way in (the rail's `+`, the hidden rail's `기기 추가…`, Add a project's host list) opens Settings › Devices with that dialog in front.
The dialog asks first `컴퓨터` (`링크로 짝짓거나 SSH로 고르면 Hide가 대신 깔아요`) or `폰` (`QR을 찍으면 5분 안에 연결돼요`); `컴퓨터` goes on to the two ways below, and `폰` closes it and opens the QR window of the phone group (below), the one way to pair a phone; Add a project's host list, which adds a computer, opens it on `컴퓨터`'s two ways.
The dialog closes by itself once the device is listed, which it finds by the alias it added: the core gives the device its id, the machine's own once it first connects ([ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md#a-devices-id)), and the row shows its name and alias, never the id.
A device whose connection reaches a machine its id does not name, or that cannot take its machine's id yet, is not connected and its row says why in one line: the alias now reaching another machine (add it as a new device), a machine already added under another name, this Mac itself, Hide needing a restart to finish naming a device added by an earlier version, or its records not having moved, with the reason in the diagnostic log.
A device this computer dials over SSH, or a WSL distribution of this PC, is used over that login until the operator chooses to connect the two as Hides (operator decision 2026-11-24, screen confirmed that evening; PRD device-connect-iroh B30, B36, B43; the parts in `web/src/settings/DeviceWays.tsx`, the rules in `web/src/settings/switchWays.ts`).
An update switches no device, and neither does a start or a connection: nothing is read on the machine and nothing is started there until the operator asks.
A device row carries one outline mark beside its name for how it is reached, `SSH`, `WSL` or, once it was switched, `서버`; a computer paired by a link carries none.
On this computer's own window a marked row opens when it is pressed, and a row opened stays open until Settings › Devices is left; any other window shows the mark and opens nothing.
Opened, the row shows one bordered box under it: the two ways as two rows, `SSH로 바로 연결` (`WSL로 바로 연결` for a distribution) and `서버로 연결`, each with one sentence of what it is for and `지금` on the one in use; then `대신`, `서버로 연결하면 메모리, 상시 지침, 폰 연결은 그 기기에서 따로 해요`; then `조건`, what that machine was read as, with the `서버로 연결` button under it.
A Windows device shows one faint line in the box's place and nothing to press, in 1.2.12 (operator decision 2026-11-24): `Windows는 아직 버튼으로 서버로 연결할 수 없어요 · 서버로 쓰려면 그 PC에 Hide를 설치하고, 이 기기를 뺀 뒤 기기 추가에서 연결해요`; `hide device switch` leaves it with the same words ([INSTALL.md](INSTALL.md), What each system supports).
Opening the row reads the machine once over the device's own login, an `ssh` that asks nobody, and changes nothing there (`pairing_look_switch`); the place says `확인하는 중` until it answers, what was read stays while the row is open, and opening it again reads again.
A device that is not connected is not read and says `연결되면 확인해요`.
What was read decides the button, in one place hided owns (`pairing_ssh::Condition`), which `hide device switch` passes too:

| The machine | Button | The line under `조건` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Mac, automatic login on | on | `자동 로그인 켜짐` |
| Mac, automatic login off | on | a warning, `자동 로그인이 꺼져 있어요 · <기기> 기기가 재시동되면 그 Mac에 한 번 로그인해야 다시 연결돼요`, and `방법` |
| Mac, FileVault on | on | the same warning with no `방법`, since macOS offers no automatic login there |
| Mac, nobody logged in to its desktop | off | `<기기>에 로그인한 사람이 없어요 · 한 번 로그인한 뒤 다시 눌러 주세요` |
| Linux, lingering on | on | `linger 켜짐` |
| Linux, lingering off | on | `linger 꺼짐`, then `연결할 때 Hide가 켜 봐요` |
| Linux, only an administrator can turn it on | off | `linger 꺼짐 · 관리자만 켤 수 있어요`, the command to run there with its copy button, and `다시 확인` |
| No systemd (a WSL distribution) | none | `이 배포판에는 systemd가 없어서 서버로 연결할 수 없어요` |
| Its SSH login asks for a password, a passphrase or an answer | none | `SSH 로그인 때 비밀번호를 물어서 서버로 옮길 수 없어요` |

`방법` opens a small window under the button with the three steps that turn automatic login on, on that Mac; closing it reads the Mac again, so the warning leaves once the setting is on.
Lingering is turned on only by the press: opening a row never changes the account, and a press that could not turn it on leaves the device as it is and shows the administrator's line, which stays until the account is read as lingering.
A read that failed for another reason says `조건을 읽지 못했어요` with `다시 확인`.
Pressed, the button's place shows the five steps hided runs, `조건 다시 확인`, `<기기>에 Hide 띄우기`, `두 Hide 연결`, `열린 터미널 넘기기`, `프로젝트 등록`: the ones behind are checked, the one under way turns and is the only bright one, and there is nothing to cancel.
The steps are the core's (`Device.switch_step`), so every window of this computer draws the same one, and a switch asked at a terminal draws them too.
The press is the consent to start a Hide on a machine that runs none, so nothing more is asked.
A switch the operator asked for wakes a distribution that is off, as its Reconnect or its choice in Add device does (B43); nothing else starts one.
A press that did not switch the device leaves it as it was and says why in one warning line where the steps were, with `다시 시도`: `<기기>에 Hide를 띄우지 못했어요 · 다시 시도해 주세요`, the same for the connection, which is also what a login that asked or ended during the press says (the password line is only the row's read, made before any press; `switch_cut` at a terminal), or, when another computer switched the device first and this one still runs terminals on it, `<컴퓨터> 컴퓨터가 먼저 <기기> 기기를 서버로 옮겼어요 · 이 컴퓨터의 <기기> 터미널 N개를 닫고 다시 해 주세요`; no switch closes a terminal.
That line stands until the next press, until the row is folded or opened again, whose read draws what the machine says now, the password line among them, or until the device's connection drops, which shows `연결되면 확인해요` instead.
When the new connection is confirmed the device keeps its id, its name and its place on the rail, its row is the paired machine's with the `서버` mark, open as it was, and its running terminals carry on.
A switch that paired the two machines and stopped before the device took the new connection still leaves one row and one tile, the device's: the paired machine has none of its own while it is that device (`Peer.helper_device`, which the core writes, read by `machinesWithOwnRow` in `web/src/pairing.ts`), in Settings, on the rail and in `hide device list`.
A machine that also opens this computer keeps its own row, since that direction is the operator's to see, pause and remove.
A device added from `SSH로 추가`, a Host or a distribution, is added and used over SSH whatever its machine can do, a Mac nobody is logged in to and a Linux account that cannot linger among them, so an Add never ends without its device.
At a terminal `hide device switch <device>` passes the same read: it prints the warning of a Mac without automatic login and goes on, and for a machine the table leaves it answers why with the one thing to change; `--install` allows a Hide to be started on a machine that runs none, and without it the command names the machine and starts nothing ([INSTALL.md](INSTALL.md)).
Once a switch lands, the window that watched it says once, at the top of the opened row, `메모리와 상시 지침은 <기기>에서 새로 시작해요`, with how many of this computer's projects were registered in that machine's Hide under it and `확인`, which takes the notice away; no strip is drawn over the window, and a window that did not watch the switch says nothing.
The machine keeps the name its device had here, not the one it calls itself; the projects this computer had registered on the device are projects of that machine's own Hide from then on.
A terminal the new connection could not take up again is closed, never started again as a new shell: the machine's row then says `터미널 N개를 이어받지 못했어요 · AI 대화는 이어서 열 수 있어요` under its name, and each closed agent's conversation is under `보관됨` for `이어가기`; the line goes when that Hide restarts.
A switched machine's row opens on the same box with `지금` on `서버로 연결`, its condition and no button, and under it `되돌리려면 이 기기를 빼고 SSH로 다시 추가해요`; a Mac switched with automatic login off keeps the warning line there.
That way back keeps the terminals: removing the machine leaves its shells running there, on that machine's own screen as any removed computer's work is, and adding it again over SSH takes each one up in its pane, the same shell, with nothing restarted on either computer.
A terminal is taken up so only once the computer that switched it is no longer paired with that machine; while it still is, the terminals stay that computer's and the re-added device restores none of them.
A switched Mac that is not connected is read once over the login it was switched through, when Settings › Devices opens and when its row opens, and only a Mac read as having nobody logged in says `<기기>에 로그인하면 다시 연결돼요` under its name; one SSH does not reach either says only that it is not connected.
The Add dialog opens on a choice of what to add, `Hide가 있는 컴퓨터` or `SSH 기계`, starting on the first: a field for the link another Hide shared with `연결`, which closes the dialog for the pairing's window or says in one line why the link was not taken (not a whole link, a newer Hide's, this computer's own, already connected); the second is the SSH list below.
`SSH로 추가` lists the Host entries of the daemon machine's `~/.ssh/config` with the address each leads to (`user@host:port`, which `ssh -G` resolves without connecting); it asks hided for them each time it opens (`ssh_hosts_list`, answered in `status.ssh_hosts`) and says it is reading until they arrive (PRD settings-cleanup B50 to B52).
Choosing a Host fills the Name with the alias, which the person can rewrite and which later choices no longer replace; Add stores only that name and the alias, so the dialog has no input for a username, port or key and no way to type an alias.
Add adds the device at once, so the button reads `추가`.
Choosing a Host also asks it once, over an `ssh` that asks nobody, whether a Hide of its own runs there (PRD device-connect-iroh B7; `sshLookLine` in `web/src/pairing.ts`); choosing a distribution asks it the same through `wsl.exe` (D-27), which starts it when it is off and starts its own Hide when that stopped with it, and what follows is the same for both.
Until it answers the dialog says `거기 Hide가 있는지 확인하는 중…` and Add waits, so a machine that runs its own Hide is never added as an SSH device in that moment; a look hided refused looked at nothing, and the Host is added as before.
A Host that runs one shows `이미 Hide가 있어요 · 연결만 해요` under the list, the Name and Advanced leave since nothing is installed there, and the button reads `연결`: pressed, the two connect as Hides with no words to compare, the dialog closes and the machine is a paired row, or one line says why not (`이미 연결된 기기예요` for a machine already paired).
Beside `연결` a text button, `SSH로 추가`, adds that Host as an SSH device all the same, as a Host with no Hide is added: the Name and Advanced are back, the button reads `추가`, and the Hide on that machine is left running; it is how a machine that was connected as a server is used over SSH again after its row is removed (operator decision 2026-11-24).
A Host whose Hide is too old to connect that way shows `그 PC의 Hide를 업데이트하세요`, one whose Hide refused the look for another reason `그 컴퓨터의 Hide가 SSH로 온 요청을 받지 않았어요`, and one whose Hide is installed and not running shows `그 Mac에 로그인해 주세요` (`그 컴퓨터에서 Hide를 열어 주세요` off a Mac); each can still be added as before.
A Host with no Hide of its own, and one `ssh` could not sign in to without asking, show nothing new and are added as before.
A Host a registered device already uses, or one that reaches the same machine as one (its host name in any case) on that device's default server, is dimmed with `Added as <name>` and cannot be chosen, and the first-run Devices step reads the same Host as the same device; a Host whose address could not be read is dimmed with a short reason (no `ssh`, `ssh` could not read it, `ssh` took too long); a wildcard Host is not listed, and a config with more concrete Hosts than the list holds says so.
With no Host and no WSL distribution the dialog shows only `Not listed? Add a Host entry…` with an example entry, and no form.
In the Windows app this PC's WSL distributions come first in the same list, the default one first, each named `WSL <distribution>` with `WSL on this PC` under it (PRD wsl-device B4); the list is read from Windows without starting WSL, so opening the dialog never wakes a distribution.
Choosing one fills the Name with that name, which cannot be rewritten, and Advanced shows what Hide installs; Add sends the distribution with the same consent as a Host, and the dialog closes once the device is listed.
A distribution already added is dimmed with `Added as <name>`; one whose first-run setup is not finished is dimmed with `Finish setting up the distribution first`, and one on WSL 1 is dimmed with the command that switches it, `wsl --set-version <distribution> 2`.
A PC without WSL lists no WSL row, and a WSL whose list could not be read says so in one line under the list while the Hosts stay choosable.
The list is a radio group: the arrow keys move between the Hosts that can be chosen and Enter adds.
Advanced, closed at first, holds `What Hide installs there` in three lines (the helper and the `hide` command, the setup for the agents turned on, and that removing the device takes them off); the description above the list says Hide installs its helper on the device, so adding is still the one place the kit is agreed to (device-parity D-12).
A device's ⋯ menu offers `이름 바꾸기`, `테스트`, `연결 정보…`, Allow and install… (a device whose helper is not allowed) or Revoke helper… (one that is), and, after a separator, `제거…`; This Mac's menu has `연결 정보…` only, and a node's own row adds the end of its link to the core.
`이름 바꾸기` opens a field with the name and `저장`, one `rename_device` event, and the core keeps the name in the device's registration (`Runtime::rename_device`), so the rail tile, its tabs and the row show it; `hide device rename <device> <name>` does the same from a terminal, for a paired machine and a device alike.
`테스트` runs the device's test and opens `연결 정보…`, where its stages come in.
Revoke helper… and Remove… ask before they act, with the same confirmations and events as before; no button is focused when a confirmation opens.
Revoking the helper stops the device's terminals with its link, and the Revoke confirmation says so: its panes read `helper not allowed · allow it in Settings > Devices` in their body at once, take no key, and come back when Allow and install… gives the link again.
Connection details is a dialog with the SSH alias, the helper's state and folder, the helper program, the folder of the `hide` command and the SSH host key the consent is bound to, what the connection and the helper last said, the latest test with its stages, then every part of Hide's kit with a mark, its name and where it is when installed or its state and reason when not.
Installed is ✓, not on this machine is –, turned off is ○, outdated, not installed or removed is !, and failed is ✕; the state is also read out, since the mark is hidden from assistive technology, and a part that is not on the machine says so once, without repeating its reason.
A healthy kit is on no row.
Under the reason line a device grows only what the operator acts on: what its connection waits on, a changed server key's `ssh <alias>` with Copy, Retry helper for a failed helper even before the session connects, Allow and install… for a helper not allowed, a switch under way or stopped, and a part of Hide's kit that failed or was removed, outdated or never installed, as one line naming it with Reinstall.
What a device's ssh asks is answered on its row, in place of the failure line (`web/src/settings/DeviceSignIn.tsx`, PRD system-ssh-device-link B4-B12): a password, a key's passphrase or a one-time code shows ssh's own sentence over a masked field with `Sign in` and `Cancel`, and for a passphrase the `ssh-add` command (`ssh-add --apple-use-keychain <key>` on a Mac) with Copy, so the key is asked once; a server not yet in known_hosts shows its address and key fingerprint with `Trust this server and connect` and `Cancel`, neither focused nor primary; ssh's yes or no shows its sentence with `Continue` and `Cancel`; a security key shows `Touch your security key`; a sign-in page shows `Waiting for confirmation in the browser` with `Open`, which opens it in the default browser, its address, and that it waits up to 5 minutes; a device waiting for its turn behind the four that dial at once reads as connecting and shows no line, since there is nothing to do about it.
An answer is sent for the question on screen and cleared from the field at once; a wrong answer brings a new, empty field when ssh asks again, and no Retry shows while a question waits.
A connection that did not come up says one of three states as its reason line, each with its one action (the operator's 2026-10-30 decision, superseding the per-class row of PRD system-ssh-device-link B18-B21): `Sign-in needed` or `Sign-in refused`, with `Sign in` in Retry's place, after a reconnect Hide started on its own, a question that went unanswered or was cancelled, a browser check that timed out, or a sign-in the server refused; `Server key changed`, with `Check in a terminal` and `ssh <alias>` with Copy as its one action and no Retry, since a retry before that check is refused again; `Couldn't connect` with `Hide was updated after it started. Quit Hide and open it again to connect.` and no Retry, when an update replaced the running Hide on disk, since every attempt of that Hide is refused the same way and only opening it again connects; and `Couldn't connect` with Retry, ssh's last line in monospace in `연결 정보…` (a name not found, no answer, a failed proxy command, an access policy's refusal, any other failure), or, where ssh has no useful line, Hide's sentence: `ssh` is missing, with how to add it on the core's system (Optional features on Windows, `openssh-client` on Linux), OpenSSH is older than 8.4, or the device's system is one Hide does not run on (an Intel Mac, for one).
ssh's other words and the finer class stay in the diagnostic log, and a helper's `unavailable` or `unsupported` line is not repeated under an ssh failure, since the helper is the link (`deviceProblemLine` in `web/src/settings.ts`, B13).
The helper failure includes a device kit download or integrity failure and a build with no matching device release.
A device reached over ssh whose system Hide does not run on, such as an Intel Mac or a Windows ARM PC (`contracts/device-kits.json`, `refused`), shows `Couldn't connect` with `Hide does not run on this device's system.` and the row's Retry, not its helper's `unsupported` line, and offers no helper retry; the contract's reason goes to the diagnostic log; only a device not reached over ssh keeps the helper's `unsupported` line with its reason.
A Windows device dialed by a core that runs Herdr's engine shows `Couldn't connect` with `Windows devices connect only on the new engine. Nothing was installed on this one.` and the row's Retry, and no helper line: the device is not one Hide cannot run on, so neither its row nor its kit calls it unsupported.
Reinstall repairs only the parts the line names, reads Reinstalling… while the machine's kit work runs (B8), and the line goes when the parts are whole again; nothing else on the screen reacts, and the detail of every install goes to the diagnostic log (B18).
A kit pass that stopped before changing anything (a folder another account can change or a link in the way, an earlier Hide's open work or unreadable record, another Hide changing the same account) is one line under the machine with Retry, never a line per part: `Hide stopped before changing anything.`, what stopped it with the folder or file at fault, and what to change; when the folder is the state folder `HIDE_STATE_DIR` or `XDG_STATE_HOME` chose, the line names that variable and both ways back, another folder only the operator can change or unsetting it to return to `~/.hide/state`, and on the machine that runs the app the command that stops Hide's daemon there, which reads the variable only when it starts and outlives the app (INSTALL.md, When a kit pass stops before changing anything).
More registered projects than a pass checks is its own line, and a cause this build cannot name says only that the pass stopped and to update Hide, with Retry like the rest.
Retry runs the same pass again, which checks again what stopped it, and reads Installing Hide's kit… while it runs; a read while Settings is open answers the same way, so the line goes only once a pass can run (`web/src/settings/MachineKit.tsx`, `kitRefusalLine` in `web/src/settings.ts`).
A machine whose kit does not run says why in that place: a daemon run from neither the installed app nor a build Hide placed (a moved core runs from one), a device not allowed yet, a device that must be allowed again, or a platform this build does not carry (B11, B17, B21).
The first install reads Installing Hide's kit… under the device; the tab reads every machine once when it opens.
The add form has one Add button and lists, once, what the kit puts on the device and where (B12); a device registered earlier without the helper offers Allow and install… in its menu, with the same list.
The list says what Hide puts on the device: a helper and the `hide` command, the setup for the agents turned on, and that removing the device takes them off again and never touches another tool's files.
Removing a device asks once, names in one line what comes off that device (with its helper folder, `~/.hide/host-helper` by default) and that the records in `~/.hide` stay, or, when its helper is not connected, that the kit stays there; no button is focused when the confirmation opens (B22).
When another registered device reaches the same account on that machine, the line says the kit stays for it instead.
The page has no per-device agents line, no coordination retirement row and no Codex per pane row: the kit no longer turns Codex's shared daemon off, and a machine's agents are on the Agents tab.

The rows put the window's own machine first, named This Mac, and name the core's machine on a window of another machine by the name it was registered under before the core moved to it, else the name it gives itself (PRD core-host-node-move B2, `web/src/screenMachine.ts`).
The row of the machine that runs the core always wears a `core` badge with the hint `core 실행 중`, a one-Mac setup included, unlike the rail's crown (Q11 of the plan; B1 keeps the one-Mac rail unchanged).
Test and Retry are offered for a device this core dials, never for a machine that dials in, which has no SSH alias here.
No row offers to move the core, and no command does: every computer runs a core of its own (PRD device-connect-iroh D-35, B44).
On a window of a machine whose core runs elsewhere, which only a core moved before 1.2.0 left, that window's own row offers `Mac mini core와 연결 끊기…`, and its menu has no Remove…, since a machine does not remove itself.
`연결 끊기…` asks once, saying that the core refuses this Mac's pane input, file reads and browser actions at once and that reconnecting allows them again, and the window's strip then offers `다시 연결` (B16).
When that machine could not be made a Hide of its own on this connection, its own row says so in one line with `다시 시도`: `새 연결로 이전하지 못했어요 · ` and the cause, `SSH로 Mac mini에 연결하지 못했어요`, `Mac mini의 Hide가 거절했어요`, `Mac mini에 새 연결로 닿지 못했어요` or `이 컴퓨터에 저장하지 못했어요` (PRD device-connect-iroh B45; `splitFailureLine` in `web/src/coreLink.ts`).
The window goes on as the node of its core meanwhile; `다시 시도` ends the link and tries once more, and the line goes with it.

Settings > Agents shows the seven agents Hide supports, Claude Code, Codex, Grok, OpenCode, Pi, omp and Antigravity, in that order, for one machine at a time (PRD settings-cleanup B8 to B20, B67).
With a device registered, a switch at the top chooses This Mac or a device and the list below is that machine's; with none there is no switch.
This Mac comes first, as in Devices, and each machine is listed once.
A paired machine is not one of them: it runs its own Hide, so its agents, profiles and models are set in its own Settings, and Hide AI here offers none of its agents (PRD device-connect-iroh, Settings per machine).
`Checking this machine's agents…` shows only while a read runs or before the machine's first answer; a machine whose kit answered without listing an agent (another Hide held its account, a retirement it refused) says `Hide couldn't read this machine's agents.` with Check again.
A device that cannot be reached shows one line and Try again above its last installed-agent and profile list, dimmed and read-only; no permission, switch, add or edit control is offered until it reconnects.
A device connecting or switched off keeps the same read-only list below its connection state.
A machine whose kit cannot run says why above a list it may still have.
`Installed N` lists the agents whose program is found on the machine ([agent-hooks.md: Installed means the program is found](agent-hooks.md#installed-means-the-program-is-found)), or that are on and whose program went away, so they can still be switched off; a folder an agent creates does not count.
An agent installed where Hide has nothing to attach to on that machine's system yet is listed here too, with `Not on Windows yet` (that system's name) in place of its switch (PRD first-run-onboarding D-25, B49).
`↻ Check again` at its right asks the kit to read the machines again, reads `Checking…` while it runs and rests again after a moment, since a read that changes nothing sends no frame; a failed read leaves the list as it was and shows its reason in one line.
`Not installed N` is a folded list of the rest, each with its official mark, its name and `Install ↗`, which opens the vendor's installation guide (`web/src/settings/agentRows.ts`); an agent with no program has no switch.
An installed row is one line: the agent's official mark (the same in light and dark), its name, the `일부 기능` chip when it has one, how many of its sessions run when any do, its permission and a switch, with no line about what the switch puts there; `Docs ↗` to the kit's skills page for the agent shows only while the row is hovered or has keyboard focus, and always on a screen with no hover.
The switch is that machine's: turning an agent on installs what Hide adds for it there, turning it off takes out only what Hide installed (docs/agent-hooks.md), and an agent that is off wears no status.
No part of what Hide installs for an agent (its skill, its hook, plugin or extension) is named anywhere on the page: to the operator they are one connection between the agent and Hide.
Above the card, two small heads, `권한` (`Permissions`) and `켬` (`On`), name the columns every installed row ends with; on a sheet narrow enough that a row's controls wrap under its name, the heads are left out.
In the `권한` column of each installed row that Hide can start there is its permission on that machine, a select showing the choice alone; the open menu shows, under each choice, the command Hide will run for it in mono caption text, such as `claude --dangerously-skip-permissions` under `전부 허용` and `claude` under `매번 물어보기` (`codex -c approval_policy=never -c sandbox_mode=danger-full-access` for Codex, whose two overrides are one option): the program and the option that turns its questions off, leaving out the helper option a start also carries so the CLI does not stop to confirm that one (Claude's warning setting, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`), which says nothing the operator chooses.
The select offers `전부 허용` (`Allow everything`, the default for every operator, existing ones included, with no notice), `매번 물어보기` (`Ask every time`, the program alone, so the CLI asks as its own configuration says) and, after a separator, `직접 입력…` (`Custom…`), which opens a field under the row after the program's name.
The field is saved on Enter or when it loses focus, and Escape puts back what is saved; text that is not only options (a second command, a redirection, a substitution, an unclosed quote) is refused under the row with `옵션만 적어 주세요…`, and the saved text stays.
Pi asks no permission question, so its column says `묻지 않음` (`Never asks`) and offers no select; an agent shown with `Not on Windows yet` has no permission.
Right under the installed card, a tinted box of its own says what the permission covers, in three lines: `권한` as its heading, `✓ Hide가 띄우는 모든 에이전트에 적용돼요 (에이전트가 띄운 에이전트도 포함)`, and `✕ 터미널에 직접 친 claude, codex는 CLI 원래 설정을 따라요`; it names no start path, since every start Hide makes (a new tab, a worktree, a hand-off, `hide agent spawn`, a mission worker, a wake, a fork, a reopen) reads the same choice and there is no permission of a child's own.
The choice is the core's, per agent and per machine (`agent_permission.rs`; [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md) says how each start and restart reads it), and Hide never edits the agent's own configuration files.
Under each installed agent, its profiles unfold indented to its name (PRD agent-profiles B1, B2, B30).
With no profile of its own, only `프로필 추가` (`Add profile`) is there; once it has one, the built-in `기본` (`Default`) row comes first, then each profile, one line each: its name, `기본으로 시작` (`Starts by default`) on the agent's default, and its one state at the right end before a chevron.
Where a profile runs (its account folder or omp name, its program, its variables) is shown only in the dialog the row opens: the whole row opens it, lit on hover and marked by the chevron.
The states, most urgent first, are `설치 실패` with `다시 설치`, `제거 실패` with `다시 시도`, `설치 중`, `제거 중`, `래퍼가 다른 폴더를 써요` (`The program uses another folder`) when the profile's last start ran in another of the machine's accounts, as its hook reported (B25), `로그인 필요` (`Sign-in needed`) with `열어서 로그인`, which closes Settings and starts the agent with that profile in a new tab, in the checkout a start over Settings would take on that machine (the start panel opens instead where the machine has no checkout), so the agent shows its own sign-in, and once it is signed in the row shows no sign-in state on its next read, and a muted `확인 불가` (`Can't check`) when the sign-in did not answer (B10).
A profile that runs a program of its own, or an agent whose sign-in Hide cannot look at (omp, OpenCode), shows no sign-in state, and a signed-in account shows nothing for it.
With no state, the right end shows the account's weekly percent left on this machine (`72% 남음`), and `—` with the hint `다른 프로필과 같은 계정 위치예요` for a profile in an account a row above it already shows, such as a wrapper in the default account (B23); a device's profiles and an account not read yet show no usage.
A machine whose profiles were never read draws nothing here, a device whose Hide predates profiles says so in one line, and a device that is not connected keeps its list dimmed and read-only, with only names and the default mark: no state or usage.
`프로필 추가` opens a dialog whose fields follow the agent: an account folder with `선택…` (the folder chooser, only on the machine this window runs on), omp's profile name, or Antigravity's disabled account field with its reason; every agent has a program to run instead and extra environment variables, with the note that secrets belong in that program (B3).
`추가` sends one `agent_profile_change`; a field the machine refuses shows its reason under that field in the screen's language, worded from the reason code the machine sends (`profile_reason`; a machine whose Hide sends none, or one this build does not know, reads `기기가 이 값을 받지 않았어요`), and keeps what was typed, any other refusal shows one line under the form, and the dialog closes once the core says the profile was added (B4, B5, B13).
A row opens the same form to edit: its account is locked with the reason, its variables' values are read from the machine first and their fields wait until then, and each field saves as it is left, so there is no Save button; running agents keep what they started with (B26).
What was typed while the values were being read stays, and a change made while the last one is on its way is sent once that one is answered.
Closing the form saves its last change first: it stays open, busy, until the machine took it, and a refusal keeps it open with what was typed and the reason; values that could not be read are never saved.
The edit form's `기본으로 시작` makes it the agent's default, and `프로필 삭제` first asks the core what the delete would meet: with agents running with it, sleeping ones included, it lists them each with `보기` to its pane and deletes nothing; otherwise it says Hide takes its hooks out of that location and lists what uses the profile, each with what the delete does to it, before `삭제` (B27, B28): each project that starts with it goes back to the agent's default, and the favorite models in Settings › Agents that name it (`자주 쓰는 모델 N줄`, counted for this machine's profiles) stay as `없는 프로필` (PRD agent-profiles-phase2 B22, D-16).
When Settings › Hide AI's Runs on or fallback list names the profile on that machine, a `Hide AI` row says it stays as `없는 프로필` and is passed over (B21, B22).
The dialog stays, its `삭제` disabled, until the core answers: an agent that started with the profile since the check refuses the delete, and the dialog then lists it with `보기` as the check would have, while an agent still starting is listed `시작 중` with nothing to show yet.
The built-in row's form has no fields to change and no delete.
The list, its states and every check are the core's (`hide-core/src/agent_profiles.rs`, `web/src/settings/AgentProfiles.tsx`), the same as `hide agent profile` (B11).
A Claude Code, Codex, Grok, Pi, omp or Antigravity row that is on, whose sessions Hide counts from their session files, says `N sessions` (`세션 N`, muted) for its sessions running on that machine now, and nothing when it has none: a healthy agent says nothing; a sleeping agent is not running and is not counted.
The count is all the row says about sessions: it lists none and does not say whether Hide hears each one, because a session that runs without Hide is fixed from its own pane header (docs/status-model.md).
Grok wears a `일부 기능` chip (`Some features`, `部分功能`, `一部の機能`), on or off; Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Pi, omp and Antigravity have both prompt intake and spawn refusal and wear no chip.
Every other agent's row shows no status, on or off.
The chip opens a popover with every feature of the kit's feature table, grouped under Basics, session reading and multi-agent collaboration, with a heading, `✓ Supported` or `– Not supported` rather than color alone.
The chip, headings and feature text wrap within the popover in every interface language.
Every supported agent has Hide's integration (its hook, plugin or extension), so no row says its status is judged from the screen; a row whose id this build does not know is omitted and its id and device are diagnosed once.
The support order, names, links, logo ids and start eligibility come from the shared adapter's generated contract rather than web-owned lists.
Pi has native titles, Sleep and Fork from its complete session reader beside letters, Memory and the opener guard from Hide's extension; it has no structured question/plan wait or subagent count.
Pi starts with the existing first-prompt argument boundary and CLI model default, without an extra-folder argument.
omp has native titles, Sleep and Fork from its complete session reader beside guidance, letters, Memory, subagents and the opener guard from Hide's extension; its row wears no `일부 기능` chip.
Its current native/manual title wins over a generated goal; an explicit clear returns to the existing fallback without replaying the conversation.
An unanswered native `ask` enters the existing question state with bounded text and choices even with summaries off, and a correlated answer clears it.
Its question keeps the existing Answer tag in the pane header even while the native session holds for an operator reply.
Grok has native titles, Sleep and Fork from its complete session reader beside the opener guard, subagent count and letters from its own hook.
At rest its bell opens a turn that takes letters at the first tool call or at Stop; while working it takes due letters at either hook without a bell.
It has no new-prompt letter context or Memory, so its row keeps the `일부 기능` chip.
Its `/rename` title wins over a generated goal, an unanswered `ask_user_question` enters the question state with bounded text and choices, and a plan awaiting approval enters the approval state with the plan text.
Cursor is retired from new agent choice, onboarding and installation.
Its retained native chat reader preserves existing conversations, search and minute-precision timestamps without enabling a new start, resume or Sleep action.
Its `일부 기능` chip and existing guidance facts remain, and it offers no model or effort choice.
Archive resume sends the selected source path to the core, which decides whether the native reader requires that exact file proof before starting.
OpenCode has native titles, Sleep and Fork from its database reader beside letters, Memory, subagents and the opener guard from Hide's plugin; an unanswered native `question` enters the existing question state with its text and choices even with summaries off.
Its question keeps the existing Answer tag in the pane header, as omp's does.
