Antigravity has titles from agy's conversation index, Sleep, Fork (Hide opens the same conversation in a new pane with `agy --conversation <id>` and types `/fork` there once its composer reads empty by agy's composer declaration, first erasing what it holds (8 Backspaces a read, at most 64, within 10 s of its own), since the answer to agy's start-up mode query can leave text such as `27;1$y` in it and `/fork` typed behind text is a prompt, not the command (#1529); a screen with no composer found (an editor agy opened on a stray key, a question) is typed into never: the fork fails as `pane.fork_failed` with `fork.composer_unconfirmed` (`not_found`) logged, and a screen the declaration cannot otherwise confirm empty (a cut screen, text that outlasts the erase) is typed into as before and logged the same way; it is refused in a folder agy does not trust, where the new pane would open on agy's trust question; a read of agy's index that meets agy writing it is read again, up to 20 reads in a row inside the fork's 30 s wait; a fork that still fails closes its pane like any agent's failed fork, logged as `pane.fork_failed`, and leaves the conversation `/fork` made in agy's history, since agy 1.4.0 has no command that deletes one) and its conversation from its transcript, beside guidance, letters, Memory, subagents and the spawn guard from Hide's `hide` block; its row wears no `일부 기능` chip, so no feature popover opens for it, and the bell it does not ring yet is named in [agent-hooks.md](agent-hooks.md#antigravity-hides-hook-block).
Its tool-permission question reaches Needs You as an approval, as the engine reads it (first seen under Herdr 0.9.3 on 2026-10-16); its folder-trust question is unmeasured ([agent-hooks.md](agent-hooks.md#antigravity-hides-hook-block)).
Escape closes the popover and focus returns to the chip.
Any part that failed, was removed or is outdated shows one line under that agent's row, `Hide 연결이 끊겼어요` (`Disconnected from Hide`), with Reinstall, which reinstalls every piece of that agent, only while the agent is on; which piece it is, its state and its path (`Hook: Removed · ~/.claude/settings.json`, `Plugin: Outdated: …` for OpenCode's hook piece) are the line's hover and Reinstall's accessible description, never visible text; a hook the operator removed stays removed until Reinstall or switching the agent off and on.
An agent that is on whose copy is older than Hide's minimum shows, under its row and without Reinstall (which would be refused again), the versions (`2.1.200 → 2.1.278 이상으로 업데이트하면 Hide와 연결돼요`), the one command that updates that copy with Copy, or the agent's install page when Hide cannot tell how it was installed, and the program's path; its refused pieces leave the problem line, and the next kit read after the update puts them in without a press ([agent-hooks.md](agent-hooks.md#updating-an-agent-that-is-too-old)).
An agent that is on and found whose hook Hide could not put in says why in one muted line without Reinstall and without naming the piece, which is its hover, since nothing is broken: its program has not created its configuration folder yet, a file of that name is not Hide's, or Hide does not write that hook on this system (`Hide puts it in once the agent makes this folder: ~/.config/opencode`).
A switch-off whose removal did not finish keeps the row from reading Off and says so in its own line.
There is no row or switch for Codex per pane, no per-agent CLI group (that is Hide AI's) and no hooks diagnosis list: the row statuses are the diagnosis.
`자주 쓰는 모델` (Favorite models), under the installed card on this machine and with no table for a remote one, lists the agent items missions and Desk hand work to, at most 16 (the Conductor's candidate list, kept as `conductor_settings.candidates`): each row's agent item, its model from that item's own list (disabled with why when that list cannot be read), its effort automatic or fixed, and what it is for; an agent this machine has not installed is dimmed with `설치 안 됨`.
The agent cell is the start panel's item list for this machine, named the same way (PRD agent-profiles-phase2 D-01, D-06, B7): every agent Hide starts, its built-in item first and then the profiles this machine lists for it (`Claude Code`, `Claude Code · work`, `Claude Code · opengateway`), so the same agent can take several rows that differ by item; with no profile on the machine the cell names the agents as before (B1).
Picking another item clears the row's model and effort, which were the other item's; the built-in item is kept as a row was before profiles, so an older row reads as the agent's built-in item (B14).
The model cell lists the picked item's models by the names its agent shows (a gateway profile's `deepseek` or `glm` names), and the agent's own list where the profile's could not be read (B8, B9).
A row whose profile this machine no longer lists is dimmed as `Claude Code · 없는 프로필` with `프로필이 지워졌어요` in place of its other cells, its item is in no list to pick again, and no worker starts as it; picking another item mends the row, and nothing removes it by itself (B15).
Profiles are managed above, in the agent rows: the cell has no profile control of its own (B24).
With no favorite models the Conductor chooses among the installed agents; with some, Hide refuses a worker that is none of them, judged by the profile the worker would actually start with, so one that would inherit its Conductor's profile is refused by a candidate of the built-in default, and the refusal names the item it would have started (`mission_candidate_refused`, [delivery.md: Missions](delivery.md#missions); B13).
A mission admits the favorite models it was created with and the ones this group holds now, so a model added or changed applies to a running mission at once (a changed row's new form is in this group now), while one removed, like a changed row's old form, stays admitted in a mission created with it, so a node planned on it is not stranded; a mission created with none keeps admitting any installed agent, and models added later do not narrow it.
The group's footer says this: `미션과 Desk가 일을 맡길 때 여기서 먼저 골라요. 비워 두면 미션이 이 기기에 설치된 에이전트 중에서 알아서 골라요. 추가하거나 바꾼 모델은 진행 중인 미션에도 바로 쓰이고, 뺀 모델은 다음 미션부터 빠져요.`
A plan names each worker's and checker's item as a favorite model does, its profile by the `--profile` value, and is refused when a node not yet started names an item none of the favorite models admits, so the map never shows a worker that cannot start.
A node that started keeps the items its worker and checkers were planned with, so a profile renamed or removed since does not refuse the replan.
A row's purpose is a text field with a menu beside it of the wording Hide suggests (`범용`, `어려운 작업`, `간단한 작업`, `리뷰·검증 전용`); a pick fills the field with that text, which stays free text the operator can write over, and the core keeps the text only: no role is stored, and a mission reads the text as it always has.
Desk's agent menu lists these models first under `자주 쓰는 모델`, each with its model, its agent and its purpose line, one pick setting the agent, model and effort together as one `desk_select`; a model that names a profile, an agent Settings switched off, or a model the agent's last session did not offer is not listed, and the agents below it are as they were ([desk.md in with-hide/hide-private](desk.md)).
Idle agents (`Sleep after`) and Starting work (`Link the issue in pull requests`) follow the list.
`Sleep after` offers Never, 1 hour, 2 hours, 4 hours, 12 hours and 24 hours, and reads 12 hours until the operator chooses; a choice the operator made, Never and 24 hours included, stays across restarts, and a choice an earlier build stored moves once (2 or 3 days to 24 hours, its 24-hour default to 12).
The note under it counts this machine's sleeping agents and says when an agent sleeps: `{{count}} sleeping now. A child agent sleeps as soon as it reports to its parent. Working and on-screen agents never sleep, and unread ones wait until you read them.`

The first-run agent choice is the first step of the first-run wizard (see First run below), PRD agent-adapters-onboarding and PRD first-run-onboarding, shown when this Mac's kit has never run.
It lists the seven agents Hide supports, Claude Code, Codex, Grok, OpenCode, Pi, omp and Antigravity in that order, as tiles in a grid, each with the agent's own mark (or a two-letter monogram where no official mark is bundled, `docs/BRAND.md`) and its state: the agents installed here are on and show a check, an agent not installed is dimmed, has no switch and links its official install page (`Install ↗`), an agent installed on a system where Hide has nothing to attach to yet (the kit's `unsupported_system`) is dimmed and says `Not on Windows yet` with that system's name, with no switch, no install link and no entry under `What changes` (D-25, B49), and a tile is a switch (`role="switch"`) pressed with Space or Enter.
Claude Code and Codex are on when they are installed; every other installed agent is on too, since the operator chose the full set, and `Continue` installs what is left on.
Under each name the tile says what Hide knows: `Checking…`, then `Ready` or `Not signed in` for the agents whose sign-in Hide checks (Claude Code, Codex, Grok, Pi and Antigravity; Antigravity says `Installed` until Hide AI's folder for it is made, because Hide reads agy's sign-in only once that folder is ready), and `Installed` for OpenCode and omp, whose sign-in Hide cannot read (`tileStatus` in `web/src/firstRun.ts`).
With no agent installed, one line says so and links Claude Code's and Codex's install pages, and Continue still goes on.
One sentence above the tiles says that Hide adds its own entries to the config of each agent turned on and changes nothing before Continue, and `What changes` opens the list of the places Continue writes for each agent left on, each with the agents it is for and no piece named (at the places the kit reports, with the account home written as `~` on every system), and the `hide` command's link; the list follows each flip (B11, D-26).
`Continue` is the only button and the only way on: Escape, a click outside and a close button do nothing, so a stray key cannot finish a choice that leaves Claude Code and Codex off with no hooks; with every tile off, Continue installs nothing and moves on.
Continue holds, reading `Applying…`, from the press until this Mac's kit pass lands, and a second press meanwhile changes nothing; a chosen agent whose piece failed keeps the step on screen with the agent and the kit's reason in one line, and the button reads `Try again`.
The question belongs to the kit record (`awaiting_choice` in `~/.hide/kit/installed.json`), not to the window: closing the app, reloading the page or a failed save leaves it asked, and the next launch asks again until Continue has saved.
The tiles are drawn from the live availability plus the operator's own flips, so an agent that becomes installed while the step is open appears on, and Continue sends exactly what is shown.
A Mac whose kit has already run (an existing install) never sees it, and nothing is held back from it.
Until the choice is made the kit puts nothing for Claude Code or Codex on a fresh Mac either, so the hooks wait for Continue or for Settings, Agents.
A device whose own record waits gets the saved choice once per run, by what is installed there, as soon as it reports (also after it was not connected when Continue was pressed); with nothing chosen it is told that, so its record stops waiting, and nothing is installed for the agents this Mac has.
The choice decides only the agents installed on this Mac, the ones that had a switch here: an agent the device has and this Mac does not is on there, as that device's own first step would show it, and an agent the operator left off here stays off there (a Windows PC without Codex no longer leaves the Codex of a WSL distribution it adds off).
A device that still waits after it was sent the choice is logged once (`first_run_choice.unanswered`) and not sent it again until it reconnects.
Under `Continue`, one line names the agent Hide AI will use: the first agent in that fixed order that is switched on in the step and signed in (`firstRunAgent` in `web/src/hideAi.ts`, "Hide AI uses Claude Code", `Hide AI가 쓰는 에이전트: Claude Code`), read from this machine's rows of `status.background_ai.providers[].state`, which the step asks the core to probe while it is open; with no such agent the line is absent, and it follows each flip of a tile (PRD settings-cleanup D-18, B45, B46).
An agent installed later appears in these rows off and is never turned on by a pass.

### Skills & MCP

Skills & MCP is a page of Settings' machine group, after Agents, and shows one machine's store of skills and MCP servers at a time (PRD skill-mcp-manager; layout C, then the visual the operator approved on 2026-11-20).
Under the page title one muted line says what the page is for (`Add once, for every agent on <machine>`), and at its right stand `n updates` while a check found newer versions, the machine picker when Hide knows more than one machine, `Copy from <machine>` on another machine's page, and the primary Add.
Each kind is one table, with no other view and no box: its small uppercase title and count head the name column, hairlines separate the rows, and spacing separates the sections.
Each cell column is headed by its agent's logo alone (`AgentMark`, on its plate where the logo manifest says), named in its hint; a skill row has one cell per skill folder (Claude, Shared, Antigravity), and Shared's head is the overlapped logos of the installed agents that read that folder (Codex, Grok, OpenCode, Pi, omp) with `Shared` under them, whose hint names them and says that Grok and omp read the Claude folder as well; when only one installed agent reads it, the head is that agent's logo alone.
An MCP row has one cell per agent, since each agent keeps its own MCP file.
Only agents installed and switched on in Settings › Agents have a cell.
Above the tables, `Hide 스킬` (`Hide skills`) lists the three skills Hide installs for every agent that is on (`hide`, `hide-mission`, `hide-issue`), each with its mark, what it is for and the help it points at; they are the kit's own, come and go with each agent's switch in Settings › Agents, and have no remove or switch here, so the group's note says they cannot be removed.
A row is a 32-pixel icon tile, the name, the short version as a small pill (the tag, else the commit's first seven characters, `folder`, `remote` or `npm latest`) with a dot when a newer version was found (B36), and one muted line under the name: the item's description, else what names it without a path (a server's package, image or host, a skill's repository or folder name); where it comes from is shown only when the row unfolds.
A GitHub skill's tile is its owner's picture as the store keeps it, never loaded from the web by the screen ([ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md), the store), and the GitHub mark until one is kept; another git host, a folder, a package and a remote server have their own marks.
A cell shows its state and nothing else, and is no switch: ✓ in a soft lime circle for applied, a faint hollow ring for off, — for not supported (its hint says by which agent), ! in a soft red circle for failed, and a hand in a soft amber circle for a copy of the same name Hide did not write; a cell on that an agent session started before still runs without says `From new sessions` in its hint.
A legend under the tables names the marks and says that a logo's hint names its agent.
Each row has one switch at its end, which turns the item on or off for every agent; the switch column has one width in both tables, so they end at the same edge; a row on somewhere but not everywhere draws its switch on, and pressing it turns the item on everywhere (D-40).
`hide skill off <name> --agent <agent>` can still leave a row partly on, which its cells show.
A press anywhere on a row but its switch unfolds it below itself, and a second press folds it; the unfolded band is indented under the name, with one column of labels: a skill's source (a path with home as `~`, its front shortened before the last name and the whole path in its hint, and `using the last copy` when a followed folder is gone) and its file count; a server's command or address and its keys as `NAME ••••••` with Change, which opens a dialog that takes new values and never shows the old ones; for a row partly on, which agents it is off for and that the switch turns it on for all.
Then `Problem`, one line for each cell that needs a press: the agent's logo, its name and a few words for the kind of failure (`The config file is not valid, so Hide left it as it was`, `Hide could not change it`, `Not in place yet`) with the file, and Retry, the kit's own sentence going to the diagnostic log; or a copy Hide did not write with Take over, which makes that copy the item once its confirm is applied.
Then one row of quiet buttons: `Update` when a newer version was found, `Show files`, `Open folder` or `Open source`, and Delete.
Delete goes on a second press, the first turning it into `Confirm delete` in red in place and a press elsewhere putting it back; it takes the item out of every agent at once and leaves a notice with Undo, which brings it back whole; the undo list under the tables, folded behind its count, keeps the newest twenty, and bringing back a name the store holds again says it cannot come back while an item of that name is in the list.
A delete whose agent-file cleanup fails keeps the row switched off with the failed cell and the refusal, so the operator can repair the file and delete again; it offers no Undo for an item that has not left the list.
`Only in agents` lists, in the same row style, what agents hold that Hide did not write, each with `Apply to all`, or a choice of whose copy when the copies differ.
Apply to all and Take over first show what comes in, in the install confirm's style (B20): a skill's copy is read like a folder added, with its source, skill name and description, and the agents it goes to; a server's is shown as MCP 추가's confirm shows one (B9), with the command and arguments or the address, its keys' names and never a value, and the agents it goes to, all read from that copy's own place, not from the store's server of the same name.
A server Hide cannot read says so and offers no Apply; Cancel takes nothing in, and only Apply does.
If the chosen copy changes after the confirm was read, Apply refuses it without replacing the stored item or changing agent settings; close and reopen to confirm the new copy.
The confirm shows the reading its own request made: another pane reading the same or another source at the same time never changes what it shows.
Add takes a GitHub address, a git URL or a folder, reads it, and opens one confirmation with Source, Skills (names and descriptions), and Agents; several skills are a checkbox list with available skills checked by default, one skill has no checkbox, and Install N skills follows the checked count and is disabled at zero, with no separate selection step; anything else is read as an MCP paste (a command, an address or a README's JSON), whose fields the operator checks, the command and each of its arguments a field of its own as the paste was read, so an argument with a space in it stays one, and whose key values are typed into hidden fields on this screen only.
A paste that yields no server opens these same fields empty for manual input (B8).
An update opens with the old and new version and the commits' subjects, and takes nothing until confirmed; `n updates` walks every item a check found newer and checks again as it opens; opening the page also checks every git item once, even while no update is known.
An agent's `hide skill add`, `hide skill update`, `hide mcp add` or `hide mcp update` stands as that agent's person's turn, `Install confirm · <name>` ([status-model.md](status-model.md#a-persons-turn-an-agent-declares)), and 확인하기 opens this page on that machine with the same confirm, whose Install or Cancel the command waits for; the page also lists the confirms waiting on it at its head.
Each such confirm also stands in the window's lower right corner as an important notice with its line (`스킬 설치 요청: <name>`) and 확인하기, which opens the same confirm and takes the notice away; the notice stays until the confirm is answered or gone, so an operator who is in another screen is not left to find it in 내 차례 (`web/src/storeAskNotice.ts`).
Before the machine's first report the page shows three quiet rows, and a machine with nothing yet says `Nothing on <machine> yet` with Add (and `Copy from` on another machine).
A machine that is not connected shows its last tables dimmed with no control and one line saying so; a store whose MCP keys' secret was lost says that nothing can change and links the start-over by hand ([agent-hooks.md](agent-hooks.md#when-the-mcp-keys-secret-is-lost)).
A failed device copy shows the same failed mark beside Copy from, with its cause in the hint; the button is available again for a retry, and a new attempt clears that failure while it waits.
Test owners: `web/e2e/skills-mcp.spec.ts`, `web/e2e/skills-mcp-cli.spec.ts`, `web/src/settings/SkillsTab.test.tsx`, `web/src/settings/skillsMcp.test.ts` and `hide-kit/src/store/avatar.rs`.

### Mission

Settings › Mission (`미션`; the tab was named Conductor until the skill and the map became hide-mission), in the machine group after Hide AI, keeps what a Conductor plans with on this machine (PRD software-factory-conductor D-31, B48, B49).
The page's words call the agent that runs a mission the mission (`미션이 알아서 골라요`, `미션이 하는 일`) and never Conductor, a name it does not introduce; it says `워크트리` and `브랜치` for what the cleanup removes and keeps, `검증` for the section's review and run check (`리뷰`, `실행 확인`), and `생각 깊이` for a model's effort, as Desk's agent bar does.
`동시 실행 상한` is how many agents one mission runs at once, reviewers and verifiers included and the Conductor not counted, from 1 to 16; `기본 범위` is how far a mission goes when the operator does not say, up to ready pull requests or through the merge.
`머지되면 워크트리 정리` (Clean up a worktree once merged), right under them and on by default, is a switch with one line under it: `작업이 도착 브랜치에 머지되면 그 워크트리와 빌드 캐시를 지워요. 브랜치는 남기고, 고치던 파일이 남아 있거나 쓰는 중이면 그대로 둬요.`
On, a done node whose pull request GitHub reports merged, or a done `direct` change whose branch Hide read in the mission's destination, has that branch's worktree removed with its build cache when its tree is clean and no agent pane or running program is in it, the branch always kept; a worktree that holds changes or is in use stays, is named in the diagnostic log and stays in the project's disk cleanup (`머지됨 N → 정리`), and nothing is removed while GitHub's answer is missing ([hide-private missions.md: What Hide settles in a mission by itself](delivery.md#missions)).
Off, missions delete nothing. Unlike the values below, the switch applies to every mission at once, a running or closed one included.
`일 맡길 에이전트와 모델` is one row with a link, `자주 쓰는 모델 열기 · N개`, that opens Settings › Agents; the list the Conductor chooses workers and checkers from is kept there as the favorite models (Settings › Agents, above), and this page has no table of its own.
`검증` holds one control, and the checkers come from the favorite models.
`검증 강도` is a new mission's verification strength, `빠르게`, `보통` (the default) or `꼼꼼하게`, one segmented choice with what it has the Conductor do, how often a node is reviewed again and the work it suits under it, as `hide mission help` states the rules.
The page has no switch for which kind of agent checks: the Conductor always prefers a checker of a different kind of agent than the node's worker when the favorite models have one, by `hide mission help`; a setting kept with the switch that once chose it reads without it.
It is only the default: the Conductor proposes each mission's strength in its plan and changes it when the operator says so, before or during the mission, and the map's head shows it ([A mission's map](#tasks-card-tabs-and-the-pr-list)).
A setting kept before strength (the review and verify defaults) reads as `보통`.
Every edit sends only the field it changes and the core checks the result; a refusal shows inline, and a mission already created keeps the cap, scope and strength it was created with, and its favorite models, with any added or changed since.

### Hide AI

Settings > Hide AI is the agent Hide runs behind the scenes (labels, worktree names, Project Memory analysis), separate from the agents the operator runs (PRD settings-cleanup D-14 to D-18, B33 to B47).
Every value is the core's `status.background_ai`; the tab sends `ai_settings` events, one per intent, and an edit is pending until the snapshot carries it, so a choice the core refused simply returns to what the snapshot says.

- **Use Hide AI** is a switch on its own at the top.
  Off, Hide makes no model call: agent titles show each session's own text, worktree names are typed, and Project Memory analysis waits while saved Memory search keeps working.
  Everything below it dims and is out of reach (`inert`), and the stored values stay as they were, so turning it on resumes with them.
- **Runs on** has an Agent select and a Model select.
  The Agent select lists agent items, named as the start panel names them (PRD agent-profiles-phase2 D-01, D-06, B16): each agent's built-in item, then the profiles its machine lists for it (`Claude Code`, `Claude Code · work`, `Claude Code · opengateway`); with no profile anywhere it names the agents alone, as before (B1).
  It lists only items the core would accept (`selectable`: installed, signed in as that item's account, a Hide AI backend, a call that cannot change files) with each official mark, a profile item's wearing its profile badge as in the start menu (`AgentMark`), and the chosen item stays in it with its state even when it has a problem; the same row shows its state as symbol and words (Signed in, Not signed in, Out of usage until 3:10 PM).
  A chosen item whose profile its machine no longer lists stays as `Claude Code · 없는 프로필`, which cannot be chosen again, with no state of its own; Hide AI passes over it, and nothing removes it but the operator (B21).
  An agent signed in on a connected device is listed beside this machine's as its name and the device's, such as `Claude Code · WSL Ubuntu`, and choosing it runs Hide AI there on the device's own login (PRD wsl-device B11, B12).
  A first run takes an agent signed in on the core's machine before a device's, and a choice the operator made never moves on its own, not even when the same agent signs in here later.
  While the chosen device is not connected its row reads Unavailable, nothing wakes the device for Hide AI, and its work resumes once the device is back (B13); it cannot be chosen again until then.
  The Model select lists what the item reported, by the names its agent shows (a gateway profile's `glm-4.6`), with `CLI default` first where the agent can be asked without a model, and a marker "Fixed list" when an older peer reports that catalog form; a profile whose list could not be read offers its agent's list (B9); the stored model is always offered, and a list that failed to load leaves it in place with one line.
- **If <Runs on> can't answer** lists the items the operator added, in the order added, each with a number, its mark and state, its own model select and a remove ×; there is no switch and no reordering, so an empty list is no fallback and a different order is a remove and an add.
  Another item of the Runs on agent may be one, such as `Claude Code · opengateway` under `Claude Code · work` (B16); an entry whose profile is gone is dimmed as `Claude Code · 없는 프로필` with its model disabled, and keeps its remove ×.
  `+ Add agent` opens a menu of selectable items not yet used; installed agents Hide AI cannot use yet are listed under "Hide AI can't use these yet", dimmed, each with its reason (not signed in, can't guarantee it only reads, not supported yet), and an agent that is not installed is not listed.
  Its note explains that exceeding the configured usage threshold, signing out, exhausting provider usage or provider unavailability tries the next listed agent.
  Only an empty list adds that Hide AI waits for Runs on to become available again.
  With nothing to offer the button is disabled.
  Choosing a listed agent as Runs on takes it off the list.
- **Usage limit** (PRD hide-ai-usage-limit D-01 to D-09) is one select of 80%, 90%, 95% or No limit under the fallback list, 90% by default and for an install that never chose; a stored percent that is none of them is still shown.
  One value covers every agent: the row says "Up to 90% of each agent's week" and a note says that past it Hide AI moves to the next agent in the list while work the operator starts (worktree names, issue review) still runs.
  The select shows the core's value, so a choice stays as it was until the core accepts it.
  The Runs on and fallback rows add the week to the state when the weekly usage read gave a figure ("Signed in · 86% this week"), and an agent at or past the limit reads "Past your limit · 93% this week · resets Mon 9:00"; an agent with no figure shows none and is never held back, and the Add agent menu shows the state alone.
- While another item answers, one line under Runs on reads "Using Codex · Claude Code is out of usage until 3:10 PM" (or "is past your 90% limit until Mon 9:00"), naming both items by their names, "Using Claude Code · opengateway · Claude Code · work is signed out", or the gone item itself, "· Claude Code · 없는 프로필" (PRD agent-profiles-phase2 B18), and disappears when Runs on answers again.
  With the list empty and Runs on unable to answer, the Agent row says why and that Hide AI is paused; with every listed agent past the limit it reads "Hide AI is paused · every agent is past your 90% limit this week", agent labels already made stay as they are while no new one is made (a turn that ends meanwhile shows its session's own text) and Memory analysis waits, and both resume on their own when the limit is raised or the week turns; nothing else on screen changes, and a failure the operator cannot act on stays in the diagnostic log.
- With no agent signed in, one line says "Sign in to <first agent that is on> to use Hide AI" (or to turn an agent on in Agents when none is); it appears only after every agent has been probed, and Hide's other features keep working without AI.
- Hide AI runs the core machine's agents unless the operator chose a device's, so in a window on another machine every sign-in state and line above names the core's machine ("Not signed in on Mac mini", "Claude Code is signed out on Mac mini", "Sign in to Codex on Mac mini to use Hide AI"); the agents have no one sign-in command, so the fix is the machine in words, as the move's Hide AI check says it; on the core's own machine nothing names a machine, and a device's agent is already named with its device (PRD core-host-node-move B15, B1).
- **Features** holds Agent summaries (the `agent_summary` switch of `ai_settings`: off, no agent label is asked for, the one being made is dropped, and every surface names each agent by its session's own title or its provider with no AI line or written question; on again, the kept labels return at once and each pane's current turn is asked for) and Worktree names (the same `issue_settings` value the Issues tab held).

## Mobile companion

The default connection works the same way on macOS, Windows and Linux: enable Mobile, show the QR and scan it with the phone camera.
The phone and the computer are the only endpoints that can read their messages; Hide's relay cannot read the content it forwards.
No Hide account, login, payment or Tailscale setup appears in this connection flow.

The hosted phone app at `app.withhide.dev` and the advanced Tailscale app at `/m/` share every list, detail and command screen.
The hosted start page shows the paired-device list; choosing a device opens the bundle matching its phone protocol at `/p/<n>/`, and until it opens the page shows the blinking owl over the device name, `연결 중…` and 다른 기기 고르기 (`DeviceConnecting`, `web/src/mobile/HostList.tsx`).
With exactly one saved device that has not refused this phone, the start page skips the list and opens that device as if its row had been chosen, so the phone app opens on it at launch (`hostToOpen`, `web/src/mobile/hosts.ts`); two or more devices, or one marked 연결 해제됨, keep the list.
That device's own screen reaches the list through the header's device sheet and the Settings tab, which keep 기기 추가; the demo stays on the list and the first screen only.
Every way back to the list, 다른 기기 고르기 on the `연결 중…`, version and refusal screens and leaving the demo, lands on a list that does not open the device again: the start page's own `연결 중…` shows it in place, and the others open it at `/?list=1`; an address that names a device (a link or a notification tap), a pairing link, 기기 추가 (`/?add=1`) and a computer's own add page (`/?add=computer`) go where they did.
A QR opens the shared device-first page described below, with an escaped, wrapping device name, a short line about viewing and replying to agents, 연결, the encryption assurance and five-minute expiry guidance.
Its code leaves the address before connection and is never stored.
Expired and already-used codes retain the device's existing `code_expired` wording, and four paired phones retain the existing limit guidance.
An unsupported device uses the shared version guidance below, with the device name, labelled phone app and device protocol versions, and 다른 기기 고르기.

In the hosted app, the header's device chip opens the shared bottom sheet, titled 기기, where each saved device is one row of two lines: its name on one line, cut short when long, and under it the state's dot and word with, at the right, its 내 차례 and 작업 중 counts as two small numbers in the desktop's style (내 차례 in the hold colour, 작업 중 grey), or its last successful contact when offline.
Each row leads with the device's icon in a small rounded tile, a laptop for a paired computer; the device being viewed is outlined and its word is 지금 보는 중, with no extra line.
A device whose protocol differs from the open bundle shows a warning dot and both versions in its state's place, with no counts; selecting it asks the root loader for its matching bundle or update guidance.
Neither the root list nor a bundle's picker authenticates to an incompatible device to read its status.
Choosing another device replaces the page and closes the old connection; drafts and pending actions do not move between devices.
기기 추가, the sheet's last row, opens a choice first, titled 기기 추가 under a back arrow: `Hide가 있는 컴퓨터` (Mac · Windows · Linux, its QR scanned to connect it to this phone directly) with a chevron, and `SSH 서버`, which presses nothing and says it is added in the computer's Hide (설정 › 기기 › 기기 추가 › 컴퓨터) and then shows under that computer by itself, because a phone never reaches an SSH server on its own.
`Hide가 있는 컴퓨터` opens the add page, which is the first screen's title and three steps under a back arrow, the big `QR 스캔` button, and `QR 링크 붙여넣기 ›` as one small row under it that opens a link field and 연결 on one line in place, so the keyboard never covers 연결; its back returns to the choice, and the choice's back to the list, or to the first screen when no device is kept.
다시 연결 on a disconnected device and the demo's way to a real computer open the add page directly (`/?add=computer`), since what they add is known.
The start page has no computer to ask its language, so the choice and the add page speak the phone's own language choice, else the language the last computer this phone showed chose (kept in this browser as `hide.phone.macLanguage`, absent while that computer follows its system), else the phone's language: the same language the app spoke.
The browser holds at most sixteen devices: a full browser says so above `QR 스캔` before anything is scanned, and a new device is refused while a kept one can still reconnect; a failed storage write is reported inline.
기기 지우기 is a device row's menu, opened by a long press (or a right-click or the menu key), never a button on the row; it asks for confirmation before removing that browser's saved connection and its notification subscription; scanning a fresh QR connects it again.
A device that refused this phone's credential (`revoked`) stays listed as a dimmed row marked 연결 해제됨, with the one line 7일 동안 쓰지 않았거나 이 기기에서 해제했어요 and 다시 연결, which opens the QR scanner, and 기기 지우기 in its long-press menu.
The browser keeps that device's name and room but not the refused credential, opens no status check, reconnect or notification worker for it, and a link to it shows the revoked guidance without dialing; a fresh QR from the same device clears the mark.
When a relay device is unreachable, the list stays dimmed and the list, detail and start sheet say `<device>의 Hide가 꺼져 있거나 잠자고 있어요 · 다시 시도 중`, with no Tailscale wording.
The existing automatic reconnect resumes when the device answers.

The first hosted pairing offers the home-screen hint until dismissed, once per browser storage, and keeps the existing 알림 켜기 action, denied-permission line and Safari-tab installation line.
Each saved device owns its own notification subscription; tapping a notification selects that device and opens its agent even when another device was last shown, on 볼 것 while the agent has a View to look at, as its eye row does.
The hosted app asks for no account, login or payment and loads no external script or analytics.
The following Tailscale-specific address and transport guidance remains for the advanced direct connection; the list, conversation, reply, quick keys, start and wake behavior is common to both transports.

### Settings › Devices' phones

A phone is one more device: Settings has no page of its own for phones, and Settings › Devices lists them in a group `폰` after `다른 기기와 연결` (operator review 2026-11-23; `web/src/settings/PhoneRows.tsx`, the rules in `web/src/mobileSettings.ts`).
The group's head carries only the switch 폰에서 Hide 열기, off at first; the default method is Hide's Tokyo relay, without a login, account or payment step.
An upgraded setup that already used Tailscale keeps its method and paired phones until the operator changes it.
While off, no relay link or phone connection remains and retry cannot create one.
Each paired phone is one row drawn as a computer's is: its name, a line under it with when it was last seen while it is away, whether notifications are on or off, and the days left before the seven-day revoke once it has been away a day, one state (`● 연결됨` or `연결 안 됨`), and ⋯ with `이름 바꾸기`, `연결 정보…` and, after a line, `연결 해제…`.
The pairing record carries no system of the phone's own, so the row shows none; the name it paired with comes from what the phone said it is (`iPhone`, `Android`).
`이름 바꾸기` opens the devices' rename field: `저장` keeps the operator's name beside the one the phone paired with in this computer's phone records (`mobile_rename`, saved before any list shows it), every list and `hide phone list` show it, and an empty field gives the phone its own name back; `hide phone rename <phone> <name>` does the same from a terminal.
`연결 정보…` says the way it connects, when it was last seen, its notifications and, once renamed, the name it paired with; `연결 해제…` asks `<name> 연결을 해제할까요?` with `바로 끊겨요. 다시 쓰려면 QR을 새로 찍어야 해요.` and `연결 해제`, which closes that phone at once.
With no phone the group says `연결한 폰이 없어요. + 기기 추가에서 폰을 고르세요.`.
One row at the group's foot, `폰 연결 방식`, names the method (`Hide 중계 (도쿄)` or `Tailscale로 직접 연결`) and opens a window with the relay's one connection row (연결 중…, 연결됨 · Hide 중계 (도쿄), 중계에 닿지 않아요 · 다시 시도 중 with 지금 다시, 이 Hide가 너무 오래됐어요 with 업데이트, or the relay's capacity refusal reason) over the note that only the phone and this device can read the contents, and under 고급 Tailscale로 직접 연결.
Only while 폰에서 Hide 열기 is on (or a removal is still owed) and the method is down or refused does one line over the phone rows say why, with 지금 다시 or 업데이트 where they help (`phoneProblem`); a relay still connecting, or one that works, says nothing there.
A removed Tailscale CLI cannot block relay pairing; its unfinished cleanup is that line while the connected relay's QR remains available.
The update action opens Settings > About; failures stay in that line without a banner or popup.
Opening the page makes no code and voids none.
Add device › 폰 opens the QR window: it turns 폰에서 Hide 열기 on when it is off, shows the method's connection row or Tailscale's failing steps until the way is ready, then makes one code and shows the QR, 폰으로 스캔하세요, app.withhide.dev and the code's m:ss countdown; on the relay two lines say how each phone scans it: `iPhone: App Store에서 Hide 앱을 받아 ‘QR 스캔’을 누르세요.` with an App Store link that opens outside Hide, and `다른 폰: 카메라로 스캔하고 열리는 페이지에서 ‘연결’을 누르세요.` with Add to Home Screen after it (Tailscale keeps its one line); an expired code offers QR 보기 for a fresh one, a phone that pairs closes the window, and closing it takes the code with it.
Pairing spends the five-minute code; a fifth phone is refused on the phone with the limit.

Switching Tailscale로 직접 연결 selects that method and closes the previous method's phone connections.
Only the selected method's phones appear and count toward its four-phone limit; switching back restores its previous phones.
Tailscale's checks, troubleshooting links and owned-serve failures stay in the 폰 연결 방식 window and the QR window, and the first failing step is the line over the phone rows.
Tailscale runs on the core's machine, so its failing steps name that machine.
The page rechecks Tailscale every three seconds while open; passed checks collapse to Tailscale is ready.
A foreign HTTPS entry or Funnel is refused; a failed removal remains visible until cleanup finishes, including after switch-off.
The phone pause switch is on Settings > Notifications, and its choice survives a restart.
A phone is told only when an agent waits on the operator’s answer, a question, an approval or a block, never when it finishes (status-model.md, Phone notices).
On a screen another machine shows of this one, the group says in one muted line that turning phones on or off and pairing a phone happen on that machine, then shows the reason line when there is one and the phones, each ⋯ with `연결 해제…` alone, and no switch, method row, rename or QR: a phone pairs only from this machine's own window or its terminal, hided sends that screen no code, and it refuses that screen's switch, method and rename, the first two of which would end a code shown at this machine (PRD device-connect-iroh D-53, D-70).
A computer with no window pairs a phone from its terminal with `hide phone pair`, which turns phones on when they are off, draws the same QR in the terminal and says which phone connected, every line and every refusal (a window too small for the QR, a run inside Hide's panes) one sentence in the operator's interface language; `hide phone list`, `hide phone rename <phone> <name>` and `hide phone remove <phone>` are this group's list, 이름 바꾸기 and 연결 해제 ([INSTALL.md](INSTALL.md#connect-your-phone)).
When a new phone pairs, each phone already paired with this computer is told once, 새 폰이 연결됐어요 under the computer's name with the new phone's, whether or not the computer is in use, so a pairing the operator did not make is seen.
Each connected computer that may open this one is told once too: its window shows a notification titled 새 폰이 연결됐어요 with this computer's name and the phone's, whichever of its notification switches is on, even over a focused Hide window, and clicking it selects this computer's tile.

### Settings > Bots

Bots (봇) is a page of Settings' machine group, after Mobile, drawn from the core's `access` section (`web/src/settings/ExternalToolsTab.tsx`, `web/src/externalTools.ts`).
A bot is an AI or a program outside Hide's panes that works in Hide under a name, and this page is the only place one is made or approved: a bot waiting or a request never reaches 내 차례, a badge, a notice or the phone, and no `hide` command makes, approves, refuses, changes or disconnects a bot.
Every bot runs on the core's machine, so each line names that machine the way Settings names it.
Each bot is drawn as its owl on its tile colour, the same as its rail tile; a 전체 bot's owl wears a small lime square with a person in its lower corner, and a bot in its own space none.
Making a bot and the bots waiting for their AI are this machine's own window's alone: another machine's view of this one and the phone list the connected bots and make none.

봇 만들기, a secondary button at the head of 연결된 봇, opens the make dialog: the name the AI will use in Hide (영문·숫자·`. _ -`, 64자까지) with the owl it gets drawn beside the field as it is typed, and the two modes with one line each, 봇 공간 · 봇이 띄운 일은 봇 칸에 따로 모이고, 질문도 봇이 받아요. 내 작업은 못 건드려요 and 전체 · 봇이 띄운 일도 내 목록에 들어오고, 질문은 나에게 와요. 내 작업도 다룰 수 있어요, with 봇 공간 chosen, the narrower.
만들기 is off until the name is one the core takes; a name the core refuses says why under the field (같은 이름의 봇이 이미 있어요, the 16 bots full, four bots already waiting).
The core answers with the bot and a one-time code, and the dialog gives way to the sentence for the AI that will use it, `Hide의 <name> 봇으로 일해 주세요. <machine>에서 \`hide access join <code>\` 명령을 실행하고, 연결되면 \`hide guide\`의 Bots 부분을 읽고 그대로 따라 주세요.`, where the machine is the core's by its own name, never This Mac, since the reader is on another machine's screen; 복사 copies it, and the note says 코드는 10분 안에 한 번만 쓸 수 있어요.
The sentence closes on its own once its bot no longer waits: joined, taken back, or gone with a restart.

연결 기다리는 봇 (with its count) lists the bots made here whose AI has not joined yet, at most four: the owl dimmed, the name with its mode as a pill, and `n분 남음` from the code's expiry; 문장 복사 reads the live code again from the core and copies the sentence, and 취소 takes the bot back.
A bot whose code ran out keeps its row and its name, holds none of the 16 places, and says 코드 만료 with 새 코드, which opens the sentence with a new code and ten more minutes.
The bot moves to 연결된 봇 when its AI runs `hide access join` with the code; the codes live in the daemon's memory only, so a restart drops the bots still waiting.

승인 대기 (with its count) lists the requests a program put up itself with `hide access request --name <name>`, one row per request: the owl the bot will get and the name it asked under, then the machine, the program that asked (or 알 수 없는 프로그램 when the system did not say) and when it asked.
Under them the same two modes, with 봇 공간 chosen; the approve button names the choice, 봇 공간으로 승인 or 전체로 승인, a secondary button beside 거절; the core's answer takes the request out of the group, and an approved one appears in the list below.

연결된 봇 · n / 16 lists the approved bots in the order they were approved.
Each row shows the owl, the bot's name with its mode as a pill (봇 공간 or 전체), and one line, its machine, its last use (moved at most once a minute) and `에이전트 N · 미션 N`, each piece kept whole when the line wraps; 모드 바꾸기, a menu of the two modes whose other one changes the mode from the bot's next command; and 끊기, which disconnects at once with no confirmation.
A bot in its own space that started agents or missions also says 끊으면 이 봇이 하던 일은 내 것이 돼요 under its line; a 전체 bot's work is already the operator's, so it does not.
With no bot connected the list holds 연결된 봇이 없어요 alone.

A row opens nothing: the counts are the whole of what it says about the bot's work, which the operator finds in the bot's cell on the device rail, by ⌘K and by the pane head's bot name (operator decision 2026-11-14).
The gallery draws the page from invented bots at `?scene=external-tools&state=pending|connected|invites|empty`, where making a bot answers with an invented code, and the rail's bot tiles alone at `state=rail`.

### The phone app

In the iOS app, a rightward swipe from the left edge uses the visible back control: a conversation, mission or pull request returns to its list, a View returns to its Views, and the start settings and add-computer pages return through their own back action.
An open sheet prevents a swipe from pressing a back control behind it; swipes starting inside the page keep their horizontal scrolling or panning.
A notification cold start keeps the launch background until its agent can open, without showing the computer or agent list on the way.
An offline, refused or unavailable connection still shows its existing recovery actions.
The browser phone app keeps its existing navigation.

A reply or quick key sent while its pane is being forked waits for that fork under the same 64 KiB and 30-second holder bounds as desktop input.
The phone reports success only after the engine confirms the write; a holder refusal keeps the reply text and shows the existing send-failure line so the operator can retry once the pane is ready.
A pane outside the desktop's attached tabs remains writable without attaching it or moving the desktop's keyboard.

The QR opens a quiet device-first page: the hide wordmark, a device icon and wrapping device name, one short line on what the phone can do, and a compact secondary 연결 button.
The five-minute expiry and, on the hosted app, end-to-end encryption note sit below the main content.
Pairing refusals use the same layout and keep their existing recovery actions.
연결 opens the list and a one-time hint to keep hide on the Home Screen; an expired or spent code says so, a fifth phone is told the limit and to revoke one on the Mac, and a page opened with no code or credential says to scan the QR from Settings > Devices > Add device > Phone.
The list's header on the hosted app is one line at the title's size, cut short when long: the computer's icon in a small tile, its name without the `.local` macOS adds, its connection dot (green while connected, grey otherwise, its word read to assistive technology), `+N` for the devices the computer shows through it now (its connected reached devices), and a chevron; under it how many other phones are connected.
Pressing it opens the device sheet, and a press leaves no filled box behind on a touch screen: a hover fill lights only where a pointer can hover (`@media (hover: hover)`, the shared `hover` variant).
Its accessible name is the paired computer's full name followed by the devices connected through it (`+ Mac mini`), which the Tailscale app shows as its title.
On the hosted app the head opens the shared bottom sheet described above, with 기기 추가 as its last row.
Under the Mac being viewed the sheet nests the devices it reaches on a rail, as compact rows of the same two lines, each led by a server tile: its name with the desktop's `SSH` or `WSL` mark when the computer reaches it that way (none for a paired computer, and none from a computer that predates the mark), its state, and its counts while connected; a device that is not connected is dimmed and marked 연결 안 됨, and one short line says `이 Mac을 거쳐 보여요. 에이전트는 목록에 함께 나와요.`; there is no device filter.
A phone paired with two computers or more gathers them (PRD device-connect-iroh B55, B62), once the open computer's build offers connections between two Hides (its hello says `device_connect`, from 1.2.12): the header carries the computer row in the device name's place, 모두 and then each paired computer by name, in one line that scrolls sideways.
The phone keeps each computer's last answer with its pairing, so a page opened on a computer that is off from the start still gathers the others.
모두 lists 내 차례 and 읽을 것 of every paired computer in one list, newest first, each row led by an outlined chip with its computer's name; a row of the open computer opens its detail, and another computer's row opens that computer on that agent, as its notification does.
Each group's title adds every computer's count, and each computer's own fold stays behind one `더 보기 N`; 작업 중 and 확인함 stay on each computer's own list.
An automation that stopped for a person on any computer is one more line of 내 차례 with its computer's chip: the open computer's keeps its [다시 켜기], and another computer's opens that computer on the automation's page.
Every computer's missions that are their own item stand there too, each as its mission row with its computer's chip and counted as that computer counts it, so 모두 shows the number the desktop does: the open computer's opens its mission page here, and another computer's opens that computer on the mission's page.
A computer whose list left agents out to fit one relay message says so in one line under the list, with its name and the count, as its own list does.
Under the list each computer whose rows are not there, the open one included, has one line: a spinner with its name and 연결 중…, its name with 연결 안 됨, or `<컴퓨터> 업데이트 필요` when its phone protocol differs from the open bundle's, whose notifications still arrive.
The other computers are connected only while 모두 is on screen and the page is visible, each on its own read-only connection, so one that is slow or away never holds the others back, and a computer that goes away takes its rows with it.
A computer's chip shows that computer's own list, the list above: the open computer's at once, another's through the start page, which loads the bundle its protocol needs; the choice stays in the address (`one=1`), so a reload keeps it.
A phone paired with one computer, or whose computer keeps connections off, has no computer row and opens as before.
When the open computer opens another machine that now runs work of its own (after [바꾸기]), and this phone keeps no pairing with that machine, the agents tab shows one line under the header, `<machine>도 폰에 추가할 수 있어요` with [추가] and a close (PRD device-connect-iroh B56): that machine's own work shows only once the phone adds it, while the work the open computer handed it stays in the open computer's list; [추가] opens 기기 추가's choice, and either it or the close ends that machine's hint for good.
A protocol mismatch uses the same device-first layout, the two version numbers with labels, and a secondary action to choose another device.
Home-screen and notification prompts are plain rows separated by spacing, without repeated filled accent buttons or enclosing cards.
Agents sit in 내 차례, 읽을 것, 작업 중 and 확인함, the sidebar's own section names, with their counts, each row in two lines, and the list updates live.
The first line is the status mark (the sidebar's five marks and the word that leads its line), the task name, the marks below and the elapsed time; the second, under the name, is the children chip, the project, the SSH device's chip and the request or news line.
The row names the project alone when the Mac sends it (`project`), and a Mac that predates it shows its project and branch as before.
Groups are separated by their titles and spacing, with no line between rows.
After the task name come the desktop row's marks, which never shrink, so a narrow phone cuts the name first: the pull requests that name the agent (one as its state's mark and `#number`, several as each state's mark and count) and, on a Conductor's row only, its open mission's chip with `!` while the mission waits on the operator and `k/n` while it runs; the marks only show, and the row opens the detail, whose chips open the pages.
A task the operator starred to revisit wears the desktop's ★: in the mark's place in 다시 볼 것, and small after the name anywhere else; the phone cannot star or unstar, and a Mac that predates the star sends none, so its rows show no ★.
A group's title counts every top row of the group, the folded ones too.
Inside a group the rows of this Mac and of its connected devices are mixed and ordered by the same last activity as the sidebar, newest first, so an agent that just moved on the Mac mini stands above an older row of this Mac.
A group shows its newest rows and folds the rest under `더 보기 N`, which opens them in place: 내 차례 5, 읽을 것 3, 작업 중 5 and 확인함 3 (the first two are the sidebar's own raised caps); 다시 볼 것 does not fold.
When the folded rows include sleeping conversations the fold reads `더 보기 N · 잠든 것 M 포함`, and sleeping rows follow the same order and fold as the others.
A delegated child is not a row of its group.
It hangs under its parent as an indented row behind a vertical rail, two levels deep at most, with its own status mark, title, elapsed time and request or news line, in the sidebar's child order.
A parent leads its second line with the desktop pane header's children chip, `≡ ● 2 · • 3 ⌄` (the first child's mark, the children count, then the subagents), or `≡ • 3 ⌄` with only subagents; pressing it opens or folds the children, and the rest of the parent row opens the parent's detail.
Every chip on the phone, the children chip, a row's mission chip and the detail's mission and pull request chips, has the desktop chip's shape, a fill with small corners and a border only while a child asks the operator, and draws the desktop's face inside it: the mission's mark (✓ once done), `!` for the operator's turn and `k/n`, and the missions tab wears the same mission mark.
With nothing to open the chip is drawn but does not press.
A parent opens expanded when a descendant asks the operator for something (an error, an approval or a question) and folded otherwise, and a change made on the phone stays on that phone: the desktop sidebar's folds never move.
A parent keeps at most five children in view and folds the rest under `더 보기 N`, which opens them in place.
What lies deeper than the second level waits behind that row's `더 보기 N`, so every agent can still be opened and answered from the phone.
A child whose parent is not in the list is a row of its group again.
Local sleeping conversations appear as separate rows in the core-selected group, with Wake or Check status and no conversation detail or reply bar.
Those controls are disabled while disconnected or while one sleep request is waiting; a refused request keeps the row and asks the operator to check connection and session status before trying again.
With no agents the list is one line, 실행 중인 에이전트가 없어요.
The header's `+`, 에이전트 시작, opens the start sheet in the desktop [Start panel](#start-panel)'s order: where, one line of words (the device, the project `›` the branch), then the text box, `에이전트에게 무엇을 요청할까요?`, which takes the keyboard, then who, one line of words (the agent with its provider mark, and the model), and 시작.
The device word reads the machine as its system does in the phone's language (`이 Mac`) and another device by its name, filled in the other-device colour; the project word says `컴퓨터에서 프로젝트를 먼저 추가하세요` and opens nothing when the machine has no project, and no word shows before the targets arrive.
Each word opens its own list in a sheet over the start sheet, a row as tall as a finger with a check on the chosen one; the device word opens the project list.
The project list has a search field, then This Mac's projects, then each device's under its name, each project once with `브랜치 N` when it has more than one checkout; a device that is not connected is dimmed under `<device> · 연결 안 됨` and its projects cannot be chosen, and past 256 places the list keeps the ones used last and ends with one dimmed `외 N개` (en `N more`).
Choosing a project starts on its main checkout, and the sheet opens on the first project's main checkout.
The branch list has a search field and only the chosen project's checkouts, its main one first, marked `기본`, then the rest as the Mac lists them, each name on one line, cut short when long; a folder that is not a Git project has no branch word.
A Mac older than the project words sends each place whole, and the sheet lists each as a project of its own named `project · branch`, with no branch word.
The agent list holds the agents the phone can start with their marks, and the model list `CLI 기본값` then the chosen agent's catalog, both preselected from the desktop's remembered choice; changing the agent takes the model last chosen for it, the model word is disabled while the catalog lists none, and an agent whose start takes no model (OpenCode) has no model word and sends none.
시작 starts the agent there; once it appears in the list the phone opens its detail.
The desktop does not follow a phone start: the visible tab and the keyboard stay where the operator has them, and the new agent only appears in the sidebar and the lists like any other.
Pressing 시작 again for the same text and choice after a lost answer sends the same request id, and hided starts one agent for it.
While hided is out of reach the sheet shows the unreachable line and keeps what was written; a refused start shows its reason inside the sheet and keeps the text, which is cleared only by a start that went.
When hided is out of reach the last list stays dimmed under "연결 안 됨 · Mac의 Hide가 꺼져 있거나 폰의 Tailscale이 꺼져 있어요. 다시 시도 중", the app retries on its own, and it shows the same line when opened without a network.
A Mac that went to sleep shows it too: the phone asks hided when it has heard nothing for 20 seconds and shows the line 10 seconds after an unanswered ask, and on returning to the phone it asks at once, so the connected state never outlasts a hided that has stopped answering by more than 30 seconds.
A row opens its detail, whose head follows the desktop pane header: back, the status mark and the agent's logo, the title with the chips of what the agent belongs to at its right, and `project · branch` under it with the tabs at that line's right, so the head stays two lines tall.
The tabs are 대화 | 터미널 | 볼 것 (each tab only when it has something to show; 볼 것 is described below), drawn a control tall and pressed a touch target tall; `project · branch` gives up its width to them first. Under the head a state strip draws the state's word in its colour, the row's line and how long ago the state changed, from what the Mac already sends.
The strip is led by the parent's children chip, where the desktop pane header's turn row leads with it; pressing it opens the agent's children, then its subagents, in a sheet from the bottom, the same rows the list hangs under the parent, and a child's row there opens that child's detail; with nothing the phone can open the chip is drawn but does not press.
대화 is offered for a Claude Code, Codex, Grok, Pi, omp, Cursor or OpenCode agent on this Mac whose session the engine reports.
The head's title wraps beside its chips, which never shrink, so a narrow phone cuts the title's line rather than a chip.
Beside the title a chip names each open mission and listed pull request the agent belongs to, by its own row only: the mission's mark in its state's colour with `!` for the operator's turn and `k/n` steps done, and the pull request's state mark with `#number`; the chip's accessible name carries the mission's title and, for a step's worker, the step's.
A chip closes the detail and opens that mission's or pull request's page on its tab, whose back arrow returns to that tab's list; a finished mission and an agent that merely works in the same checkout get no chip.
대화 is the agent's own conversation, its newest 30 messages with the newest at the bottom: the operator's messages as right-aligned bubbles on a grey ground, the agent's Markdown drawn at full width (headings, emphasis, lists, tables, code wrapped without highlighting and a `mermaid` fence as its diagram the way Markdown Live draws it, shown as code until it is drawn, a link or an image as its text), an interruption as 중단됨, the time after each turn, and while the agent works one line at the foot, `생각 중 · 4초`, counted on the phone from its last message or state change, or, while it runs a command, the terminal mark and `명령 실행 중 · 0:42` by a clock from the command's start, never naming the command (from a computer that answers from the phone), and neither line while the computer cannot say whether a command runs; pulling to the top loads 30 older messages at a time up to the first, and at 300 it says 최근 300개까지 볼 수 있어요.
What the agent writes next arrives on its own, and tool output and injected context are never shown.
Pi, omp and Grok use their recorded native message units and times (a Grok prompt's blocks and a turn's answer runs joined into one message each), with the same 30-message paging and text bounds; every page, idle poll and copied pager proves the reported native owner and exact checkout under its default root again.
Cursor uses its native graph units under the same page and text bounds; a recorded time is shown at minute precision and an unrecorded time is omitted.
OpenCode uses its own messages from its database, paged by message with the same 30-message and text bounds; every page, idle poll and copied pager proves the root session and exact checkout again, and a session rewound under the phone is read anew.
An unavailable or changed native source falls back through the existing feature-local refusal path; no transcript is borrowed from another session and no new banner is added.
터미널, and the whole detail of any other agent, is the pane's recent rows read-only in the terminal's colours with the newest at the bottom, in the monospace face at the caption text size (a step under the body text, so a row keeps more of its columns on a phone, as the desktop pane's smaller terminal text does), each row wrapped at the phone's width so the detail never scrolls sideways; pulling to the top loads older rows until the pane has no more.
When the holder reports an alternate screen and there are no older rows to load, the top of the terminal says quietly, `전체 화면 프로그램이라 지난 줄은 여기 없어요. 지난 내용은 대화에서 보세요.`
Korean words in this line wrap at spaces, with a long unbroken word allowed to wrap to fit the phone.
The line opens Conversation when that tab exists, and is plain text otherwise; it scrolls with the output, so it appears only at the top.
Primary-screen panes and computers that do not report the screen mode show no line, regardless of the agent kind.
Pulling the phone's output never sends input to the program or moves the desktop's view.
볼 것 is the third tab, `대화 | 터미널 | 볼 것` (`터미널 | 볼 것` for an agent with no conversation), and it is there only while the agent has a View open now; the number of Views not seen is on it.
The list's eye row, the 볼 것 mark, opens the detail on this tab; a row that asks a question or needs an approval, and every other row, opens on the conversation as before, and 대화 goes straight to the agent's conversation.
The tab lists the Views the agent opened, `안 본 것 N` above `본 것 N`, newest first: a tile with the kind's icon and a dot when the View is not seen, its name, its kind (변경 내용, 문서, 이미지, 웹 화면, 파일) and how long ago it was opened.
A View the desktop closes leaves the list, and one the agent opens again is not seen again and returns to 안 본 것.
At most 64 are listed, and one line says how many more are on the Mac.
A row opens its View full screen: `‹ 볼 것`, its name, its kind, the name of the agent that opened it and how long ago; `‹ 볼 것` goes back to the list.
A change list shows how many files and lines changed, then each file with its name, its folder and its +/- counts (`새 파일` for an added file, `바이너리` where git counts no lines), and the line `코드 내용은 Mac에서 보세요`; no line of code is sent.
A file Git does not track yet shows `새 파일` alone, with no number, as the desktop's list does; `바이너리` is only for a tracked file whose lines Git cannot count.
When nothing is left to show it reads `지금은 바뀐 파일이 없어요`.
A Markdown document is drawn with its headings, lists, tables and code blocks by the renderer the conversation uses; any other text is fixed-width and wrapped to the phone's width, never scrolling sideways, and text over 1 MiB shows its first 1 MiB and `나머지는 Mac에서 보세요`.
An image is fitted to the width, zoomed and moved with two fingers (a double tap toggles the zoom), with its size and its pixels below it; one over 10 MiB is not opened and says `이미지가 커서 Mac에서 보세요`.
A PDF, a video, any other binary and a file that is not text show their name, their kind and `Mac에서 열어 보세요`.
An HTML file of a checkout (`HTML 페이지`), opened as a file or as a page, is drawn as the page it is, the width of the phone and scrolling down, with the images, stylesheets, scripts and fonts of its checkout that it names folded in and what it names on the internet loaded by the phone; it needs neither the desktop app nor the page on screen there, a file it names that is missing shows as missing, and one that passes 10 MiB with its files says `페이지가 커서 Mac에서 보세요`.
A file outside every project folder that the agent opened (a mockup in a temporary folder, say) opens like any other file: the Mac's desktop app reads it with the opening it already holds, at that moment, each time the phone opens it, so the phone shows what is on the disk now and nothing is copied or kept, and an HTML page's images, stylesheets, scripts and fonts come from its own folder (Doop 10).
When that read cannot happen the phone says why under the file's icon, with no `다시`: `이 파일을 열 권한이 끝났어요` over `Mac에서 다시 열면 여기서도 볼 수 있어요` when the desktop app no longer holds the opening (it restarted since), `이 파일이 다른 것으로 바뀌었어요` over `에이전트가 연 뒤에 링크나 폴더로 바뀌었어요`, `이 파일은 지금 없어요` as for any file gone, and `Mac의 Hide 앱이 닫혀 있어요` over `프로젝트 밖 파일은 Mac 앱이 열려 있을 때 볼 수 있어요`; a phone bundle from before these shows each as a read that failed.
A web page shows its address and the screen the desktop app has drawn of it, as a picture the width of the phone that scrolls down, with `<기기>에서 찍은 화면 · <몇 초> 전` under it: the device is the Mac whose desktop app took it, and the time keeps counting on the phone (PRD mobile-companion-ui B20, D-11).
A refresh button beside the address takes it again under a new request; the picture on view stays, and the button waits with a spinner, until the new one comes or says it cannot.
A page behind another tab of its View area is taken too, as it draws in the desktop app, with no button to ask for it (Doop 10).
When the screen cannot be taken the picture is replaced by the address and one line, and refresh stays pressable to try again (B21): `Mac의 Hide 앱이 닫혀 있어요` when no desktop app is open, `다른 컴퓨터 화면에 있어요` when the page is in a window of another computer, `Mac에서 이 페이지를 한 번 띄우면 여기서도 볼 수 있어요` for a page the desktop app has not drawn (the agent opened it while the operator looked elsewhere and it was never on screen) or let go to save memory, with nothing drawn or brought forward on the Mac, and `지금은 화면을 가져올 수 없어요` for any other reason (another agent or the operator's Comment mode holds the page, it does not answer), whose why goes only to the diagnostic log as `look.page_unavailable` (design principle 13); a phone bundle from before these lines shows that last one for all of them.
Taking the picture never selects a tab, moves the keyboard or raises a window, so no page is brought forward and nothing is drawn on the operator's screen (B22).
Without a connection the last picture stays dimmed under the unreachable line and refresh does not press (B45).
While a View loads its place shows a spinner; a read that fails says `불러오지 못했어요` with `다시`, which starts the read again from the beginning under a new request; a View that is no longer open says `이 결과물은 이제 열려 있지 않아요`; one in a checkout on a device that is not connected says `기기가 연결되어 있지 않아요`.
A file moved or deleted since its View opened says `이 파일은 지금 없어요` over `에이전트가 연 뒤에 옮겨지거나 지워졌어요`, and a file outside every project folder says `이 파일은 폰에서 볼 수 없어요` over `프로젝트 폴더 밖에 있어요`, each under its file icon and with no `다시`, since reading it again would fail the same way; a phone bundle from before these two shows either as a read that failed.
A View the phone drew an answer about is seen, on the phone and on the desktop alike, through the Desk's own seen-by-id path: the dot and the eye leave both, and an agent with no View left unseen returns to its own group.
That is its content (a change list, a document, an image) and also what it shows when it has no content to show: the name of a PDF or a video, an image over 10 MiB, a file gone, outside every project folder, replaced or no longer opened by the desktop app, a web page's picture or, when none can be taken, its address.
A loading screen and a View that is closed, failed, on a device that is not connected or outside the project with no desktop app open to read it are not seen, nor is a page whose screen came with one of the three reasons above (no desktop app, another computer, not drawn yet), since the Mac can still give it a picture.
An agent with a View not seen stands in 내 차례 on the phone as on the desktop while its row is unread, and returns to its own group when the row is read or every View is seen; a notice still follows the group it would be in without it, so a View sends none.
The reply bar is on the tab and on a View, and the quick keys are not.
A reply sent while a View is open leaves with its name in front (`[경로 구분자 수정] …`), so the agent knows which View it is about, while the field's hint says only `이 페이지에 대해 답장` on a page or an HTML file and `이 파일에 대해 답장` on any other View, which no long name or large text cuts (Doop 11, 12).
While hided is out of reach the last list or content stays dimmed under the unreachable line, and nothing new can be opened.
The phone asks for a View only by its id, and only for one open now; the rule that keeps it so is in [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md#the-mobile-companion).
Every other detail view has a rounded reply field with a round send at its end, and the terminal tab alone has the quick keys above it, filled chips that read `Enter`, `Esc`, `↑`, `↓` and `Ctrl-C` (Enter, Escape, 위 화살표, 아래 화살표, Ctrl-C by accessible name), where the field's hint is `터미널에 보내기` (Doop 05); a reply is sent with Enter after it and clears on success, and a failure keeps the text with the reason under it.
On a computer that answers from the phone (its hello says `answer`; every computer from this version on) the field takes several lines: the keyboard's return key writes a new line and only the send sends, the field grows to four lines with its corners turning to a box's, and the reply reaches the agent as one message, new lines and tabs kept, as the desktop conversation's input sends it; a reply too long for one phone message is named before it is sent (`답장이 한 번에 보내기엔 너무 길어요. 나눠서 보내세요`).
A computer before it keeps the one-line field, whose reply over 2,000 characters is named before it is sent.
On a computer that takes files from the phone (its hello says `attachments`; every computer from this version on), the reply field starts with a round `+` (`파일 붙이기`), in the conversation and on a View alike, that opens the phone's own picker, for any number of files at once: the photo library or files on iOS, the system's chooser on Android and in a browser (Doop 08).
A photo is made smaller on the phone before it leaves, its longer side at most 2,048 px as a JPEG at 0.85 without the original's metadata, so its place does not go with it; text, code, logs, PDFs and any other file go as they are.
A photo the phone cannot draw or make smaller is not added, and one line names it (`IMG_0042.HEIC 사진을 줄이지 못해 넣지 않았어요`); the original is never sent in its place.
Picked files stand in one row above the field, scrolled sideways past its width: a photo as its thumbnail, any other file as a chip with its type's icon, its name and its size, each with a `×` at its corner that takes only that file off (`IMG_2041.jpg 빼기`).
While a file uploads, a band under its mark fills as the computer takes its bytes; files upload one at a time, in the order they were picked, as soon as they are picked.
A file over 10 MB, a photo measured after it was made smaller, is not added, and one line under the files names it (`trace.json (14 MB)는 10 MB가 넘어 넣지 않았어요`) while the others stay; a reply takes at most 8 files and 40 MB together, and a pick past either is named in the same line.
A file whose upload failed is drawn with a red edge and `눌러서 다시 올리기` under its name, and a press on it uploads it again.
The send is pressable once every file is in, with words or with none, and the words and the files leave as one reply: the computer types each file's path in double quotes, then the words, as the desktop conversation sends a reply with files.
The reply owns the files on the bar when it was sent: once it lands only those leave the bar, and a file picked while it was on its way stays for the next reply.
When the computer no longer holds a file the reply names (the connection dropped since it went up), the phone uploads the files again and says `파일을 다시 올리는 중이에요. 다 올라가면 다시 보내세요.`
When the relay's 1 GB a day for this computer and its phones is spent, the files not yet in fail with one line, `이 Mac과 하루에 주고받는 1 GB를 다 써서 올리지 못했어요. 24시간 안에 다시 보낼 수 있어요.`, and nothing retries by itself.
A sent reply's files stand above its bubble, a photo as its thumbnail and a file as its chip (Doop 09).
The thumbnails are this phone's own, kept in its memory for the conversation they were sent in (the last 32 replies with files) and gone when the app closes, and a message shows them only when it names a file of that reply by its upload's id: a path at the front of its words, or a file the agent recorded beside them by its path, as Codex does when it takes a photo in as an image; so the same words sent again, or another photo, attach nothing.
Any other view of the message, another phone, the app after a restart, an older reply or the desktop, reads the files' names from those paths and draws them as chips.
An image the agent recorded with no path names no upload, so on every phone it is a chip with the photo mark and `사진`, with no thumbnail and no name, and a message of images alone stands as its chips with no bubble, as does a message of files alone; it is never tied to a sent photo by its order or its count.
A pane of a connected device takes the files the same way, staged on that device so the agent reads the device's own paths; that pane has no conversation on the phone, so its files are typed into the terminal and drawn over no bubble.
A computer before this version shows no `+`, and the reply bar is as before.
On a computer that answers from the phone, what the agent asks on its own screen stands at the foot of its conversation as a card with a warning edge (Doop 03, 04): `질문 · 3분째 기다리는 중` and the question with each choice as a radio row and what the agent wrote under it (a choice the agent marked recommended carries `추천`); or `승인 · 명령 실행 · 1분째 기다리는 중`, `이 명령을 실행해도 될까요?` and the first of the waiting command in a code box, with `허용`, `이 명령은 계속 허용` and its line `같은 명령은 다시 묻지 않아요` (where the agent offers it; on a card that shows no command, such as Codex's, `계속 허용` and `다시 묻지 않아요`, since what it keeps allowing is the agent's) and `거절하고 다르게 하라고 하기`, whose line says the reason goes in the reply below; or a plan with `계획대로 진행` and `계획 더 다듬기`.
A plan approved from the phone never changes the agent's permission mode (폰의 계획 승인은 권한 모드를 바꾸지 않아요): on Claude Code `계획대로 진행` picks `Yes, manually approve edits`, never the row that switches it to auto mode, and a menu whose row reads otherwise is answered in the terminal.
