Codex writes a tool call to its session only after it is approved, so its card takes the command from its permission request hook instead: the shell command it asks to run, or `apply_patch: <files>` for an edit, in the same code box and under the same one-line rule, and only while Codex's own dialog shows that same command (an edit's files are answered in the terminal); a request that names none (a tool whose input has no `command`) asks `이 작업을 허용할까요?` with no command, and every agent that gives Hide no command before the operator answers does the same (INSTALL.md, What each system supports).
An approval the computer cannot tie to the one command or plan it waits on (Claude Code writing a file, several calls open at once, or a call its session has not recorded yet) asks `이 작업을 허용할까요?` with no command and no choices, and only `터미널에서 답하기`, since it may be a plan's, whose choices differ.
A command of more than one line, or one the card cuts, is drawn as the card shows it with no choices and only `터미널에서 답하기`, beside `여러 줄이거나 긴 명령은 터미널에서 답해요`: the phone allows only a command the operator sees whole on one line, so a multi-line command is answered in the terminal.
A press marks a choice and sends nothing; `답하기` turns on with `선택했어요. 답하기를 눌러 보내세요` beside it, and the computer picks that choice on the agent's screen with its own keys, never typing the choice's words; the card then says `보냈어요` until the agent moves on, and a choice for an ask the agent has left is refused with `에이전트가 그새 다른 화면으로 넘어갔어요. 다시 보고 골라 주세요.`.
A choice is refused, with nothing more typed, when the terminal no longer shows what the card asks, and the card offers `터미널에서 답하기` in place of `답하기`: with no menu of that ask on the screen, `터미널에 이 물음이 더 없어요. 이미 답했거나 명령이 실행 중이에요. 터미널 탭에서 확인해 주세요.`; with another command than the card's, including one that starts with the card's command and goes on to a second line, the command broken over two screen lines (where it cannot be told from a command that differs by one space), or the highlight on another row than the next key expects, such as one the operator moved in the terminal, `터미널 화면이 이 카드와 달라졌어요. 터미널 탭에서 답해 주세요.`.
A choice the computer cannot say landed, or one still on its way when the connection drops, gets the same `터미널에서 답하기`, since a second send could press twice; the card keeps it for that ask through a reconnect or the detail opening again, and only a new ask, a new call's or a new question's, offers `답하기` again.
The phone lets such an answer go only once the agent has moved on from that ask for certain; with 256 of them still held, a new choice is not sent and the detail says `에이전트를 기다리는 답이 너무 많아요. 이 물음은 터미널 탭에서 답해 주세요.`
An agent whose screen Hide has no keys for shows the choices dimmed with `터미널에서 답하기`, which opens the terminal tab and its keys ([INSTALL.md](INSTALL.md#what-each-system-supports)).
While a question is asked the field's hint is `또는 직접 답장 쓰기`, and while a reply is written the card folds to one line between the conversation and the field, `질문` and the question cut to the line, with `선택지 보기`, which leaves the field and unfolds the card; the written text stays (Doop 06).
When hide cannot tell whether a reply reached the pane, the line says so and asks the operator to check the terminal before sending it again.
An agent whose process Hide ended and whose pane holds a bare shell (a keep-pane sleep, described with the pane body above) shows its state in the terminal's place, in the desktop pane's words: Sleeping with its last progress line and Wake agent; Waking… with how old the resumed conversation is; or `Couldn’t resume this conversation` with the core's reason, Retry and Start new session.
The 대화 | 터미널 switch is not offered there, the reply bar and the quick keys are disabled, and the core refuses an input to such a pane at the write (`asleep`, drawn as one line under the bar), from the moment its end begins until the agent is back, so nothing a phone sends reaches the shell under the agent; a repeated request keeps the answer its first send recorded.
Wake agent, Retry and Start new session send the desktop's own wake for that pane, and the agent resumes in the same pane.
Opening the detail does not wake it, and neither does a tap on its row: the desktop wakes on a committed move of its own tab, and a look from the phone is not one, so the row reads `Sleeping`, without the desktop's "wakes when opened".
Opening an agent's detail reads it as the desktop's focus on its pane does: an unread finish in 읽을 것 or an unread question goes to read on the phone and on the desktop at once, and a turn it finishes while the detail stays open is read too; once every phone has closed it, a new finish is unread again ([status-model.md](status-model.md#hide-owns-the-read-axis-at-pane-level)).
Reading is not waking, so this holds for a sleeping agent too, which stays asleep.
A closed pane shows "이 pane은 더 이상 열려 있지 않아요" and disables the reply bar; a disconnected SSH device shows that its device is not connected.
The phone’s Settings offers `알림 받기`; a Safari tab without push is told to open Hide from the Home Screen first, and a refused browser permission shows "알림이 꺼져 있어요 · 설정 > 알림에서 hide를 켜세요".
A notification's title is the root's row title and, with a sentence, its body is `<translated row status> · <exact sentence> · <project>`; without a sentence it is the row's status word with the project (`승인 · <project>` in Korean), and a payload with no status code says the section (내 차례 or 읽을 것) in the phone's language.
The sentence is the core's chosen row detail, untranslated, and may appear in lock-screen previews according to the phone's setting (operator-approved update 2026-10-13); tapping the notice opens that agent's detail.
See [With and without Hide AI](status-model.md#with-and-without-hide-ai) for native question and plain-text question behavior, and cached-worker compatibility.
A revoked phone shows "이 폰의 연결이 해제됐어요. Mac에서 QR을 다시 여세요." and drops its own push subscription; a subscription it could not drop goes to the log, and the storage line appears only when browser storage failed.
A bar at the foot of the lists has 에이전트, 미션 and PR, each an icon, its name and, when something waits on the operator, a count: 에이전트 shows the 내 차례 rows and 미션 the missions that have anything in their 내 차례, PR carries no count, and a count of 0 draws no badge.
The bar is on the three lists only; a detail, an agent's or a mission's, has none, and its back arrow returns to the list it was opened from.
미션 shows the same loaded missions as the desktop mission screen (open, closed still asking someone, and the newest 128 other closed missions) in its four groups, 내 차례, 진행 중, 머지 기다림 and 끝남, with the same counts and order; 끝남 shows its newest three and `모두 ›` opens the rest in place.
A row has its state word, the title, one line (the question, the blocked step, the merge order or the step now running), a cell per step with `n/m`, the time it has run, and, for a mission on another device, that device's chip; a mission whose device does not answer keeps its last values dimmed and counts no time.
With no mission the tab is one line, 진행 중인 미션이 없어요, and under it 아무 에이전트 터미널에서 /hide-mission으로 시작해요; a record Hide could not read says so in one line, as the desktop's does.
A row opens the mission's page, whose head is back, the title, and under it one line that never wraps: the Conductor's state with `대화 ›` (the Conductor's own detail) and how many steps are done (`2/6 끝남`), then where results go, which gives way first and is cut with `…`; the map button sits at the right and the cells for every step under the line.
The line then names where the results go as the desktop's head does, `→ <branch>` in the lime of where results land, in the line's own text rather than a box, cut with `…` when long, only while a step lands there; a screen reader hears it as the branch results go to, and the phone has no hover or popover for it, nor the desktop's options line (PRD mission-destination D-18, D-38).
When the Conductor asks a decision it stands first on the page: the question, why it matters, the node it stopped, its choices as the desktop's card draws them, and a written answer at the foot of the page.
A choice is a radio row whose text wraps in full, the recommended one in a tinted frame and bold with a thumbs-up `추천`; pressing a row only marks it, and `답하기` under the rows sends the marked one, off until one is marked, so one press never sends a choice (PRD mission-human-gate D-19, B19); while a choice is marked and not sent, `답하기` is the primary button and one line beside it says `선택했어요. 답하기를 눌러 보내세요`, on the desktop's card and the phone's alike, and both go once it is sent.
Under why it matters the page lists the Views the decision links, as the desktop's card does (`볼 자료 N`, each row its name, a mark while unseen and `Conductor` or `일꾼`, the first three and `+N 더 보기` for the rest); a row opens the 볼 것 of the agent that opened it, where the View opens full screen, a View whose agent is closed is listed dimmed and presses nothing, and a View closed since the decision linked it stays listed with `닫힘` until the decision is answered or cleared (B20).
Pressing a closed row asks the Mac to open it again first, as the View of the agent that had it open, and the row says `Mac에서 여는 중…` while it waits; once it is open again that agent's 볼 것 opens with it listed, and a View that will not open, a question that moved on or a Mac out of reach says so under the card, one press at a time; a closed row whose agent is closed is dimmed and presses nothing.
With none linked, `자료 요청` is a dashed button of its own above the choices, with why to ask under it (`볼 자료가 없으면 요청해 보세요`), which asks the Conductor for one page to look at, once per question as on the desktop, and reads `자료 요청함` once the Mac says it went; it never holds the answer back, and a request that did not reach the Mac says so in its own words (`web/src/mobile/MissionDetail.tsx`).
Under the decision stands the safety net as the desktop's 내 차례 draws it, its head, a line per held step with its reason and how long it stood, and `대화` to the Conductor while one holds the mission; the phone starts no Conductor, so with none the card only says so.
`Conductor가 정한 것 N` is folded below the graph and lists each line the Conductor skipped on its own as the desktop's summary does.
The answer goes to the Conductor as the desktop's decision banner sends it, and the same words (`보냄 · 차례 기다림`, `Conductor가 읽는 중`, and `전달 확인 안 됨` with 다시 보내기, which types nothing a second time) say where the answer is.
A question that changed while the phone was open, a mission with no Conductor, or a device that stopped answering shows its reason under the question in the desktop's own words and keeps what was written; pressing the same answer twice reaches the Conductor once, and a press that could not leave the phone says the Mac is out of reach.
Away from the Mac the page keeps its last content dimmed under the unreachable line and nothing on it presses: no choice, no written answer.
Under the decision `단계 N` lists the steps, one two-line row each: its mark (the operator's turn mark while it waits on them) and title with its pull request's `#number`, then one line.
A step that waits on the operator stands in the hold band with why and `내 차례`; a stopped step says `멈춤` (`멈춤 · 일꾼 없음` when no worker stands for it) and one whose worker Hide put to sleep `일꾼 잠듦`, neither with a time; a working step names its worker's task, `작업 중` and, from a Mac that sends when it went to work (`worked_at_unix_ms`, the return to work after a finish when there was one), for how long, and a Mac before that sends no time, so the line ends at `작업 중`; a finished step says its pull request's state and `→ <branch>`; a step nobody has started is its title alone.
Pressing a step opens its worker's conversation while the phone's list holds it.
The map button in the head switches the list for the desktop's mission graph, using its dependency layout, connection styles, node card frame and report sink (`MissionGraph`, `MissionGraphCard`, `MissionMapCanvas`), and back.
The graph fits the phone width in a bounded viewport and starts framed on working steps and steps waiting on someone; when none are active, including a finished mission, it fits the whole graph.
A finger pans from a card or the background, two fingers pinch around their midpoint, and the desktop's direction, zoom and Fit controls remain available with phone touch targets.
Panning or zooming keeps the chosen view while frames arrive; Fit shows the whole graph again.
Every received node, finished and dropped ones included, stays in the graph as on the desktop; parallel steps stand side by side and connections show their dependencies.
Tapping a node whose worker the phone's list holds opens that worker's conversation, whose back arrow returns to the mission; an unavailable worker opens nothing.
The phone draws only the metadata its frame carries, including its current PR, worker kind, waiting reason and review gate; desktop-only plans, launch profiles, checker satellites and defect edges are absent, with no invented values.
A step's card has the desktop's rows from what the frame carries, read from the same `web/src/missionLine.ts`: its mark and title, the operator's band (the gate's `볼 것` and `머지할 차례`), its worker's mark and name, why it waits when it does, the wait its Conductor set as the desktop's card says it (`기다리는 중 · <why> · <time>`), and under a hairline the branch line without the branch name, `#n → <destination>` behind its pull request's mark as the PR list draws it, a person before the destination when the operator merges, or a general step's preset icon and name, done or not; a waiting step says `⏳ 기다림` in its worker row, and a passed start gate leaves its band as on the desktop (PRD mission-destination D-51-D-55, B59; PRD node-presets B10).
The frame carries no model, conclusion or verdict, and its one time is when a working step went to work, so the phone's card leaves those rows off ([INSTALL.md](INSTALL.md#what-each-system-supports)); `내 차례` stands at the right end of the line's first row when the step waits on the operator for more than its merge, and a tap still opens the worker's conversation.
The line wraps as the desktop's does (D-56): `#n → <destination>` is one row while it fits and no `내 차례` stands at its end; otherwise the number keeps the first row, `내 차례` at its right end, and `→ <destination>` takes a second row from the card's left edge; a line with no number and a `내 차례` keeps its icon and `내 차례` on the first row and moves its path to the second only when it does not fit between them.
A stacked step's destination is the pull request it goes into, its mark lit by the state of the step whose current pull request it is and grey when none is, then its number in grey (D-57); the frame carries no step's older pull requests, so one of those stays grey where the desktop lights it.
The card's accessible name says the line's words and states, as the desktop's does.
When a node leaves its ready merge to the operator, its band says `머지할 차례`, the person before its destination turns yellow, and it joins the initial framing even though its work is done.
A Mac before destinations sends no destination, base or outside fields: the line stops before `→`, an outside step names the pull request the frame carries, and the head has no tag (B60).
With no steps the section shows the planning or finished state in one line; when a closed mission's steps were omitted from its frame, the omission count replaces the list and the map.
The header, progress cells, decision card and written answer retain their existing behavior.
PR lists the open pull requests of every project this Mac holds (PRD mobile-companion-ui B37-B45), under the title and `열린 PR N`, with the project's name when there is more than one.
They stand in four groups, 머지할 수 있음, CI 도는 중, 실패 and 리뷰 기다림, each with its count and an empty group not drawn, 머지할 수 있음 leading in the warning colour as 내 차례 leads the agent list and the rest muted, and rows set apart by spacing with no dividers or arrows (Doop 17): the core's reason for a row decides its group (ready to merge; CI, an agent's work or the merge queue; a failed check, a change request, a conflict or a branch behind its base; the rest waiting on a person, a draft included).
A row has the PR mark in its state's colour, `#number`, the title and, under it, the line the desktop's PR list says for it with the approvals; a project on another device carries that device's chip.
What the tab does not list is what the desktop's list does not count: a pull request GitHub has merged or closed, a team's review ask and a pull request nothing has moved on for a week.
At most 48 are listed, and a line says how many more are not shown and that the desktop has them.
While GitHub cannot be read the last list stays dimmed under `GitHub에서 읽지 못했어요 · 다시 시도 중`, a missing or signed-out `gh` is one line as the desktop's, and with nothing open the tab says `열린 PR이 없어요`.
Away from the Mac the list keeps its last content dimmed under the unreachable line, and a row still opens its page.
A row opens the pull request's page, headed by the one line every phone page has (Doop 18): `←`, the number (`#2090`, `!12` on a GitLab project) with `branch → base` under it in a fixed-width face, and at the right an icon out to the pull request on its host (named for the host, `Open on GitLab`); the title is the body's first line, wrapping when long, with its state under it, then the agent working on it (opening that agent's detail); the state is words in its colour, with no mark.
The checks, the review and the changed files read as one table of rows ruled across the page, a name at the left and its value at the right (Doop 18): `검사` with `All 4 CI checks passed` (the failed ones named in rows under it), `리뷰` with the approvals and then the reviewers on the same line (`승인 1/1 · reviewer`, a reviewer's state after the name unless the count already says it), and `바뀐 파일 3` with the totals once every file is read, then a row for each file with its `+n −n` (generated ones folded into one line, and `+N more` for a long list).
At the foot are the reason Merge is off in the desktop's words, the write line (`머지 큐에 들어갔어요`, a refused write and the desktop's `다시 확인`), `머지` (`머지 큐에 넣기` when the repository has a merge queue), words with no mark, and `에이전트에게 맡기기`.
`머지` is off for the same reasons as on the desktop card (the card is not read yet, checks are running or failed, a conflict, a branch behind its base, a change request, a review still owed, an agent working on it, a write already in, a refused write not yet looked at again), is shown for the operator's own pull requests only, and says why in one line above it.
Pressing it opens one sheet with nothing preselected, `PR #n: main에 머지할까요?` with the method the desktop card would use (none on a host that merges by its own settings' method, such as gitlab.com, on the phone as on the desktop's confirm line) and the head's short hash, or `PR #n: main 머지 큐에 넣을까요?` when the repository has a merge queue, and the desktop's own `#n 머지` or `#n 큐에 넣기` confirms.
The merge goes out on the head commit the page showed, through the desktop's own path: GitHub refuses a head that moved in between, the page says `PR이 그사이 바뀌었어요`, nothing merges and `머지` stays off until `다시 확인` has read the pull request again.
A press repeated, from a slow connection or a second tap, reaches GitHub once; the sheet closes on its own without sending when the page no longer shows the head, queue and method it named.
`에이전트에게 맡기기` opens a sheet, `에이전트에게 PR #n 맡기기`, with the branch, the agent kind and model the desktop remembers, `이 브랜치의 워크트리를 만든 뒤 시작해요` when no checkout holds the branch, and the first instruction the desktop card would start with, filled from the failed checks and change requests once they are read and editable, with `다시 읽기` when that read failed.
`맡기기` starts the agent where the desktop would, in the checkout of the branch or in a new worktree of it, and the desktop's screen, tab and keyboard stay where they are; the list shows the agent shortly and the page says it was handed over.
Away from the Mac the page keeps its last content dimmed under the unreachable line, and `머지`, `맡기기` and `다시 확인` are off.
Left out on purpose: a `초록 N개 머지` chip on the list and the `머지 순서` line on a page, since merging many pull requests in order is the lead's and the desktop's job (D-12).

The 자동화 tab lists the automations the computer's core runs, in its order (PRD automations B34, B35, `web/src/mobile/AutomationsScreen.tsx`).
Each row is two lines: the unseen dot when a finished run was not looked at and the name, then the schedule as it reads and the next run (`평일 09:00 · 내일 09:00`), or `도는 중` in the working colour while a run goes, or `멈춤` with why it is paused; an automation that stopped for a person to fix says why in the warning colour, and a paused row is dimmed.
A once automation reads its date with the clock in the once colour, `◷ 12-02(수) 09:00 · 한 번`, with `· 끝남` once its time ran, and the done ones are listed after the rest (mission-104 S-5).
Only a row that stopped for a person to fix carries a button, `다시 켜기` in words; running, pausing and turning a paused one on again are on its page.
A press on the row opens its page: the name with the same schedule line under it, whose next run reads `다음 <when>`, `지금 실행` and `멈춤`, which turns into `다시 켜기`, and the runs newest first, each a two-line row with its mark (✓, ✗, a spinner, –), its start time with `수동` or `놓친 회차`, then its result (`끝남 · 3분`, `실패 · 3번 시도`, `건너뜀 · 이전 회차 진행 중`, `건너뜀 · <이유> · N번 · <처음>–<마지막>` for times skipped one after another, `<처음> ~ <마지막> · 놓침 N회 · <이유>`, and one missed time alone as that time with `놓침 · <이유>`, never `A ~ A`), and the unseen dot, with what a run spent under its result in the desktop's words; the phone lists the newest 20, and `이전 회차 N개는 데스크톱에서 보세요` counts the rest.
How a schedule, a next run and a record read is `web/src/automationWords.ts`, shared with the desktop's automation screen.
A press goes to the core as the desktop's own button does, once per press however often it is sent again; what the core did comes back on the automation, and a refusal (a run already going, an automation that is gone, the computer out of reach) is one line under the page's buttons.
The phone has no button to make, edit or remove an automation: that is said to an agent, on the phone as on the desktop.
With none, the tab says `자동화가 아직 없어요` and `에이전트에게 말로 만들 수 있어요`.
When the computer could not read its automation list and set it aside, one warning line above the list says `컴퓨터의 자동화 목록을 읽지 못해 따로 두었어요 · 데스크톱에서 보세요` until the core drops it (making an automation does).
Away from the computer the tab keeps its last list dimmed under the unreachable line, as the other tabs do, and its buttons are off; the list is filled again on reconnect.
An automation that stopped for a person to fix (`agent_scope.automation_asks`, PRD automations D-12, B23) is also one line of the agent list's 내 차례, above the agents there and counted in its number and the 에이전트 tab's: `<name> · <why> · 고치고` with `▶ 다시 켜기`, the row's own press, and a press on the line opens its page; the line goes when the core takes the ask away.
A once automation whose run ended (`looks` on the automations frame, the computer's `automation_looks`) is one more line there, after the stopped automations and above the agents: the clock badge, its name and `자동화 · 한 번 · ✓ 끝남 · 4분` with no quoted answer, counted in the group's number and the 에이전트 tab's; pressing it opens the automation's page and tells the computer the run was looked at (`look_seen` with the run's number), so the line and its count go, here and on the computer; in the list gathered from every computer, another computer's line only opens its page there, and goes once it is pressed on that computer's own list (mission-104 S-5).
A Mac from before once automations sends none of this, and a phone from before them reads a once automation's schedule as an empty line rather than failing (S-6).
A mission that is its own Needs You item (the computer's `mission_asks`, no Conductor's or worker's row carrying it) is one more row of the agent list's 내 차례 too, drawn as its row on the 미션 tab with its pill, title and line, after the automations and counted in the group's number and the 에이전트 tab's, so the phone's 내 차례 says the number the desktop's says; the row opens the mission's page, and it goes when the computer names it no more (operator decision 2026-11-23).
An automation's own folder is no project the phone lists: the start sheet and the PR tab leave out a project the core marks `unlisted`.

The phone's bottom bar has five places, 에이전트, 미션, 자동화 (once the computer sends automations), PR and 설정, and the last is the Settings tab (`web/src/mobile/SettingsScreen.tsx`).
It replaces the gear and its sheet that the list's header carried, and it reads top to bottom as the computers this phone is connected to, 알림, 표시, 정보.
Each is a group as the desktop's Settings draws one (`Group`, `web/src/components/settings-rows.tsx`): its heading, a card of rows split by hairlines, each row one thing to read or press and a touch target tall, and a group's note under the card; a row carries a line under its name only where the desktop's has one, which on the phone is the notification row's.
연결된 컴퓨터 lists each paired computer as the device sheet's rows, split by the card's hairlines; a press switches to it, `기기 추가` opens 기기 추가's choice, and a row's long-press menu offers 기기 지우기, which asks `컴퓨터 이름: 이 폰에서 연결을 지울까요? 다시 연결하려면 새 QR이 필요해요.` before it removes the computer from this phone.
알림 has the same `알림 받기` switch in the PWA and iOS app, with one line below for the current state.
Off says `에이전트가 내 답을 기다리면, Hide를 닫아 둬도 알려 줘요.` and On says `내 차례가 되면 알려 줘요.`
Turning it on asks the platform’s notification permission only from that press; while it waits, the switch is held and the line names the browser’s or iOS’s question.
A browser without push holds the switch off and keeps the Home Screen guidance; a refused browser permission holds it off with the system-settings guidance.
When iOS refuses, the switch is off and held, the line says `iOS에서 Hide 알림이 꺼져 있어요.`, and `iOS 설정 열기` opens this app’s page there.
A registration failure shows `알림을 켜지 못했어요. 다시 시도해 주세요.` and allows another press; an older computer in the iOS app asks for a computer update.
The browser keeps its receiving choice on this phone across reloads and reconnects; without a saved choice it preserves the existing OS-permission behavior.
Turning it off tells the connected computer and removes subscriptions for every paired computer in this browser, while keeping the workers and their cached app usable.
A late registration cannot turn receiving back on; a cleanup failure says that not every notification could be turned off and offers the switch for another attempt.
While Off, reopening or reconnecting checks every paired computer’s subscriptions again, so a failed cleanup remains retryable after reload.
Returning to On restores each computer’s actual subscription when connected, even if that computer remembers receiving as On.
An unsaved browser choice shows the existing storage-failure guidance and survives visibility changes and reopening Settings until this page closes.
The iOS app removes this phone’s keys for every computer when turned off; removing a computer removes only that computer’s subscription or key.
In the demo 연결된 컴퓨터 holds three rows, the sample host with its `샘플` mark, `내 컴퓨터 연결하기` and `체험 끝내기`, with the note that nothing goes to a computer under the card.
In the demo the switch asks the same question and posts one sample notification on this phone, without actions: the first sample agent's title, then the status word, its question and its project, worded by the code that words a real notification; it names no computer, and no key is made and nothing is registered or sent.
In the iOS app a real notification tap opens its conversation, where the person can reply; long pressing a notification offers no Reply action, including the demo sample.
The browser demo keeps its notification row as `샘플` without a receiving control; only the iOS app can post the demo’s local sample notification.
The first screen's 언어 value reads `폰 따라가기` (the phone's own language) while no computer is connected, and `Mac 따라가기` only inside a computer; the camera view says the camera is already open (`카메라가 이미 열려 있어요. 닫고 다시 시도해 주세요.`) when another screen holds it, and keeps the permission wording for a camera that cannot be used.
표시 has 언어 and 화면 as one-line values that open a bottom sheet to choose from, and under the card `이 폰에만 저장돼요`.
언어 offers Mac 따라가기, 한국어, English, 简体中文 and 日本語, each language named in itself; 화면 offers 시스템 따라가기, 밝게 and 어둡게.
Both start on the follow choice: Mac 따라가기 keeps the rule the phone already had (the core's language from the last agents frame, else the phone's own language), and 시스템 따라가기 keeps the phone's light or dark setting.
A choice applies as soon as it is tapped, with no reload, and in the iOS app a 화면 choice also sets the app's own light or dark style, so its status bar and keyboard follow.
It is kept in that browser's storage (one key, `hide.phone.settings`), so it survives a reload, and the start page that lists the paired devices reads the same key, so its first paint is already in the chosen language and theme.
Nothing is sent to the Mac: the desktop's language and mode, the phone protocol and the core are unchanged, and a different Mac's phone settings never move this phone's choice.
When the browser refuses the write, or its storage cannot be read, the page still works: a read failure shows the follow choices, a refused write keeps the choice for this visit and the note says `이 폰에 저장하지 못했어요. 앱을 닫으면 원래대로 돌아가요.`
The notification words follow the language in effect, so an explicit language also changes the words of the next notification.
정보 holds `개인정보 처리방침` (`https://withhide.dev/privacy`) and `지원` (`https://withhide.dev/support`), which open outside the app, then, in the iOS app, the app's version and build, and the phone protocol number.
The app follows the phone's light or dark choice, draws text a quarter larger than the desktop, and keeps every control at least 44 points tall.
In the iOS app every screen keeps clear of the notch, the home indicator and the keyboard, the page does not rubber-band or zoom, and a press on a control selects nothing and opens no callout.
In a browser and a Home Screen app the page is as tall as what the keyboard leaves of the screen, so the reply bar stays above it there too, and a detail read at its bottom keeps its newest line in view above the reply bar when the keyboard comes up; one scrolled up to older lines stays where it was.
While a field being written in has the keyboard, the page stays at its top whenever the browser moves it, before, with or after the keyboard's resize (Safari often pans a few hundred milliseconds after it), so every raise of the keyboard, the first or a later one, after a list scroll or on another agent, shows the header, the newest line and the reply bar above the keyboard, and the page is back in place when it goes down.
Only the keyboard moves the page back to its top: a list scrolled in a browser stays where the reader left it while the browser folds its toolbar away and brings it back.

The start page with no computer connected is a first screen that says what to do, in order (`web/src/mobile/StartScreens.tsx`).
It has `hide` with a gear at the right, the title `컴퓨터를 연결하세요`, one line (`Hide는 내 컴퓨터에서 도는 에이전트를 폰에서 보고 답하는 앱이에요.`) and three steps: `컴퓨터에 Hide 설치` (`맥, 윈도우, 리눅스` and a `withhide.dev` link that opens outside the app), `설정 > 기기 > 기기 추가 > 폰 열기` (`컴퓨터의 Hide에서 QR이 보여요`) and `아래 버튼으로 QR 스캔`.
At the bottom is the big `QR 스캔` button, and under it `체험해 보기` with its one line.
With two computers or more, or a computer whose connection was refused, the page is the list of computers it was; with one, it opens that computer ([Mobile companion](#mobile-companion)).
In the iOS app `QR 스캔` opens the camera at once; in a browser, which has no native scanner, it opens the add page, where `QR 스캔` opens the browser's own camera drawn as the app's scanner and a pasted link connects too.
The gear opens the same display and about rows as the Settings tab, with `연결한 컴퓨터가 아직 없어요` under 연결된 컴퓨터, and a back arrow returns to the first screen; a language chosen there applies at once.
The demo's own Settings tab is unchanged.

The iOS app's scanner is a full-screen camera view (`HideScannerPlugin.swift`) with the title `QR 코드 스캔`, the line `틀 안에 QR을 맞추면 바로 읽어요`, a frame with the rest dimmed (only what is inside the frame is read), `QR은 어디 있나요? 컴퓨터의 Hide > 설정 > 기기 > 기기 추가 > 폰` at the bottom, and a close button at the top right.
The camera permission is asked when `QR 스캔` is pressed, never at launch.
Only a Hide pairing link ends the scan; any other QR code is ignored and the camera keeps looking.
When the camera is refused, or cannot be opened (another app holds it), the view is not black: it says `카메라를 쓸 수 없어요` with `QR을 읽으려면 카메라 권한이 필요해요. iPhone 설정에서 Hide의 카메라를 켜 주세요.` and a `설정 열기` button that opens the app's page in iPhone Settings, and the close button is there in every state.
The words are sent by the page, in the language and with the catalog it shows, so the scanner never disagrees with the page.
A browser draws the same view itself (`WebScanner` in `web/src/mobile/ScanQr.tsx`), dark whatever the page's theme, with the frame where the app draws it and only what is inside it read; it asks for the camera when `QR 스캔` is pressed, stops the camera when it closes, connects or the page is hidden, and ignores any QR that is not a Hide pairing link.
A camera the browser refuses or cannot open says `카메라를 쓸 수 없어요` with `브라우저에서 이 페이지의 카메라를 허용하거나, QR 링크를 붙여넣으세요.` and a `QR 링크 붙여넣기` button that closes the view and opens the link field, since a page cannot open the browser's settings.

Directional terminal focus stays within the keyboard owner's drawn tab and Agent area, on local and remote devices.
It selects the pane sharing the requested edge, preferring the greatest shared edge, then the closest perpendicular centre, then layout order.
At the outside edge, in a single-pane or zoomed tab, or while a sheet, menu, find bar or dialog holds the keyboard, it does nothing.
It never switches tabs or projects, and the destination receives the next terminal input through the existing pane-focus command.
The four commands are listed in the shortcut sheet, Settings and the desktop Pane menu; each can be rebound or reset.

### Phone demo mode

Web owner: `web/src/mobile/demoSocket.ts` (the sample host), `demoData.ts` (its content), `Demo.tsx` (the marks and the host sheet's panel), `connection.ts` (`startDemo`, `leaveDemo`), `loader.tsx` (the entry); test owners: `web/src/mobile/demo.test.ts`, `web/src/mobile/demoScreens.test.tsx`.

A person with no computer running Hide can look around every screen of the phone app with sample data.
The start page that lists the paired devices ends with `체험해 보기` and one line under it, `샘플 데이터로 둘러보세요. 컴퓨터가 없어도 돼요.`; one tap opens the hosted phone at `/p/<protocol>/?demo=1`.
The entry is a plain row of the page, not a hidden switch, and it is there whether or not a device is paired.
Only the hosted phone takes `?demo`; the page the desktop serves at `/m/` does not.
The sample host is a socket the phone's connection opens in place of the relay's, so the store and every screen read the frames a real host sends and no screen branches on it.
It sends the lists, a conversation per agent, 볼 것 and the content of its Views, the start sheet's catalog and a card per pull request, and answers each request the way a Mac would, a moment later.
The list shows 내 차례, 끝남, 다시 볼 것 (one starred task), 작업 중 and 확인함 in the core's order with a parent's children under it and a folded `더 보기`, the first agent as the release mission's Conductor with its chip, and a working agent with its pull request's mark; an agent's detail shows its conversation, and a reply gets a sample answer in the same conversation.
The start sheet and the pull request's `맡기기` sheet add a sample row to 작업 중, which opens as a started agent's detail does; an answer to the mission's decision clears it, and `머지` takes the pull request off the list.
볼 것 holds one HTML page, drawn in the sandboxed frame, and one image.
An agent's 터미널 shows a sample pane in the terminal's colours: the request, the file it read and changed with the diff, the command it ran with its output, and its question or that it is still at work; the agent's own words follow the phone's language, and file names, commands and their output are code text.
The release mission's steps wait on each other, two side by side and two not begun after them, so its page shows the rail.
The sample agents are of a kind Hide has no adapter for, and no step names a worker, so a sample screen draws the neutral terminal mark and no vendor's name or logo (a store screenshot of the demo shows no other company's mark); the start sheet's agent word and list, which name the agents the phone can start with their marks, are the one place a product name or mark appears.
The sample text is the app's own and is worded in all four languages (`web/src/i18n/resources/mobileDemo.ts`), and it is worded again when the phone's language changes; pull request titles, branches and file names are code text and stay as written.
The demo opens no WebSocket and makes no request, so it works offline once the page is loaded; a first visit on the web with no network cannot load the page, while the iOS app carries it.
It writes no host entry, credential or notification subscription, offers no notification row, and a visit leaves only the phone's own language and theme choice, which the demo shares with the real app.
A visit adds at most 10 sample agents and 40 sentences to one conversation; past that the start sheet or the reply bar shows its ordinary failure line.
A slim bar, `체험 중 · 샘플 데이터` with `체험 끝내기`, sits over every screen of a demo.
`샘플` marks the host's name in the list's header, and the host sheet holds only the sample host, a note that nothing goes to a computer and nothing from the demo is kept, `내 컴퓨터 연결하기` and `체험 끝내기`.
`내 컴퓨터 연결하기` opens the real QR scan and paste page (`/?add=1`, with the camera permission), and `체험 끝내기` opens the start page, which has no trace of the visit.

## Keyboard shortcuts per system

Web owner: `web/src/shortcuts.ts` (the registry, the rule and its exceptions), `web/src/keys.ts` (terminal keys), `web/src/shortcutLabels.ts` (every chord a screen prints), `web/src/keyboard.ts` (the window listener); desktop owner: `desktop/src/main/menu.ts` (the menu's accelerators); test owners: `web/src/shortcuts.test.ts`, `web/src/shortcutsDoc.test.ts` (fails when the Every command table below and the registry disagree), `web/src/keys.test.ts`, `desktop/src/main/menu.test.ts`, `web/e2e/korean-input.spec.ts` (Hangul composed through the browser's input-method path: the ⇧↩ order and a composing Escape), and `web/e2e/chords.ts`, through which every web e2e presses its chords as the runner's system does.

The rest of this document writes chords as macOS has them; this section is how Windows and Linux press each one.
Which system applies is the one the operator types on: the desktop app's, or the browser's for a browser tab, whatever system the daemon runs on.

### The rule

On Windows and Linux every ⌘ is Ctrl+Shift, as in Windows Terminal, GNOME Terminal, WezTerm and kitty, so a plain Ctrl key reaches the shell and the agent CLIs (Ctrl+C, Ctrl+R, Ctrl+W, Ctrl+K, Ctrl+D and the rest); the only plain Ctrl keys the app takes are text size and, in a browser tab, the ones Chrome keeps for itself.
A ⇧ or ⌥ that a macOS chord adds to ⌘ makes it Alt+Shift there, the layer Windows Terminal puts its panes on (Alt+Shift+D splits a pane there too): ⌘T is Ctrl+Shift+T and ⇧⌘T is Alt+Shift+T, so no chord needs more than three keys.
The chords the rule puts on that layer take no key a shell or an agent CLI documents: readline reads Alt+Shift+B as Alt+B (`do-lowercase-version`), so Alt+B, Alt+D and Alt+T still reach the shell and Claude Code.
None of them is a key Windows, GNOME or Chrome lists as its own, except Chrome's Alt+Shift+T (below).
Windows can be set to switch the input language on Alt+Shift, and a Linux layout can be too (`grp:alt_shift_toggle`); a chord still runs there, because chords match the physical key, but whether the layout switches as well is unconfirmed on a real Windows machine.
A chord without ⌘ is the same keys on every system: ⌃Tab is Ctrl+Tab and ⌥1 is Alt+1, so those Alt chords take keys a shell reads as Meta, as they do on a Mac whose terminal sends Option as Meta.
Chords are written in words joined with `+`, in Windows' order Ctrl, Alt, Shift, as Windows Terminal writes them, so every chord the rule makes begins with `Ctrl+Shift+` or `Alt+Shift+`.
AltGr types a character on Windows and Linux layouts (Windows reports it as Ctrl+Alt), and a key pressed with it is never a chord.
A stored chord set keeps macOS chords, so a set a Mac saved means the same keys through the rule on Windows and Linux, except that ⇧⌘X, ⌥⌘X and ⌥⇧X are all Alt+Shift+X there, so a set binding two of them, or binding one onto a default that is another, is refused whole and the defaults run, and a chord recorded there as Alt+Shift+X is stored as ⇧⌘X; on those systems a desktop pane chord needs Ctrl+Shift or Alt+Shift, a browser chord Ctrl or Alt, and the Windows or Super key is refused because the system keeps it.

### Exceptions

Each exception has a dominant convention on Windows and Linux, or a chord the system keeps, behind it.

- Text size is Ctrl and =, - or 0, as in Windows Terminal, GNOME Terminal, WezTerm, VS Code and every browser.
- The tab beside is Ctrl+PageDown (previous Ctrl+PageUp), as in Chrome, Firefox, VS Code and GNOME Terminal; the rule's Ctrl+Shift+] would be a chord no one there knows.
- The recent project cycle is Ctrl+Shift+` (previous Ctrl+Alt+Shift+`): Alt+Tab is the system's window switcher on Windows and Linux and never reaches an app, so the cycle moves to ` under the rule, the key macOS keeps for its own window cycle.
  Going back adds Alt to the Ctrl+Shift the cycle already holds, the one four-key desktop chord: a held cycle commits when its Ctrl is released, so the rule's Alt+Shift+` could not step back inside it, and GNOME keeps Alt+Shift+` for switching between an app's windows.
- In a browser tab, Reopen closed tab is Ctrl+Alt+Shift+T: Chrome keeps Alt+Shift+T to focus its toolbar, which keyboard users rely on, and the macOS browser chord ⌥⇧T is those same keys there.
- In a browser tab, Switch sidebar list is Ctrl+Alt+Shift+B for the same reason: Chrome keeps Alt+Shift+B to focus its bookmarks bar.
- Not yet handled: a browser tab's area cycle is Alt+` and Alt+Shift+` on Windows and Linux (Chrome keeps Ctrl+Tab, as it keeps ⌃Tab on macOS), and stock GNOME and KDE take both to switch between an app's windows, so on those desktops the cycle reaches the page only once the desktop's keys are changed or the cycle is bound to another chord in Settings, Shortcuts.
- Move to Trash in the Explorer is Delete, as in Windows Explorer and the Linux file managers.
- Copy Relative Path is Alt+Shift+H: the rule would give it Copy Path's Alt+Shift+C, VS Code's own chord for it is a two-step sequence (Ctrl+K, then Ctrl+Shift+C) that the registry cannot hold, and Alt+Shift+R opens the PR list.
  A browser tab has neither path chord, because Chrome keeps ⌥⌘C (Inspect); the menu items work there.
- Cut, Copy and Paste in the Explorer are the system's own clipboard chords: ⌘X, ⌘C and ⌘V on a Mac, Ctrl+X, Ctrl+C and Ctrl+V on Windows and Linux.
  The tree answers them only while it holds the keyboard, so a terminal keeps ⌘C for its selection and Ctrl+C for the interrupt, and a text field keeps its own.
- Inside a text field, a palette, a board or a document, no terminal holds the keyboard, so the system's own command key applies: ⌘↵ is Ctrl+Enter (send a form, open a ⌘P result to the side, open a pull request on GitHub), ⌘-click is Ctrl-click (a link, a pull request, an issue), and Ctrl+S saves the document in front as well as Ctrl+Shift+S.
  A file or diff View's comment chord ⇧⌘. is Ctrl+Shift+. there for the same reason ([View openers and comments](#view-openers-and-comments)).
- The digits follow the rule: a tab is ⌘1-9, so it is Ctrl+Shift+1-9 there, a device is ⌥⌘1-9, so it is Alt+Shift+1-9, and an agent row stays ⌥1-9, Alt+1-9.
  No digit is on plain Ctrl: ⌃1-9 would take the control bytes ⌃2 to ⌃8 send a terminal (NUL, Escape, ^\ to ^_).

### In a terminal

| Action | macOS | Windows and Linux |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Copy the selection | `⌘C` | `Ctrl+Shift+C` (the terminal's even with nothing selected, so Chrome's element inspector never opens), or `Ctrl+C` while text is selected |
| Interrupt the program | `⌃C` | `Ctrl+C` with nothing selected |
| Paste | `⌘V` | `Ctrl+Shift+V` |
| Delete to the line start, go to the line start or end | `⌘⌫`, `⌘←`, `⌘→` (sent as ^U, ^A, ^E) | `Ctrl+U`, `Home` and `End`, which the terminal sends as typed |
| A newline the agent keeps in its prompt | `⇧↩` | `Shift+Enter` |

Ctrl+C copies only while text is selected and clears the selection as it copies, as Windows Terminal does, so the next Ctrl+C interrupts; with nothing selected it reaches the program.
Every other plain Ctrl key reaches the program, apart from text size (Ctrl+=, Ctrl+- and Ctrl+0) and the tab beside (Ctrl+PageDown and Ctrl+PageUp).
Settings, Shortcuts refuses a binding on the terminal's copy or paste chord.

While an input method composes (Hangul, kana, pinyin), the keys in this table are the input method's and none is sent; a ⇧↩ that ends a syllable reaches the program after that syllable, never before it (`web/src/terminals.ts`).
The syllable being composed is drawn in the terminal's own colours, underlined.
Anywhere in the window, an Escape pressed while a field composes is the input method's too: it never closes the dialog, sheet or palette the field is in (`web/src/keyboard.ts`).

### Every command

The hold hint follows the same table: holding Ctrl+Shift alone in the desktop app on Windows and Linux floats the tab numbers, holding Alt+Shift alone the device numbers, and holding Alt alone the Agents-list numbers.
A command marked none has no chord until the operator binds one in Settings, Shortcuts, where it can be bound; the browser tab's moved chords are the ones Chrome keeps for itself on that system.

Settings, Shortcuts (PRD settings-cleanup B60-B63, B66) lists the editable pane and navigation commands, each as its name and a chip holding its chord.
Pressing the chip, or Enter on it, waits for the next chord, which applies at once; there is no second Apply step, Escape cancels the recording without closing the sheet, and a refused chord leaves the old one with the reason beside the row.
The chip's accessible name carries the command and its current chord.
The restore-default and clear controls of a row show while the pointer is on the row or the keyboard is inside it (and always where there is no hover), and keep their place so the chip does not move.
The eight area commands have no chord until the operator sets one, so they fold into one line that says how many are still unset and opens to a row each.
The numbered chords are fixed and read as one row per family under Fixed, whose subtitle names the modifier to hold to see the numbers ("Hold ⌘ to see the numbers", the system's own modifier names elsewhere); a browser tab has no numbered chords, so those rows say so and carry no subtitle.
The sheet carries no row or explanation for a command the removed native app alone ran (`toggle_conversation` stays in a stored set and is never run here).

## Keycaps, tooltips, and icon buttons

Every icon-only control has a tooltip and an accessible name carrying the same words as the tooltip.
A chorded tooltip reads the label followed by the shortcut chord; a chordless control shows only the label.
The sidebar Settings tooltip and accessible name append the host's current shortcut in parentheses to the translated label.
There is no native platform tooltip layered underneath the shared one; the shared tooltip is the only tooltip in the main shell.
A shortcut keycap (`Kbd`), such as the sidebar's `⌘K` or a session head's `⌘E`, is an outline with no fill and muted letters, so the chord reads without a grey box above the words it sits beside.
Tooltip hover has a short reveal delay, and an exact modifier hold reveals shortcut hints faster than a hover tooltip does.
In the desktop app, holding ⌘ alone (Ctrl+Shift on Windows and Linux) floats each tab's number at its top left in the Agent area in use, holding ⌥ alone floats each Agents-list row's number at its top right, and holding ⌥⌘ alone (Alt+Shift on Windows and Linux) floats each device tile's number at its top left, the corner a tile's marks leave free: a keycap in the popover colors with a border, a small shadow and one mono digit, positioned over the row or tile rather than in it, so a title, a row's time, its fold slot and a tile's marks never move.
The number is the screen order at that moment, first to ninth, top to bottom for the rows the Agents list draws (a folded parent's descendants are not rows) and for the rail's tiles, and an item past the ninth carries none.
A tab is the exception, as in a browser: ⌘1-8 select the first eight tabs of the Agent area in use, left to right, and ⌘9 its last, so with ten or more tabs the last one carries 9 and the ninth to the second-to-last carry none, and with fewer the last tab carries its own place and ⌘9 reaches it too.
Adding ⌥ to a revealed ⌘ hold turns the numbers to the devices at once, and releasing it turns them back.
A chord an earlier version let the operator bind that a numbered chord now holds (⌥⌘1-9 went to the devices) is dropped from the stored set with a diagnostic, the command keeping its default, and the rest of the set stays.
While Projects is on screen the same hold shows those Agents-list numbers, since ⌥n still selects by them, each once: on the agent's raised row, else on its row under the checkout that owns its pane.
The hint appears only after a short hold of the exact modifier; releasing it before then shows nothing, so a ⌘C never flashes numbers.
Releasing the modifier, adding another, pressing any key during the hold (including the numbered chord itself), losing the window, hiding the page, or opening a sheet, menu, dialog, palette, or cycle clears the numbers at once; the same modifiers still held after that show nothing until they are released and held again.
The keycaps and the hover tooltip never share space: a tooltip hangs beside its trigger and a keycap sits inside the trigger's own box.
In the desktop app a tooltip avoids the browser pages on screen: it opens on its usual side, else the opposite one, else across, whichever first fits the window clear of every page, because a page is drawn over the shell (Browser displays).
A browser host has no numbered chords, so holding ⌘ or ⌥ (Ctrl+Shift or Alt on Windows and Linux) there shows nothing.
Pane focus, active tab, tab order, zoom state, and disappearing anchors all update which controls can show a hint or tooltip; pointer exit, mouse down, scroll, key down, losing key window status, and anchor removal all dismiss an open tooltip.

Destructive buttons are named by their result (`Move to Trash`, `Close 3 panes and remove`, `Stop work and close`), never by a generic "Delete" or "OK" that hides the consequence; the non-destructive option is the default/cancel action, except in the sheet that closes an agent with the agents it spawned.
There the operator chose `모두 닫기` as the Enter default (PRD close-agent-subtree D-07, D-18), and a descendant whose status is unknown gives the default back to `취소`; the removal dialogs that ask the same question keep no default.
Escape closes the innermost open layer and returns focus to whatever held it before that layer opened, including a terminal that was focused when a sheet, menu, or overlay opened over it.
A context menu returns focus to its real target; a delayed focus return from any layer leaves a newer control or layer's focus in place.
A tooltip or hover card is not a layer: an Escape pressed while one shows still reaches the terminal or the screen it was meant for, and closes the tooltip on its way, whether the tooltip's own dismiss or the shell answers the press.
A disabled control cannot activate, and destructive meaning always comes from the control's role rather than from its text color alone.
Hover and focus are local presentation state: they never publish core snapshots, dispatch core events, or trigger Git/disk work by themselves.
Pending operations show explicit progress and remain disabled for the duration; a shared control style never invents its own pending or error state independent of the actual operation.

## Sheets, overlays, and abnormal states

Search, New Agent, Settings, and file search each host the same tooltip overlay and the same sheet container language: headline titles, supporting text, and consistent spacing.
The Settings sheet grows with the presenting window up to a maximum, so an ordinary window reads a tab without scrolling; the Settings scene (its own window) keeps its smallest size.
A selected provider card uses a stronger fill and border while keeping its status readable; disabled Start/Add controls keep their existing enablement conditions.

Loading, local-only, no-workspace, disconnected, and unreadable states are each distinct and each drawn in its smallest form: a badge or a dimmed row before a banner, following design principle 9.
A value Hide cannot read is shown as unreadable (a question mark or equivalent), never as a false zero or a silently empty state.
An alert, banner, or sheet for a failure is a deliberate product decision, never a default: a failure the operator cannot act on goes to the diagnostic log instead (design principle 13).
Retryable failures offer Retry attached to the exact target that failed; a definitive refusal and an unconfirmed/timed-out result are told apart in their copy, because only the definitive case can safely remove state (such as a reopen entry or a pending pane).

### Action menu presentation

The terminal, pane header, Agent and View tabs, Projects session rows and Tasks rows show a shared leading icon column and one muted heading naming the target.
Opening a menu still acts on that target without selecting another tab or marking a session read.
The heading follows the target's current name; the terminal close action reads `Close terminal` without repeating the name.
Copy, Paste, Select all, Find, Split right, Split down and Zoom remain direct terminal actions, with the existing platform-specific chords.
Related sessions, fork destinations and copied session details are submenus; Agent and View tab directions are grouped under Split and Move, with Rename beside New tab on an Agent tab.
Only directions already offered by the layout are shown under Move.
The command identities and their eligibility stay the same; a submenu whose choices share the same refusal shows that reason once on its disabled trigger, and mixed eligibility keeps the reason beside each disabled choice.
Escape closes the innermost submenu first, returns focus to its trigger, then closes the root menu and restores the original target's focus.
Close and archive stay neutral, while removing an archived session keeps the destructive color and existing confirmation.
The Tasks action formerly labelled `pane으로` reads `터미널로 이동`; the four supported interface languages use the same structure.
The approved B layout is implemented by the shared action-menu components; original visual boards are maintained separately.
